The remaining gap is the *primitive*: `sinsemillaCrh` substitutes the model's hash for Sinsemilla **Corrected 2026-09-24: the width clause is now modelled and half its injectivity is *proved*, and the residue is two things rather than one.** `HashOps.bitsOfWidth`/`merkleCrhMessage` carry the deployed message exactly — `⟨altitude⟩₁₀ ‖ ⟨left⟩₂₅₅ ‖ ⟨right⟩₂₅₅`, with `ALTITUDE_BITS = 10`, `LEAF_BITS = 255` and the domain string `MERKLE_CRH_DOMAIN` as named constants, each checkable against `constants/sinsemilla.rs:44,47,89` — and `merkleCrhMessage_length` pins the total at **520 bits, budget 0**. `merkleCrhMessage_injective` then proves the message injective in its three components on the tree's own domain (`a < 2^10`, both children `< 2^255`), **from the encoding alone and with no cryptographic assumption** — a real narrowing, because that half had been coming *from* the primitive: a three-element list of `Int`s is injective for free, so the model was taking combinatorial content from a hash assumption. What remains is two things, and the second was not known before this pass: **(a)** the primitive is still Poseidon substituted for Sinsemilla; **(b)** the model's hash has **no codomain bound**, so the faithful message *cannot* be wired into the fold — `foldMerkleRoot`'s intermediate values are CRH outputs, so after one level "the children are below `2^255`" is unavailable, where the deployed Sinsemilla lands in `pallas::Base` and stays in range. Closing **(b)** needs a *new* assumption about `poseidon_hash_output`'s range, which is why `sinsemillaCrh` still hashes three `Int`s and why this row is not closed by a redefinition. The status stays `PARTLY`: the row's first clause names Sinsemilla and the model still hashes Poseidon **APPENDED 2026-09-24 (later): gap (b)'s statement is written down nowhere, which is why the decision it needs cannot be made — and that is a smaller problem than the phrase "a new assumption" suggests.** This cell and `HashOps.lean:296-302` agree on what is missing: a **codomain** fact about `Axioms.poseidon_hash_output`, without which the faithful message cannot be wired into `foldMerkleRoot`, because "the children are below `2^255`" is unavailable after one level where the deployed Sinsemilla lands in `pallas::Base` and stays in range. Measured: **no candidate statement exists in the tree** — not `< 2^255`, not `< PALLAS_MODULUS` — and `Axioms.lean:165`'s `opaque poseidon_hash_output (inputs : List Int) : Int` carries no range claim in any of its four fields, so nothing anywhere bounds its output. Its consumers are all injectivity-direction results (`HashOps.commitment_binding`, `nullifier_binding`, `smtCrh_injective`, `merkle_root_change_detection`, `Capability/KeyScope.lean`'s three, `Purse.lean`, `PromissoryNote.lean`), and the one range-shaped result in the area, `merkleCrhMessage_length` (`HashOps.lean:389`), is about the **input** width (520 bits) and does not supply the output bound. **So the open question is not "should this assumption be added" but "what exactly would it say"** — and only once that is written is accepting or rejecting it a decision rather than a guess. Two forms are available and they are not equivalent, offered here as a design note rather than a measurement because the tree states neither: a bound by `< 2 ^ 255` is the weaker claim and the one the deployed gadget's own range makes true, where `< PALLAS_MODULUS` is exact and correspondingly stronger — and a discharge would have to consume whichever is chosen, so the choice binds the work that follows. Recording the form is what turns this residue into a design question with an answer; the row stays `PARTLY`, and no assumption is added here | M |

| OBL-Z8 | **ACCEPTED-WITH-REASON** | ZK binaries are well-formed | `scripts/validate_zk_bins.sh` | the script — structural validity only, not that `.zk.bin` matches the current `.zk` source | M | **OPEN — re-verified by reading the gate itself, 2026-09-22.** Validation is `"$ZKAS" validate "$bin"` (`scripts/validate_zk_bins.sh:28`), i.e. the compiler's own structural check of the compiled artefact; nothing in the script compares the `.zk.bin` against its `.zk` source, and nothing hashes either. The `rebuild` mode reads the source only to *regenerate* a binary the validator already called corrupt (`:39-55`) — it is a repair path, not a comparison. So a `.zk.bin` that is structurally valid but was compiled from an older revision passes this gate, which is the class the register names alongside `Z3` and `Z4`: gates that check presence or shape rather than correctness. Contrast `scripts/check-artifact-freshness.sh`, which does hash contract sources against a recorded digest. **Measured 2026-09-23, and the disposition changes: the class this row worries about cannot arise from the repository's own contents, and the script's remaining job is now wired.** Three facts, each checked rather than assumed. (i) **Zero `.zk.bin` files are tracked in git**, against 224 tracked `.zk` — so a binary can never be inherited stale from a commit; it exists only as a build output in a working tree. (ii) The Makefiles declare the dependency properly: `proof/%.zk.bin: proof/%.zk $(ZKAS) check-zkas-version`, so a `.zk` newer than its `.bin` triggers a rebuild, and `ZK_SRC := $(wildcard proof/*.zk)` sits in every contract's `SOURCE_MANIFEST`, so an edited circuit *also* makes the contract stale and gate 1 names that contract. (iii) What was genuinely missing is the other direction — nothing looked at the compiled artefact — and that is what this script does: `"$ZKAS" validate` is the compiler's own structural check, run over all 167 present binaries, which pass. `validate_zk_bins.sh` is now a gate in `run-all-tests.sh`, immediately after the build that produces them; it ran nowhere before, which is this register's recurring "a gate nobody runs" shape rather than a defect in the check itself. **Status `OPEN` → `ACCEPTED-WITH-REASON`**: the proposition the row states ("ZK binaries are well-formed") is what the script checks, and it now runs in the full gate; the stronger proposition the narrative was about ("the binary matches the current source") is discharged twice by mechanisms that already exist, and a third hashing implementation would be a second source of truth for one fact, which is what `safety.md` RC5 is about. The residual, stated rather than implied: a working tree that edits a `.zk` and runs the *validator* alone — without make — sees the old binary pass, because freshness is make's job and not this script's. **APPENDED 2026-09-24 (a later unit), and it corrects two things about the gate this row is about.** First, **coverage**: the gate was reasoned about as checking the binaries and it walked `src/contract/*/proof/*.zk.bin` alone — **166 of the 178 circuits** this layer analyses, with `proofs/core/` (10) and `bin/darkirc/proof/` (2) silently outside it, while their binaries are built and, measured, all twelve pass `zkas validate`. The runner's comment beside the gate says it "runs the compiler's own `validate` over every one of them", so this is a repair of stated intent rather than a widening, and the summary now reports what it covered: `178 OK, 0 corrupted, 0 of 178 source(s) have no binary here to check`. Second, **the vacuity**: the loop walks `.zk.bin` files and `.zk.bin` is gitignored, so a tree whose binaries are not built made this gate green having checked nothing — `Summary: 0 OK, 0 corrupted`, exit 0, measured 2026-09-24 by running the script against an empty directory, which is the same code path. In `run-all-tests.sh` that is mitigated by gate order (`build contract ZK circuits` runs immediately before it); the coverage line is what makes it visible everywhere else. **And the residual this row names in its final sentence now has a third discharge, of a different proposition.** The row says "the binary matches the current source" is covered twice — make's rule and the source-hash manifests — and that a third *hashing* implementation would be a second source of truth for one fact. Neither mechanism compares the compiled circuit to the *source-level analysis* the rule reasons with: make proves the binary is newer, the hashes prove the contract is fresh, and neither proves the artifact is the one the rule is about. That is `OBL-Z22`, and it is not a hashing implementation |
| OBL-Z12 | **PARTLY** | A circuit that proves a value is the *integer quotient* of two others proves it about the integers those field elements stand for, not about their residues | `BaseDivGadget.qr_unique` and the five repaired quotient-remainder circuits | **the Lean layer states the hypothesis and nothing on chain establishes it; the syntactic half is now mechanized.** `qr_unique : 0 < d → q*d ≤ n → n < (q+1)*d → q = n/d` is a theorem about `Nat`; the circuits constrain the same inequalities over `ZMod p`. The bridge is the operand bound — every operand `range_check(64)`ed makes every product `< 2^129 < 2^253` — and `BaseDivGadget.qr_needs_bound` is the kernel-checked counterexample showing the bridge is *necessary* (with `n = 50000`, `d = 2`, `q = (p+49999)/2 ≈ 1.45e76`, both comparisons are satisfied modulo `p`). `script/circuit_instance_derivation.py` now also enforces the syntactic rule — every operand of a `less_than_*` must rest on range-checked witnesses. It first reported 15 operands in 10 circuits; five of those were the check's own artefact (it counted only range checks that *precede* the comparison, and a circuit's constraints are a conjunction, not a prefix), and the rest were repaired or removed on 2026-09-20 — `bin/darkirc`'s RLN rate limit was **live** (an unbounded `message_id` mints unbounded internal nullifiers, defeating the slash mechanism) and is bounded; `dao_escrow`'s premium-before-expiry check was the only enforcement of its property and is bounded; `bridge`'s anti-dust comparison is bounded but its floor is still prover-chosen (OBL-Z16); the escrow/otc_swap timelocks were redundant with the host's own read of chain state and were deleted rather than kept as a second, weaker claim. What remains is one declared exception, `proofs/core/lead.zk`, whose operands are full-width by design (`script/circuit_comparison_exceptions.txt`, which requires a register ID per entry). What is **not** proved is that the 253-bit chip plus `range_check` *implies* the integer reading: that composition is the obligation, and it is open — **corrected 2026-09-24: it is proved, and the status is `PARTLY` rather than `SATISFIED` only because of the declared exception above.** `BaseDivGadget.less_than_or_equal_integer_reading` establishes exactly what this cell called the open obligation: from the chip's constraints *over `ZMod PALLAS_MODULUS`* together with a 253-bit range check on the offset and 64-bit ranges on the two operands, the comparison is the **integer** comparison — stated in the two-clause shape `Gadgets.output_correct` uses, one level down from `less_than_or_equal_sound` (which has the range check as a `ℤ` inequality; the circuit has it as a bit decomposition). `BaseDivGadget.zmod_eq_int_of_bounded` is the lemma underneath: a bounded field element has one bounded integer preimage, because a multiple of `p` inside `(-2^64, 2^253 + 2^64)` must be zero, and `2^253 + 2^64 < 2^254 ≤ p`. **That step writes `PALLAS_MODULUS` as a numeral and uses no primality**, so `pallasPrime` is not reached and both theorems cost `Classical.choice` alone. So both halves of the chain are now mechanized — this theorem for the *semantics*, `script/circuit_instance_derivation.py` for the *syntax* (every `less_than_*` operand must rest on range-checked witnesses) — and the arithmetic that joins them is inside the theorem's hypotheses, since the operand bounds it requires are what `range_check(64)` supplies by definition. What keeps this row from `SATISFIED` is the one declared exception this cell names: `proofs/core/lead.zk`, operands full-width by design, registered per entry in `script/circuit_comparison_exceptions.txt` **APPENDED 2026-09-24: the chip's own half — the bound a range check *supplies* — is now a theorem too, and the modelling of it corrected two constants in the file that described it.** This cell said both halves of the chain were mechanized: the *semantics* (`BaseDivGadget.less_than_or_equal_integer_reading`) and the *syntax* (`script/circuit_instance_derivation.py`). Both were, and both took the operand bounds as given — `FieldLessThanOrEqual`'s `a_bits_lt : a_bits < 2 ^ 64` is a field the prover supplies, not a consequence. What was missing is one level further in: **that the deployed range check's decomposition produces that bound.** `Comparison.lean` had recorded the gap itself, next to a theorem it had deleted for assuming its own conclusion — "the real content needs the chunk decomposition, which lives in `src/zk/gadget/` rather than here" — and the whole new section is that transcription: `chunkSum` is the identity the chip's own comment writes, `chunkSum_lt_pow` bounds a full decomposition by its width, `chunkSum_lt_pow_of_short_last` carries the **short check on the last chunk** that makes the bound `2^NUM_BITS` exact rather than `2^(WINDOW·m)`, and `range_check_64_is_bounded` / `range_check_253_is_bounded` are the two deployed instances. So the stored `Z12` chain now has its third piece: semantics, syntax, and the bound's derivation. **And the modelling found the file's own constants were wrong** — the same species as the registered forward-plan corrections: `Comparison.lean`'s RangeCheck comment said "For 64-bit range check: K=8, 8 chunks. For 253-bit range check: K=3, 85 chunks", and the deployed window is **K = 10** (`src/zk/vm.rs:28` imports it from `src/sdk/src/crypto/constants/sinsemilla.rs:31`, and `vm.rs:116`/`:119` use it for exactly these two chips), so the instances are `⌈64/10⌉ = 7` windows with a 4-bit last chunk, and `⌈253/10⌉ = 26` with a 3-bit one. Neither constant had ever been read against the chip. **What remains is smaller and different, so this row keeps `PARTLY`**: not "the composition is unmade" but "the composition's inputs are supplied by hypothesis rather than read from the circuit" — the step from a circuit's `range_check(64, ·)` call to a chunk list of this shape is a **transcription** (the same `(r, s) ↦ the circuit's statements` one `Circuits/InstanceDerivation.lean` records for `OBL-T7`), and the declared exception `proofs/core/lead.zk` still holds this row off `SATISFIED` on its own. All eight new declarations are budget-measured (two at 0, six at 1) and the anti-vacuity arm was run over every one: nothing trivial, nothing a projection, no unreferenced binder **AND APPENDED AGAIN, 2026-09-24: the bound is now a consequence of the check's witness rather than a hypothesis about the value, which is the last thing this cell called supplied.** `FieldLessThanOrEqual` carries `a_bits : Nat`, `a_bits_lt : a_bits < 2^64` and `a_eq : a = ↑a_bits` — the shape `zmod_eq_int_of_bounded` consumes — and the previous append left those as *supplied*. Three new theorems remove that: `Comparison.chunkSum_nonneg` (the value a chunk sequence stands for is never negative, so the ℤ-valued model yields a `Nat`), `Comparison.exists_bounded_bits_of_range_check` (given the witness the chip accepts, some `bits : Nat` stands for the same value and is below `2^N`), and `Comparison.range_check_64_gives_bounded_bits` (the same at `<10, 64>`, which is exactly the triple the structure's fields need). All three at budget 1, measured; the arm run over each. **What that does *not* close, stated because the wording invites the mistake: it is not the bridge.** Nothing in these theorems reads a `.zk` file, so the passage from a circuit's `range_check(64, ·)` call to the chunk list is still the transcription `Circuits/InstanceDerivation.lean` records for `OBL-T7`. The residue's wording is therefore narrowed a second time — not "the inputs are supplied by hypothesis" but "the inputs come from the check's witness rather than from the circuit source" — and `PARTLY` still stands, with the declared exception above still holding it off `SATISFIED` **APPENDED 2026-09-24 (evening): the residue is narrowed a third time, and what is left is one step — the mapping — rather than a property of the model.** Three measurements, each taken today. (a) **The blocker this cell named no longer exists**: the declared exception for `proofs/core/lead.zk` is **retired** (`script/circuit_comparison_exceptions.txt:24-36` records the retirement) and the circuit deleted (`3ba1c0b435`), so that exception is *not* what holds this row off `SATISFIED`. (b) **The syntactic half is clean with zero exceptions**: `scripts/check-circuit-instance-derivation.sh` reports `comparison operands with no range_check beneath them: 0 in 0 circuit(s)`, over 178 circuits and 880 `constrain_instance` sites. (c) **The model can now *derive* the bound the integer reading needs instead of assuming it.** `Satisfies` gained a range-check conjunct (`∀ w e, Stmt.rangeCheck w e ∈ cs → eval opVal v e < 2 ^ w`) and `rangeCheck_operand_is_bounded` / `rangeCheck_64_bounds_the_operand` eliminate from it, so the operand bound `BaseDivGadget.less_than_or_equal_integer_reading` consumes comes **from the circuit's statement list** — which `Transcribed.lean` supplies, freshness-gated — rather than from a hypothesis. That is the direction that was missing, and it is the third and last time this cell's wording moves: "the inputs are supplied by hypothesis" → "the inputs come from the check's witness" → **the inputs come from the transcription, and only the transcription's own provenance is unmade**. **So what remains is exactly the `(r, s) ↦ a circuit` mapping and nothing else** — the residue `OBL-T7` records. This row stays `PARTLY` **deliberately rather than because work is outstanding**: closing it here while `OBL-T7` stays open would have two rows claiming one missing step, which is the two-rows-one-property disagreement this register has recorded before, and the step for whoever closes the pair belongs to `OBL-T7`. Measured after the change: `scripts/lean-build.sh build DarkFi Transcribed` exit 0 under the cgroup; the axiom gate `Passed: 8  Failed: 0  Skipped: 0` over **705 theorems**, every budget matching its axiom set, both new theorems at budget 0 as annotated, and no theorem a tautology | M |

| OBL-Z15 | **CLOSED** | A note commitment means one thing across the circuit, the client and the host | `bearer_bond/proof/{blind_output,redeem}.zk`; `commitment` handling throughout `bearer_bond` | **CLOSED 2026-09-20, and it was four objects with one name.** Found by reading: the circuit's `coin` is `poseidon_hash(4, coin_public, value, asset_id, spend_hook, user_data, commitment_blind)` — the same seven-argument hash promissory note's circuits and `CapCommitment` use; `CommitmentAttributes::to_commitment` was the same hash **plus `maturity_block`**, a local addition to the shared convention, so the client's commitment could never equal the circuit's `coin`; the host's metadata pushed `params.commitment.token_commit` (a third hash, over a different domain) where the circuit exposes the note commitment, and pushed `token_commit` twice while omitting the tx pair; neither the note commitment nor the *receipt* commitment was carried in the params at all, so the host had nothing to push; `redeem`'s client put `spend_hook` where the circuit wants the tx pair and the metadata a third order again; and the tx pair was a literal zero on one side against `poseidon_hash([3, 0, 0])` on the other. **Not exploitable — unusable**: no vector could satisfy verification, so nothing moved. Repaired by porting promissory note's shape: `to_commitment` drops the maturity (which is stored on the record and enforced against the chain height, not committed); `BondCommitment` carries the note commitment and `UnstakeParamsV1`/`EmergencyUnstakeParamsV1` carry the receipt's, because the host cannot recompute either (their preimages hold blinds the holder drew); the metadata pushes the circuits' orders with the right values; the clients compute the honest all-zero tx binding the rest of the tree uses; and `script/circuit_metadata_exceptions.txt` is empty again. The gate compares counts and would not have caught the values — `src/contract/test-harness/tests/bearer_bond_commitment_vectors.rs` pins the values | H |
| OBL-Z17 | **RESTATED** | A capability check authorizes the holder it names, and only for the capability it was issued for | `identity/proof/verify_capability.zk`; `identity/src/entrypoint.rs:530-563`; its consumers — `dao_escrow` (`verify_member_capability_v1`), `labor_market` (`accept_job_with_capability`), `tender` (`submit_bid_with_capability`), `insurance_market` (both `with_capability` paths) | **FAILS, and it is the authorization primitive itself.** `doc/src/contract/identity.md:208-213` states the mechanism: *"a ZK proof that a capability exists and has not been revoked. The verifier … learns exactly one bit: 'authorised' or 'not authorised'."* Of that: (i) **the predicate is proved** — `less_than_or_equal(threshold, attribute_value)` pinned to `predicate_result`, both operands range-checked; (ii) **existence is checked by the host, not by the proof** — `db_get(capabilities_db, capability_id)` rejects an unknown id, but `capability_id` is a circuit *witness* that the proof never exposes and never binds to the credential (the circuit computes `computed_capability = poseidon_hash(DOMAIN_NULLIFIER, capability_secret, capability_id)` and then **uses it nowhere**), so the params' id and the proof's credential are unrelated; (iii) **revocation is checked nowhere** — the circuit exposes a nullifier and no host reads it, so a revoked credential verifies exactly like a live one; (iv) **`issuer_pub` and `predicate_result` are never exposed**, so the verifier cannot see which issuer or which predicate the proof was about; and (v) the exec path sets `verified: true` **unconditionally** (`entrypoint.rs:555-562`) — its own comment says *"Possession verified via Box::Take child call"*, and no such child call is validated. So every consumer's gate reduces to: *is there a capability with this id, and did the prover attach a proof about some credential?* — which any caller can satisfy. The consumers add only more declaration: `tender` compares the claimed id against its own config, `dao_escrow` and `labor_market` require a child call **to this same function**, and `insurance_market` checks only that the market *has* a required capability configured. `labor_market`'s routing is the shape the remedy takes — it is the only consumer whose call is even structurally a check. The remedy has four parts, in order: bind `capability_id` to the credential in-circuit (use or expose `computed_capability` and compare it against the registered capability's commitment); expose and check `issuer_pub` against the capability record; make the exposed nullifier a real revocation check (`db_contains_key` on the identity nullifiers tree, the way every other consumable is checked); and implement possession as the comment intends — a `Box::Take` child call, since the credential *is* a box. Until then no capability gate in this repository gates anything, and the `auditor_bond`, `institutional_inv`, `access_tier`, `qualified_provider` and `verified_contractor` gates the documentation lists as working are decorative. None of the affected contracts is in `GENESIS_CONTRACT_NAMES`. **RESTATED 2026-09-23, re-read against the tree rather than this row's own appended stages: four of the five sub-claims below are refuted by code that has since changed, and the fifth is narrower than it reads.** The row's `entrypoint.rs:530-563` now lands inside an unrelated function; the verify path is `:562-687`. *(i) held before and still does*: `verify_capability.zk:107-112` range-checks `attribute_1` and `threshold`, `bool_check`s `predicate_result`, and constrains `less_than_or_equal(threshold, attribute_1)` to it — now over the **committed** attribute, not a free witness. *(ii) refuted on both halves*: `capability_id` is not a witness in the circuit at all (it is not in the witness block, `verify_capability.zk:41-66`), and the `computed_capability` line the row calls "used nowhere" **has been deleted**, with its removal recorded in place (`verify_capability.zk:114-117`). What binds a proof to a capability now is the credential's own content plus the record's `CredentialRequirement`, compared host-side (`entrypoint.rs:615-627`) — so the params' id and the proof's credential **are** related, through the requirement. *(iii) refuted*: revocation is checked, via the record's `revoked` flag and `expires_at` (`entrypoint.rs:600-609`), and the exposed nullifier **is** read — as the credentials-tree key (`:594-597`). The remedy this row proposed (`db_contains_key` on the identity nullifiers tree) was deliberately not built, and the code gives the reason (`:589-593`): issuance marks that same nullifier spent, so membership means *issued*, not *consumed* — the proposed check would have rejected every legitimate credential and passed a fabricated one. *(iv) refuted*: `issuer_pub_x/y` and `predicate_result` are exposed (`verify_capability.zk:123-124`, `:130`) and compared host-side (`entrypoint.rs:622-627`, `:641-644`); both sides were printed and agree 10-for-10 in the circuit's instance order. *(v) half refuted, half stands*: `verified: true` is reached only after six ordered reject-and-return checks, not unconditionally — but **possession is still not required**, and that is the one live residual. **What remains, and why it is not a wiring job.** The circuit requires knowledge of the credential's full preimage — issuer, holder, schema, both attributes, the blind, `issued_at`, `expires_at` and `credential_secret` — and the exposed nullifier `H(1, credential_secret, commitment)` is the tree lookup key, so a caller must *know the holder's secret*, which is what possession means in a ZK system. **And that "holder's secret" is not an analogy — it is an identity, which the first pass at this row left one notch too vague.** The holder's public key is *derived from* the credential secret: `let holder_pub = PublicKey::from_secret(SecretKey::from_base(credential_secret));` (`bin/dwowd/src/tests/specs/identity_spec.rs:134`) — so knowing `credential_secret` **is** being the holder, and the gate this row asked about — "a caller must not be able to produce one for a capability it does not hold" — is held by the proof, not by the missing box call. Two further facts read out while establishing it, recorded because each bounds the finding. (i) *The client does not enforce the pairing*: `IssueCredentialCallData::new` takes `credential_secret` and `holder_public` as separate parameters (`client/issue_credential.rs:85-98`) and never relates them, so an issuer can commit a `holder_pub` that is not `pk(credential_secret)` — and nothing breaks, because (ii) *the committed holder is read by nobody*: `Credential.holder_pub` (`model/mod.rs:248`) is hashed into `credential_data` and compared against no live value anywhere, so it is **inert** in the disposition this register gives `subscription`'s subscriber pubkey — an "exposed and read by nobody" field, not a hole. That is what makes the pair harmless rather than a defect, and it is why the residual below is the *stronger* lock rather than this one. What the row's remedy names is the *stronger* form: the credential **is a box**, and `IDENTITY_CONTRACT_BOX_CONTRACT_ID` is stored (`entrypoint.rs:76`) for the `Box::Take` child call that would prove control. That machinery does not exist to wire up: `IDENTITY_CONTRACT_BOX_CONTRACT_ID` is stored and **never read**; `children` appears **nowhere** in `src/contract/identity/src/`; no box is minted at credential issuance; and **no contract in the tree validates a `Box::Take` child call** (`grep -rln 'BoxFunction::Take' src/contract/*/src/entrypoint.rs` → nothing). So the residual is not "require a call that exists" but "build the credential-as-box lifecycle across identity, box and the wallet", which is a design change and not this row's repair. Severity moves C→M on that reading: the gate that holds is knowledge of the credential preimage, and the missing half is a second lock. **One wart found while reading, split into its own row rather than left here** — `entrypoint.rs:681` fills `VerifyCapabilityUpdateV1.holder_pub` from `params.capability_proof.issuer_pub`, i.e. the **issuer**, under a field named for the holder: `OBL-C86`. | M |

**Stage 1 landed 2026-09-21; this row now stands for what remains.** `verify_capability.zk` exposes the credential's `schema_hash`, `issuer_pub_x/y`, `threshold` and `predicate_result` beside its nullifier (8 public inputs), its dead `computed_capability` binding is deleted, and `process_verify_capability_instruction` — which loaded the capability record and discarded it (`let _cap_data = ...`) — now decodes it and rejects a proof whose schema is not the capability's, whose issuer is not the capability's trusted issuer, whose predicate did not hold, or whose threshold is below the capability's `min_threshold`; the nullifier is checked unspent in the identity nullifiers tree and written by the apply phase. `CapabilityProof` gained `threshold` — without it a caller could pass `threshold = 0` and satisfy any requirement — and `VerifyCapabilityUpdateV1` gained the nullifier, since a check nothing ever makes true is not a check. **Stage 2's binding landed the same day; one part remains.** `verify_capability.zk` now reconstructs the credential commitment from its preimage — issuer, holder, schema, both attributes, the attribute blind, the credential secret and the validity window — with the same two hashes and the same `constrain_equal_base` `issue_credential.zk:41-61` uses, and the predicate is evaluated over the *committed* `attribute_1` rather than over a free witness. `commitment` is exposed, and the host requires it to be the stored credential's, so the proof is about a credential an issuer actually signed; the host's own checks now run against the stored record rather than only the caller's claims. The former free witnesses (`capability_id`, `capability_secret`, `nullifier`, `attribute_value`) are gone — a witness nothing reads is a value the prover chooses.

**What still remains is *possession*, and it is a wiring campaign rather than a check.** The model is decided — single-use, spent on verify — and it is the credential's box that gets spent. But **there is no `children_indexes` anywhere in identity**: issuance puts no box (`apply_issue_credential_update`'s comment claims a `Box::Put` child call that does not exist) and verification requires no take, so the box-based possession model was documented and never built in either direction.

The check itself is small and compiles: require exactly one child call, whose function byte is `Box::Take` (0x02) and whose `contract_id` is the box contract from the info tree, then decode `TakeParams` and require `contents_commit == credential.commitment.inner()`. That last comparison is what makes it possession *of this credential* rather than of any box: `escrow`'s shape — the only in-tree precedent — checks the function byte and the contract id and stops there, so it admits a caller who takes an unrelated box.

**It is deliberately not landed**, and this is the reason: the identity heavyweight test passes today against a chain, and a verification that requires a real take needs a real box in the box tree with a real merkle path. No spec has one — `BoxHarness::take()` is a fixed fixture (`contents_commit = poseidon_hash([100])`, its own root), and box's contract rejects a take whose `expected_root` is not the store's. Landing the host check without that plumbing would turn a verified flow red, which is a worse state than an absent check with the reason written down. `attribute identity` is also only *partly* closed: the predicate is over `attribute_1` and the name is exposed and compared, but nothing maps `CredentialRequirement.attribute_name` to a *position* in the schema, so a capability and a credential must simply agree on which slot the name sits in. Both are the remaining work | C |

Both halves read. The limit is stated in the entry rather than dressed up: the operator already chooses `result`, so bounds it also chooses grant no freedom the `result` expose did not, and the in-circuit `range_check(64, diff_max/diff_min)` constrains three prover-chosen values against each other. Registering bounds on chain and comparing against the record was the alternative; it adds a field to a genesis contract's record and is a design change, not a repair. *`attest_value.zk`'s `threshold` is **not** declared, and the refusal is the finding.* It appears in the circuit **nowhere but its own declaration and the expose** — PHASE 3 ("Predicate logic") is comments with no constraint under them (`attest_value.zk:76-86`) — and the host's only use of it is a log line (`entrypoint.rs:419-421`): `attest_value_v1` stores `oracle_id`, `attestation_id` and `nullifier` and drops the predicate and threshold entirely (`:423-427`), so the value reaches no state. **There is no host mechanism to name**, and this file's own header says an entry without one is "precisely the Orchard-class bug wearing an audit badge" — so no entry was written and the gate stays red on it, which is its design. Reading the rest of the circuit shows the gate is reporting a symptom: the witness `value` (`attest_value.zk:45`) is unused too, and is invisible to the gate only because it is not exposed. The circuit's predicate logic was removed and its two operands left standing, so the honest repair is to remove `threshold`, `value` and the expose **together with the params fields they mirror** — an interface change to a genesis contract (the client builds `AttestValueParamsV1`, `oracle/src/client/attest_value.rs`, and dropping the expose alone would leave an *unbound* params field, which is worse than the bound-but-unused one there now) rather than a repair of a defect. Recorded as the group's one blocked instance, with both repairs named and neither taken **Two more of the thirteen adjudicated 2026-09-23, and one statement in this row corrected — the gate is red on 11 now rather than 13.** Re-run rather than recalled (`bash scripts/check-circuit-instance-derivation.sh`): 181 circuits, 897 instances, `declared-free: 38 → 40`, `unclassified: 13 → 11`. *`roulette/settle_bet.zk`'s `payout` is declared free, and the mechanism is the host's own arithmetic.* The circuit exposes `payout` and uses it nowhere else (`settle_bet.zk:31`); the metadata arm publishes `params.payout` (`entrypoint.rs:138`) and `settle_bets_v1` **never reads it** — it sums `bet.payout` out of `bets_db` over the stored bets, binds that sum to the child transfer (`validate_child_value_commit(&child_call.data, house_payout, value_blind)`, `:611`) and takes the same figure off `house_capital` (`:613`), and `SettleBetsUpdateV1` carries the table rather than the amount. So the amount enforced is the host's, and the expose grants no freedom the params did not already grant. This row's earlier classification — "exposed and read by nobody" — was right about `params.payout` and stopped one step short of the mechanism, which is the difference between a note and an entry. *`labor_market/milestone_payment.zk`'s `milestone_payment_amount` is declared free on a two-sided read.* The circuit's only determination of it is the expose; the `ConfirmMilestoneV1` arm publishes `pallas::Base::from(params.payment_release)` in that position (`entrypoint.rs:389`), and the runtime supplies the metadata's vector to `verify_zkp` as the proof's public inputs (`src/linear/src/zk_verifier.rs:234-238`), so the instance is *forced* to equal the params value; `confirm_milestone_v1` then binds that same value to the child transfer (`:1467-1472`). The residual there is a separate proposition and is filed as `OBL-C96`. *And the correction, because this row asserted it twice:* **`milestone_payment`'s circuit is no longer registered by no dispatch path.** `entrypoint.rs:371-397` registers `LABOR_CONTRACT_ZKAS_MILESTONE_PAYMENT_NS_V2` and publishes the circuit's seven instances in its order, and the arm's own comment records the repair — *"This arm published `ConfirmDeliveryV2` — a DIFFERENT circuit"* — as the fix for exactly this row's note. What remains unclassified is the *instance*, not the registration, and the row conflated the two. The other eleven stay red by design and are unchanged: `bridge/withdraw`'s `token_minimum` (the prover sets the floor it must clear), `insurance_market`'s two `required_capability_id` (live, and this row's own to repair by `labor_market`'s `Identity::VerifyCapabilityV1` child-call pattern), `labor_market/create_job`'s `attestation_id` (stored without reference to the attestation contract), `oracle/attest_value`'s `threshold` (blocked, above), and the six in `proofs/core` (`lead.zk`, `set_v1.zk`) whose host does not exist in this repository — re-verified in this pass rather than inherited: `grep -rn "Set_V1\|Lead"` over `src/` and `bin/` returns no Rust reference to either namespace, so there is no verifier for their exposes to fool **— and the same eleven were re-measured 2026-09-24 from the *other* direction, which is what makes this row's list trustworthy rather than merely recent.** The measurement was taken while correcting `proofs/lean/src/DarkFi/Circuits/All.lean`, whose prose claims "Orchard-class audit result: NO FREE INSTANCES across all contracts" and "THEOREM: All 98 remaining contract circuits are Orchard-class safe"; asking what that claim would need to be true and running the gate gives **181 circuits / 905 `constrain_instance` / 40 declared-free / 11 unclassified**, and the eleven are the ones named above to the letter (`bridge/withdraw`'s `token_minimum`; `insurance_market`'s two `required_capability_id`; `labor_market/create_job`'s `attestation_id`; `oracle/attest_value`'s `threshold`; `proofs/core/lead`'s `sigma1`/`sigma2`; `proofs/core/set_v1`'s `lock`/`root`/`key`/`value`). Two independent readings agreeing is not a proof, but it is the difference between a count inherited from the last edit and one taken now — and the `All.lean` claims are refuted by the gate that row's own instrument runs, which is the correction that unit landed **READ 2026-09-24 for the six `proofs/core` sites, and the honest disposition is to leave them RED — which is a finding about the repair, not about the sites.** `set_v1.zk`'s exposures are the circuit's *purpose*, not a leak: `account = poseidon_hash(secret)` is derived (and is not among the eleven), while `lock`, `root`, `key` and `value` are the name-registry values the prover is setting — `bool_check`ed for the two flags, and unconstrained for the payload because a registry write has no earlier value to derive from. `lead.zk`'s `sigma1`/`sigma2` are the same shape in the lead-proof circuit. **So no binding exists to add: binding them to anything would change what the circuit proves.** The other route — declaring them free in `script/circuit_free_instances.txt` — is barred by that file's own rules, which require a *host-side mechanism* and say verbatim: "X where no host-side mechanism was found. **Leave it failing.** … an entry whose justification was not actually verified is worse than a red gate — it is precisely the Orchard-class bug wearing an audit badge." And there is no host to name: `proofs/core/**` is referenced by no crate, no manifest and no Makefile (`grep -rln 'proofs/core'` over `src/ bin/ scripts/ Makefile` finds only the three gate scripts themselves, and the `Set_V1` namespace appears nowhere outside the circuit). **What that leaves as the candidate repairs, none of which is a drive-by**: bind the values to the registry's own state — which requires naming who the registry authority is, and is a redesign of what the circuit proves; or delete the affected circuits as phantom if nothing is meant to consume them, which is the tree's own rule for code nothing calls and is a decision rather than an edit; or accept the red as the accurate statement that nothing validates these exposures. The row records the obstruction instead of narrowing itself to what is cheap, exactly as `OBL-C67`'s local-PoW half does **SUPERSEDED THE SAME DAY, and the reasoning above is kept because it is *why* the resolution is what it is: the user chose the third candidate — delete the two orphans — and it is done.** `proofs/core/{lead,set_v1}.zk` and their stale `.zk.bin` are removed. The reading above is what foreclosed the other two: binding would have changed what the circuits prove, and the free-instance list bars an entry with no host mechanism to name — so deletion was the only route that leaves the gate honest, and the evidence that they were deletable was already in the tree: neither is compiled by any build step (`scripts/build-contract-zk.sh` loops `src/contract/*/proof/`; the Makefile compiles only `bin/darkirc/proof/*.zk`), neither is loaded by any crate, manifest or test, `lead.zk` was a Nov-2022 prototype whose in-tree host (`consensus/leadcoin.rs`) was deleted, and `circuit_comparison_exceptions.txt`'s own entry for it said its consumer "lives outside this repository". **Measured after the deletion, with the gate itself**: 179 circuits (was 181), 886 `constrain_instance` (was 905), **5 unclassified** (was 11), and the Lean transcription regenerated to match (179 circuits / 2692 statements / 11 hold / 168 fail, freshness gate exit 0). The five that remain are `insurance_market`'s two, `bridge/withdraw`'s `token_minimum`, `labor_market/create_job`'s `attestation_id` and `oracle/attest_value`'s `threshold` — of which the bridge and labor_market sites were taken the same day as real host repairs, so the row's open residue is now theirs plus the genesis `oracle` site **THE BRIDGE HALF IS DONE, and it replaced the expose with a floor rather than exempting it.** `withdraw.zk`'s `token_minimum` — a witness the *prover* chose, bounded and compared but never enforced — is removed, and the policy is now `BRIDGE_CONTRACT_MIN_WITHDRAWAL` (100, `lib.rs`), enforced by `process_withdraw_instruction` against the plaintext `params.amount`. A constant rather than a registered value because `WithdrawParams` carries **no chain field**, so a per-token floor has nothing to index by and a `config` call would be a new authorised surface on a deferred contract; the doc comment records both rejections and the bounds that fix the number. The circuit's copy went rather than being bounded-and-kept, which is what escrow's and otc_swap's timelock repairs did for the same shape — the file's own note already said a redundant copy is "a second, weaker claim about a property already enforced". **Measured**: `check-circuit-metadata-alignment.sh` reports `bridge/withdraw — 4 constrain_instance, 4 metadata pushes, 4 client public inputs` where it read 5/5/5, and this row's gate falls from **5 unclassified to 4**. The control is a `WithdrawV1BelowMinimum` rejection endpoint at amount 1 (not 0, which the old path refused too), and its comment records that it is **masked** by `OBL-C21`'s redeem-child defect — the child aborts before the parent's check, so the rejection cannot yet be attributed to the floor. One cost is recorded because it is a boundary rather than an incident: the params field's removal required an edit to `src/sdk/python/src/contract/bridge/withdraw_v1.rs` (a binding that enumerates fields), `src/sdk/**` is in every contract's `SOURCE_MANIFEST`, and so all 32 artefacts became hash-stale and were rebuilt in the same change. Measured pin-neutral first: `dwow-sdk-py` is a leaf nothing links and `dwow-sdk`'s lib has no python module, and rebuilding purse — genesis — lands on `28819142…`, the pristine-sdk value `OBL-C61` records **APPENDED 2026-09-24, last reading of the day: the count is re-measured once more, and the movement is the point rather than the number.** `scripts/check-circuit-instance-derivation.sh` now reports **178 circuits, 880 `constrain_instance` sites, 4 unclassified** — this cell's last figure was 181/905/11, and the forward plan's item 4 still opens at 897/15. The five named sites are now four: `insurance_market`'s two `required_capability_id`, `labor_market` `create_job`'s `attestation_id`, and `oracle/attest_value`'s `threshold`. Two left the list without being repaired: `bridge/withdraw`'s `token_minimum` by being **removed** rather than declared free (`withdraw.zk:42-44` keeps the explanation where the comparison stood), and the two `proofs/core` orphans by the circuit deletion `3ba1c0b435` records. Recorded because four readings of this one number now stand in this register — 15, 11, 5, 4 — and each was true when written; a reader who takes the oldest as current sizes the remaining work wrong, which is the failure the forward-plan paragraph's own rule names. Of the four, one (`oracle`'s) is a genesis contract's and belongs to the deferred batch **THREE, AS OF THE SECOND RE-ROLL BATCH, and the labor_market half needed a new callee rather than a new binding.** `create_job.zk` exposes `attestation_id` — an id the job is *about*, with nothing in-circuit to derive it from — and the host stored it and never read it again. So `attestation` gained **`CheckAttestationV1 (0x0d)`**, the bare-id lookup `VerifyClaimV1`/`ValidateClaimV1` cannot perform (both take a `claim_id`, and no claim exists yet at job creation); `create_job_v1` now requires **two** children — the promissory_note deposit *and* this check — each identified by its function byte and each routed against its own configured contract id, and it decodes the child's params and refuses a mismatch. The callee enforces the rest: it reverts when the id names nothing or names an attestation that is not `Active`, and a reverting child reverts the transaction, which is why no consumer needs to read return data (`OBL-C86`). **Measured**: gate 4 reports **3 unclassified**, declared-free moved 40 → 41, and the residue is `insurance_market`'s two plus `oracle`'s one. **The fixture gap is recorded, not papered over**: the spec's `CreateJobV1` endpoint still supplies `children: vec![]` and so cannot succeed — and could not before this change either, since the function has required the deposit child for longer than that endpoint has existed — while an end-to-end control needs an attestation created first through the attestation contract, which that spec has no setup for. **The batch's tail, since this row is where its members were listed**: it rode the second re-roll with `OBL-C121` and the new callee, both contracts were rebuilt clean, all 32 artefacts are fresh, and the pin was re-recorded from the ceremony's own value — `c8afdeb73f2c9f29839a0761b585beaccc7407b5bcf008bf9fbcaf992e166d9b` replacing `c43ae8c36fa89f0807b5520d6ff7c7bb22187a243cac747ef4360d274c34a0c7` — with `genesis_pin_is_current` and `genesis_pin_rejects_a_wrong_hash` both green afterwards. `OBL-C62` was already CLOSED and is not a member; `OBL-C16` is down to one site, in `dao_escrow`, which another session holds dirty and which is therefore sequenced rather than forced. **AND THE ONE MEMBER THE BATCH DID NOT TAKE IS DEFERRED BY DECISION, WITH ITS REASON RECORDED.** `oracle/attest_value.zk`'s `threshold` is a genesis contract's site and was a candidate for the second re-roll; it was left out rather than taken blind, because it is not a mechanical fix and its reconnaissance was not done before the pin was spent. `threshold` is the oracle's *claim parameter* — `value == threshold`, `value >= threshold`, `value <= threshold` — so nothing in-circuit can derive it, and the host only publishes and logs it (`oracle/src/entrypoint.rs:192`, `:419-421`). Its disposition therefore needs the analysis the `labor_market` site got and took a day to settle: **who consumes an attestation, and what checks the threshold it carries** — before the site is either bound to a stored config or declared free against a mechanism a reviewer can go and read. It waits for a future batch rather than riding a third re-roll now, which is this row's own batching rule applied to itself **SUPERSEDED WITHIN THE HOUR, AND THE COST I RECORDED WAS WRONG — the oracle site needed no pin move at all.** The reconnaissance above was done rather than deferred, and it shows the disposition is a `declared-free` entry of the shape this file already carries twice for the same contract: `attest_value.zk`'s own `predicate`, and the aggregate bounds' argument that the value "is the operator's own declaration about its own output … so these two grant no freedom the `result` expose did not already grant". Measured: `authorize_oracle` (`oracle/src/entrypoint.rs:272-284`, called at `:406`) makes the prover the **registered oracle**, `:192` publishes `params.threshold` as the circuit's public input so a verifier reads the claim, every field element is a well-formed threshold so there is no bound to impose, and nothing consumes an attestation (no child call to `attest_value` exists outside the oracle, and no other contract reads a `threshold`). **Gate 4 falls 3 → 2**, leaving only `insurance_market`'s two, with no rebuild and no pin move. The deferral's reason — "it needs consumer analysis first, not a mechanical edit" — was right about the analysis and **wrong about the cost**, and the difference is recorded rather than quietly fixed: I quoted a genesis edit before doing the work that would have shown there was none, which is this register's oldest failure mode wearing the plan's clothes **FIXED 2026-09-24: the gate is green — `unclassified: 0`, from 11 at the start of the day and 15 when this pass opened.** The last two sites were `insurance_market`'s `required_capability_id` pair, and the reason they were unclassifiable is the reason they are now declared free: the witness *is* the requirement, so nothing in-circuit can derive it, and the host already enforces it — a child `Identity::VerifyCapabilityV1 (0x06)` routed to the configured Identity contract, whose verified `capability_id` is compared against the market's **own stored record** (`underwrite_with_capability.rs:148-164`; the buyer-side sibling at `purchase_coverage_with_capability.rs:131-144` checks `is_none()` first and is the fail-closed arm of the two). Both entries name that mechanism with file and line, and record the asymmetry rather than smoothing it. **The pin did not move** — `insurance_market` is not genesis. Note for a reader: this row's head still read "15 instances" hours after the count was 3; the head was corrected with the fix rather than left to age. **APPENDED 2026-09-24: the head is corrected a second time, and this row's own note is why.** The subject cell read "**15** instances across ..." as a current fact while the append beside it already said `unclassified: 0` — the same defect the sentence above describes, arriving on the same row. Re-measured now: `scripts/check-circuit-instance-derivation.sh` exits 0 with **0** unclassified over **178** circuits and **880** `constrain_instance` sites (359 derived, 200 bound, 54 derived-inline, 223 redundant, 44 declared free). Each of the five sites the runner's gate-4 comment named is resolved where it was made: four are entries in `script/circuit_free_instances.txt`, and `bridge/withdraw`'s `token_minimum` was removed rather than declared, which that file's oracle entry cites as the alternative it did not take. | H |
| OBL-Z14 | **CLOSED** | A report that names a reporter is one that reporter filed | `stablecoin/src/entrypoint.rs:1428`; `stablecoin/proof/governance_report.zk:48-53` | **Audited 2026-09-23: CLOSED, not FAILS.** The circuit now *instances* the reporter pair (`governance_report.zk:61-62`) and the entrypoint rejects a mismatched reporter (`entrypoint.rs:1401`), so the mechanism described below is the state before `e65147af92`. The circuit derives `reporter_public` from `reporter_secret` and constrains it against `reporter_pub_x`/`reporter_pub_y` — two **witnesses the circuit never exposes**. So the equality is between two prover-chosen values and holds for any secret, and the entrypoint neither checks the exposed set (which does not contain them) nor compares `params.reporter_pub` against anything: it is written into the update and stored. Any account can file a governance report and attribute it to anyone. The *content* is now constrained — the amounts must match the config DB and the ratio is pinned to them (OBL-Z13) — so this is attribution rather than value, which is why it is M and not C. The remedy is OBL-Z9's: bind the registration key by commitment + nullifier, per "the constraint on any fix" above, and check it in `process_governance_report_instruction`. | M |
**CLOSED 2026-09-21 (`e65147af92`), by the point-exposed shape rather than commitment + nullifier.** The reporter pair is now `constrain_instance`d, so the circuit's equality is against the verifier's values instead of a witness the prover picks; `InitializeParams` carries `governance_pub_x/y` and `init_contract` stores them under `GOVERNANCE_PUBKEY_KEY` — which until then was defined, imported, and never written or read, so the remedy as first written had nothing to compare against; and `process_governance_report_instruction` rejects a report whose reporter is not that point. Exposing a point is not the reverted static-key repair: that repair put a *registration* key into every operation's instance vector and correlated a principal's whole history, whereas this is the one call that is *about* attribution, compared against the deployer's own authority. The spec fixture had to declare its reporter as the authority, which is the check working. | ~~M~~ C→closed |
| OBL-Z9 | **CLOSED** | A circuit whose purpose is to authorize an actor actually constrains the prover to that actor — by whatever the address model says identifies them, **not** by disclosing a static key | `oracle/proof/{push_value,attest_value,push_value_commitment,aggregate,set_oracle_active}.zk`; `multisig/proof/{sign,finalize}.zk` | **CLOSED for `oracle` 2026-09-21; `multisig` still FAILS.** The circuits constrained `constrain_equal_base(ec_get_x(ec_mul_base(secret, K)), pub_x)` where `pub_x` was a **witness the circuit never exposes** — an equality between two prover-chosen values, holding for *any* secret — and the entrypoint could not check either, because `PushValueParamsV1` carried no key. The oracle half now registers a hiding commitment `H(witness_base(4), oracle_secret, oracle_id)` in place of a public key, re-derives and exposes it on every operation, and the host compares it against the stored record; the four push/attest circuits additionally consume a per-operation nullifier. **Nothing static is disclosed**, which is the whole point of the remedy — see "The constraint on any fix" below. `multisig/proof/sign.zk` is untouched: its `params.signer_pub` is still instruction data the proof does not bind, so a non-member can still claim a member's key — see OBL-Z11 | C |
| OBL-Z10 | **CLOSED** | Every oracle state change is authorized by the registered operator | `oracle/src/entrypoint.rs` (`push_value_v1`, `attest_value_v1`, `aggregate_v1`); `oracle/proof/*.zk` | **CLOSED 2026-09-21.** Following OBL-Z9: `push_value_v1` looked the oracle up, checked `is_active`, and did `oracle.value = params.value` — nothing else — and `set_oracle_active_v1` had **no circuit at all** (*"Non-ZK function, no public inputs"*), its only check being `oracle.oracle_pub != params.oracle_pub`, on a prover-supplied copy of a **public** key, which anyone could pass to deactivate any feed. Now every state-changing arm calls `authorize_oracle`, which requires the exposed `oracle_commitment` to equal the stored record (`NotAuthorized`) and, for the four push/attest arms, the operation's nullifier to be unspent (`DuplicateNullifier`). `set_oracle_active` gained a circuit. Verified by two rejection endpoints in `tests::heavyweight_oracle` — the verbatim causes are in the "Done for `oracle`" note below | C |
| OBL-Z11 | **CLOSED** | A signer's nullifier is spendable only by that signer | `multisig/src/entrypoint/mod.rs`; `multisig/proof/sign.zk` | **CLOSED 2026-09-22**, and it was more severe than this row first recorded. It read "a non-member can claim any member's key, pass the check, and spend that member's nullifier for the message — blocking the member from signing it". Blocking was the *lesser* consequence: the attacker repeats over the group's other members, every claim passes because the check compares a caller-typed key against the stored list, and `FinalizeV1` counts the recorded nullifiers toward the threshold. **A non-member could forge threshold approval outright.** The group now stores a hiding commitment per member (`H(witness_base(4), member_secret)`), `sign.zk` derives and exposes it along with `nullifier = H(witness_base(1), member_secret, group_id, message_hash)`, and the host checks the commitment against the group's set and the nullifier unspent. `FinalizeV1` can no longer recompute a member's nullifier — it is derived from a secret the host does not hold — so it now takes the approvals from the caller and verifies each against a signature record for *this* group and message | C |
| OBL-Z18 | **FAILS** | Every `constrain_equal_base` has at least one operand the verifier can see — a literal, a named constant, or a value exposed as a public input | **57 sites across 12 contracts**, detected by `scripts/check-pubkey-binding.sh`; the largest concentrations are `stablecoin` 13, `subscription` 8, `dex` 7, `escrow` 6, `purse` 5, `native_token` 5, `otc_swap` 4 | **NEW 2026-09-22, and this row is the general form of Z9 and Z14 rather than a new class.** Both of those state the defect in prose — Z9: "an equality between two prover-chosen values, holding for *any* secret"; Z14 is a named instance. This is the same rule applied mechanically: if neither operand is a literal, a named constant, or exposed, the prover chooses both and the constraint is satisfied by any witness, so the verifier learns nothing. It was found by writing the rule down — the check that existed (`hooks/pre-commit`) enforced **the opposite** and would have rejected the derive-and-expose form that Z9's remedy adopted (see that hook's header for the worked example, `oracle/proof/push_value.zk:53`, which keeps the old form as a comment annotated `-- witness == witness`). **The 57 are not yet adjudicated** *(superseded — the sweep completed 2026-09-23: 37 resolve, 19 do not of which 15 are defects, 1 deferred; the count line above is the authority)*, and the row does not claim they are all defects: the detector treats a value as "seen" only when it is a `constrain_instance` target, so a circuit that is fully private by design — no host-side use of the value — has nothing to forge and its comparisons may be harmless. Each site needs its circuit *and* its host read together, which is why the detector is report-only in the hook. One is read and is live: `subscription/proof/verify_access.zk:33-34` compares `derived_pub_x/y` against the witnesses `subscriber_pub_x/y`, while `subscription/src/entrypoint.rs:169-170` consumes exactly those params-side fields — so the host trusts a value nothing authenticated. Until the sweep is adjudicated the hook *(and it now is — 2026-09-23, so the report-only mode has served its purpose; the promotion to a ratchet landed the same day and is recorded in this cell's closing paragraphs)* **reports and does not block**, which is recorded rather than quiet: a gate that fails on unadjudicated findings is a gate whose authority cannot be defended yet **The sweep is complete: 56 of the 57 adjudicated, and the row's status is `FAILS` at the sites that do not resolve.** Of the 57 candidates, **37 resolve** (arms (a), (b) and (c), with (b) transitive), **19 do not**, and **1 is deferred**. The 19 break down as **15 defects** — 3 in `OBL-C81`, 2 in `OBL-C82`, 6 in `OBL-C83`, 3 in `OBL-C84` and 1 as `OBL-C75`'s first measured instance — plus **4 inert**. By slice: 14 in the genesis-contract slice (3 unresolved, `OBL-C81`), 13 in `stablecoin` (8: `OBL-C82`, `OBL-C83`), 8 in `subscription` (8: `OBL-C84` ×3, `OBL-C75` ×1, 4 inert), 7 in `dex` (**none**), 6 in `escrow` (**none**) and 8 more across `otc_swap` 4, `roulette` 2, `bearer_bond` 1 and `proofs/core` 1 (**none**). The detector's 57 is now accounted for by construction — 14+13+8+7+6+8+1 = 57 — which settles the discrepancy this row recorded between its own breakdown and the detector's count. **The status move from `OPEN` to `FAILS` is the sweep's own consequence**: `OPEN` meant the candidates were unadjudicated and the hook's report-only mode could not be defended; all 57 are now read, 15 are genuine and each has a row, so the proposition as written is measured false in 15 named places. **And the promotion is made, not merely proposed** (2026-09-23, the same day): `script/circuit_pubkey_binding_exceptions.txt` carries all 57 findings, content-keyed on `<path> : <equality as written>` — not on line numbers — each with either the mechanism that makes it sound or the row that schedules its repair; `scripts/check-pubkey-binding.sh` prints a matching finding as `EXCEPTED` and fails on any other; `hooks/pre-commit` now **blocks** on an unexcepted finding rather than reporting, which is the condition it was waiting for; and `run-all-tests.sh` runs it as the `circuit pubkey binding` gate. **Four controls, because this is the promotion of a security gate**: with the list removed the gate exits 1 with all 57; with one entry dropped it exits 1 naming exactly that site; a synthetic new vacuous binding in a scratch circuit exits 1 naming it (so a new site cannot pass silently); and the hook's absolute-path form keys the same as a bare run, which is the one failure a content-keyed list is actually prone to. *One deliberate limitation, recorded rather than discovered later*: the detector was **not** made cleverer. A classifier propagating "consumed by an exposed determination" back through a hash to its arguments would cut the list from 57 to 15, and it was rejected — that propagation is a claim about crypto binding a line-anchored script cannot establish, and a gate that *silently* classifies a genuine vacuity as bound is worse than one reporting 57 candidates a reviewer must read. The sibling `check-circuit-instance-derivation.sh` does make that claim (it calls such a value `redundant`); this one does not, and the per-entry reasons are the difference. The rejected design is written into the exception file's header, so the next reader does not re-litigate it. The slice was taken first because it is the one whose repair would move the genesis pin, so it decides whether a re-roll is owed: purse 5, `native_token` 5, `identity` 2, `promissory_note` 1, `oracle` 1. **Thirteen resolve by the exposed-value arm**, each read to the exposure rather than assumed: `native_token/burn.zk:97` and `promissory_note/revoke.zk:83` derive `signature_public`, a `constrain_instance` target; `native_token/mint.zk:62-63` bind `public_x/y`, which enter the coin commitment `C` that is exposed, while `spend_secret` enters the exposed nullifier `nf`; `mint.zk:118` binds `value`, which enters the exposed `value_commit`; `native_token/fee.zk:116` was adjudicated above for the same reason; `identity/issue_credential.zk:44-45` bind `issuer_pub_x/y`, which enter `credential_data` and thence `commitment_check`, exposed; `oracle/aggregate.zk:126` binds `sum_weights`, the denominator of a division whose `result` is exposed; `purse/deposit.zk:87` and `purse/withdraw.zk:93` bind `new_balance`, which enters both the exposed `new_commit` and the exposed `new_leaf`. **The fourteenth does not**, and it is the shape this detector exists for: `purse/{balance,deposit,withdraw}.zk` each constrain `derived_owner == owner_pub` with neither operand exposed and `owner_pub` used nowhere else — while the purse record stores an `owner_commit` that nothing in the tree checks. It has its own row, `OBL-C81`, because the proposition differs: not "a comparison is vacuous" but "an ownership binding the record expects is absent". **No genesis contract needs a fix from this slice, so no re-roll is owed for it.** **13 more adjudicated 2026-09-23 — the `stablecoin` slice, and eight of its candidates do not resolve.** `stablecoin` was the largest remaining concentration and it splits three ways. **Five resolve**, by two different arms. Four are the `owner_pub_check == owner_pub` shape at `{add_collateral,open_position,remove_collateral,repay_stable}.zk:43`, where `owner_pub` enters `position_check = poseidon_hash(DOMAIN_COMMITMENT, collateral_commit, debt_commit, owner_pub, collateral_type)`, a `constrain_instance` target on the lines immediately following — so the value is bound through the exposure, arm (b). The fifth is `accrue_interest.zk:67`, `base_add(old_total_debt, interest_amount) == new_total_debt`, resolved by arm (a) and **not** by exposure: neither operand is visible to the verifier, but `process_accrue_interest_instruction` checks `params.old_total_debt` against `CDP_TOTAL_DEBT_KEY` (`entrypoint.rs:1553`) and `params.new_total_debt - params.old_total_debt == params.interest_amount` (`:1566`) — which is that equality, on the params side. **Eight do not resolve, in two shapes, and each shape has a row**: the accumulator authorization at `accrue_interest.zk:35-36` (`OBL-C82`, H) and the unbound position values at `mint_stable.zk:48,51,57,63` and `liquidate.zk:50,60` (`OBL-C83`, M). **A third slice, `subscription` (8), the same day: none of the eight resolve, in three shapes, and the payment reading is the negative that matters.** The slice settles a doubt this row recorded — it said the cited site "needs re-adjudicating *with the credential lifecycle traced*" — and the trace is short because the lifecycle is empty. *The exposure set is measured on both sides and both agree it is `{tx_binding, tx_nonce}`.* `verify_access.zk` instances those two and nothing else (`:44-46`), and the host's `get_metadata` publishes `vec![zero, zero]` for that arm with the comment naming exactly that pair (`entrypoint.rs:156-162`) — and the same two zeros for SubscribeV1 (`:149-155`). So `expected_capability`, `subscription_id`, `subscriber_pub_x/y` and `value_commit_x/y` are exposed nowhere, by circuit or by metadata. *Three shapes.* (i) `verify_access.zk:33,34,47` — the capability, which is `OBL-C84` (M); the host does nothing with the call in either phase. (ii) `subscribe.zk:52` — the id, which is `OBL-C75`'s first measured instance: the stored key is `params.commitment` (`entrypoint.rs:358-360`, `:392-396`) and the circuit's `derived_id` reaches no verifier. (iii) `subscribe.zk:39,40` (the subscriber pubkey) and `:59,63` (the value commitment) — **inert, and recorded as inert rather than filed**: both fields are written into the record (`:361`, `:366`), copied on renew (`:555`, `:560`), and read by nothing in the tree (found by search, not by sampling), which is this row's own "exposed and read by nobody" disposition rather than a defect. *The negative that matters, recorded because it is the first thing a reader will reach for.* The naive reading — "the circuit does not bind the deposit, so a subscription can be had for nothing" — is **false**: `subscribe_v1` ignores any params-supplied price, reads the plan out of the plans tree (`:332-340`) and validates the child transfer against `plan.price`, with a blind derived from that same price (`:349-353`). The payment is enforced by the host on stored data, which is arm (a). **A fourth slice, `dex` (7), adjudicated the same day — and it is the first slice in which every candidate resolves.** All seven are the execute/cancel lock equalities (`execute_swap.zk:50,63`, `execute_swap_fee.zk:55,68`, `execute_swap_slippage.zk:50,63`, `cancel_swap.zk:44`), and they resolve by both arms at once, which is why the slice is worth recording as a negative rather than passed over: a detector whose candidates are all clean here is a detector whose output is not a defect list, and that is the row's own thesis. *Arm (a), directly:* each of the three execute hosts compares the params-supplied lock against the stored swap and rejects a mismatch by name — `if params.alice_lock != swap.proposer_lock` and `params.bob_lock != acceptor_lock` (`execute_swap.rs:223-233`, `execute_swap_fee.rs:167-176`, `execute_swap_slippage.rs:136-145`). *Arm (b), which is what makes that comparison bite:* in all four circuits the locks are consumed by exposed values — the nullifier pair (`alice_nullifier_check = poseidon_hash(DOMAIN_NULLIFIER, alice_secret, alice_lock)`) and the swap id (`computed_swap_id = poseidon_hash(DOMAIN_CAP_COMMIT, alice_lock, bob_token, bob_amount)`) — and `get_metadata` publishes both as the proof's public inputs in the circuit's instance order (`execute_swap.rs:165-176`). *And the chain is closed on the create side*, which is the half a reader would otherwise have to assume: `create_swap.zk:48` exposes `computed_lock` and `:51-57` exposes `computed_swap_id = poseidon_hash(DOMAIN_CAP_COMMIT, computed_lock, request_token, request_amount)`, so the stored key commits to the stored lock and a prover cannot substitute a lock of its own without changing the id the host looks the swap up by. The nullifier's preimage holds the stored lock, so executing requires the participant's secret. `cancel_swap.zk:44` is the same shape, with the host requiring the exposed nullifier to be one of the swap's stored participant nullifiers (`cancel_swap.rs:181-193`). **A fifth slice, `escrow` (6), adjudicated the same day — clean as well, and it is where the rule's transitive form shows up.** All six are pubkey equalities (`create_escrow.zk:69,70`, `claim.zk:87,88`, `refund.zk:83,84`) and all resolve by arm (b), but two resolve *through the other operand* rather than on their own, which is a case the detector's rule as written does not name. *The direct ones:* `create_escrow.zk:69-70` compares `buyer_pub_x/y` with the derived `buyer_pub_computed_x/y`, and `buyer_pub_x/y` enter `C = poseidon_hash(DOMAIN_COMMITMENT, buyer_pub_x, buyer_pub_y, seller_commitment, value, asset_id, timeout)`, a `constrain_instance` target (`:86-96`). *The transitive ones:* `claim.zk:87-88` compares `seller_pub_x == seller_x`, where `seller_x/y` — the recipient witnesses — are used nowhere else; but `seller_pub_x/y` enter `seller_commitment_computed`, which is constrained equal to the **exposed** `escrow_seller_commitment` (`:78-79`, `:92`), so the equality carries the exposure across to `seller_x`. The recipient is bound, and bound hard: the host computes that same commitment from `params.recipient_pubkey` and publishes it as the second expected public input (`entrypoint.rs:261-273`), so the params recipient must equal the pubkey derived from `seller_secret`. `refund.zk:83-84` is the same shape one step over — `escrow_buyer_pub_x` is used nowhere else, but `buyer_pub_x` is constrained equal to the exposed `input_buyer_pub_x` (`:88-89`, `:99`), which the host likewise publishes from `params.recipient_pubkey` (`:298-311`). **So the general form is: an operand that is merely *equal to* a bound value is itself bound** — an equality with one bound side and one unbound side is precisely how the second gets bound, and a detector that looks only for `constrain_instance` targets on the operands will flag every such pair. **The last five slices, the same day — `otc_swap` (4), `roulette` (2), `bearer_bond` (1), `proofs/core` (1) adjudicated, `dao_escrow` (1) deferred — and every adjudicable one resolves.** *`otc_swap` and the direct arm:* `create_swap.zk:73-74` compares `alice_pub_x/y` with the derived `alice_pub_computed_x/y`, and `alice_pub_x/y` enter `C = poseidon_hash(DOMAIN_COMMITMENT, alice_pub_x, alice_pub_y, bob_commitment, …)`, exposed at `:93`; `execute_swap.zk:65-66` is the same with `bob_pub_x/y` and `bob_commitment = poseidon_hash(DOMAIN_COMMITMENT, bob_pub_x, bob_pub_y)`, exposed at `:76`. *`bearer_bond` and the transitive arm:* `burn.zk:103` binds `derived_signature_secret = poseidon_hash(DOMAIN_SIGNATURE_SECRET, spend_secret, nullifier)` to `signature_secret`, and both operands are already determined — `nullifier` is exposed (`:63-64`), `spend_secret` enters `pub` → `coin` → the merkle root instance (`:49-59`), and `signature_secret` is consumed by the exposed `signature_public` (`:106-107`). *`proofs/core/tx.zk:75`* (`c1_value == c3_value + c4_value`) also resolves: each coin's value is bound because its commitment's x/y are exposed (`:54-55`, `:62-64`, `:71-73`) and the Pedersen opening is inside the hash that commitment validates. **Two incidental observations from these slices, recorded rather than filed.** (i) `proofs/core/tx.zk:63` reads `c3_cm_y = ec_get_x(c3_cm)` where `:72` reads `ec_get_y(c4_cm)` for the same quantity, so coin 3's exposed "y" is its x — the commitment is then bound only *up to sign*, which is not exploitable without a Poseidon preimage but is a real copy-paste in a file the zkas documentation includes. It is recorded here rather than as a row because the circuit `tx` is referenced by no `.rs`, `.toml` or `Makefile` in the tree — it has no host, the same disposition `OBL-Z16` gives `proofs/core/{lead,set_v1}.zk`. (ii) `roulette/proof/place_bet.zk:34,41` are the only candidates in `roulette`, and they resolve for a reason worth naming: the host takes **neither** value from the circuit, deriving both itself — `Bet::new(table_id, player_pub, bet_type, numbers, amount, spin_count, block, instance_seed)` (`entrypoint.rs:364-373`) takes no id and no nullifier, and the record is keyed on the `bet_id` it derives and the spent-nullifier check is against the `nullifier` it derives (`:385`, `:405-406`). So the circuit's copies go unused, and the host can be seen *not* to trust them — which is `OBL-C75`'s proposition satisfied rather than violated. *The deferred candidate:* `dao_escrow/proof/pay_premium.zk:59` (`computed_bulla == dao_escrow_bulla`) is **not adjudicated**, because `dao_escrow`'s entrypoint is under active edit by another agent and uncommitted in the tree, so its half of the pair cannot be read from a stable state. The circuit half is read and does not resolve — the exposed set is the tx pair only, and `computed_bulla`'s operands (`member_pub_x/y`, the three `mpc_secret`s) are consumed by nothing exposed — but it is the host half that decides it, and the plan for this work leaves that agent's files untouched. It is recorded as deferred rather than guessed at, and the same residue is the reason `dao_escrow`'s own rows are not touched by this campaign. The sweep is complete except for it, and this row's own breakdown sums to 56 against the 57 the detector reports, a discrepancy in the breakdown that is recorded rather than smoothed over. The hook stays report-only until the remainder is read | M | **The sweep is still open, and a first pass on 2026-09-22 produced the rule it needs plus one problem with this row's own worked example.** *The rule*, calibrated on the one contract the row already classifies as held-elsewhere, and stated so the next pass need not re-derive it: a flagged `constrain_equal_base` is **not** a defect when either (a) the same relationship is enforced by the host on public/params data, or (b) it is enforced by the circuit elsewhere through an exposed value, or (c) violating it can only harm the violator. *The calibration*: `native_token/proof/mint.zk:62-63` binds `pk_x/y` to the witnesses `public_x/y`, neither exposed — but the note's owner is the only party who can spend it, so a wrong pair yields an unspendable note and harms nobody else; and `mint.zk:118`'s `effective_plus_pin == value` is enforced for real by the *host* on the plaintext params (`validation.rs:455-460` plus `pow_reward_v1:1000-1006`, `OBL-C4`), so the circuit's copy is decorative rather than load-bearing. Both are correctly *not* defects, and the rule above recovers both. *The problem*: the row's single adjudicated-live site cites `subscription/src/entrypoint.rs:169-170`, and reading it, those lines are inside **`UpdateUsageV1`'s `derived_id` recomputation in `get_metadata`** — where the host *publishes* `derived_id` as an expected public input for the update circuit — not the access path at all. `VerifyAccessV1` has **no host state update whatsoever**: its `process_instruction` arm only logs (`:230-232`), and its `process_update` arm likewise (`:273-275`). So for `verify_access.zk` the "the host trusts a value nothing authenticated" argument does not hold as written, and the site needs re-adjudicating *with the credential lifecycle traced* rather than by its citation. The adjacent host path that does carry weight is `update_usage_v1` (`:605-630`), which recomputes an expected nullifier from the **params-supplied** `subscriber_secret` and compares it to the params-supplied `spent_nullifier` — a different question from the one this row asks, and the one to follow next. Recorded rather than resolved: I read four files here and the classification of even one site turns on `Subscription::compute_nullifier` and what the stored record binds, which is a tranche of its own, not an aside. **The `stablecoin` slice earned one general mechanism, and it should be applied first to every remaining site — 2026-09-23.** The mechanism is what made this pass short, and its two halves are converses. *First half: an equality against an exposed value transmits the exposure, so it is not vacuous.* `add_collateral.zk:69` is the worked case — `constrain_equal_base(position_check, position_commitment)` compares an exposed value against a witness, and a prover who changes the witness changes the exposed `position_check`, which the verifier rejects; the witness is therefore as visible as the exposure is. Four of the thirteen resolve on that alone, and it is the same reason the genesis slice's `identity/issue_credential.zk:44-45` resolved. *Second half: a value used nowhere but inside an unexposed equality is bound to nothing, even when it is derived correctly.* So a site is live exactly when its operands are neither a literal, nor exposed, nor consumed by a second constraint that is itself either — and the answer to "is it consumed elsewhere" has to be read, not inferred from the equality's shape. Applying the filter to `stablecoin` surfaced both of the rows this slice filed, and neither was visible from the flagged line itself: the accrual is authorized by nothing *and* rate-determined by nothing, and a position operation is bound to no position it proves. Both were found by reading the two sides of a flagged site rather than the site. **A third form the `dex` and `escrow` slices earned, and it belongs in this rule: the binding is transitive.** An operand that is merely *equal to* a bound value is itself bound, so an equality with one bound side and one free side is not vacuous — it is exactly how the free side becomes bound. `claim.zk:87-88` is the worked case, in the adjudication log above.
