The consequence for the next reader is that the filter must be run to a fixed point over the equality graph rather than one step: an operand is seen if it reaches a literal, an exposure, or a host comparison — and it may reach one through another equality rather than directly **The genesis-contract slice re-adjudicated end-to-end 2026-09-23, circuit and host read together for all fourteen, and the result changes the slice's count by one.** Every entry under the nine genesis contracts' `proof/` directories was read on both sides rather than against the exception list's prose. **Thirteen resolve by arm (b)**, each to a named `constrain_instance` line: `identity/issue_credential.zk:44,45` (`issuer_pub_x/y` → `credential_data` → `commitment_check`, exposed at `:72`); `native_token/burn.zk:97` (`signature_secret` → `signature_public`, exposed at `:106-107`); `native_token/fee.zk:116` (`input_value` → `input_coin`/`input_value_commit`, exposed at `:66`, `:71-72`); `native_token/mint.zk:62,63` (`public_x/y` → the exposed coin commitment `C` at `:79`) and `:118` (`value` → exposed `value_commit` at `:89-90`); `oracle/aggregate.zk:126` (`sum_weights` is the divisor of the division whose `result` is exposed at `:186`); `promissory_note/revoke.zk:83` (`signature_secret` → `signature_public`, exposed at `:87`); `purse/deposit.zk:87` and `purse/withdraw.zk:93` (`new_balance` → exposed `new_commit` and `new_leaf`). **The fourteenth does not, and the slice's count against `OBL-C81` is two, not three.** `purse/proof/balance.zk:44` was recorded as an instance of `OBL-C81`; it is not one. Reading the file, `owner_pub` is consumed at `balance.zk:38` — `dp_circuit = poseidon_hash(DOMAIN_COMMITMENT, owner_pub, asset_id, purse_id)`, constrained to the caller's `derived_purse_id` (`:39`) and **exposed** (`:40`) — so the equality at `:44` transmits that exposure and the site resolves by arm (b), transitively. The claim that survived in two places (this row's genesis log and the exception-list entry) was "`owner_pub` appears nowhere else", and it was wrong; both were corrected, and `OBL-C81`'s own extent narrowed to the two circuits it actually holds for. **The consequence for the re-roll question this slice exists to answer is unchanged in direction and smaller in size**: the slice contributed three candidates to the "does a fix move the pin" question, and it now contributes two, both inside `OBL-C81`, a row whose stated repair is itself blocked because the `Purse` record is persisted nowhere. So **the genesis slice owes no circuit change and no re-roll** — which is the same answer it gave before, reached this time by reading all fourteen rather than by reading the ones that looked doubtful |

| OBL-Z19 | **CLOSED** | No two differently-purposed derivations inside one circuit share a domain constant, so a value derived for one purpose cannot be re-read as a value derived for another | `scripts/check-circuit-domain-separation.sh` (the within-circuit collision rule); five adjudicated sites in `script/circuit_domain_exceptions.txt`, of which `oracle/proof/push_value_commitment.zk` is the one whose collision is constructible | M | **The finding is real and it was already known; what was missing is that it is a row.** Five collisions are adjudicated in `script/circuit_domain_exceptions.txt`, each labelled `OBL-Z19` — and `git log -S"OBL-Z19" -- doc/src/arch/verification-hazop.md` returns **nothing**: the id was cited by a gate's exception list and **never given a row**, so `register-status.sh` walked 113 rows and none of them was this one, and `check-doc-index.sh` did not catch it because it resolves ids in `doc/`, not in `script/`. The row exists now because the defect does; the bookkeeping gap is recorded in the same breath rather than repaired silently. **The defect, stated once.** A domain constant is baked into one circuit's zkas and never meets another circuit's, so separation is a *within-circuit* property: a circuit that defines two names for one value has two derivations the prover may choose to make equal. Four of the five exceptions are unseparated; **`push_value_commitment.zk` is constructible**, and it is the one that matters. It defines `DOMAIN_COMMITMENT = witness_base(4)` (`:60`) and `DOMAIN_OPERATOR_COMMITMENT = witness_base(4)` (`:61`), and both are 3-argument Poseidon hashes over prover-chosen field elements: `computed_commitment = poseidon_hash(DOMAIN_COMMITMENT, value, nonce)` (`:78`) and `oracle_commitment = poseidon_hash(DOMAIN_OPERATOR_COMMITMENT, staker_secret, oracle_id)` (`:72`). Choosing `(value, nonce) = (staker_secret, oracle_id)` makes the two **equal by construction**, and both are exposed (`:94`, `:95`) — so `constrain_equal_base(computed_commitment, commitment)` (`:79`) is discharged by setting the operation's "data point commitment" to the operator's own public registration commitment. The operation then carries no data point at all, and no verifier can tell. **The decision, made here rather than deferred: the operator commitment gets its own domain constant, uniformly across all six oracle circuits and the client that derives it.** The alternative — leaving `4` and recording the collision as accepted — was rejected on the register's own standard, because "the two domains are unseparated" and "the collision cannot be constructed" are different claims and this one is false: no preimage search is needed. *Why it cannot be a one-circuit edit*: `oracle_commitment` is **stored** at registration and compared against the proof's copy on every later operation (`entrypoint.rs:294`-region `NotAuthorized`), so all six circuits that derive it (`register_oracle`, `push_value`, `push_value_commitment`, `attest_value`, `aggregate`, `set_oracle_active`) must agree on the constant, as must the five client `compute_commitment()` bodies that today hard-code `pallas::Base::from(4u64)`. *Why it cannot be a doc-only edit*: the exception list's own note says the value is "not an oversight — OBL-Z9 records the operator commitment as `H(witness_base(4), oracle_secret, oracle_id)` deliberately", i.e. the collision is inherited from `OBL-Z9`'s remedy, which is why this is a decision about that remedy. *Cost, stated before the edit*: `oracle` is genesis contract 5, so a `.zk` change rebuilds its artifact, moves its source hash and moves the genesis pin — it is batched with `OBL-C62` and `OBL-C86` into one re-roll rather than dripped. No registration exists to invalidate: the changed constant changes the *value* of every `oracle_commitment`, and the chain this lands on has never been launched **CLOSED 2026-09-23, in the batch, and the re-roll happened once rather than three times.** `DOMAIN_OPERATOR_COMMITMENT` is `witness_base(8)` in all six oracle circuits and `pallas::Base::from(8u64)` in all six client sites; the generic `DOMAIN_COMMITMENT` stays at 4, where every other commitment in the tree uses it. Each circuit states in a comment why the more specific purpose is the one that moved. *Verified end to end, which is the only verification that means anything here*: `cargo test --release -p dwowd --all-features test_heavyweight_oracle -- --test-threads 4` → **1 passed, 401.61s** — it drives register, push_value, push_value_commitment, attest_value, aggregate and set_oracle_active through the real client against the real circuits, so a constant that disagreed between the two sides would fail there rather than pass a unit test. *The re-roll, recorded once for the three findings that needed it* (`OBL-C62`, `OBL-C86`, and this): **`538b16342123b0db76f6f39dc827ffeb6487ba399b9428799aad525fca8c5735` → `0211c14e3456e9b077c987b594c271831450bacb293726277c05727ad67612da`**. The new value is the one the build computed, read from the disagreement `genesis_pin_is_current` reports and then confirmed green (`1 passed, 1.56s`), not written by hand; no other file in the tree records the old value. *Residual, stated rather than implied*: the four remaining entries in `script/circuit_domain_exceptions.txt` are unseparated but not constructible, and the domain gate still checks presence rather than correctness (`OBL-Z3`, which stands) — what closed here is the one instance that was exploitable **Re-measured on a *later* tree, 2026-09-23: 1 passed, 384.90s.** The first run was taken before a concurrent session's `src/sdk/src/blockchain.rs` edit, and `src/sdk/**` links into every contract, so the earlier figure was a measurement of a superseded state. This one is the same test on the same commit of this work with that edit in the tree — and it is why the number is recorded twice rather than once: a green measurement carries the tree it was taken on |
| OBL-Z20 | **SATISFIED** | `BaseLtStrict` (0x57) is sound: the bit it returns is 1 exactly when `a < b`, so neither value is available when the comparison says otherwise | `proofs/lean/src/DarkFi/Comparison.lean` (`base_lt_strict_sound`); the gate it is about is `src/zk/gadget/less_than.rs`'s `s_lt_out` (`:163-180`) | the proof itself; the gate's shape is read off `less_than.rs:172,177` rather than taken from prose; all three docs that claimed the opcode verified now cite this theorem | M | **NEW 2026-09-24 — minted for absence rather than for a defect, like `OBL-C115`/`C116` and `OBL-T13`: the opcode had no soundness theorem anywhere, and two documentation trees and this layer's own module note all said it was SOUND.** `proofs/lean/src/DarkFi/Comparison.lean`'s section described the gate's shape and then claimed "VERIFIED SOUND by `less_than_strict_sound` below" — **and that theorem is about a different constraint system**: its hypothesis is `offset = a + 2^m - b`, which is the `s_lt` selector's gate (`less_than.rs:126-137`, opcode **0x51**), where this opcode's gate is `s_lt_out` (`:163-180`) constraining `a_offset = out·(b − a − 1) + (1 − out)·(a − b)` with `out·(1 − out) = 0` — the shape the section's own docstring printed correctly. So the correction the section had already made, replacing "VERIFIED SOUND in Main.lean (exhaustive 1000×1000)" with a theorem name, **replaced one unusable citation with another that does not apply**, which is how an absent proof survived a reader finding a citation in both places. Both citations are wrong for their own reason and the record keeps both: `src/Main.lean` — which holds that search at `:91-110` — is in **no `lean_lib`** (`lakefile.lean` declares `DarkFi` and `Transcribed` and has no `lean_exe`; measured 2026-09-24), so `lake build DarkFi` never compiles it, no gate or script names it (`scripts/`, `hooks/`, `Makefile`, `contrib/ci/` — grepped), and it does not compile at all (21 errors, this layer's own note). **What landed**: `base_lt_strict_sound`, stated over the deployed gate's shape with `0 ≤ a_offset` as its only range hypothesis — the upper bound `< 2^253` is deliberately *not* carried, because soundness uses the lower half alone and an unused hypothesis is what this layer deletes rather than keeps (the same reason `less_than_strict_sound` records for dropping `h_a_range`); its role in the *wrap-around* argument is stated at `Gadgets.lean:116` and belongs to `OBL-Z12`'s subject. Budget 1, measured; the anti-vacuity arm run over it: not trivial, not a projection, no unreferenced binder. **And the documentation's evidence columns were corrected with it**: `opcodes.md`'s table and `zkvm_primitives.md`'s both cited "exhaustive 1000×1000" for 0x55 and 0x57 and now cite `Gadgets.less_than_or_equal_sound` and this theorem, with the correction recorded under the table rather than by silent rewrite. The 0x55 row's second clause was stale in the ordinary way as well: it said "range_check audit: all 37 uses safe", where `less_than_or_equal` has **20 call sites across 14 `.zk` files** today, and the audit's own instrument is a gate whose measured output is **0 comparison operands with no range check beneath them, 1 declared exception** (`proofs/core/lead.zk`). `opcode_universe.md:509`'s "✅ **Verified Sound**" for 0x57 needed no edit: it was true as of this theorem, and had been unsupported before it |
| OBL-Z21 | **SATISFIED** | `NotBase` (0x56) is sound: `bool_check` forces the operand to `{0,1}`, the returned cell is its negation, and the result is boolean so it can feed the next gate | `proofs/lean/src/DarkFi/Comparison.lean` (`not_base_correct`); the opcode is `src/zk/vm.rs:1507-1530` (`small_range_check` at `:1515`, `sub(one, value)` at `:1521-1525`) | the proof, which **depends on** `Comparison.boolcheck_sound` rather than restating it — the operand's booleanness is that theorem applied to the polynomial `bool_check` constrains | M | **NEW 2026-09-24 — the second opcode of the comparison family minted for absence in one day, and the pair is the reason the family was then read as a set rather than one opcode at a time.** Four places documented this opcode as verified — `opcodes.md`'s table (`:72`) and its `### NotBase (0x56) ✅ VERIFIED` section (`:453`), `opcodes-status.md:60`, `zkvm_primitives.md:263` ("Input range-checked to `{0,1}`"), `opcode_universe.md:136`/`:508` — and **not one named a theorem, because there was none.** `opcodes.md`'s section gives its "Lean 4 Verification" as `def not_base_satisfied (a out : Int) : Bool := (a = 0 ∨ a = 1) && out = (1 - a)`, which is the species this file has already had to delete once: **a statement-shaped thing that nothing proves**. A `def : Bool` claims nothing, and running it is not evidence unless the running is checked — the same defect `OBL-Z20` records for `base_lt_strict`, where the "verification" was an IO loop in a file that is in no `lean_lib`. **What landed**: `not_base_correct`, stated as three clauses — `value = 0 → out = 1`, `value = 1 → out = 0`, and `out` boolean — with the first two depending on `boolcheck_sound` so that the opcode's "deterministic" verdict is seen to rest on the *other* opcode's theorem rather than on the subtraction alone, which over a field or over `Int` is a subtraction and not a negation. Budget 1, measured; the anti-vacuity arm run over it. **And the docs were corrected with it**: the four sites now cite the theorem, and `opcodes.md`'s section's `def` is labelled as what it is. **What this pair leaves, named rather than implied**: the audit so far covers the **comparison and boolean family** only — EC operations, hash operations, field arithmetic and the constraint operations tables in `opcodes.md`'s Layer 1 make the same kind of "SOUND ✓"/"Correct ✓" claims and **have not been checked against the existence of a theorem**. That is the next unit of this kind, and it should be a set at a time for the reason above |

| OBL-Z22 | **MECHANIZED** | A circuit's compiled artifact is the one the source-level rule is about: its `constrain_instance` cardinality and its identity agree with the source | every `.zk` under `src/contract/*/proof/`, `proofs/core/` and `bin/darkirc/proof/` — 178 circuits | **mechanized** — `scripts/check-circuit-fidelity.py`, gated as `run_gate "circuit fidelity (source vs compiled)"`. It compiles each source with the compiler's `-e` and compares the decoded bytecode against the analysis: the source's `constrain_instance` count must equal the compiled `ConstrainInstance` opcode count, and the compiled `namespace` must equal the source's `circuit "..."` string. Measured 2026-09-24: **178 checked, 0 failures, 0.9 s**, compiler `zkas 0.5.0`; and **negative-controlled** by planting the drift it exists to catch — a copy with one `constrain_instance` removed, compared against the real source's compiled form, reports 2 against 3. It walks *sources*, so it is immune to the empty-tree vacuity this row's neighbour `OBL-Z8` records, and it compiles **temp copies** (`zkas` writes `<input>.zk.bin` beside its input and ignores `-o`, measured) so the gate writes nothing into the tree. **It does not compare the opcode *arguments* to the source's operands** — that needs name-to-index resolution through the witness and constant tables, and it is the natural next step rather than part of this. **APPENDED 2026-09-24, same unit, and it corrects that sentence: the operand comparison was probed and it is not a bounded next step, it is a project.** `zkas -e` renders an opcode's arguments as `(Lit, n)` or `(Var, n)` — *indices*, not names. Measured on `box/take.zk`: `PoseidonHash` takes `(Var, 11) (Var, 5) (Var, 0) (Var, 2)`, and that circuit declares 11 witnesses, so `11` is not a witness entry — these are stack positions, not table lookups. Comparing them to the source's operands therefore needs a model of the zkas VM's stack discipline, which is the same class of work as the Halo2 constraint-system semantics the layer's entire ZK premise is conditional on. So what stands is the cardinality and the identity, and the sentence this append replaces called the rest a step when it is a research item — the same correction this register has now made to four other rows | H | **NEW 2026-09-24, minted with the gate.** The proposition is the one neither of the two mechanisms `OBL-Z8` counts can state: make's pattern rule proves a binary is *newer* than its source and the source-hash manifests prove a contract is *fresh*, and neither proves the compiled circuit is the artifact the instance-derivation rule reasons about. What this row does **not** claim is that the compiler is correct: `zkas` is a prebuilt binary at the repository root, gitignored, so it is *provided* rather than committed, and this check trusts it exactly as the build does. What it *does* claim is that two independent implementations — the Rust compiler and `script/circuit_instance_derivation.py`'s lexer — agree on the cardinality of the property, so a regression in either is caught by the other |

#### The constraint on any fix: no static key, in or out

An attempted repair — exposing the registered `oracle_pub` (and the multisig member key) as a public
input so the host could compare it — was **reverted**, and the reason belongs on the record because
it is the obvious repair to reach for and it is wrong here.

`doc/src/arch/verification-hazop.md` and the address model say what the first attempt ignored:
**addresses are cycled per transaction**, derived by `derive_instance(secret, contract_id, instance)`
(`src/sdk/src/crypto/keypair.rs:202-222`), and a **static address is the anti-pattern** — a privacy
break, not a default. Putting the registered key into every `push_value`/`attest`/`sign` instance
vector would disclose a static identity and correlate every operation that principal ever performs.
There is no "public key exposure" in this design; there are cycled addresses and nullifiers.

**The in-model pattern is already in the tree.** `native_token/proof/burn.zk` authorizes without
disclosing anything static:

    signature_secret = poseidon_hash(DOMAIN_SIGNATURE_SECRET, spend_secret, nullifier);
    signature_public = ec_mul_base(signature_secret, NULLIFIER_K);
    constrain_instance(signature_public_x);
    constrain_instance(signature_public_y);

A **per-instance derived key** — from the root secret *and* the instance — with authorization
resting on the nullifier being unspent plus Merkle inclusion, not on the host comparing a key to a
registered one.

**The remedy is chosen: commitment + nullifier, expressed as a capability type.** Decided
2026-09-20; the type-level half has landed, the circuit and entrypoint halves have not.

* the oracle registers a **hiding commitment** to its secret, `H(DOMAIN_ORACLE, oracle_secret,
  oracle_id)`, rather than a public key — nothing static is disclosed and a non-operator cannot
  open it;
* every push or attestation proves the opening in-circuit and consumes a **per-operation
  nullifier** the host checks unspent, exactly as `native_token/proof/burn.zk` does for a coin;
* the operation's **barbs** carry it in the type system, so the same `coversBarbs` obligation the
  other thirteen capability types carry applies here.

The type-level half is `Capability.Composition.oracleOperatorType` — resource
`{commit, nullify, prove, dispatch}`, primitives `[commitment, nullifier, dleqProof, contractId]`,
with `coversBarbs := by decide` proved (kernel-checked, as with the other twelve). That the proof
closes is itself the useful check: it says the four barbs the operation exhibits are *exactly* the
four those primitives compose to, with nothing smuggled in.

**Done for `oracle`, 2026-09-21** (OBL-Z9's oracle half and OBL-Z10; `multisig`, OBL-Z11's half, is
still open). In the order they depended on each other:

1. `Oracle.oracle_pub: PublicKey` → `oracle_commitment: pallas::Base`
   (`oracle/src/model/mod.rs`), and the same swap in `RegisterOracleParamsV1`,
   `PushValueParamsV1`, `AttestValueParamsV1`, `PushValueCommitmentParamsV1`,
   `AggregateParamsV1` and `SetOracleActiveParamsV1`.
2. `ORACLE_CONTRACT_NULLIFIERS_TREE` added; `init_contract` creates it.
3. All five circuits re-derive `oracle_commitment = poseidon_hash(DOMAIN_OPERATOR_COMMITMENT,
   oracle_secret, oracle_id)` and expose it; the four push/attest circuits also derive and expose a
   per-operation nullifier bound to the operation's own payload (`value` for push, `attestation_id`
   for attest, `commitment` for commitment-push, `result` for aggregate). `set_oracle_active` got a
   **new circuit** (`proof/set_oracle_active.zk`) — it had none.
4. `authorize_oracle` in the entrypoint compares the exposed commitment against the stored record
   (`NotAuthorized`), checks the nullifier unspent (`DuplicateNullifier`, new error, custom 9), and
   `process_update` marks it spent.

Two design points worth recording, because both were decided rather than discovered:

* **`set_oracle_active` carries no nullifier.** A nullifier over `(oracle_id, is_active)` would
  permit one deactivation and then forbid ever reactivating — bricking a feed whose operator merely
  paused it. Authorization there rests on the commitment opening; `tx_binding` covers replay.
* **The operator commitment and the data commitment in `push_value_commitment.zk` both use
  `witness_base(4)`**, and both are `poseidon_hash(4, a, b)`. This is a domain overlap and is
  recorded rather than hidden. It is not exploitable: the two values are compared against different
  state (stored record vs. params) and are never interchangeable in any check, and a party who does
  not know `oracle_secret` cannot produce the operator commitment at all. The pre-existing
  `DOMAIN_COMMITMENT = witness_base(4)` for a *data* commitment is the half that is arguably
  mis-domained; changing it is a separate decision, not part of this remedy.

Also recorded: the circuit that used to be here compared against `oracle_pub_x`/`oracle_pub_y`, and
those lines were deleted rather than kept — a reader who finds the old note must not find the old
code.

**Evidence.** `tests::heavyweight_oracle` PASS, including two *rejection* endpoints added for this
purpose, because a happy-path-only suite cannot tell an authorization check from its absence — all
six endpoints passed before the fix, when the contract authorized nobody. The two rejections read
verbatim from the run's `DWOW_TEST_LOGS=1` output, each after `Successfully got metadata` (so the
proof itself verified, and the refusal is the host's, not the verifier's):

    [oracle] ERROR: Not authorized: commitment does not match the registered operator
    [DIAG] reconstructed error: Custom(3)
    Block 8 rejected at local height 7: canonical call failed at exec ... call_idx=0 fn_code=0x01

    [oracle] ERROR: Nullifier already spent
    [DIAG] reconstructed error: Custom(9)
    Block 8 rejected at local height 7: canonical call failed at exec ... call_idx=0 fn_code=0x01

`scripts/check-circuit-metadata-alignment.sh` PASS (70 pairs, three-way). The derivation gate
`scripts/check-circuit-instance-derivation.sh` stays red on its 15 pre-existing instances and added
none; the five dead `oracle_id` manifest entries were retired, since the id is now inside an exposed
determination and the checker reads the manifest only for bare-witness exposes.

**Done for `multisig`, 2026-09-22** (OBL-Z11). The same shape, with two differences worth stating.

1. `MultiSigGroup.pubkeys: Vec<PublicKey>` → `member_commitments: Vec<pallas::Base>`, and the same
   swap in `CreateGroupParamsV1`, `CreateGroupUpdateV1` and `SignParamsV1`. The record layout is
   unchanged in shape — N × 32 bytes — so the encode/decode offsets are the same fields.
2. `derive_group_id` hashes the first *commitment* rather than the first *key*:
   `H(commitment_0, threshold, total_keys)`. It has to: the commitment cannot depend on
   `group_id`, because `group_id` is derived from it. **The consequence is recorded rather than
   hidden** — a member's commitment is therefore identical in every group they join, so two groups
   with an overlapping member are linkable by that shared pseudonym. A member who must not be
   linked across groups should use a distinct secret per group. This is the one property the
   oracle's version of the remedy does not share, because there the commitment *is* bound to its
   id.
3. `sign.zk` derives and exposes `member_commitment = H(witness_base(4), member_secret)` and
   `nullifier = H(witness_base(1), member_secret, group_id, message_hash)`.
4. **`FinalizeV1` had to change shape.** It counted approvals by iterating the group's keys and
   recomputing each member's nullifier. That is impossible now — the nullifier comes from a secret
   the host does not hold — so the params carry the approvals and the host verifies each one
   against a signature record that exists, is for *this* group, and is for *this* message. An
   approval repeated in the list counts once. Nothing can be fabricated: a nullifier reaches the
   signatures tree only through a `SignV1` that passed the proof and membership checks.
5. `finalize.zk` exposed `approval_commit`, which also repaired a live inconsistency: the circuit
   computed `H(witness_base(4), group_id, message_hash)` while the entrypoint computed
   `H(group_id, message_hash)` with no domain prefix, and because the value was never exposed
   nothing compared them. The circuit's dead `threshold`/`signature_count` witnesses were removed —
   neither was used or exposed.

**Evidence.** `tests::heavyweight_multisig` PASS. Verbatim from the run's `DWOW_TEST_LOGS=1`
output, both after the proof's public inputs verified:

    [multisig::SignV1] Error: signer is not a member of the group
    [DIAG] reconstructed error: Custom(5)      -- NotAMember, the OBL-Z11 attack
    Block 5 rejected at local height 4: ... fn_code=0x01

    [DIAG] reconstructed error: Custom(7)      -- InsufficientSignatures, 2 approvals vs threshold 3
    Block 5 rejected at local height 4: ... fn_code=0x01

A third rejection, `Custom(2)` (`GroupAlreadyExists`), is the runner's own §3.6 replay check: it
re-submits `spec.endpoints[first_zk_index()]`, which for multisig is `CreateGroupV1`. Expected, and
listed here so the next reader does not read it as a symptom.

`scripts/check-circuit-metadata-alignment.sh` PASS (70 pairs). The derivation gate stays red on its
15 pre-existing instances and added none; the four dead `multisig` manifest entries were retired,
and one of them — `sign.zk : message_hash`, which described the old nullifier — had become *wrong*
rather than merely stale.

**Where the pieces are** (surveyed 2026-09-20, before any of the above was written):

* *There is no shared helper.* `promissory_note` and `bearer_bond` have a `validation.rs` and
  neither contains the nullifier check; the glue is ~4 lines per contract. The canonical shape is
  `native_token`: derive in-circuit (`burn.zk:67`, `poseidon_hash(DOMAIN_NULLIFIER, spend_secret,
  coin)` where the coin is itself derived from the secret, so the nullifier is bound to the secret
  rather than to a free witness), expose (`burn.zk:68`), check unspent (`entrypoint/mod.rs:899-902`,
  `db_contains_key` → `DuplicateNullifier`), mark spent in `apply_burn`
  (`entrypoint/mod.rs:1471-1480`). `purse` and `box` repeat it; the two SDK primitives are
  `Nullifier::new` (`src/sdk/src/crypto/nullifier.rs:99-101`, domain
  `DRK_POSEIDON_DOMAIN_NULLIFIER = 1`) and `db_mark_spent` (`src/sdk/src/wasm/db.rs:185-187`).
* *The oracle has no nullifier tree at all.* `init_contract` creates `info`, `oracles` and
  `attestations` only (`oracle/src/entrypoint.rs:76-104`), so one has to be created there and in
  `src/lib.rs:115-117`. `Oracle.oracle_pub: PublicKey` (`model/mod.rs:118-134`) is where the
  commitment goes, and `set_oracle_active_v1` has **no circuit** — its only check is an equality
  against a prover-supplied copy of a public key (`entrypoint.rs:475-478`).
* *Genesis impact:* oracle **and** multisig are in `GENESIS_CONTRACT_NAMES`
  (`bin/dwowd/src/tests/blockchain.rs:69-72`); stablecoin is not, so OBL-Z14's remedy moves no pin.
* *The warm-up:* OBL-Z14 closes with two lines in `process_governance_report_instruction` —
  compare `params.reporter_pub` against the stored `governance_pubkey`
  (`stablecoin/src/lib.rs:157`, set at init from `params.deployer_auth`) — plus
  `constrain_instance(reporter_pub_x/y)` in the circuit so the comparison is proof-bound. It is
  smaller than the oracle work and touches no genesis contract.

So OBL-Z9's "FAILS" stands, its **remedy is now specified**, and what must not happen is the
disclosure of a static key — that would trade an authorization failure for a correlation failure,
and the second is the one this project's model is built to prevent.

### OBL-Z9–Z11: the residue was not paperwork

The 33 unclassified instances were the checker saying "I cannot tell whether these are safe". That
is a signal, and following it into the entrypoints found three authorization failures — two of them
in **genesis contracts** (`oracle` and `multisig` are both in `GENESIS_CONTRACT_NAMES`).

**The residue, triaged (2026-09-20).** The 33 were walked one at a time, and they are not one
class: **14** turned out to have a readable host mechanism and are now entries in
`script/circuit_free_instances.txt` — mostly id lookups (`db_get` → `NotFound`) and membership
checks, plus two worth naming because they are stronger than the rest: `multisig/sign`'s
`message_hash` is bound into the nullifier the host deduplicates on (`entrypoint/mod.rs:319`), and
`labor_market/accept_job_with_capability`'s `capability_id` is the repository's **only** real
on-chain capability check, a routed `Identity::VerifyCapabilityV1` child call
(`entrypoint.rs:1613-1644`). Two are benign by design (the oracle operator's own `value` and
`threshold` — publishing them *is* the operation; what is missing there is authorization, OBL-Z10).
The remaining **19** are OBL-Z16, and two of them are live — or rather, they *were* the live end of something larger: following `insurance_market`'s two `with_capability` instances into `Identity::VerifyCapabilityV1` found that the capability check every one of these contracts relies on verifies nothing at all. That is OBL-Z17, and it is the reason the insurance holes were recorded rather than patched: the in-tree pattern they would have been patched *with* is the empty one.

Also found in that pass, and tracked separately: `insurance_market`'s three `with_capability`
metadata functions push three public inputs where their circuits expose six and five
(`entrypoint.rs:175,198`) — the same instance-vector misalignment as OBL-Z15, found by reading
rather than by any gate, because the metadata gate covers eleven contracts and this is not one of
them.

**Deliberately not repaired yet**, and the reason is worth a line of its own: making those vectors
agree is a small change, and it is the wrong one *now*. The params cannot supply the missing
`tx_binding`, `tx_nonce` or `required_capability_id` at all (`UnderwriteWithCapabilityParamsV1` has
none of them; its third field is `capability_secret`, which the metadata pushes where the circuit
wants `tx_binding`), so those two circuits cannot be proven today — and OBL-Z17 says the capability
check they gate on authorizes everyone. Repairing the vectors first would turn "cannot run" into
"runs and gates nothing". Repair the capability model, then these.

**The shape, which is one shape.** An oracle or signer circuit authorizes an actor like this:

    oracle_pub = ec_mul_base(oracle_secret, NULLIFIER_K);
    derived_pub_x = ec_get_x(oracle_pub);
    constrain_equal_base(derived_pub_x, oracle_pub_x);     -- witness == witness
    constrain_equal_base(derived_pub_y, oracle_pub_y);
    constrain_instance(oracle_id);
    constrain_instance(value);
    -- `oracle_pub_x`/`oracle_pub_y` are declared `witness` and are NEVER exposed

The equality is between the derived public key and the prover's own witness, so it holds for *any*
`oracle_secret`: the proof establishes "the prover knows some curve secret", not "the prover is the
registered oracle". The link that would make it an authorization — the witness equalling the
registered key — is absent, and cannot be supplied host-side either: `PushValueParamsV1` is
`{proof, oracle_id, value, tx_binding, tx_nonce}`, with no pubkey in it.

**`oracle`.** `push_value_v1`, `attest_value_v1`, `push_value_commitment_v1` and `aggregate_v1` all
look the oracle up by id, check `is_active`, and then write: `oracle.value = params.value`. Nothing
checks the caller. Anyone can push any value to any registered oracle, attest any predicate, or
aggregate to any result. Separately, `set_oracle_active_v1` has **no circuit at all** — the
entrypoint marks it *"Non-ZK function, no public inputs"* — and its only check is
`oracle.oracle_pub != params.oracle_pub`, comparing a prover-supplied key against stored state. A
public key is public data, so anyone can deactivate any oracle.

**`multisig`.** This one is subtler and more instructive, because the check that *is* there is
correct. `mod.rs:303` rejects a signer not in `group.pubkeys` — but `params.signer_pub` is
instruction data, and the proof does not bind it. The nullifier,
`poseidon_hash([group_id, msg_hash, pk_x, pk_y])`, is built from that same claimed key. So a
non-member copies a member's public key into `params.signer_pub`, supplies a proof over its own
secret, passes the membership check, and spends the member's nullifier for that message. The result
is not theft but denial: the real member can no longer sign it.

**What the checker did and did not see.** It flagged the exposed instances — `oracle_id`, `value`,
`predicate`, `threshold`, `attestation_id`, `result`, `min_result`, `max_result`, `group_id`,
`message_hash` — which is exactly the right set of circuits to look at. It did **not** flag the
pubkey witnesses, because they are not exposed, and the rule as mechanized only asks about exposed
values. So the residue pointed at the right place for a reason it could not state. That is worth
recording as a property of the instrument: `unclassified` means "a human must look", and the first
three genesis contracts a human looked at had authorization failures.

**What is still untriaged.** These three account for 8 of the 33 instances (`oracle_id`, `value`,
`predicate`, `threshold`, `attestation_id`, `result`, `min_result`, `max_result` in `oracle`;
`group_id`, `message_hash` in `multisig`). The remaining twenty-odd sit in `tender` (3 circuits),
`insurance_market` (2), `labor_market/accept_job_with_capability`, `bridge/withdraw`,
`baccarat/draw_cards`, `darktoshi_dice/reveal_roll`, `slot/reveal_spin`, `roulette/settle_bet` (whose
`payout` is recorded above as inert), `stablecoin/governance_report` (`report_timestamp` only),
`proofs/core/{lead,set_v1}` and `bin/darkirc/rlnv2-diff-signal`. They are not cleared — the gate
stays red at 33, and this section says what has been looked at rather than implying the rest is fine.

`set_membership` (0x59) is implemented and forces `expected_root` into the instance column, but
**no deployed `.zk` calls it** — only a comment in `oracle/proof/push_value_commitment.zk` warns
against it. `opcodes-status.md` lists it as "SOUND ✓" regardless.

### What the existing gates do and do not cover

The first three scripts are worth keeping as cheap structural checks. **None of them implements
OBL-Z1**, and the gap between them and it is where the Orchard bug lives. The fourth now does:
`check-circuit-instance-derivation.sh` (over the first two, which stay as fast filters).

* `check-circuit-metadata-alignment.sh` — **count parity**. It compares the number of
  `constrain_instance` calls against the number of values pushed for that circuit's namespace. Its
  own tail text says the real invariant is positional; it never checks position or names.
* `check-circuit-domain-separation.sh` — **prefix presence**. `witness_base` anywhere in the
  argument list satisfies it.
* `hooks/pre-commit` — **one binding pattern**, on staged files.

### OBL-Z1: what the mechanized check actually found

`script/circuit_instance_derivation.py` walks all 180 circuits and classifies every
`constrain_instance`. It is conservative by construction — anything it cannot classify is a
FAIL, because a false alarm is triageable and a false pass is not — and it reads `.zk` source
rather than the `.zk.bin` that actually ships (`validate_zk_bins.sh` covers binary validity).

Three classifications are mechanical and need no manifest: **derived** (the exposed name was
assigned a pure opcode expression), **bound** (a `constrain_equal_base(derived, X)` precedes the
expose) and **derived-inline**. Two are not, and the distinction between them is the whole
finding:

**The redundant class — 208 instances, and the reason the first count was wrong.** The initial
run reported 263 unclassified, 166 of them `tx_nonce`. The dominant `tx_binding` pattern is

```
tx_binding = poseidon_hash(DOMAIN_TX_BINDING, tx_commitment, tx_nonce);
constrain_instance(tx_binding);
constrain_instance(tx_nonce);
```

Exposing `tx_nonce` beside a *derived* `tx_binding` grants the prover **no freedom it did not
already have** — it was going to choose `tx_nonce` regardless; the expose discloses that choice
to the host so the host can check it. That is a sound information-flow argument, and it makes
`tx_nonce` redundant rather than free. Getting there required two fixes to the checker that are
themselves the point: a `bound` exposure stores the *name* it is bound to, so the redundancy test
must follow the assignment chain (`computed_nullifier` → `poseidon_hash(DOMAIN_NULLIFIER, bet_id,
house_secret)`), and it must follow it *transitively*, because `root = merkle_root(pos, path,
coin_incl)` pins `coin_incl`, which pins `coin`, which pins `coin_spend_hook`. A one-level scan
misses both and reported 166 phantom findings. Fixing them removed 208 sites — including every
`coin_spend_hook`, `escrow_id`, `job_id` and `subscription_id` — and left the residue below.

**What the redundant class does NOT say.** It says the expose adds no freedom, not that the
witness is safe. `tx_nonce` is still prover-chosen; whether that matters depends on what the host
does with `tx_binding`, which is a property of the entrypoint and invisible to a `.zk` reader.
The checker records, for each redundancy, the exposed expression that discharges it, so the
residual obligation is addressable by name rather than diffuse.

**The unclassified residue — 33 instances across 22 circuits.** The 22 with a verified host
mechanism are in `script/circuit_free_instances.txt`. The rest are open, and several have the
shape of real defects rather than paperwork:

*(Re-measured 2026-09-24 through the gate itself — `bash scripts/check-circuit-instance-derivation.sh`
— the residue is **11 instances across 7 circuits**: 181 circuits walked, 905 `constrain_instance`
statements, 40 declared free, 11 unclassified. The 33/22 above is this section's original figure and
the number moved because the checker did: the same pass that removed 208 sites also reclassified the
ones it had been unable to see. `OBL-Z16` carries the current eleven by name, and the measurement below
was taken from the other direction and agrees with it to the letter.)*

**Appended the same day, and the residue is now empty — read the figures here rather than above.**
Re-run at the end of the day, the gate exits **0**: **178 circuits walked, 880 `constrain_instance`
sites, 44 declared free, 0 unclassified.** So the seven circuits and the eleven instances the paragraph
above enumerates were each adjudicated, and the resolution is readable where it was made: four of the
five sites the runner's own gate-4 comment named are entries in `script/circuit_free_instances.txt`,
and `bridge/withdraw`'s `token_minimum` was **removed** rather than declared free — which that file's
own oracle entry cites as the alternative it did not take. The population then fell further than those
adjudications alone explain, because circuits were **deleted** rather than repaired: `proofs/core/lead.zk`
and `set_v1.zk` (181 → 179) and `lottery/proof/initialize.zk` (179 → 178). A green gate and a smaller
population are two facts, and this section's subject is the population — `OBL-Z1`'s cell now carries
both, and `OBL-Z16`'s count is **0**.

* `roulette/proof/settle_bet.zk` and `slot/proof/settle_bet.zk` — `payout` is exposed with **no
  constraint of any kind** in the circuit, under a comment reading *"Payout is calculated
  externally and verified here"*. The two differ in the host, and the difference is the point:
  * `slot` is **safe**. `slot/src/entrypoint.rs:596-607` recomputes
    `crate::model::calculate_payout(spin.bet_value, &wins, spin.house_edge)` and rejects
    `params.payout != payout`. The circuit's comment is decorative, the host check is real.
    Manifest entry.
  * `roulette` is **not**. `roulette/src/entrypoint.rs:130-138` pushes `params.payout` straight
    into the instance vector and never compares it to anything; the plaintext path computes
    `house_payout` from `bet.check_win` and moves *that* (`entrypoint.rs:593-611`,
    `validate_child_value_commit(..., house_payout, ...)`). So nothing is stolen — the proof's
    `payout` is inert — but the comment at `entrypoint.rs:129` claims *"The ZK circuit
    constrains payout as a public instance — this catches bugs in the entrypoint logic"*, and it
    does not: the binding runs the wrong way, from a value nobody reads. A circuit whose
    public-input claim is inert is not a defence-in-depth layer, and reading it as one is what
    the Orchard-class rule exists to prevent.
* `tender/proof/reveal_bid.zk` — `tender_id`, `bid_id`, `revealed_amount` are all exposed
  undetermined, so the circuit does not tie a revealed amount to the sealed bid. The entrypoint
  does (`entrypoint.rs:635`), which is why this is H and not C: the check exists, but the
  circuit's own comment claims *"Revealed amount matches the sealed bid"* as a circuit property.
* `stablecoin/proof/governance_report.zk` — the ratio check (`crb_times_debt <= coll_times_bps`)
  and the interest check are arithmetic over prover-supplied numbers, so **the circuit's checks
  prove nothing on their own**: they constrain the prover's own values against each other. What
  makes them mean anything is `entrypoint.rs:1331`, which compares `total_collateral`,
  `total_debt` and `interest_accrued` each against the config DB and rejects a mismatch — the
  third carrying an explicit HAZOP CRIT-1 comment naming itself as the defence-in-depth. Those
  three are manifest entries. `report_timestamp` is not: it appears **only** in `get_metadata`
  (`entrypoint.rs:398`) and is never validated or even stored, so an expose that nothing consumes
  remains in the residue. Same shape, same disposition at `accrue_interest.zk` — `old_total_debt`
  is checked at `entrypoint.rs:1483`.
* `multisig/proof/{create_group,sign,finalize}.zk` — `group_id`/`threshold`/`total_keys` are
  host-supplied, and `threshold` **is** bounded: `entrypoint/mod.rs:260`
  `if params.threshold == 0 || params.threshold as usize > params.pubkeys.len()` (the
  `InvalidThreshold` message in `error.rs:12` names the same rule). `total_keys` is *not* checked
  against `pubkeys.len()` at any of the sites read. The live item is `sign`/`finalize`: both
  expose `group_id` and `message_hash` from `params` with nothing in-circuit binding the signer
  to that group, so the group-membership check is entirely the entrypoint's to make.
* `oracle/proof/attest_value.zk` — `attestation_id`, `predicate` and `threshold` are exposed
  undetermined; the entrypoint stores `attestation_id` without checking it against the
  attestation contract (`entrypoint.rs:363`).
* `labor_market/proof/milestone_payment.zk` — `milestone_payment_amount` is range-checked and
  passed to the child transfer, but nothing compares it to the job's schedule.
* `dex/proof/execute_swap.zk` — resolved (host-derived FuncRefs, see the manifest).
* `proofs/core/set_v1.zk`, `proofs/core/lead.zk`, `bin/darkirc/proof/rlnv2-diff-signal.zk` —
  these are not contract circuits and their free inputs are protocol parameters, but they are
  unclassified all the same and belong in the same triage.

**Adversarially tested.** Deleting `constrain_equal_base(tx_binding_circuit, tx_binding)` from
`box/proof/put.zk` makes the checker name exactly `put.zk: tx_binding` **and** `tx_nonce` — the
second because its pin was the now-unexposed `tx_binding_circuit`. Adding both to the manifest
clears it. A gate that has never failed is not a gate; this one has.

### Why the residue is a triage list and not a bug list

Writing the residue up required reading the entrypoint for each candidate, and **four of the
findings did not survive contact with the Rust.** `slot`'s `payout` is recomputed and compared
(`entrypoint.rs:596`); `multisig`'s threshold is bounds-checked (`mod.rs:260`); `roulette`'s
`payout` binding runs the wrong way rather than being absent; and `stablecoin`'s three reports are
each compared against the config DB. Each had been asserted from the `.zk` source alone.

That is the structural point of OBL-Z1, and it belongs in the method rather than in a footnote:
**the `.zk` source cannot tell you whether a free public input is safe.** It can tell you that the
input is not determined in-circuit, which is a necessary condition for the Orchard bug, not a
sufficient one. The sufficiency question — does anything downstream pin this value? — lives in the
entrypoint, and no amount of care with the circuit text substitutes for reading it. A checker that
reported the residue as *vulnerabilities* would have been wrong four times out of the fifteen
cases examined here. It reports the residue as *what needs a host-side answer*, and the manifest
is where the answers go once someone has read the Rust.

**Two cross-cutting findings fell out of that triage**, and neither is visible from a single
circuit:

* **`tx_binding` is a constant in 11 of the 31 contracts that expose it.** `auction`, `baccarat`,
  `bridge`, `escrow`, `identity`, `lottery`, `otc_swap`, `relayer_endowment`, `roulette`, `slot`
  and `stablecoin` push `poseidon_hash([3, 0, 0])` into the `tx_binding` instance slot and
  `Base::zero()` into `tx_nonce`, so the prover must set `tx_commitment = tx_nonce = 0` and the
  pair is a fixed constant, identical in every transaction. `lottery/src/entrypoint.rs:89` says
  this is deliberate — *"tx fields are zero in heavyweight; the V2 clients commit to
  `poseidon_hash([3, 0, 0])`"* — so it is an unimplemented mechanism rather than a mistake. But
  it means the 49 circuits in those contracts have a `constrain_instance(tx_binding)` that binds
  nothing, and any document describing that pair as cross-transaction replay protection is
  asserting something the code does not do. **Counted, not fixed: 49 circuits.**
* **`dao_escrow`'s placeholder is not the same shape and is unsatisfiable.** It passes a bare
  `pallas::Base::zero()` (`entrypoint.rs:189`, commented *"Pattern A: pass-through placeholder"*,
  12 occurrences), while seven of its circuits — `init`, `pay_premium`, `propose_claim`,
  `resolve_dispute`, `set_governance_config`, `verify_member_capability`, `vote_claim` — assign
  `tx_binding = poseidon_hash(DOMAIN_TX_BINDING, tx_commitment, tx_nonce)` and expose it. The
  instance must equal that assignment, so verification requires `H(3, txc, txn) = 0`: a Poseidon
  preimage. Unlike the `H(3, 0, 0)` convention, whose constant is at least *reachable* by setting
  both fields to zero, this one has no satisfying witness. It is consistent with the standing
  `v1-vs-v2-client-hazard` note that `dao_escrow`'s clients are V1 against V2 circuits. **Not
  fixed here: it needs a decision about the convention, not a patch.**

---

## Surface 3 — Type system (`OBL-T`)

The normative document is `arch/type-system.md`; §7 lists seven "compiler-enforced invariants" and
§11 the proved properties. The barb alphabet is §1.1.

| ID | Status | Proposition | Enforced at | Checked today by | Sev |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| OBL-T11 | **SATISFIED** | The tautology arm refuses a statement that is true of nothing — including one that merely **reduces** to a trivial statement, and one whose proof ignores every argument it was given | `script/check_lean_axioms.py` (the tautology arm); `proofs/lean/src/CheckAxioms.lean` (`whnfType`, `unreferencedExplicitBinders`) | The arm itself, falsified: reintroducing the shape fails the gate. The three theorems it found are recorded below. | M | **SATISFIED, and the row is new — this is the gap `OBL-C111`-adjacent Step 0 recorded as "a real candidate for a later unit and is not attempted here", now attempted and closed for a class.** **What the arm could not see, measured:** the old test compared the statement *syntactically* against `True` / `x = x` / `x ≤ x`, and the proof term against a bare binder. Between those two sits a statement that is neither an identity nor a restatement but **reduces** to `True` — `theorem txCommitment_source_bindable (nf pf : List String) : bindable txCommitment nf pf`, which is literally `∀ nf pf, True` because `bindable`'s `_` catch-all sends that constructor to `True`, while the syntactic test saw an unreduced `def` application. **Three such theorems were live in `Capability/Prover.lean`** and all three were consumer-less (`grep` across `.md`/`.lean`/`.rs`/`.py` finds no reference outside the file). **Two tightenings closed it.** `whnfType` reduces the statement's body — via `forallTelescope`, because a manual strip leaves loose `bvar`s and `Meta.whnf` *panics* on those, which is recorded in that function's docstring as a mistake kept rather than smoothed over; and `unreferencedExplicitBinders` counts explicit binders the proof never mentions, which catches the same three independently. **Measured across all 454 theorems: the tightened arm catches exactly those three and nothing else** — no false positives, and each was flagged by both arms, which is corroboration rather than two defects. **The three were deleted and their claim restated once, in the form that has content** (`bindable_intrinsic_sources_are_unconditional`: the three unconditional cases *beside* the conditional one, which is the negative `undeclared_field_blocks` already states) — because "always bindable" for a `True`-valued case says nothing on its own, and the way to say it checkably is next to the case where bindability *is* conditional. **Two soft signals were added and are soft on purpose:** "some explicit binder unused" and the pre-existing "mentions no constant this project declares" both have real false positives (a proof legitimately ignores a parameter another binder's type already carries — `derived_chain_rejects_cycle`, `hash_ne_of_component_ne`, `smtCrh_injective_of_injective` are exactly that, 3 theorems), and the arm's own docstring gives the falsification test for which signals may block: a gate that fails on false positives gets turned off within a week. **Falsified before landing:** reintroducing the vacuous shape makes the gate exit 1 with `1 tautology`, naming the theorem under both arms; restoring it exits 0. **Residual, stated rather than implied:** a statement whose *head* is an inductive (`(∀ …, True) ∧ (∀ …, True)` written literally) is not reduced further, so that shape still escapes — recorded because the arm's completeness is now a claim someone will rely on. |
| OBL-T12 | **FIXED** | Every theorem the budget check verifies is one the gate *finds* — a declaration inside a `mutual … end` block is keyed by its real name, not by a mangled one | `script/check_lean_axioms.py` (`qualified_theorems`); `proofs/lean/src/CheckAxioms.lean` | The arm itself, by measurement: the parser saw 1 of 9 theorems before the fix and 9 after | M | **FIXED 2026-09-24. The budget arm's *name extraction* silently dropped every theorem declared after a `mutual … end`, and the failure direction is what makes it severe: the parser simply produced fewer names, so those theorems were never queried, their rows never appeared, and nothing reported them missing.** `qualified_theorems` tracks `namespace`/`section` nesting by pushing a frame per opener and popping on `end` — but `mutual … end` opens a block whose `end` was popped off a *namespace*, so every theorem after the first `mutual` was keyed under a mangled name and did not resolve. **Found by the first module in this tree to use `mutual`** (`Circuits/InstanceDerivation.lean`): the collector reported **1** of its 9 theorems, and `--require-collector` passed, because a name that is never queried is never reported unresolved. **Measured, and the measurement is the argument:** before the fix the gate checked **455** theorems and my module's four agreement/walk lemmas carried annotations of `1` that were never checked. After it, the gate checks **463** and those four measure `0` — so eight theorems had been unverified and four of them were wrong. That is the `OBL-T11` class exactly: an arm whose completeness was assumed and whose reach was smaller than its claim, found by measurement rather than by reading. The fix is a `mutual` frame in the parser, with the reason recorded at the site. |
| OBL-T13 | **SATISFIED** | `subst`'s identity law holds under subterm-freeness with `subst`'s own shadowing structure, and **not** under freeness — the layer carries that condition and the law it was blocking | `proofs/lean/src/DarkFi/Semantics/Substitution.lean` (`NoSub`, `subst_eq_self_of_noSub`) | the proofs themselves; the converse is refuted in the same file (`noSub_not_of_freshFree`), the hypothesis's necessity by `subst_identity_is_false`, and non-vacuity by `noSub_witness` | M | **NEW 2026-09-24 — minted for absence rather than for a defect, like `OBL-C115`/`C116`: the substitution theory's side condition was the α-unit's stated prerequisite, and no row carried it.** `Substitution.lean` recorded the obstacle as a *measurement* rather than a guess — the obvious law `FreshFree z P → P ≠ z → subst P z y = P` is **false** (`subst_of_freshFree_is_false`, witness `z = nil`, `P = nil \| ⌈nil⌉`, `y = ⌈nil⌉`: `FreeOccurs z nil` is `False` **by definition** for every `z`, so `nil` is "free of `z`" and is a *position* `subst` replaces anyway) — and the note named the repair, "subterm-freeness with `subst`'s own shadowing structure", while recording that `Proc.lean`'s `Occurs` cannot supply it: its `nil` clause is exactly right for freshness and exactly wrong here, and `FreshFree` conflates the two uses. **`NoSub` is that predicate, and the clause a reader would have guessed is wrong twice.** It is defined in lockstep with `subst`'s recursion, so it constrains exactly the nodes `subst` visits and nothing else; `inp a b P` visits its *channel* `a` but keeps its binder `b` and visits the body only when the binder does not shadow `z`, so the body's clause is discharged by `b = z ∨ ·`; and `nu a P` does not visit its binder at all — unlike `inp`'s channel — so its clause carries no condition on `a`. "No subterm equals `z`" would get both wrong. **What landed**: `subst_eq_self_of_noSub` (the missing law, for every `y`, with no capture reasoning — the condition is about `subst`'s positions and the law is about those positions alone); `subst_nil_of_ne` recognised as its `nil` instance (`NoSub z nil` **is** `z ≠ nil`); `noSub_self` — no term satisfies `NoSub z z`, which is `subst_self`, the equality test firing, read as a property of the condition rather than of the definition; `noSub_ne` (the root condition, uniformly); `noSub_implies_freshFree` — and `noSub_not_of_freshFree`, refuting the converse **on the same witness that refutes the `FreshFree` law**, so freeness neither implies subterm-freeness nor follows from it and the two are not near-misses of each other; `subst_identity_is_false`, that the hypothesis is necessary rather than decorative; and `noSub_witness`, a concrete term on which the equality test is evaluated at three nodes and fires at none. **The α-unit's four parts, and which part this is**: (1) the subterm-freeness predicate and the laws `subst` needs — **done here**; (2) what a label's channel means when names are defined only up to renaming; (3) the constructor on `SCong`/`SCong0`; (4) the invariance re-proofs. Parts 2–4 are the redesign `Semantics/LTS.lean`'s `subst_moves_the_label` and `alpha_variants_not_strongbisim` measure, and this row does **not** narrow them. **Severity `M` and status `SATISFIED` are both deliberate**: nothing in the tree is unsound today — the false law was refuted rather than assumed, and `Step.tau`'s proviso is `CaptureFree` — so this is an incompleteness row, and it is `SATISFIED` rather than `PARTLY` because the proposition it states is now proved in full **APPENDED 2026-09-24: the four parts are no longer all open, and this is a correction to this row's own sentence about them.** "Parts 2–4 are the redesign … and this row does not narrow them" is right about their *size* and wrong about their *state*, and the difference matters because the queue has carried "the α-unit's parts (2)–(4)" as one item for a dozen units. **Part (2) is answered rather than deferred**, and it was answered by measurement rather than by a proof about a repair: `LTS.lean`'s Part 10 (`:1684-1686`) — *"at this label type, nothing — a label's channel and payload are **free** names, so renaming a bound name is relabelling, and a relabelling is observable"* — with `alpha_variants_not_strongbisim` (`:1722`) and `alpha_variants_not_barbedEq` (`:1742`) carrying it. So the question part (2) asks, what a label's channel *means* when names are defined only up to renaming, has an answer here, and the answer is that the obvious repair — relaxing "the same channel" to an α-aware notion — cannot be applied at this label type. **Parts (3)–(4) are a redesign whose size is now stated rather than implied**, and the size is the label type: closing the gap needs the *bound-output* form `x!(⌈y⌉)` that `Label`'s own docstring records as absent (`LTS.lean:186-193`), which is "a change to `Label`, `Label.subject`, `CanStep`, `CanBarb` and every rule that reads a label" (`:1691-1694`) — because `Step.scong` closes steps under the congruence, and a larger congruence is a larger `Step`. That is work across `Semantics/LTS.lean` and `Semantics/Congruence.lean`, not a lemma to add, and it should not be planned as one session's unit. Status and severity are unchanged: this appends what has been settled inside the gap the row already records, and the row's own reason for `SATISFIED`/`M` is untouched **APPENDED 2026-09-24 (later): both halves of that last sentence are wrong, and they are why the item kept being deferred.** Costed by reading every site rather than sampling. **It is one file of code, not two.** `Label`, `Label.subject`, `IsAction`, `Step`, `CanStep`, `Barb` and `CanBarb` are used in *code* only in `LTS.lean`; `Congruence.lean:135` and `Substitution.lean:4,36,79,82,83,93` mention them in **comment prose only** (measured — seven occurrences, every one a docstring line), and `Ledger.lean` does not import the module at all. So "across `Semantics/LTS.lean` and `Semantics/Congruence.lean`" overstates it by one file, and the overstatement is load-bearing: a two-file redesign of a semantics is a different decision from a one-file type change, which is the decision that was actually in front of the queue. **And the size is ~20 of `LTS.lean`'s 85 top-level declarations**, not "not a lemma to add": 7 definitions whose *statements* change (`Label:194`, `Label.subject:206`, `IsAction:219`, `Step:231`, `CanStep:302`, `Barb:562`, `CanBarb:692`); 4 declarations needing a new case with statements unchanged (`canStep_of_step:371`, `noStep_of_actionFree:469`, `canBarb_of_step:765`, and `Label.subject` again); **2 theorems whose conclusions must go** (below); and ~12 proofs that unfold the changed definitions or `rcases` their new disjuncts (`barb_out_iff:573`, `barb_inp_iff:595`, `barb_of_scong:639`, `canBarb_of_barb:783`, `barb_nu_of_not_scong:795`, `barb_of_canBarb:812`, `strongbisim_barb_eq:1109`, `canStep_of_scong:321`, `canStep_occurs_up_to_scong:490`, `subst_moves_the_label:540`, `canBarb_of_scong:719`, `canBarb_has_free_name:971`). **What makes it a judgement rather than a repair**: closing the gap by bound output makes `alpha_variants_not_strongbisim:1722` and `alpha_variants_not_barbedEq:1742` **false** — `νx.(out b x)` and `νy.(out b y)` become bisimilar through `b!(⌈x⌉)` — so the unit's deliverable includes deleting or restating two recorded results that this row's Part 10 note cites as its answer to part (2), and the file records their hypotheses as satisfiable (`:1719-1720`), so they are separations rather than vacuous. **Slicing, because Lean will not accept a half-edited file**: (1) `Label.bout` + its `subject` arm + the `IsAction` disjunct (compiles; an unused constructor is legal), (2) the `Step` constructor together with the three `Step`-induction cases *and* the `CanStep`/`Barb`/`CanBarb` clauses, which must land together because the new induction case needs the clause to exist, (3) the two theorems' restatement and the Part 10 / scope notes. **Undecided, and it must be decided before (2)**: the exact shape of the new clauses — whether `Step.nu`'s proviso becomes two-part (`¬ SCong x (subject μ) ∧ ¬ SCong x (boundName μ)`) and whether `Barb`/`IsAction` take a third disjunct. The touched count is stable at ~20 either way. So the honest status of parts (3)–(4) is **a sized unit with one design decision and two theorem retractions**, which is what a session can plan; "research-scale" was a statement about a two-file redesign that does not exist. **APPENDED 2026-09-25: slices (1) and (2) have landed, the design decision is made, and the closure is measured to be in a third place this row does not count.** `Label.bout` (`x!(⌈y⌉)`), its `Label.subject` arm, the `IsAction` disjunct, `Step.extrude` (the standard `open` rule), the `CanStep` clause that admits the label and `Barb`'s third disjunct are in `LTS.lean`, with `extrude_step` and `canStep_bout_of_extrude` as consumers and `bound_label_separates` as the measurement. **The decision this row said must be settled before slice (2) is taken, and it is that the bound output is an ADDITIONAL transition rather than a replacement**: `Step.nu` still passes the free output `x!(y)` for a name the restriction binds, so `νy.(out b y)` steps with both `b!(y)` and `b!(⌈y⌉)`. **Two consequences, and the second corrects this row.** First, **the two retractions this row expects are NOT needed**: `alpha_variants_not_strongbisim` and `alpha_variants_not_barbedEq` remain true, because the free-output step that separates the pair survives — so this row's "one design decision and two theorem retractions" is right about the decision and wrong about the retractions being part of *this* slice. Second, **the closure is not in the label type or in the rules**, and that is now a theorem rather than an argument: `bound_label_separates` proves the two variants are still separated **at the bound label** — `νx.(out b x)` has `b!(⌈x⌉)`, `νy.(out b y)` has `b!(⌈y⌉)`, and the label *carries* the bound name, so `¬ SCong x y` is exactly what makes them differ. `IsStrongBisim` matches labels by **exact equality**, so what a renaming-aware bisimulation needs is the *successor* matched up to the renaming of the bound name — whose statement is about `IsStrongBisim`/`IsWeakBisim` and whose proof obligations are their metatheory (reflexivity, symmetry and transitivity under a renaming). This row's ~20-declaration count covers the label type and the rules, which is what landed; it does not cover that, and the two retractions are **downstream of it** rather than of the slices above. **Two Lean-level findings, both measured, both recorded at the site**: `induction h generalizing μ` does **not** generalize the induction hypothesis when the clause names two labels — the build said "argument μ has type Label but is expected to have type CanStep P μ", and binding the label *inside* the motive is what works; and `Barb`'s third disjunct propagates to exactly **13** elimination or construction sites across `LTS.lean`, every one named by the compiler and **none needing a new argument**, which is what makes this change additive in the semantics and mechanical in its consumers. Measured: `build DarkFi.Semantics.LTS` and `build DarkFi` both exit 0, the axiom gate is `Passed: 8 Failed: 0` with all five new-or-changed declarations at budget **0**, and the distribution is 647/130/9/7 over 793 theorems. **What is left of parts (3)–(4)**: the renaming-matching clause and the metatheory, which is where this row's next reader should start rather than at the label type. **APPENDED 2026-09-25 (later): the closure is sized at 21 declarations, and the α-pair is proved to need none of it beyond the label.** `alpha_variants_bound_steps_match` proves the clause holds *at the pair*: both variants' bound-label steps exist — `b!(⌈x⌉)` for the first, `b!(⌈y⌉)` for the second — and their successors are **congruent** (`νx.0 ≡ 0 ≡ νy.0`, by `nu_nil` twice), so **no renaming of the bound name is needed there at all**. That is the measurement that separates the two halves of the residual: the *label-level* obstruction `bound_label_separates` names is removable at this pair, and what is genuinely owed is the **general** relation. **And its blast radius is measured rather than estimated: 21 declarations** — `IsStrongBisim` and `IsWeakBisim` each carry reflexivity, symmetry, transitivity, the three parallel laws (`parallel_commutative`, `_associative`, `_nil` and their weak counterparts) and `scong` compatibility (`scong_isStrongBisim`), plus `strong_is_weak` and the two `stepTauStar` lemmas — and the parallel laws with bound output are where π-calculus metatheory is hard rather than routine. So the closure is a **metatheory unit of that size**, not a lemma to add, and that is the answer to "how big is it" that this row's chain has been asking since it was minted. **Where the α-unit therefore stands, in three statements a reader can act on**: the label type and the rules are **in** (slices 1–2, with `Step.extrude`, the `CanStep` clause, `Barb`'s third disjunct and the three consuming theorems); the α-pair's clause is **proved** at the label it needs; and the general renaming clause with its 21-law metatheory is **owed**, with the row no longer claiming any of it is small |
| OBL-T14 | **SATISFIED** | `P \| P` is indistinguishable from `P` — and `!!P` from `!P` — by **every** observable this layer defines; so the observation level cannot justify a structural "absorb one copy" step, and the equation level's answer to `!!P ≡ !P` is the fixpoint equation `!!P ≡ !P \| !!P` | `proofs/lean/src/DarkFi/Semantics/LTS.lean` (the four `_par_self` laws: `barb_par_self`, `canBarb_par_self`, `canStep_par_self`, `actionFree_par_self`; the four `_rep_rep` laws: `barb_rep_rep_iff`, `canBarb_rep_rep`, `canStep_rep_rep`, `actionFree_rep_rep`); `proofs/lean/src/DarkFi/Semantics/Congruence.lean` (`rep_rep_fixpoint`) | the proofs themselves; all nine at budget 0 | L | **NEW 2026-09-24, minted for absence and for a corrected route — the second time in two units that the correction is to a route the tree itself names.** `Congruence.lean`'s replication note had recorded the omission of `!0 ≡ 0` and `!(P \| Q) ≡ !P \| !Q` with a stated reason to add them: the merge law would "turn `!!P` into `!P \| !P` and then use the first equation to absorb one copy", so `!!P ≡ !P` would follow. **Reading the route shows it does not exist, and the measurement splits by level.** At the **equation** level, every derivation that uses the merge law produces `!!P ≡ !P \| !!P` — which is `rep_unfold` at `!P`, i.e. `rep_rep_fixpoint`, available today with no new rule; and the "absorb one copy" step is `!P \| !P ≡ !P`, whose reverse needs `!(P\|P) ≡ !P`, i.e. `P \| P ≡ P`, which this calculus does not have. At the **observation** level nothing justifies the step either, and that is the four `_par_self` laws: `Barb`, `CanBarb`, `CanStep` and `ActionFree` all have `∨`/`∧` clauses over the parallel components, so **every observable here is idempotent under duplicating a component** — `P \| P` and `P` cannot be told apart by any of them. **And the collapse itself is already unobservable**: the four `_rep_rep` laws say `!!P` and `!P` agree on every observable the layer defines, so the observation level is *ahead* of the equation level rather than behind it — which is the sharpest statement of where the gap sits, and it is the opposite of what the note implied. **What the row does not claim**: that `!!P ≡ !P` is underivable — non-derivability is not proved here and no countermodel is built — only that *this* route does not exist and that `rep_rep_fixpoint` is what the equations on hand give. The note now carries the correction verbatim above the new text, and says a later unit should read it before deciding the omission is what blocks the collapse. **Severity `L`**: nothing is unsound, no proof rests on the corrected sentence, and what changed is a reader's route plus a statement of the layer's observational reach |
| OBL-T1 | **SATISFIED** | No barb distinction among the primitives is redundant: distinct names ⇒ distinct barb sets, and equal barb sets ⇒ equal names | `Types.lean` (`allPrimitiveTypes`, `typesDistinct`) | `Capability/Pareto.lean` — **proved**, and it was **false** until `↓shard` was added (see below) | C |
| OBL-T2 | **DEFINITIONAL** | Two types are distinct iff their barb sets differ (§2) | `Types.lean` (`typesDistinct`); 10 pair theorems in `Distinction.lean` | `decide` in the Lean; the criterion is *sufficient* for distinctness but does not by itself separate every pair — which is exactly how OBL-T1 failed | C | **DEFINITIONAL, not a theorem — read and recorded 2026-09-22, and the row cannot be closed by a proof because there is nothing to prove.** `typesDistinct` **is** `t1.barbs ≠ t2.barbs` (`Types.lean:268-269`), with `typesEquivalent` its negation (`:271-272`): the "iff" is the model's *definition* of type identity (the bisimulation reading of `type-system.md` §2), so it holds by construction and no edit to the code could falsify it. The content is entirely in the two directions the definition leaves open. *Sufficiency is proved* for the ten pairs the spec names (§8.4): `Distinction.lean:39`–`:124`, each a `theorem` closed by `decide` — the file's own header lists all ten, and `allUnifiablePairsProved` (`:136`) conjoins them. Those ten used to be `native_decide` and are `decide` now, which is why they cost no axioms beyond `Classical.choice`. *Completeness — that no two types which should be distinct share a barb set — is **not** proved, and is measured **FALSE**: `OBL-T9` (13 of 14 capability resources subsumed, and `purse_deposit`/`purse_withdrawal` with identical barb sets). So this row is not an obligation of its own: its sufficiency half is the ten theorems, and the obligation it gestures at is `T9`'s **— and since 2026-09-24 the failure is machine-checked rather than script-measured.** `Capability.Composition.resourceDistinctness_is_false` refutes the tempting resource-level reading of `Pareto.primitiveTypesAreParetoEfficient` **in Lean, at budget 0** — no axiom, not even `Classical.choice` — with the purse pair as its witness: `purseWithdrawType` and `purseDepositType` compose the same primitive list, so two opposite operations on one purse are one type, and their resources' `requiredBarbs` are equal as well. So this row's completeness half is not merely unproved, it is **disproved**, in the kernel, and the bare-`def` shape of `capTypesDistinct` that this row recorded as the symptom now has its cause written beside it in `Composition.lean`: a capability type is `compose ct.primitives`, which never consults the resource |
