| OBL-T7 | **RESTATED** | Every public input of the circuit for `(r, s)` is `constrain_instance`-derived — **that is the *strict* reading, and the tree does not meet it**; what the tree enforces is the checker's four-verdict rule, and the cell below says by how much | **deleted 2026-09-24** (was `Axioms.NoFreeInstances`); the premise is now a field of `Capability/Inversion.lean`'s `CircuitDerivable`, carrying the circuit's transcribed `held` names and `stmts` | `Circuits/InstanceDerivation.lean` defines the rule over a statement list — **two** predicates, the strict one and the checker's `redundant`-admitting `DisclosureRule`; `proofs/lean/src/CircuitIndex.lean`, generated and freshness-gated as the third Lean library, supplies one inhabitant per pair for the **12** pairs this tree has a circuit for, and the **2** without one as recorded decisions | M | **RESTATED 2026-09-24: the property is modelled at the circuit level, the axiom is not replaced, and the reason the plan's own expectation was wrong is worth recording.** `Circuits/InstanceDerivation.lean` defines `NoFreeInstance` over a statement list — `Expr`/`Stmt` as a `.zk` `circuit` block declares them, no PLONK, no PCS, no Fiat–Shamir — and proves the two things the bare axiom cannot state: **`soundness`** (every exposure determined before it ⟹ two *satisfying* valuations agreeing on the held inputs give the same public vector) and **`free_instance_is_observable`** (a bare-witness instance is *free*, so the predicate is falsifiable in the model rather than true by construction). A worked four-statement circuit is closed by `decide` and its negative control fails, so both verdicts are exercised. **Reading the existing checker gave the rule the model has to get right, and it is subtler than the obvious statement:** `script/circuit_instance_derivation.py` (`OBL-Z1`) distinguishes `is_derived_expr` — where a bare witness counts, because it is a leaf a derivation is built from — from `is_determined`, where it does **not** ("`constrain_equal_base(some_witness, X)` does not pin `X`, it merely re-exposes a variable the prover was already holding"). A model with one predicate would prove the wrong theorem, and this row's prose did not distinguish them. **The modelling content the checker has nothing to say about is `Satisfies`**, and it was found by a failed proof: the binding rule says an equality against a *determined* side binds the other name, sound only because the circuit's equality forces them equal — a fact about the **valuation**, not about the name set, and so is an assignment. Stating the theorem without it does not close. The checker needs no such hypothesis because it analyses **one source** rather than comparing **two evaluations** — so this row's "the model's job is to be the thing that check is a check *of*" is only half the relation: the model is also asked for something the check is not. **And the axiom is *not* replaced, which the plan expected.** Turning `Axioms.NoFreeInstances (r : Resource) (s : Action)` into a definition needs the function `(r, s) ↦ the circuit source`, and a Lean term cannot read a `.zk` file — so the bridge stays the residual, the same transcription-fidelity gap the checker's own docstring names (it reads `.zk` source, not `.zk.bin`). What changed is that the property now *has* a definition for that bridge to be about; before this module there was nothing for it to relate two things to. Unconsumed still: `Capability/Inversion.lean`'s field is `(r, s)`-indexed and nothing connects the two yet. **— and the assumption entry's own discharge route is measured on 2026-09-24, because it named a route rather than a bridge.** `Axioms.lean`'s `NoFreeInstances` entry said it would be discharged by "the manual circuit audit over `src/contract/*/proof/*.zk`, mechanised; `OBL-Z1` is that obligation, and it is currently unchecked". Three measurements: the audit **is** mechanised (`script/circuit_instance_derivation.py`, this register's only MECHANIZED row); **and it is gated — this is the correction of the sentence that stood here for an hour, and it is worth its own line**: `scripts/check-circuit-instance-derivation.sh` is wired at `scripts/run-all-tests.sh:101` (`run_gate "circuit instance derivation"`) and `exec`s the Python checker, and the sentence here claimed the opposite from a grep that searched for the *Python* file's name while the wrapper is *hyphenated* — one side of a two-sided pair read alone, which is the error the `half-a-pair-error-pattern` memory rule exists to prevent. What is true is that the gate **exits 1** today: 11 instances across 7 circuits are neither derived, nor bound, nor redundant, nor declared free (`bridge/withdraw`'s `token_minimum`; `insurance_market/purchase_coverage_with_capability` and `underwrite_with_capability`'s `required_capability_id`; `labor_market/create_job`'s `attestation_id`; `oracle/attest_value`'s `threshold`; `proofs/core/lead`'s `sigma1`/`sigma2`; `proofs/core/set_v1`'s `lock`/`root`/`key`/`value`), each named by the gate itself and all of them `OBL-Z16`'s subjects. But the decisive measurement is the third: **even a passing audit would not discharge this predicate**, because the audit is a checker over circuit *sources* while the axiom is `(r, s)`-indexed, and the thing between them — `(r, s) ↦ the circuit source` — is the bridge no checker supplies. So the entry now names the bridge instead of the audit, and the route correction is recorded there rather than only here. **APPENDED 2026-09-24: the bridge's *data* side is supplied and gated, and its count decomposed into the checker's own classes.** `scripts/gen_circuit_transcription.py` generates `Transcribed.lean` — 181 circuits, 2747 statements, one `List Stmt` per circuit in `InstanceDerivation`'s vocabulary, and one `theorem … := by unfold NoFreeInstance; decide` per circuit, so **the kernel closes every verdict** rather than the generator asserting it. **The file is `src/Transcribed.lean` and not `Circuits/Transcribed.lean`, and the module is *not* imported by `src/DarkFi.lean` — both corrected 2026-09-24, and the correction is the interesting part rather than a rename.** It *was* imported, and that import is what took this 47 GiB host down: a `LEAN_NUM_THREADS=4` build of `DarkFi` exhausted memory and froze the machine, because that knob bounds how many `lean` *processes* run and not how much memory one of them uses — and this is the tree's most expensive elaboration, 181 `decide` proofs over 2747 statements. It is therefore a library of its own — `lean_lib Transcribed` (`lakefile.lean:31`), built by the gate as `lake build DarkFi Transcribed` — and every Lean invocation in the tree now runs under `scripts/lean-build.sh`, which keeps the thread cap and *adds* the cgroup memory ceiling the cap never was. **The axiom gate is RED, and the sentence that stood here for one commit said the opposite — corrected by measurement, which is the only thing that could have corrected it.** **Every fact in this paragraph that names a line of `CheckAxioms.lean` or `lakefile.lean` is a fact about the *working tree only*, and it says so because a clean checkout does not have them**: `git show HEAD:proofs/lean/src/CheckAxioms.lean \| grep -c Transcribed` returns **0**, so at HEAD there is no `import`, no `importModules` and no `lean_lib`. In the working tree, `import Transcribed` sits at `CheckAxioms.lean:53` and `:220` builds the collector's environment as `importModules #[{ module := `DarkFi }, { module := `Transcribed }]` — and that is precisely why a module with no `.olean` breaks the collector instead of being skipped. The line the *compiler* reported was `src/CheckAxioms.lean:47:0: error: unknown module prefix 'Transcribed'`; `:47` is where that import stood in the revision that was compiled, and it has since moved to `:53`, so a reader who re-runs it will see the same failure at a different line — which is why this cell quotes the error **and** the position it has now. So `script/check_lean_axioms.py --require-collector` — the gate that validates **every** `@[axiom_budget]` in the tree, not this module's alone — cannot run until `Transcribed` builds, and those two source lines being present was never evidence that it does; reading them is what let the wrong sentence land. **And the module has never been elaborated on this host at all, which is the larger fact**: `lake build DarkFi Transcribed` exceeded **24 GiB** in about four minutes (exit 143, `Result=oom-kill`) after an earlier `build DarkFi.Circuits.Transcribed` saturated a 16 GiB ceiling — two independent runs against the same wall, whose surviving evidence is per-run (`/tmp/lean-verify.{log,stdout,peak}`). **The log this cell first cited — `scripts/lean-build.sh`'s default `/tmp/lean-build.log` — is a shared path that an unrelated build has since overwritten** (662 KB of `Rchain/` output, zero matches for `Transcribed` or `DarkFi`), which destroyed the evidence a register row pointed at and is why this row now names the per-run files instead — and **no `Transcribed.olean` exists** after either, checked rather than inferred: `proofs/lean/.lake/build/lib/Transcribed.olean` is absent while `DarkFi/Capability/KeyScope.olean` is present *from that same build*, so the failure is this module's and not the build's. Therefore this row's own "**the kernel closes every verdict**" is **unverified** as it stands: what exists is the generator's second implementation predicting 181 verdicts plus one `decide` theorem text per circuit. Partitioning the 181 so that no single elaboration is unbounded is the disposition under consideration, **and the working tree has since been cut into shard modules — `proofs/lean/src/Transcribed/Part*.lean`, imported by the umbrella — none of which has compiled output either; the shard count is re-measured at each attempt rather than quoted here because it moved three times in twenty minutes.** **And the *cause* of the cost was characterised wrongly here first, which is worth recording because the wrong shape would have driven the wrong fix.** What was measured was one shard: 6.7 GiB `RssAnon` / 10.2 GiB charged for ~12 circuits' verdicts — from which "the constraint is size, plus concurrency" follows only if the cost is a *rate* per circuit. The session that holds `proofs/lean/` has since measured it as a heavy-tailed **per-circuit** distribution instead: a single circuit dominated its shard's entire cost until an extraction in the generator collapsed it by two orders of magnitude. So the figure above characterises *that shard*, not the shape of the problem, and the repair is to find and restructure the expensive circuits rather than to shard smaller or serialise harder. The measurement instrument matters here too — the honest figure is `RssAnon` sampled from `/proc`, because the cgroup's `memory.peak` counts reclaimable page cache as well as anonymous memory and overstates the charged footprint in exactly the misleading direction. Until it builds, both this row's kernel claim and the tree's budget gate are owed, and the row says so rather than quoting the generator's own verdicts as the kernel's. The generator remains a gate — `run_gate "circuit transcription freshness (OBL-T7)"`, `--check`, byte for byte — so the artefact is what the sources produce today or the run fails. **What the count turned out to be is the finding, and it is larger than this row's framing assumed.** Under the model's rule **170 of 181 circuits are refuted**, and the generator decomposes those 170 by asking the *checker* — `classify`, the gate's own classifier, called with the repository-relative path its manifest is keyed by — what it made of the exposure the model refused: **155 are the checker's `redundant`** (pinned by another exposed determination; the model's rule is sequential and does not follow that), **11 are `declared-free`**, **3 are the checker's own failures** (`OBL-Z16`'s instances, where the two agree), and **1 is `bound` through a `constrain_equal_base` whose determining side is a declared constant** — the model's constant boundary *met*, not hypothetical, in the direction where a constant determines rather than is exposed. The opposite direction is met by no circuit: 277 undetermined exposures across the 170, none of them a constant. So the model does not contradict the checker, it **refines** it, and `NoFreeInstances`' name is a *strict* reading this tree mostly does not meet while the property the tree enforces is the checker's four-verdict rule. The axiom is still not replaced — `(r, s) ↦ the circuit source` remains absent, and this supplies the data that bridge needs without supplying the bridge — and the register's `OBL-Z1` row is unaffected, since the checker reads sources and the model reads its own transcription of them. **One defect in the generator, recorded because it was a reproducibility bug rather than a style one**: its first version derived the 181 Lean identifiers from the absolute paths `zk_files()` yields, so every name read `__home_patrick_…` and the generated file (and its freshness check) depended on where the tree is checked out; found by reading the emitted names, fixed before the first build. **APPENDED 2026-09-24 (evening): the transcription's own two defects, found by following `OBL-Z12`'s residue to the statement it needs.** (1) **Its range-check statements named the wrong argument.** A `.zk` call reads `range_check(64, amount)`; the generator recorded `args[0]` — the *width* — so every statement it emitted was `.rangeCheck (.lit 64)` and the checked value was recorded nowhere. Measured over the emitted module before the change: **107 × `.rangeCheck (.lit 64)`, 1 × `.lit 253`, and zero `.var`.** The tree's own checker binds `args[1]` (`script/circuit_instance_derivation.py`: `range_check\(\s*\d+\s*,\s*([A-Za-z_]\w*)\s*\)`), so the stating side and the reading side disagreed about which argument the statement is about — and because `boundWalk` discards a range check, **no verdict noticed**. Fixed by giving `Stmt.rangeCheck` two fields (`Nat → Expr → Stmt`) and emitting `.rangeCheck 64 (.var "amount")`. Carrying **both** is what makes `OBL-Z12`'s residue statable: the bound a check supplies is width-dependent (`src/zk/vm.rs:559`/`:563` are a 64-bit and a 253-bit chip, and `Comparison.range_check_64_is_bounded` is the `<10, 64>` instance). (2) **The module contradicted itself, and re-running the generator reproduced the contradiction.** Its header read `181 circuits / 2747 statements / 170 refuted` while its own summary line read `179 / 2692 / 168`, because the header's numbers were **literals in the generator** and only the summary was computed. The counts are now substituted from the data (`{n_circuits}`, `{n_refuted}`), the historical ones are dated, and the checker's `OBL-Z16` figure is stated with the day it was written rather than as a present fact. **Two committed causes of staleness, not one**: the module was stale from `185c56d8a2` (the lottery circuit deleted, whose block it still carried) *and* from `5c83f85cee` (bridge's `withdraw.zk` dropped `token_minimum`, so the module still exposed it). The freshness gate had been red since 19:15, and the second cause was **invisible to a path-existence test** because that circuit's *file* still existed — the deletion of a circuit and the change of a circuit fail freshness identically but only one leaves a missing path. **Measured after the fix**: `--check` exit 0 (`178 circuits, 2677 statements, 21 ignored opcode calls, 11 hold, 167 fail`); `scripts/lean-build.sh build DarkFi Transcribed` exit 0 under the cgroup. **The invariant that the new fields change no verdict is checked, not asserted**: 215 of the diff's 299 changed lines are the `.rangeCheck` rewrites (108 removed + 107 added — one fewer because a circuit was deleted), and the hold count is **unchanged at 11** while the refuted count moved 168 → 167 exactly as that circuit's removal requires. **Not yet run, and so not claimed: gate 22's collector.** Two attempts were blocked by the Lean lane (another session's long run, then that session's own collector), so `@[axiom_budget]` measurement is **unverified for this change**. Two facts about that instrument are recorded rather than repaired, because it is another session's in-flight file: its SKIP message reads *"this means the library does not compile"* when the library compiles and the **lock** was held — a diagnostic that would send a reader to the wrong object — and a collector run against stale `.olean`s reports the axiom inventory of an older tree (observed: a 18:27-era `Transcribed.olean` under a 20:06 source, reporting 703 theorems). **THE ROW'S OWN OWED CLAIM IS DISCHARGED, and its two sharding sentences are history.** The kernel has now closed all 181 transcription verdicts — `proofs/lean/.lake/build/lib/Transcribed.olean` exists at **6,220,160 bytes** after a 71 s / 743.6 MB build of `DarkFi Transcribed` — so "the kernel closes every verdict" is *verified* rather than predicted, and the axiom gate runs over the whole set. The shard modules were **withdrawn** rather than kept: they were a workaround for the inline-`if` cost, and extracting `bindAssign` (`InstanceDerivation.lean:189`) removed the cause, taking the worst circuit from >23.9 GiB and a timeout to 463 MB / 25 s. The two sentences this row carried about the shards were true when written and are superseded here rather than deleted, which is this register's rule for a record that measurement overtook. `OBL-T7` stays PARTLY for the one thing no transcription supplies: the `(r, s) ↦ the circuit source` mapping, which is still not in the tree **APPENDED 2026-09-24 (later still): the "impossible" is the wrong arrow, and what is missing is an un-emitted emission rather than an unmakeable term.** This cell, `Axioms.lean:573-575` and `Circuits/InstanceDerivation.lean:65-66` all infer from "a Lean term cannot read a `.zk` file" that the bridge "stays the residual" / "remains absent" / "is not in the tree". **The premise is true and the inference is not**: that sentence limits Lean *terms*, and the bridge would be supplied by a *generator* — which is the pattern this tree already uses, and which this cell's own last append already concedes ("supplies the data that bridge needs without supplying the bridge"). Measured 2026-09-24 by reading the manifests and the SDK: **the mapping exists as data and is machine-consumed today.** `src/sdk/src/prover.rs:33-40` documents the join the prover performs — "Find the action + its function; the function's `proof_circuit` names a `[[circuits]]` entry `(name, namespace)`" — and `src/sdk/src/contract_client.rs:413-425` performs it; **31 of 32** `manifest.toml` carry `proof_circuit` and **31** carry `[[circuits]]`. What does **not** carry it is `Transcribed.lean`: measured, that module contains **zero** `Resource`/`Action` symbols, because the generator enumerates circuits by globbing `proof/*.zk` and names them from the path — its `load_manifest()` call is `script/circuit_free_instances.txt`, not `manifest.toml`. **Two difficulties, measured rather than assumed.** (a) The *resource* half is thin in the manifests — only **9 of 32** carry `[[actions]]` and **8** carry `required_barbs` — while the Lean `Resource`s are already defined with their barb sets (`Capability/Composition.lean`, 14 of them), so the join should take `r` from Lean and `s → circuit` from the manifest, and the missing manifest barbs may not block it. (b) **The relation is one-to-many, and some pairs are unrecorded**: `bearer_bond`'s three functions share `Redeem_V2` (`manifest.toml:22,28,34`), `promissory_note`'s two share `Transfer_V2` (`:46,53`), and `native_token`'s `transfer`/`spend` carry **no** `proof_circuit` at all — only a comment naming `Burn_V2` + `Mint_V2` — so an emission must be `(r, s) → List circuit` and the unrecorded pairs need a decision rather than a guess. **What would close this row**: a generated, freshness-gated module indexing the transcription's existing per-circuit defs by `(Resource, Action)`, whose per-pair verdict is assembled from the per-circuit ones, plus one decision per unrecorded pair. The step is therefore a **unit of work with a known shape** rather than a research problem — narrower than this cell has claimed for months, and the correction is recorded here rather than only in a plan **APPENDED 2026-09-24 (last): the mapping's cost is now *measured*, and its first real use case is already a gap.** The join a generator must perform is `action → [[functions]].proof_circuit → [[circuits]].(name, namespace) → the .zk identity`, and `src/sdk/src/prover.rs:33-40` shows the SDK performing exactly it at runtime. Measured against the 14 `Resource`s and 14 `Action`s `Capability/Composition.lean` names, and the 32 manifests, there are **five** failure modes, not the two this cell estimated: **(1) the resource half is not recorded as data** — only **3 of 14** resource name strings equal a contract directory (`native_token`, `tender`, `attestation`); **10** are substring matches needing a rule (`native_token_coinbase` → `native_token`, `purse_balance` → `purse`, `identity_credential` → `identity`); and **one matches nothing — `dao_governance`**, because the DAO's logic lives in `dao_escrow` and shares no substring with it. So the generator needs an explicit resource→contract map, and **that map exists nowhere in the tree**. **(2) action names are ambiguous by themselves** — `"withdraw"` matches **four** contracts, `"deposit"` three, `"transfer"` three (`drain_protection`, `native_token`, `promissory_note`) — so the resource must disambiguate, which is why (1) is load-bearing rather than cosmetic. **(3) four of the fourteen action names have no manifest function at all**: `claim_coinbase`, `verify_credential`, `verify_attestation`, `oracle_push_value`. **(4) three functions carry no `proof_circuit`** — and one is **`native_token`'s `transfer`, the pair the Lean proofs actually use** (`nativeTokenResource`, `transferAction`): its circuit set (`Burn_V2` + `Mint_V2`) is recorded in a *comment* rather than in data, so the "one-to-many and unrecorded" case arrives at the first pair anyone would try. **(5) circuit names are not unique** — 178 declarations carry only **157 distinct names**; **15 names collide across files** (`UpdateConfigV2` ×3; `DepositV2`, `WithdrawV2`, `Redeem_V2`, `ExecuteSwapV2`, `CreateSwapV2`, `CancelSwapV2` ×2 each), so the manifest's `(name, namespace)` *pair* is load-bearing and a name-based join would silently pick a sibling contract's circuit. **And two by-products the measurement turned up, which are defects rather than costs**: **12 circuits declared in `.zk` sources are in no manifest** — every one of them in `proofs/core/` or `bin/darkirc/`, i.e. the circuits with no host contract, which is structural and benign rather than a gap; and **3 manifest circuit entries name a circuit with no `.zk` identity at all** — `CreateJobWithCapabilityV2`, `CreateJobWithMilestonesAndCapabilityV2`, `RedeemStableV1` — which is the *reverse* direction, a manifest claiming a proof that does not exist, and it survives `check-circuit-metadata-alignment.sh` because that gate compares per-circuit metadata vectors against circuits it finds, never manifest entries against sources. That one wants its own row rather than a paragraph. **So the unit's shape is: a resource→contract map (new data, 14 entries), one decision per unmatched action (4), one circuit *set* per ambiguous or absent `proof_circuit`, and the emission itself** — and the measurement above is what a reader should size it from, not this cell's earlier estimate **CORRECTED THE SAME DAY, on one clause of that append**: it said the three manifest entries naming a circuit with no `.zk` "survive `check-circuit-metadata-alignment.sh` because that gate compares per-circuit metadata vectors against circuits it finds, never manifest entries against sources". That is true of that *gate* and false as an implication about the tree — **`src/contract/test-harness/tests/manifest_proof_declarations.rs` checks precisely it**, in both directions, with a ratchet that lists those three sites by name under `OBL-C91`. The lesson is the one this register has now recorded twice: a search for a *check* that covers only `scripts/` and `run_gate` misses the test tree, and "nothing checks this" is a claim about the whole tree or it is not a claim. It cost a duplicate row here, minted and restated within the day **APPENDED 2026-09-24 (last): the join is executable, and it resolves — with the two pairs that resolve to nothing being two of the four the Lean proofs name.** `script/circuit_index_map.txt` is the missing half this cell's earlier append measured (the reviewed resource→contract map, 14 entries, plus the four action names that differ from their manifest function); `scripts/gen_circuit_index.py` performs the join — a `CapabilityType` definition → the resource and action name strings → the map → the manifest's `[[functions]].proof_circuit` → the `.zk` whose `circuit "…"` matches → **the transcription's def name, imported from `gen_circuit_transcription.circuit_name` rather than re-derived**, because a second implementation of the naming rule is a second thing that can disagree. **Measured: 14 pairs instantiated, 12 resolved, 2 resolved to no circuit, 0 failures.** The two are `nativeTokenTransferType` (`transfer`) and `nativeTokenCoinbaseType` (`claim_coinbase`) — and that is the fact worth keeping: **of the four pairs the Lean existence theorems name (`Inversion.lean`'s `nativeTokenTransferExists`, `daoVoteExists`, `tenderBidExists`, and the coinbase type), two are exactly the two with no circuit**, because `native_token`'s manifest is the FYI document its own header says it is and its coinbase path is host-side (`entrypoint/mod.rs:1000-1006`). So the first pairs a discharge would reach are the ones that resolve to nothing — a *decision* the map records rather than a failure the join reports, which is why its `claim_coinbase` line reads "resolves to NO CIRCUIT, and that is a decision". **Deliberately not done here: no Lean module is emitted yet.** The join is a *report* — nothing runs it — because emitting `CircuitIndex.lean` before the emission's shape is settled would put a generated artefact in the tree with no freshness gate and no consumer, and that is what this campaign deletes rather than keeps. What the emission needs is now named: the 12 resolved pairs carry the transcription's def names (`dao_escrow_vote_claim`, `purse_withdraw`, `bridge_deposit`, …, printed by the script), so a per-pair verdict can be assembled from the per-circuit ones. **Two bugs of mine died on the way, both the same class**: the first version indexed the contract directory by position in an absolute path (`path.parts[2]` is `patrick`), so every lookup missed and it reported **12 false failures**; and the map was missing `vote : vote_claim`, a decision its own `dao_governance` reason had already argued for without writing down. Both were caught by *running* it rather than by reading it. **APPENDED 2026-09-24 (later): the model now carries the rule the tree enforces, and it is weaker than this row's axiom is named.** `Circuits/InstanceDerivation.lean` gains `DisclosureRule` — the strict `NoFreeInstance` plus the checker's third classification, `redundant` (a witness already inside another exposed determination) — and the strict predicate is untouched, which is checkable: `scripts/gen_circuit_transcription.py --check` exits 0 with the same **11 hold / 167 fail**. What is new is the measurement that makes the emission above tractable: **all twelve of the pairs this row maps fail the strict rule for one reason, `redundant`, and none of the twelve has a `declared-free` site at all** — so a per-pair verdict can rest on the structural rule with no external exception list behind it, which is the opposite of the shape a weakened predicate usually takes. The strength change is recorded rather than glossed: `noFreeInstance_imp_disclosureRule` proves the new rule weaker, and the three classes it does *not* model are counted in the module note (`declared-free` 12 circuits, a constant-determined `bound` 1 circuit, an exposed `constant` 0 circuits). Two of my own errors died getting it to build and both are the file's own documented hazards: a `/--` doc comment cannot precede `mutual` (the file already used `/- -/` there), and the worked `redundant` circuit's `held` omitted its domain constant, so the rule correctly refused the very case it was written to admit. **APPENDED 2026-09-24 (later still): the mapping is IN THE TREE, generated and gated — so this row's residue is no longer the mapping.** `proofs/lean/src/CircuitIndex.lean` (`scripts/gen_circuit_index.py`, freshness-gated byte for byte beside the transcription's, built as the third library `CircuitIndex` because it imports `Transcribed`) carries one theorem per pair — `DisclosureRule` at the pair's transcribed statement list, closed by the kernel rather than predicted by the generator — for **12** of the 14 pairs `Capability/Composition.lean` instantiates, plus the 2 that resolve to no circuit as *data* (`circuitlessPairs`: `native_token/transfer` and `native_token/pow_reward`, each declaring no `proof_circuit`), so "no circuit" is distinguishable from "not reached". Measured: **all 12 have their strict verdict refuted, every one of them with `redundant` as the checker's class**, and none of the 12 has a `declared-free` instance — which the generator now checks and fails on, because `DisclosureRule` does not model that class. **What this row's residue is now**: `Axioms.NoFreeInstances` is still not *defined* over the index, so the next step is the definition rather than a mapping, and the strength change that definition has to record is already written down (the rule proved at these 12 pairs is the checker's, weaker than the axiom's name). One correction belongs here rather than only in the Lean file: the predicate as first committed refused **6 of the 12 pairs**, and the cause was `determinerOf` handling only an equality with an op expression on one side — `purse/balance` binds `tx_binding` with two *names* — plus preferring the binding over the assignment where the checker prefers the assignment. Found by `#eval`-probing the failing circuit, not by reading. **APPENDED 2026-09-24 (this unit), and the status moves to `RESTATED`: the axiom is deleted, so this row no longer describes an uninterpreted predicate.** `Axioms.NoFreeInstances` is gone; `Capability.Inversion.CircuitDerivable` carries the circuit's transcribed `held` names and `stmts` with a `noFreeInstances : DisclosureRule held stmts` field, and `CircuitIndex.lean` supplies one inhabitant per pair. **Why `RESTATED` and not `SATISFIED`**: the proposition above is the *strict* reading, and the strict reading is refuted at all twelve pairs — what the tree now proves is the checker's weaker rule. So the row's claim has been restated rather than met, and the restatement is the strength change rather than a bookkeeping edit. The inventory above drops from seven to six in the same commit, and the check that enforces it failed in exactly the direction it was built for before this edit — "declared in the register's inventory but is not in `Axioms.lean`" — which is recorded there. |
| OBL-T8 | **SATISFIED** | Genesis is a pure function of its inputs — no ambient authority, stages are pure transitions, embedded contract bytes are a quoted argument | `doc/src/arch/genesis.md` §"Genesis Is A Pure Function"; `bin/dwowd` `init_genesis` (hard-error on hash mismatch) | the pinned hash in `bin/dwowd/genesis_hash.txt` — an *observable witness*, not a proof; the audit's B-series shows the pin was broken by build non-reproducibility | C |
| OBL-T9 | **ACCEPTED-WITH-REASON** | The barb alphabet distinguishes the types it claims to: distinct capability types have distinct, non-subsumed barb sets, so a barb set identifies a capability | `Capability/Types.lean` (`allPrimitiveTypes`, 33 barbs); `Capability/Composition.lean` (13 `Resource`s) | **FALSE as it stands, and measured.** Of 13 capability resources, **12** have a barb set contained in the union of the others, so they contribute nothing to a composition the others do not; `purse_deposit` and `purse_withdrawal` have **identical** barb sets; of 16 primitives, **11** carry no barb of their own (`nullifier = {nullify}` ⊆ `intentNullifier = {gate, nullify}`). The consequence: **38** distinct barb-set unions over all `2^13 = 8192` subsets — the type count is *sublinear* in the resources, not exponential. The same defect that made `primitiveTypesAreParetoEfficient` false before `↓shard` was added | M | **Measured false as written, confirmed by re-measurement on 2026-09-22, with the figures refreshed and one of them not reproduced.** Method, stated so it can be re-run: extract every `requiredBarbs` set from `Capability/Composition.lean` and every `barbs` set from `Types.lean`, then compute subsumption, the identical-set groups, and the distinct unions over all subsets. *Resources — the row's counts are stale by one, and the conclusion holds.* **14** resources are defined (the row says 13; the register's own "stale prose" note already moved this to 14 with `oracleOperatorType`), **13 of the 14** have their barb set contained in the union of the others (row: 12 of 13), and **48** distinct barb-set unions arise over all `2^14 = 16384` subsets (row: 38 of `2^13`) — so the type count remains **sublinear** in the resources, which is the finding. *The identical pair is confirmed, and the model says so itself*: `purseWithdrawResource.requiredBarbs` (`Composition.lean:201`) and `purseDepositResource.requiredBarbs` (`:216`) are both `{spend, commit, nullify, dispatch, denominate}`, and the definition above the second one is annotated *"Purse Deposit (consumable via nullifier, **identical barbs to Withdraw**)"*. Under `OBL-T2`'s definition — `typesDistinct := barbs ≠ barbs` — **a deposit and a withdrawal are the same capability type**, which for two opposite operations on the same purse is the sharpest form of this row's defect. *Primitives — not reproduced, and recorded rather than smoothed.* The row says "of 16 primitives, **11** carry no barb of their own", with `nullifier = {nullify} ⊆ intentNullifier = {gate, nullify}` as its example; **17** primitives carry barbs (the row's 16 is stale by one — 20 `PrimitiveType` definitions exist, three of them — `rawBytes`, `rawFieldElement`, `rawCurvePoint` — are barbless *by construction* and the file says they are not valid DarkWow types), and under the row's own example's reading — one set contained in another's — I measure **6**, not 11: `nullifier` (which its example names, so the reading is the intended one), `contractId`, `funcId`, `merkleNode`, `ownedSecretKey`, `bridgeAddress`. No two primitive sets are equal. The gap between 6 and 11 is recorded, not resolved: the direction of the finding is unaffected, and the row should not be quoted for the count **ACCEPTED-WITH-REASON 2026-09-23: the numbers reproduce, the proposition does not, and the difference is that the row asks for a property the model never defines — the alphabet is held by decision, with the cost stated at the end of this cell.** Every figure below was recomputed from `Capability/Types.lean` and `Capability/Composition.lean` by parsing them, not recalled — the analysis is now a script, `script/capability_barb_analysis.py`, so the next reader re-runs it instead of re-deriving it. *What reproduces.* **14** `Resource` definitions; **13 of the 14** have their `requiredBarbs` set contained in the union of the others, the sole exception being `native_token_coinbase`, the only resource requiring `Barb.mine`; **48** distinct barb-set unions over all `2^14 = 16384` subsets — so the row's conclusion that the type count is *sublinear* in the resources stands. The identical pair is confirmed: `purseWithdrawResource.requiredBarbs` (`Composition.lean:201`) and `purseDepositResource.requiredBarbs` (`:216`) are both `{spend, commit, nullify, dispatch, denominate}`, and the definition above the second is annotated *"identical barbs to Withdraw"* — the model states the collision itself. **17** primitives carry barbs, not 16. *What does not reproduce, corrected rather than smoothed.* The row's "**11** carry no barb of their own" is the count under a **different** reading — "11 of 17 primitive barb sets are contained in the union of the others" — while under the row's own worked example's reading (a set contained in *another single* set: `nullifier ⊂ intentNullifier`) the count is **6**: `nullifier`, `contractId`, `funcId`, `merkleNode`, `ownedSecretKey`, `bridgeAddress`. No two of the 17 are equal. *The proposition is what is wrong, and this is the substance of the restatement.* The row requires "distinct **and non-subsumed** barb sets". The model's distinctness is defined as bare set inequality and **only for primitives** — `def typesDistinct (t1 t2 : PrimitiveType) : Prop := t1.barbs ≠ t2.barbs` (`Types.lean:268-272`), with no subsumption condition anywhere and no domain that includes a `Resource`: the resource-level analogue `capTypesDistinct` (`Composition.lean:288-290`) is a bare `def` with **no theorem attached to it and no application to any pair**. The stated properties are `pairsDistinctCheck_eq_true` and `primitiveTypesAreParetoEfficient` (`Pareto.lean:81`, `:90`), and both type-check under `lake build`. So "non-subsumed" is the row's own addition, and the measured subsumption is a property of an alphabet that names **permissions**, not operations — `ocap.md` §5.1 declares the trade-off on purpose: *"defined privilege containment, not least privilege"*, with the reasons. *The identical pair, read as what it is.* `purse_deposit` and `purse_withdrawal` are not two capability types sharing a barb set; they are two **actions on one capability kind**, and `purse/manifest.toml:234` and `:240` give them identical `required_barbs = ["Spend", "Nullify", "ProveInclusion", "Commit", "Dispatch", "Gate"]` for the same reason the Lean does — both consume the purse capability and produce a new one. The barb set names what the action permits, and a deposit and a withdrawal permit the same permissions; the thing that distinguishes them is the `gate` barb's function id, one level down. So the sharpest form the row cites is the alphabet working as designed, not failing. *What is genuinely missing, and it is a coverage gap rather than a modelling one.* **Nothing checks any of this.** `contrib/barb_alphabet_diff.sh` and `contrib/primitive_barbs_diff.sh` — the two scripts that compare the four representations of the alphabet — are invoked by **no** gate runner: not `scripts/run-all-tests.sh`, not `hooks/pre-commit`, not `contrib/ci/`. And `src/sdk/src/capability.rs:694`'s `test_all_primitives_have_distinct_barb_sets` lists only **10** of the 17 primitives, omitting `intentNullifier`, `bridgeCapNullifier`, `bridgeAddress`, `externalChain`, `dleqProof`, `chainDepositProof` and `relayerCap`. It passes, and it is a strictly weaker check than the Lean theorem it mirrors — which is the same class `OBL-C76` names: a test that does not run in the configuration used to claim verification. *The decision, made rather than deferred*: the alphabet is **held**. Adding a distinguishing barb per resource would make the barb set an identifier, which is a different design than the one `type-system.md` §1.1 defines (barbs are observable actions and deliberately coarse) and would change a normative 33-constructor alphabet represented in four places (`dwow_core::barb::BarbId`, `dwow_sdk::capability::Barb`, the Lean model, the docs) plus the Python model — a change to the capability kernel, offered here with its cost rather than taken. What is taken is the measurement, so this row's figures are re-run rather than recalled, and the two un-wired diff scripts are named as the coverage gap a gate would close **— corrected 2026-09-24: they are wired, so that gap is closed.** `scripts/run-all-tests.sh` now runs both as gates (`barb alphabet agreement (OBL-T3/T9)` and `primitive barb mapping (OBL-T3)`, immediately after the documentation-index gate); all five representations are therefore checked by a runner at last, which is what this cell said nothing did. The second script reports the model-vs-Rust difference directly, and it names **seven** primitives the model has and the Rust does not — `BridgeAddress`, `BridgeCapNullifier`, `ChainDepositProof`, `DLEqProof`, `ExternalChain`, `IntentNullifier`, `RelayerCapability` — which is exactly the set the sdk's `test_all_primitives_have_distinct_barb_sets` omits, so the script **subsumes** that test rather than duplicating it. The test was deliberately **not** raised to 17 primitives: `src/sdk/**` is in *every* contract's `SOURCE_MANIFEST`, so editing it invalidates all 32 recorded artifacts and fails the freshness gate until they are rebuilt, and `scripts/check-artifact-freshness.sh:13-14,58` says exactly that in the tree — a 32-artifact rebuild is the wrong price for a check a wired script already performs over all seventeen. **The resource-level distinctness this cell measured is now a theorem as well**: `Capability.Composition.resourceDistinctness_is_false`, at budget 0 |
