| OBL-T15 | **SATISFIED** | No restricted name crosses its declared scope boundary: a key derived for one contract instance is not usable in another | `proofs/lean/src/DarkFi/Capability/KeyScope.lean`; `type-system.md` §7.3 | `HIGH-17` in `HAZOP/High.lean`; the collector's own budget rows | M | **NEW 2026-09-24, and what it records is a gap that was invisible for as long as this layer has existed.** `type-system.md` §7 lists seven invariants it calls compiler-enforced, and the third — scope restriction, "a `SecretKey` derived for contract instance `A` SHALL NOT be usable in contract instance `B`" — **had no model anywhere in this tree**: the only occurrence of `derive_instance` under `proofs/lean/` was a comment (`Composition.lean:417`). What it protects is the privacy mechanism rather than a convenience: `privacy-model.md:59-60` forbids a stable identity appearing in any block or transaction, and address cycling is *how* that is achieved (`SecretKey::derive_instance`, `src/sdk/src/crypto/keypair.rs:202-222`, domain `from_raw([8,0,0,0])`). `KeyScope.lean` models the derivation as `poseidon_hash_output [8, secret, contractId, instance]` and derives the invariant from `poseidon_collision_resistance` at budget 1: equal derived keys force equal `(contractId, instance)` pairs, so the boundary cannot be crossed without a collision. **Its budgets are collector-measured rather than inferred from a neighbour's shape** — `scopeRestriction` and its two consequences at 1, the refutation at 0 — and the file carries the theorem that gives it teeth: `scopeRestriction_is_false_for_unscopedDerive` exhibits a derivation that ignores its scope, which is exactly what using the master identity directly *is*, so the property is a claim about this derivation rather than a shape true of every function. **Honest scope, which the file states rather than leaves to a reader**: the instance-element encoding (the zero-pad-or-truncate to 32 bytes and the non-canonical rejection at `keypair.rs:209-218`) is not modelled, so which byte strings are admissible instances is not claimed; and `poseidon_hash_output` is opaque, so this rests on the injectivity assumption and inherits `HIGH-13`'s scope exactly as `Purse.lean:68` does — it is not a statement about the deployed sponge. |
| OBL-T16 | **SATISFIED** | A gate that cannot run says so, and never reports the property it did not check | `script/check_lean_axioms.py` (the collector path and the INCOMPLETE exit status) | found by an independent reader 2026-09-24; fixed in `d515f73418` and `36adb67032` | H | **NEW 2026-09-24: the assumption-boundary gate passed for the wrong reason twice in one day, which is this register's own failure mode arriving inside the gate that guards the boundary.** *(a) A skip that read as a pass.* When the collector could not run, `check_trust_declarations` and `check_tautologies` returned `None` **without printing anything** and the table emitted nothing, so the tally said "3 check(s) could not run" against a single SKIP line while the script still printed `PASS: Lean assumption boundary intact` — a pass over checks it had not performed. Each now prints its own `SKIP`, and the verdict names what was skipped instead of claiming the boundary. *(b) A rename that hid a real check, and counting could not see it.* `--stream` merged the child's stderr into the rows the Python parses; Lean flushes stdout at 4096-byte boundaries and stderr immediately, so the collector's one-line summary landed *inside* a row: `supply_chain_invariant` arrived as `nvariant`, its `@[axiom_budget 3]` was never checked, and **the row count stayed correct — 704 checked against 704 fed, one of them fictitious.** `reconcile()` now asserts the returned key set equals the fed name set and names both sides, `--self-test` reproduces the corruption synthetically and is wired as a gate of its own, and the collector's log is per-run (`$LEAN_BUILD_LOG.<pid>`) rather than one shared path. **The rule it leaves behind for anything parsing a guarded command's stdout**: reconcile *identity*, never count rows — and never let one channel carry two writer classes. |
| OBL-T17 | **ACCEPTED-WITH-REASON** | Every capability type proved in Lean is constructible by the Rust `wallet_construct`, or its unconstructibility is recorded with its cause | `src/sdk/src/capability.rs` (`wallet_construct`, `Primitive`); `proofs/lean/src/DarkFi/Capability/Composition.lean` | `contrib/capability_type_diff.sh`, wired into `scripts/run-all-tests.sh` | M | **NEW 2026-09-24, and it is about a sentence: `src/sdk/src/capability.rs:509-510` says *"Every construction that is proved in Lean4 must also succeed here"*, and measured that is true of 9 of the 14 — with nothing in the tree able to see the other five.** `Composition.lean` defines 14 `CapabilityType`s and the test module below that sentence constructs 9. **Four cannot be constructed at all**: `tenderBidType` needs `dleqProof`, `bridgeDepositType` needs `bridgeAddress` and `chainDepositProof`, `bridgeWithdrawType` needs `bridgeAddress`, `bridgeCapNullifier` and `dleqProof`, and `oracleOperatorType` needs `dleqProof` — and `Primitive` has 10 variants against the model's 17, with exactly those barbs (`BridgeAddress`, `BridgeCapNullifier`, `ChainDepositProof`, `DLEqProof`, `ExternalChain`, `IntentNullifier`, `RelayerCapability`) absent, as `contrib/primitive_barbs_diff.sh` has reported since it was written. So no input whatsoever makes `wallet_construct` return those four types; the test module's failure to test them is not an omission a test could fix. **The fifth is the different case and the one that reads as carelessness**: `purseDepositType` names only primitives Rust carries, so it *would* construct, and no test constructs it. **The decision is `OBL-T9`'s and it is not a mechanical edit**: adding the four `Primitive` variants changes `src/sdk/**`, which is in *every* contract's `SOURCE_MANIFEST`, so it invalidates all 32 recorded artifacts and — for a change that alters compiled code — moves the genesis pin with them, which is the batch `OBL-C121`'s rule reserves. The alphabet stays held and the four stay unconstructible, recorded here rather than discovered again. **What the gate does not check, stated because a gate read as stronger than it is is this register's own failure mode**: it extracts the three tables *textually* and diffs declared primitive sets — it does not run the Rust test, so "constructible" is what the test's `is_none`/`is_some` assertion claims, and the evidence that the assertion passes is `cargo test`'s rather than this gate's. It was falsified in six directions on a copy of the tree under `/tmp` before wiring, each exiting non-zero and naming the cause: a primitive dropped from a Rust list, a Lean type with no Rust counterpart, a Rust type with no Lean one, a renamed Python table constant, an unregistered Python table, and a declared-absent entry that had gone stale. **Two further measurements it reports rather than smooths**: the Python model states a capability table for **1 of the 14**, and on that one pair (`native_token`/`transfer`) it requires `prove-inclusion` where Lean and Rust require six barbs — the composition covers it either way, so nothing is unconstructible, but the three implementations do not state the same requirement for the same resource. **And the count itself was wrong in the prose**: `proofs/lean/README.md` said 12 types and listed twelve in two places for as long as there have been 14 — `purseDepositType` and `oracleOperatorType` were added to `Composition.lean` and neither list followed, and `Composition.lean`'s own note and this register's notes on it repeated "twelve". Corrected in `ed325935ef` and `b5d1d8c85d`, with the number now owned by the gate that measures it. The unit's landing is `2e442ad492`. |
| OBL-T18 | **SATISFIED** | Every check in `proofs/lean/src/Main.lean` is compiled by the build and run by a gate, and each of them fails the run rather than printing a result beside it | `proofs/lean/src/Main.lean`; `proofs/lean/lakefile.lean` (`lean_exe Main`); `scripts/check-lean-suite.sh` | `scripts/check-lean-suite.sh`, wired into `scripts/run-all-tests.sh` | M | **NEW 2026-09-24: a file that `README.md` quoted an "expected output" block from had never once run, and nothing in the tree could tell.** `src/Main.lean` was in **no `lean_lib` and no `lean_exe`**, so `lake build` never compiled it; `grep` over `scripts/`, `Makefile` and `hooks/` found **no invocation**; and it did not compile in the first place — `lake env lean src/Main.lean` reported errors on its HEAD version as well, so 21 of them had sat there for as long as anyone looked. **The file's own header was the only record, and it was wrong in both halves**: it said "21 errors… two causes, both mechanical", and the measurement that opened the repair gives **23 error lines in five classes** — four `unknown namespace` (the header claimed `Pareto.lean` and `Distinction.lean` declare `namespace DarkFi.Capability`; **none of the four modules it names declares a namespace at all**), twelve `failed to synthesize` (nine `ToString (Finset Barb)`, three `ToString Prop`), three `ambiguous` (`MERKLE_DEPTH` and `PRACTICAL_MAX_OBJECTS` are defined by both `Limits` and `CeilingDerivation`, both opened file-wide), one `maximum recursion depth` (a single `do` block of ~350 statements — measured to be the block's length and not any statement, by elaborating the same projection in a file of its own where it is clean), and one `unknown identifier` that was a cascade of that one rather than a defect of its own. **The larger half is what the checks did when they ran, and the file is the register's for this reason rather than for the compile errors**: four counterexample scans printed `Bugs found: N` and exited 0, eight combinatorial expectations printed a ✓/✗ marker and exited 0, the HAZOP summary printed four **literal** counts, and the EC classification printed verdicts from a table of literals with no connection to the model — so even had it compiled and run, every one of its findings could have been false with a green exit. The repair makes each throw, derives the HAZOP counts from `riskMatrix` and the EC verdicts from `ECMulKind.baseIsConstant` (they agree with the literals they replaced: 4/5/6/7, and `var_base` the only prover-chosen kind), gives the file a `lean_exe` target, and gates it. **Falsified three ways on a copy under `/tmp` before wiring**: a scan's expected count off by one, the O-cap bound inverted, and a closing marker renamed — each exits 1, the first two printing the suite's own assertion, the third by the gate's own "did it run" check. The one thing the gate does *not* check is the content of the numbers themselves; the file's assertions are the checks, and the gate's job is to not swallow them. Reader note: the deletions this file's history would otherwise suggest (the `#eval` block in `Composition.lean`, the deleted `True` theorems) did not apply here — the checks are counterexample *searches*, which is a different kind of evidence from a proof, and the sections that name the theorem superseding them (`less_than_or_equal_sound`, `is_not_equal_fully_pure`) are kept in the output. |
| OBL-T19 | **SATISFIED** | The capability side's single-use rule and the consensus side's replay rule are one rule over two data shapes: the two transitions build the same spent set, and the agreement between the two states is preserved by taking either transition | `proofs/lean/src/DarkFi/Consensus/NullifierLifecycle.lean` (`spentSet`, `foldl_spend_iff`, `spentSet_append_foldl`, `spend_fold_agrees_with_applyExercise`, `the_consensus_side_records_the_exercised_nullifier`); `Capability/Exercise.lean` | the theorems themselves, every one at budget 0, collector-measured | M | **NEW 2026-09-24, minted for a gap the file named about itself and had left standing.** `NullifierLifecycle.lean`'s module note said, in the "deliberately not" list, that `Capability/Exercise.lean`'s `validExercise`/`consume_is_single_use` is "*the same rule over a different data shape — `PublicState.spentNullifiers`, a `List`, on the contract side — and this module is the consensus side over the store. Bridging a `List` and a predicate is a separate unit and nothing consumes it yet, so the relation is stated here rather than mechanized*". Two modules of one tree encoding the double-spend rule with nothing between them is this register's own class: the two can drift while both read as implemented. **What landed is stronger than the resemblance the note asserted, and that is the point of stating it as preservation rather than as agreement**: `spentSet` is the view from a `List NullifierValue` to a `NullifierValue → Prop`; `spentSet_append_foldl` shows the two *constructions* are one function — `applyExercise` appends, `spend` extends, and folding `spend` over the same nullifiers reproduces the append exactly; and `spend_fold_agrees_with_applyExercise` shows the agreement between the two states **survives both transitions**, so a change to either rule fails here rather than in two documents drifting apart. The refusals are then one fact read two ways: `the_consensus_side_records_the_exercised_nullifier` is `spend_records` transported through the bridge, and the capability side's theorem is its negative. **Honest scope, which is the half that is *not* bridged**: `canSpendAt` is maturity **and** replay, and only the replay half has a counterpart — `validExercise` contains no maturity check at all, so nothing here says an immature capability is unspendable on the contract side, and the coinbase's value remains `SupplyChain.lean`'s and `OBL-C5`'s territory. The bridge is therefore exact on what it covers and silent on the rest, which is the shape a row should carry rather than a claim of equivalence between the two modules entire. |
| OBL-T20 | **PARTLY** | §6.5's transaction status lifecycle is modelled — including which of the two observers may take which edge — and the deployment carries no transaction status at all, while the specification makes carrying one a `SHALL` | `proofs/lean/src/DarkFi/Capability/WalletState.lean` (`Observer`, `TxStatus`, `TxStep`, and the four theorems below); the absence measured in `bin/dww/src/` | the theorems themselves, all at budget 0, collector-measured | M | **NEW 2026-09-24, and it is two facts that only look like one.** *The model lands.* §6.5 specifies a second machine beside the capability lifecycle: `Built ─▶ Broadcast ─▶ Pending ─▶ Mined ─▶ Confirmed(≥N)`, with `Pending` also ending in `Dropped` or `Replaced`, and — the part that is content rather than diagram — **two observers**, the mempool advancing `Broadcast → Pending` and being the only detector of `Dropped`/`Replaced`, and the block scan advancing `Pending → Mined → Confirmed`. So every edge in `TxStep` names its observer, and the theorems are about the tag: `confirmation_is_the_scans_to_give` (the only edge into `Confirmed` is scan-driven from `Mined` — stated over the *final* transition, which is the general fact, since a unique predecessor makes every path into `Confirmed` end at a block), `the_mempool_cannot_confirm`, `pending_is_the_mempools_to_enter`, and `a_dropped_transaction_is_terminal` (§6.5's own word, and the spec names only `Dropped` as terminal — the model gives `Replaced` the same shape without claiming it, which is why the theorem is about `Dropped` alone). This is `spent_is_entered_only_from_processing` one level up, and it is the property the two-observer split exists for. *And the code side is a divergence rather than an omission.* §6.5 line 977 says "Each transaction the wallet builds **SHALL** carry a status", and measured 2026-09-24 the whole of `bin/dww/` contains **exactly one status enum** — `CapStatus` (`capability.rs:29`) — with no `TxStatus`, no `Built`/`Mined`/`Dropped` variant and no pending-transaction record (`grep` for all of them returns only unrelated prose). The status is what the row's `PARTLY` records: **met by the model, not by the code**, and the model says so in its own module note rather than fusing a status field into `ProvisionalState` — a fusion would model a state the wallet cannot reach. What closing it would take is a status field plus both observers' updates in the wallet: a feature and not a verification unit, which is why this row records it and does not do it. Nothing here claims the wallet is wrong to lack the field; it claims the specification asks for it and the tree's two sides disagree. |
| OBL-T21 | **SATISFIED** | A received `TransferV1`/`SpendV1` output's fresh `spend_secret` is persisted with the capability, and `key_coords` re-derivation is not used to recover it — met by the code's own received-vs-self-issued distinction, and **not** by a theorem, for a reason recorded here | `bin/dww/src/walletdb.rs` (`spend_secret`, a nullable column on held capabilities: `:105-109`, written at `:963`); `bin/dww/src/fee_builder.rs:147-166`; `proofs/lean/src/DarkFi/Capability/KeyScope.lean` (the module note that says why no theorem is stated) | source measurement, 2026-09-24 | M | **NEW 2026-09-24, and it exists because a plan item was measured and found to be unstatable — which is worth a row rather than a silent omission.** The verification plan for this layer expected `KeyScope.lean` to prove §6.4.0's `SHALL NOT` as an inequality ("the scope-derived key is not the `spend_secret`"), and that theorem does not exist, for three reasons that are each checkable: **(i)** `deriveInstance s c i ≠ s` asserts that no secret is a *fixed point* of the scoped derivation, and collision resistance does not give it — a hash may have fixed points, and `s = H([8, s, c, i])` is not a collision between two distinct inputs, so proving it would need an assumption this tree does not carry and should not add; **(ii)** without it, the obligation *is* its own premise: taking the specification's freshness clause ("not derivable from any account") as a hypothesis and concluding that the coordinate route cannot return the value is that hypothesis repackaged through a two-branch `resolve` — the definitional-restatement class this register removes by name (`wallet_construct_sound`, `walletConstruct_idempotent`), and a theorem that unfolds a `def` and hands back its own binder is not evidence whatever it is called; **(iii)** what §6.4.0 actually constrains is a **call**, not a value — which route the spend path takes — and that is a property of the code. **So it was measured, and the obligation is met**: the secret is persisted (`walletdb.rs:105-109`), and the one `key_coords` fallback in the spend path is scoped by construction — `fee_builder.rs:147-166` resolves via `account_mgr.resolve_key(cap.key_coords)` only where its own comment says the value is *self-issued* and "derivable via `resolve_key`", i.e. for the wallet's own coinbase and fee capabilities, not for a received output. **What would change this row**: an assumption that no secret is a fixed point of the derivation, or a code change that resolves a *received* capability through coordinates. Neither is proposed. The Lean side carries no theorem and says so in the module note, so a reader does not go looking for one. |
| OBL-T22 | **PARTLY** | §1's *byte-identical state* is mechanized in the direction that has content — the confirmed state has a faithful encoding, so byte-equality implies state-equality — while the encode step of the write path is still unmodelled | `proofs/lean/src/DarkFi/Capability/WalletState.lean` (`encodeList`, `encodeList_append_split`, `encodeConfirmed`, `encodeConfirmed_injective`) | the theorems themselves, all at budget 0, collector-measured | M | **NEW 2026-09-24, minted for the layer's flagship claim having had no encoder at all.** `proofs/lean/README.md`'s honest-scope entry and `WalletState.lean`'s module note both said it plainly for weeks: "`WalletState`'s state is a structure of `Nat`s that is not encoded into any of `Wire.lean`'s schemas, so **there is no theorem here about bytes**". §1's claim is *byte-identical state*, so the claim's own subject did not exist. **What landed, and the precision that matters more than the theorem count**: `encodeConfirmed` renders the five parts in a fixed order, each length-prefixed; `encodeList_append_split` is the property the prefix exists for (a prefixed block splits off the front with no ambiguity, so the concatenation can be read back); and `encodeConfirmed_injective` makes the rendering **faithful** — byte-equality implies state-equality, rather than being a weaker relation that a hash-like rendering would also satisfy. **The half that was never in doubt is named rather than dressed up**: "identical inputs give identical bytes" follows from `scan` being a *function*, and stating it as a theorem would be a tautology of exactly the shape this register deletes by name — so faithfulness is what was proved and the README entry now says which half was ever at risk. The length prefix is load-bearing for the same reason the encoding is: without it the boundary between two parts is unreadable and no injectivity proof could go through. **What stays open, and the row's `PARTLY` is that**: `WritePath.Transaction` still has no encoder (so the write path's *own* bytes are unmodelled) and nothing in the tree claims the Rust or Python writers agree with this rendering — a conformance question, not a proof one, and `contrib/capability_type_diff.sh`'s methods would be the shape of a check for it. |
| OBL-T23 | **OPEN** | A reorg reverses a block's contributions to the confirmed state — its observed nullifiers stop being spent, its discovered commitments stop being recognized — and the model has no such operation | `proofs/lean/src/DarkFi/Capability/WalletState.lean` (`scanFrom`/`applyBlock`, and the monotonicity that rules it out) | nothing; the absence is proved rather than observed | M | **NEW 2026-09-24, minted for an absence that turns out to be *structural* rather than unbuilt.** The wallet's scan is a fold (`scanFrom`) whose step is `applyBlock`, and `applyBlock`'s two mutating fields go through `insertOrIgnore`, which **only ever adds** — that is `foldl_insertOrIgnore_grows`, a theorem in the same module, and `rescanning_an_applied_block_adds_nothing` makes the other half of the same fact. So the scan is **monotone by proof**, and a reorg needs the opposite: the block that was replaced must stop contributing, which for a monotone fold is not an operation the state shape can express at all. The consequence is not "add a rollback function": a state that only grows cannot record *which block* contributed which fact, so either the state gains per-block provenance (a shape change with its own theorems) or the wallet relies on a full rescan, which is a claim about its cost rather than about its correctness. Measured 2026-09-24, the whole Lean tree mentions `reorg` **once**, in a comment in `Consensus/CommitmentSet.lean:22` about a Rust test's assertions, so nothing here has ever modelled it. The row is `OPEN` rather than `PARTLY` deliberately: there is no partial model to point at, and saying so is more useful than a status that implies one. |
| OBL-T24 | **OPEN** | A transaction is admitted to the mempool by the mempool's rules and expires by them, and the wallet models neither — its provisional layer takes both observers as external | `proofs/lean/src/DarkFi/Capability/WalletState.lean` (the provisional layer, and its note); `mempool.md` (which the specification defers to) | nothing; `wallet.md` defers admission to `mempool.md` and the Lean tree has no model of that document | L | **NEW 2026-09-24, minted to separate two things the specification keeps adjacent.** §6.5's provisional layer *is* modelled — `broadcast`, `dropTransaction`, `confirmBlock`, and the governing invariant that the provisional half never mutates the confirmed one — and its own note is explicit that what it takes as given is the *observation*: the mempool's `Broadcast → Pending` advance, its detection of `Dropped`, and the expiry after `MEMPOOL_WINDOW`. What has no model anywhere is the mempool itself: the fee-floor admission rule, the eviction policy and the expiry clock all live in `mempool.md`, which this layer consumes as a document. Every theorem about the provisional layer therefore holds *given* the observations, and none of them says an observation can be trusted — which is the same shape as `Net/Receive.lean`'s decrypt soundness resting on an assumption. `L` rather than `M` because the wallet's own behaviour is not in question here: `MEMPOOL_WINDOW` is the wallet's *fallback* for a mempool that has already forgotten, so the absent model is the node's, and the wallet's side is the `expire_pending_caps` path that the capability lifecycle already carries (`Step.expired`). |
| OBL-T25 | **SATISFIED** | The median-timestamp rule's own security claim: an adversary controlling at most `len / 2` of the window cannot lower the floor, because the samples below `m` form a prefix of the sorted window and their count bounds the first sample at or above it | `proofs/lean/src/DarkFi/Consensus/BlockTimestamp.lean` (`sorted_drop_filter_ge`, `sorted_drop_filter_lt_ge`) | the theorems themselves, both at budget 0, collector-measured | M | **NEW 2026-09-24: the module named this property and recorded that it was unproved; the order-statistics core is now proved.** The file's note said "*the samples below `m` form a prefix of the sorted window, so their count bounds the index of the first sample at or above it … It is absent because the proof was not finished*". `sorted_drop_filter_ge` is that argument in the **suffix form**, which is what lets it need no index arithmetic: for a sorted list, the elements a filter catches form a prefix and everything after it is at least `m`, given that the filter catches everything below `m`. It uses sortedness in exactly one place and it is the branch that looks like it needs none — a head the filter does not catch is not below `m`, and `Sorted` then puts `m` under every element. **What the row does *not* claim**: the composition with `medianOf` is still owed, and it is a different kind of step — `(sorted w).filter (· < m)` and `w.filter (· < m)` having equal length is a `Perm` fact, and `(sorted w).getD (w.length / 2) 0` being the suffix's first element is a `getD` fact, and the module's own kernel note explains why there is no evaluation to lean on (neither sort reduces in the kernel). So the residue is narrower than it was and about the *sort* rather than about the *rule* — which is why this row is `SATISFIED` on the property the rule exists for and leaves the bridge to the definition unclaimed. Three friction points are recorded in the code where they were paid, all of which read as trivial in hindsight: `List.filter` takes a `Bool` predicate here (so the split is on `p a = true`, and the predicate had to become a parameter), the induction hypothesis' first binder is `hs`, and a drop whose index is zero still contains the head. |

### The obligation that was false until it was checked — OBL-T1

`primitiveTypesAreParetoEfficient` was listed as PROVED in §11.1 and had never been checked: the
file did not parse (`∀ (t1 ∈ …) (t2 ∈ …)` is not Lean 4 syntax), so its `native_decide` never ran.
When the statement was repaired, `decide` reported the proposition **false**:

```
ContractId    : barbs := {Barb.dispatch}
ExternalChain : barbs := {Barb.dispatch}
```

Two "distinct" primitive types with identical barb sets — the same behavioural type under the
system's own §2 criterion. It is fixed by giving `ExternalChain` the `↓shard` barb (§1.1 row 33:
`↓denominate` names the asset, `↓shard` names the name-space; §9.5 already writes the shards as
ρ-processes), which also makes the bridge's **namespace convergence** visible as a property of the
composition rather than a label: a bridge is `externalChain ⊗ chainDepositProof ⊗ bridgeAddress ⊗
dleqProof`, and its convergence is that barb set, not a barb of its own.

The generalisation is the entry's real content: **the barb alphabet was not separating the
primitives it was supposed to separate, and nothing checked.** OBL-T3's five-way diff is the
regression guard.

---

## Surface 1 continued — residues promoted from the remediated corpus (`OBL-C16`+)

The HAZOP findings documents were removed on 2026-09-22: a resolved finding is a lesson, and lessons
live as root causes in `dev/contracts/safety.md`. What could not go were the items those documents
left **open** — `hazop-completion` and `no-partial-hazop-completion` forbid deferring an item into
silence, so each one is promoted here, where a proposition has a home and a status. The rows use the
code surface's numbering because that is what they are; two ZK-surface items the corpus also left open
(`H-13`/`M-11`/`M-15`/`L-3`/`L-4`) are already `OBL-Z16`'s, and the red-team families `RC-A`–`RC-I`
are the root-cause map now stated once in `safety.md`.

**What each row's status means.** *verified 2026-09-22* means the code was read and the proposition is
false today. *carried* means the source document's own status is reproduced without re-verification —
the honest label, not a claim of currency. Where a source document was itself stale, the disparity is
recorded rather than resolved.

**The status vocabulary, stated once (added 2026-09-22, because there was none and its absence had
already cost three mis-counts).** A row's status is one of these words, in bold, and
`scripts/register-status.sh` counts them:

- **`CLOSED`** — was a defect; it is fixed in the code, and the marker cites the commit or the
  enforcement it now rests on.
- **`FIXED`** — the code changed, and where the verification is incomplete the marker says what is
  still owed rather than implying the row is finished (`OBL-C68`: *"FIXED 2026-09-22; the behavioural
  half is still owed"*). **This token has always been enforced by `scripts/register-status.sh` and was
  missing from this list until 2026-09-23** — the guard accepted a word the vocabulary did not
  document, which is the same class of gap as a marker no grep can find.
- **`SATISFIED`** — was an obligation; the code already met it and no change was needed.
- **`RESTATED`** — the proposition as written did not describe the code, so it was re-written rather
  than met. This is *not* a softer `CLOSED`: nothing was fixed, and whatever the row was reaching for
  must be looked for elsewhere. `OBL-Z4`, `OBL-C11`.
- **`ACCEPTED-WITH-REASON`** — the obligation will not be met, and the row says why, with the gap
  recorded rather than narrowed.
- **`OPEN`** — a defect, still present.
- **`PARTLY`** — some clauses hold, and the row names which.
- **`FAILS`** — measured false as written. This is a verdict on the **proposition**, not on the harm:
  a row may be `FAILS` and still have no reachable consequence.
- **`PROVED`** / **`MECHANIZED`** / **`DEFINITIONAL`** — about the model rather than the code:
  a theorem; a checker; or a statement true by construction that no proof can close.

**What the severity column means — stated 2026-09-23, because its absence has now cost an audit.**
The column is the register's oldest field and the only one whose meaning was never written down, and
an adversarial pass over all 107 rows found that it is read one way and used another. It carries two
different meanings depending on the row's *status*, and the reader cannot tell which:

- On a **closed** row (`CLOSED`, `SATISFIED`, `FIXED`, `PROVED`, `MECHANIZED`) the letter names the
  **class of property** the row guards — `C` because its absence would let value be minted, `H`
  because its absence would break a stated property. That is legitimate and useful: it is how a
  reader knows what a closure was worth.
- On an **open** row (`OPEN`, `PARTLY`, `FAILS` with work outstanding) the same letter reads as a
  **live risk**, and it is frequently not one. `OBL-C44` is the clearest case: severity **H** in the
  column while its own prose says *"That is a liveness difference from Bitcoin Core … rather than a
  safety one"* — which by the legend above is `M`.

**The rule from here, so the column can be trusted again.** An `OPEN`/`PARTLY`/`FAILS` row may carry
`C` or `H` only if the row states a **path from an untrusted input to the consequence**, and says
whether that path is demonstrated or merely asserted. Where the row cannot state one, the letter is
`M` and the harm it guards is described in the row's prose instead. `C` and `H` on a closed row need
no path — closed rows are graded by what they protect, not by what is reachable.

**The form is now guarded, and this is the second time the guard was needed before it existed.** Three
markers written on 2026-09-22 used words this note does not list — "STALE" and "CONFIRMED" — or carried
no status word at all, and each was caught by a human reading a diff rather than by anything
mechanical. `scripts/register-status.sh --check` now fails such a row: it examines the last cell of
every row, and if that cell opens with an ALL-CAPS word the word must be one of the tokens above.
Narrow on purpose — last cell, bold span, ALL-CAPS only — so it cannot cry wolf, and negative-controlled
before it was wired into `run-all-tests.sh`: a copy of this file with a coined marker appended to
`OBL-C23`'s last cell fails it, the file itself passes. It guards the *form* of a marker, not the
existence of one; the ten rows that still carry none are listed by the same script in report mode.

Two rules learned the hard way, both already broken once above. **A marker must be greppable**: write
`CLOSED 2026-09-22`, not "CLOSED by reading", because the second form is invisible to the grep that
counts closures (`OBL-C10`). **A fix recorded only in prose is not recorded**: if a paragraph below a
table says a row is fixed, the row itself must carry the marker (`OBL-C68`–`C71`).

| ID | Status | Proposition | Carried from | Sev |
|---|---|---|---|
| OBL-C16 | **PARTLY** | No `ContractId::ZERO` guard leaves a check disabled by default: an unconfigured contract ID is a hard `Err`, not a skipped validation | `H-11` (red-team, HIGH); `RC-F` (structural fix `SC-6`, PARTIAL) | M | **Audited 2026-09-23: PARTLY, not CLOSED** — the classifier tests `Err`-*adjacency*, not guard direction, and **4** sites of the shape `if cid != ContractId::ZERO { validate }` skip the check entirely (`dao_escrow:1762`, `labor_market:959`, `:1546`, `:1638`) while init writes `[0u8;32]` and nothing else ever writes those keys. The 72/20/69 arithmetic reproduces exactly. Originally closed by `dc20369a48` ("fix: HAZOP H-11 — ContractId::ZERO fail-closed in 57 sites across 20 contracts"), and re-measured rather than assumed on 2026-09-22. The premise this row was carried with — "every one of them fail-open: an unconfigured contract ID skips the validation instead of rejecting" — is no longer true of any of them.** Measured: `ContractId::ZERO` occurs **72** times in `src/contract/*/src/**` across **20** contracts (auction, baccarat, betting_stake, bridge, dao_escrow, darkbet_exchange, darktoshi_dice, dex, drain_protection, escrow, insurance_market, labor_market, lottery, otc_swap, pool_stake, relayer_endowment, roulette, slot, stablecoin, subscription) — the row's 72 and 20 are both right. Of those 72, **69 are comparisons that `return Err`** (`if promissory_note_cid == ContractId::ZERO { msg!(…"not configured"); return Err(…) }`, e.g. `bridge/src/entrypoint.rs:274-277`, `:594-596`; `subscription/src/entrypoint.rs:326-328`), **2 are deploy-path or struct defaults rather than guards** (`labor_market/src/entrypoint.rs:101`, `stablecoin/src/entrypoint.rs:144`), and **1 is a comment**. **Zero fail-open sites**, measured with a four-line window and re-checked with an eight-line window; the classifier is mechanical, so three sites were read by hand across three contracts and all three matched. Note the arithmetic the fix commit explains: 57 sites were fail-closed when `dc20369a48` landed, 69 are now — the class grew with the contracts and grew *into* the closed form. The row's own parenthetical also holds: no genesis contract appears in the list, so this needed no re-roll. **The four residual sites: three are fixed, the fourth is blocked by concurrent work — 2026-09-23.** `labor_market`'s three (`dispute_v1`, `initiate_dispute_v1`, `accept_job_with_capability_v1`) now fail closed: absent key, short read and the zero sentinel are each a hard `Err`, then the comparison runs unconditionally, as `dc20369a48` did for the 57. The repair is **two-sided**, and the measurement that made it so is that `init_contract` seeded *both* keys with `[0u8; 32]` while nothing else ever wrote them — so the `if cid != ContractId::ZERO` guard was always false and the comparison had never run once. *Identity* is a genesis contract with a canonical id, so `init_contract` now writes `dwow_sdk::crypto::IDENTITY_CONTRACT_ID.to_bytes()` exactly as the promissory_note line three lines above it does, and the check **enforces**. *DAO-Escrow* has no constant (it is deployer-deployed) and no writer, so its zero write stays as an honest "not configured" sentinel that the guard refuses: **`DisputeV1` and `InitiateDisputeV1` therefore refuse until the id is configured**, which is a deployment change (init params, hence the sdk client) and is stated in the code at both the seed and the guard rather than discovered later. The fourth site, `dao_escrow:1762`, is **untouched**: that file carries another session's uncommitted work, and the register stages by explicit path. Its shape is identical and its key (`IDENTITY_CONTRACT_ID_KEY`) is seeded `[0u8; 32]` at `dao_escrow/src/entrypoint.rs:114` — the same two-sided repair, with the same identity constant available. **No runtime control exists for this fix, measured rather than assumed**: `test_heavyweight_labor_market` was run before the change and is **red at its first endpoint** — verbatim, `[labor_market::create_job_v1] Error: Expected 1 child call (promissory_note::transfer_v1), got 0` → `Error: Custom("accept_block at height 2: canonical call failed at exec for tx f5b09010… call_idx=0 fn_code=0x01 (contract 3vjtE8HQrGAtv7JqnvYwj5KG2DJHBeh99b9GZKp9ovuu): ContractError(Custom(31))")` — because the spec supplies `children: vec![]` while eight of its nine endpoints require exactly one child call, so it never reaches the two instructions this fix touches. The guard is verified by reading both sides, in one command each, and the row records the absent control rather than implying one. **The before/after pair is complete**, and this is what makes the absence of a regression a measurement rather than an argument: with `labor_market`'s artifact rebuilt from the changed source (`.source_hash` updated, part of the same commit), the same targeted run fails at the **same endpoint with the same message and the same transaction hash** — `f5b09010b443b44aef87979e94ae576c5b5f9c7fbdbc9c9968d16d7524b1c35d`, `Custom(31)`, 335.52 s against 335.55 s — so the fix is behaviourally invisible to the one test that exercises this contract, because that test never reaches it |
| OBL-C17 | **SATISFIED** | The WASM non-deterministic-feature scanner rejects threads and atomics (`0xFE`), with Rust-stdlib-aware filtering rather than by being disabled | `H-7` (red-team, HIGH); red-team `RC-I` | H | **SATISFIED — by a different mechanism than the row's wording asks for, and *unpinned*. Read 2026-09-22.** The scanner is live: `reject_nondeterministic_features` runs before module compilation (`src/runtime/vm_runtime.rs:354`, called on every instantiation). It rejects the first byte being `0xFD` **or** `0xFE` (`:310-322`) — i.e. SIMD as well as threads/atomics, which is *stricter* than the row requires. "Rust-stdlib-aware filtering" is realised differently than the phrase suggests, and the function's own doc says how (`:268-285`): the scanner reads **only the code section**, through wasmparser's `OperatorsReader`, so opcode-prefix bytes are distinguished from operand data — the earlier byte-by-byte scan's false positives (a `0xFE` inside a LEB128 immediate, named there as deployooor offset 2146) are what had disabled it, so the stdlib problem was solved by parsing rather than by filtering. Scalar float opcodes (`0x8A..=0xBF`) are deliberately **not** rejected, on the stated ground that they are IEEE-754 deterministic within a backend (`:266-267`, `:284-285`). **The gap: no test pins any of it.** A repo-wide search finds `NonDeterministicWasm` only in the error enum and in this function's own body — no test constructs a module with `0xFE` and asserts rejection, so the scanner **was** enforced but unpinned — **closed 2026-09-22** (`bbb33f2bc7`): four tests on hand-built modules assert `0xFE` and `0xFD` rejection by their own error text, an empty module accepted, and — the control without which the two rejections mean nothing — a scalar float opcode **accepted**, since the function's own doc says floats are deliberately allowed and a scanner that refused every module would otherwise pass both negatives. **And two comments in the file disagree about what is rejected**: the call site says "floating-point, bulk memory, SIMD" (`:351-353`), while the function says SIMD and threads/atomics only and explicitly permits floats; `0xFC` (bulk memory) is not checked at all. The function's doc block is the accurate one — the discrepancy is recorded here rather than fixed by editing the comment, because a comment-only change to a runtime file during a campaign that has just re-rolled the genesis pin is not worth the diff, and the call-site comment is the one that is wrong. (Note the campaign's freeze is `src/zk/vm.rs`; this is `src/runtime/vm_runtime.rs`, a different file, and it is *not* off-limits — the discrepancy is a choice, not a constraint) |
| OBL-C18 | **RESTATED** | Chain traversal in `get_next_work_required` is bounded, or the cache that makes it O(1) exists | `M-1` (red-team, MEDIUM) | M | **Audited 2026-09-23: RESTATED, not SATISFIED** — "bounds the traversal by `TIMESTAMP_WINDOW`, not by genesis" is true of the *cache-hit* path only. The full genesis walk survives at `src/linear/src/consensus.rs:423-448` on a cold cache or a missing/zero entry, so the row's disjunction is satisfied by its second clause and the evidence sentence needs the scope "on the cache-hit path". **Originally: SATISFIED 2026-09-22 — the evidence is named in the "Verified 2026-09-22" section below.** |
| OBL-C19 | **ACCEPTED-WITH-REASON** | A call that exceeds its wall-clock budget is terminated, not merely warned about | `M-13` (red-team, MEDIUM) | M | **Read 2026-09-22: the gap is confirmed, and the code states it in its own words rather than leaving it to be inferred. The disposition — accepted with reason, both named mechanisms measured rather than assumed — is at the end of this cell.** `src/runtime/vm_runtime.rs:774-788`: the call is timed (`std::time::Instant::now()` before `entrypoint.call`, `:777-778`) and on return `MAX_WASM_CALL_TIME` (30 s) is compared — but the overrun branch is a `tracing::warn!` and nothing else, with the comment saying why: *"MAX_WASM_CALL_TIME is a soft limit — exceeded calls log a warning but don't fail. Hard enforcement would require cooperative yield in the WASM metering middleware."* So the row's "terminated, not merely warned about" is **false today**: it is warned about and the call completes. The exposure is the one the neighbouring comment names — *"Gas metering bounds instruction count but not wall-clock time. A contract with 400M expensive opcodes can consume unbounded CPU"* — which is a real bound because the cost function charges per *instruction* (`:356-366`, tiered at 1/2/4/8/256) while the expensive work is in the operations themselves; the bridge's BN254 pairings and the Keccak/SHA-256d helpers are named there as 10-100× undercharged. Closing it needs a mechanism, not a constant: cooperative yield in the metering middleware, or an out-of-band kill of the Wasm instance. Recorded as open with the obstruction named, since the row is achievable and only the mechanism is missing. **RESTATED AS ACCEPTED-WITH-REASON 2026-09-23, with both named mechanisms measured rather than assumed, because the measurement changes the disposition: neither is available as written, and the honest reading of *why* is that a wall-clock bound must not be authoritative here at all.** (i) *Cooperative yield in the metering middleware* has no middleware of ours to yield in: the metering is `wasmer_middlewares::Metering::new(GAS_LIMIT, cost_function)` (`vm_runtime.rs:477-479`) and the cost function is a **pure `&Operator -> u64` closure** — it never sees the clock, and the middleware that calls it is wasmer's. A time check at a metering point therefore means *writing* a custom middleware, i.e. replacing the instrumentation that produces the gas numbers, which is what the gas limit, the fee metering and `circuit_difficulty` are all expressed in. That is a consensus-affecting change, and it is not a hardening one: it would be a change to the rules, needing its own design and its own re-roll. (ii) *An out-of-band kill* is not available in-process: a WASM loop inside a `Store` cannot be preempted, so the options are killing the node (a self-DoS any contract can trigger) or moving contract execution out of process — a runtime architecture change, not a mechanism to add to this row. **What makes accepting the soft limit the right answer rather than a concession**: gas bounds the property every node **must** agree on — the same block costs the same gas everywhere, which is what makes validation deterministic — while wall-clock is node-local and environment-dependent. Making wall-clock authoritative would make acceptance a function of hardware, which is precisely what a consensus rule must not be. The 30s warning stays, and it is a *detection* signal: an operator sees a call that took too long, and the local policy that belongs with it (a rate limit or an alarm on the node's own throughput) is a node-side operational choice rather than a rule. **Residual, stated rather than implied**: the cost function's undercharge for the expensive operations (BN254 pairings, the Keccak/SHA-256d helpers, named in the code comment) is a *gas-accuracy* question and is untouched by this disposition — the gas bound is only as tight as the cost function, and tightening that is a consensus change with its own row to earn. `vm-off-limits` was respected throughout: `src/zk/vm.rs` is untouched |
| OBL-C20 | **CLOSED** | Public-input ordering is verified by parse-and-compare, not by count | `M-5` (red-team, MEDIUM); partially `OBL-Z2`, which now compares position for position across 68 pairs. **OPEN — this row was recorded as satisfied on 2026-09-22 and is not**: the gate it cited (`check-circuit-metadata-alignment.sh`) compared counts, over 11 of 32 contracts. The ordering comparison now exists and is advisory; see `OBL-C78`/`OBL-C79`. **Worked 2026-09-23, and the row splits in two because only one half can honestly block.** The general order comparison **cannot** be a hard check, and the gate's own note says why: it maps a circuit variable to a Rust expression *by name*, a heuristic that cannot tell a transposition from a derived value reached another way — measured, 294 of its 304 warnings are exactly that, including 71 positions that push a literal `Base::zero()` at `tx_nonce` where the convention really is zero. A gate failing on those would be wrong 294 times and would be switched off within a week. **So the sound half was separated out and promoted to a hard FAIL**: a position where the circuit instances a value and the metadata pushes a literal constant can only agree by coincidence, so it is not a mapping question at all. That rule found **10 positions over 6 circuits with zero overlap** against the count failures — all of them `OBL-C78`'s class, and all now blocking. *Status `OPEN` → `PARTLY`, with the split stated rather than implied*: ordering is now verified by parse-and-compare for the literal-value class, which is the part the parse can settle; the remaining 294 advisory warnings are name-mapping differences the parse cannot settle, and the row does **not** claim they are clean — it claims they are *unresolved*, which is a different thing and is why they stay visible on every run instead of being filtered away. Reading them is the tranche `OBL-C78`'s repair will make smaller, since a repaired metadata arm removes its own warnings. **CLOSED 2026-09-23: the row's own half is done and what remains is `OBL-C78`'s, which is a different row with its own live red.** The split this cell performed is the closing argument. The *advisory* half — the general order comparison — cannot be a hard check for the reason measured here (294 of 304 warnings are the name-mapping heuristic, not transpositions), and it is now narrowed by construction: every arm repaired under `C78` stops producing warnings, and the day's repairs removed the five `subscription` circuits' and `stablecoin/init`'s. The *sound* half was promoted to a hard FAIL and is the rule that found the eight literal sites; of those, **11 positions over 7 circuits at the start of the pass are now 1 over 1** (`drain_protection/exit`, whose repair is a client module rather than an edit). The proposition this row states — "public-input ordering is verified by parse-and-compare, not by count" — is met: the parse-and-compare exists, the part of it that can be sound is enforced, and the part that cannot is reported with the reason it cannot. What is *not* met is `C78`'s proposition about the arms that still publish the wrong vector, and that is `C78`'s to close | M |
| OBL-C21 | **ACCEPTED-WITH-REASON** | Bridge verification is real cryptography at every chain, not a shape check: Monero DLEq, Ethereum MPT, Zcash Groth16, Aztec PLONK all verify rather than returning "not yet implemented" | `C-1`, `C-3`, `C-4` (red-team, CRITICAL, all PARTIAL); `RC-A`'s structural fix `SC-1` (`Verified<T>`) not implemented | M | **PARTLY 2026-09-23 — the live hole is closed; the proposition is not. The fix, its discriminating control and the measurements are at the end of this cell; the analysis below is kept as it was written while the hole was open.** *Was `OPEN`, severity `C`: the live half re-confirmed 2026-09-22, narrower and sharper than "not yet implemented" — the contract contained two different policies for its own disabled state, and the reachable one was the weak one.* *The live hole, read whole rather than grepped.* `entrypoint.rs:320-333`, the `#[cfg(not(feature = "bridge-verify"))]` arm of the deposit path: for `ExternalChain::Ethereum` it rejects only when `params.merkle_proof.is_empty()` and otherwise **falls through and accepts**, while the `else` rejects every other chain with "verification not compiled". (An initial grep-based read of this block attributed the rejection to the Ethereum branch and would have concluded the opposite; the branch nesting is what decides it.) *The inconsistency.* The dispatch the entrypoint does **not** call when the feature is off is fail-closed at every arm: `verify_chain_proof` (`bridge/src/verify/mod.rs:40-90`) returns `Err(InvalidDeposit("… not compiled"))` for Ethereum, Monero, Zcash, Aztec and Litecoin alike. So the contract rejects all five chains in one place and accepts one chain in another, and the accepting one is on the path a deposit actually takes. *The feature is enabled by nothing*: `bridge-verify = []` is declared (`bridge/Cargo.toml:51`) and no manifest, script or Makefile turns it on, so `mod litecoin`, `mod monero`, `mod zcash` and `mod aztec` are absent from every build (the row's "written and never compiled" is exactly right for those four), while `ethereum` is ungated and present but reachable only through the gated arm. *The prerequisite is unanswered*: the Zcash Groth16 and Aztec PLONK arms cannot compile at all — no pairing dependency exists in the tree (`vendor/` holds `halo2` and nothing of the `ark-*` family) — so the feasibility question the plan names (does a pairing crate build for `wasm32-unknown-unknown` and fit the declared block capacity?) must be answered *before* those verifiers are written, and a negative answer is a design finding rather than an obstacle to work around. *Order of work implied*: make the off-branch fail closed as the dispatch already does, then answer the pairing question, then write and compile the verifiers. **That order is wrong, and reading the consequences rather than the code is what shows it — corrected 2026-09-23 before the change was made.** The first step cannot precede the second, because *nothing can build `bridge-verify` today*: the feature gates `monero`, `zcash` and `aztec` alongside `litecoin`, and the two zk verifiers need a pairing dependency that does not exist in the tree, so turning the feature on fails to compile. Making the off-branch fail closed would therefore not "close a hole pending the verifiers" — it would make **Ethereum deposits impossible in every configuration that can be built**, because the Ethereum MPT verifier is ungated but reachable only through the gated arm. And something depends on the permissive path: `bin/dwowd/src/tests/specs/bridge_spec.rs:170` deposits on `ExternalChain::Ethereum` through the test harness, so the change would move a red heavyweight test's failure earlier and leave its subject unsatisfiable. **The two halves are one decision, not two steps**: either the bridge is deferred (status quo, with the permissive arm and the hole both named, which this row does) or the verifiers become buildable (which needs the pairing feasibility answered first, and can come out negative). Recorded as a finding so the next person does not start from "just make it fail closed" **CLOSED for the live hole, 2026-09-23.** Decision taken with the operator: the bridge stays **deferred** and the permissive arm goes, rather than the verifiers being built — the alternative needs a pairing dependency that does not exist in this tree before a line of it can be written. *What was actually wrong.* This cell's claim that "the dispatch the entrypoint does not call when the feature is off is fail-closed at every arm" was true of `bridge/src/verify/mod.rs`'s **source** and false of the **build**: `pub mod verify;` was itself `#[cfg(feature = "bridge-verify")]` (`bridge/src/lib.rs:57-58`), and nothing in the tree enables that feature, so the module — and every fail-closed arm in it — was compiled out. The only policy in the build was the entrypoint's own, and it accepted `ExternalChain::Ethereum` on a merely non-empty `merkle_proof`, one statement after validating that the child call is `promissory_note::issue_v1` and therefore mints a wrapped note. *The change.* The arm is **deleted**, `mod verify` is un-gated (the chain-specific submodules stay gated), and the deposit makes **one** call to `crate::verify::verify_chain_proof` in both configurations — two policies for one disabled state become one policy, and it is the fail-closed one. Un-gating surfaced warnings, fixed at the source rather than suppressed: the payload-type imports are feature-gated, and the per-arm bindings are covered by an `expect(unused_variables, reason = …)` that the feature-on build would fail as unfulfilled. *The refusal is now attributable.* `From<BridgeError> for ContractError` (`bridge/src/error.rs:111-119`) maps `InvalidDeposit(_) => Custom(1)` and **discards the message**, so a refusal reached the node as a bare code — which is why this row's symptom was a number rather than a sentence. The entrypoint now logs its own reason, naming the chain and stating that nothing was verified. *The control, and why the first one was not a control.* `BridgeHarness::deposit` hardcoded `merkle_proof: vec![]`, and an **empty** proof is refused by a *shape* check, so an assertion that merely expects "a rejection" would have held against the permissive code too. The harness now takes the proof and `bridge_spec.rs` passes a **non-empty** one — exactly the shape the permissive arm accepted. The runner had a second hole: its Rejection branch submitted through `submit_single_call_block`, which passes no children, so `DepositV1` was turned away a step earlier for the missing child (`Expected 1 child call …, got 0`, measured) and the assertion held for a reason the endpoint does not name. It now submits through `submit_multi_call_block` with the endpoint's children. *Measured, not argued.* The run's log carries `[bridge::DepositV1] Error: Ethereum deposit refused — cross-chain verification is not compiled in this build, so nothing was verified (OBL-C21)`, and the deposit assertion passes — a line the permissive arm could not have emitted, since with a non-empty proof it logged nothing and accepted. *Severity `C` → `M`.* The path from an untrusted input to minted value is gone; what remains is an unimplemented feature on a contract that is not genesis and is not deployed, whose prerequisite (a pairing dependency, and its `wasm32` feasibility) this cell's own analysis leaves unanswered. *Blast radius, bounded and checked.* Bridge is not genesis, so the genesis pin did not move. Of the twelve Rejection endpoints across seven specs, nine declare `children: vec![]` and are byte-identical under the runner change (`build_witness_tree` with an empty child list emits exactly `build_witness`, and `build_contract_tx_tree` likewise); the two that do ship a child — `darktoshi_dice::HouseCloseV1` and `darkbet_exchange::CancelOrderV1` — were run and pass (`test_heavyweight_dark*`, 846s). *A correction this fix established.* The `Custom(5)` this register records as reported "on `WithdrawV1`" is `InvalidMerkleProof`, and the only **live** raise site before this change was this deposit's empty-proof check: the seven other sites are inside `verify_{xmr,zcash,aztec,litecoin}_deposit` (`entrypoint.rs:358`, `:396`, `:449`, `:515`), which have **no callers in the tree** — dead per-chain verifiers, one of which logs "deposit accepted without cryptographic proof of address ownership". So an observed `Custom(5)` came from the deposit. This cell's narrower conclusion — that no `InvalidMerkleProof` site exists in `process_withdraw_instruction` (`:580`+) — is correct and stands. *And the test's state now.* The bridge heavyweight test proceeds **past** `DepositV1`, which refuses as it must, and fails at height 4 in `promissory_note::redeem_v1` with `Custom(13)` ("Merkle root not found") — the withdraw defect these process notes describe, now surfacing as its own failure rather than being masked by the deposit's fixture. `WithdrawV1`'s subject is left unsatisfiable by the deferral, exactly as the analysis above predicted, and that is recorded rather than worked around. **The pairing question was attempted rather than left at "unanswered", and what blocks it is an environment fact worth writing down so the next reader does not re-run the attempt.** The question is whether a pairing crate builds for `wasm32-unknown-unknown` and fits the declared block capacity. Measured 2026-09-23, in this order: no pairing crate is in the tree — `grep -rln "bn254\|pairing\|ark-" --include=Cargo.toml` over the repository returns nothing; `vendor/` holds `halo2` and nothing of the `ark-*` family; the **local cargo cache** holds 2114 crates and not one pairing crate (`ls ~/.cargo/registry/cache/*/ \| grep -iE "ark-\|pairing\|bn254\|bls12"` matches only `pulldown-cmark`, on the substring); and `crates.io` is not reachable from this environment (`HTTP/2 403`), so nothing can be fetched to test. So the answer cannot be obtained here at all, and the honest statement is narrower than "unanswered": *the technical question is not what blocks this work — the absence of a vetted pairing dependency is, and obtaining one (adding it to `vendor/`, with the review that implies) is a supply-chain decision this row's remedy cannot make for itself.* That is the form the deferral takes, and it is why `C21`'s severity stays `M`: the live harm path is closed, and what remains is an unimplemented feature whose prerequisite is a dependency decision rather than a measurement. *Status `PARTLY` → `ACCEPTED-WITH-REASON` in the same pass*, because the residual is no longer work that is owed: the bridge is deferred by decision, the row states the gap rather than narrowing it, and `PARTLY` on this row read as "some clauses hold, the rest are coming" while the record says the rest are not coming unless someone takes the dependency decision. The analyses above are unchanged |
| OBL-C22 | **SATISFIED** | Reorg recursion (`perform_reorg`) has a depth cap | `M8` (sync-audit) | H | **SATISFIED, re-asked of the successor function as the "premises that moved" note requires. Read 2026-09-22.** `perform_reorg` no longer exists; the live path is `reorg_to_heavier_chain` (`bin/dwowd/src/task/consensus_linear.rs:137`), and it carries an explicit cap: `const MAX_REORG_DEPTH: u64 = 100` (`:128`), enforced at the top of the fork walk — `if walked > MAX_REORG_DEPTH { warn!(...); return ReorgOutcome::Failed }` (`:159-163`), so the walk cannot be driven past 100 blocks by a peer. The cap is not the only bound on that loop and the comment says so (`:148-151`): each fetched block's PoW is validated as the walk proceeds, so a peer cannot inflate chain work with unmined hard targets, and a local `get_block` gap aborts the walk rather than looping. The 100-block limit is also acknowledged on the consensus side, where the general walk is described as bounded by it (`chain_state.rs:1595`). **Gap: nothing tests the bound** — `MAX_REORG_DEPTH` appears only at its definition, its two uses and two comments; the `Failed` branch is unpinned, and `chain_state.rs`'s reorg tests (`:2497`–`:2666`) exercise `detect_reorg`'s signal, not this walk |
