| OBL-C63 | **CLOSED** | Finality is conferred by a *verified* anchor, not by a header field the relaying peer chooses. **CLOSED 2026-09-22 (`5c6bf01a7c`).** Both enforcement sites (`chain_state.rs:1011`, `:1546`) now require `caribina::verify_anchor_proof(&header)` — a pure local function, so consensus can call it — and share one predicate, so a block `connect_block` treats as un-replaceable cannot be one `detect_reorg` is willing to reorg away. The mirror direction the row insisted on holds: a block asserting an anchor it cannot prove confers **no** finality, and is deliberately not rejected either, because rejection would let any peer halt the chain with a malformed relay whereas ignoring the claim costs only that block's finality. That asymmetry is what removes the free chain-freeze. Proven by the positive control and the forged-key control in `chain_state::tests::test_finality_conferred_without_any_anchor_verification`, and the eight negatives in `caribina::verify::tests` | `chain_state.rs:1011`, `:1546`; `caribina/verify.rs` | C |
| OBL-C64 | **CLOSED** | The finality fields are authenticated by the proof-of-work that produced the block. **CLOSED for the field that decides finality, 2026-09-22 (`5c6bf01a7c`).** `anchor_owner` — a fresh per-block Ed25519 public key — is now **inside** the mining blob (bytes 260..292; blob 260 → 292), so a relaying peer cannot re-attribute an anchor to a block it did not mine without redoing the proof-of-work, and one PoW solution no longer admits headers differing in the authenticating field. The four older fields (`anchor_tx_id`, `anchor_monero_*`, `finality_flags`) remain outside the blob, because they are set after the nonce and must not invalidate the solution they annotate — and they are no longer consulted for finality at all, so their malleability buys an attacker nothing. Asserted by `block::tests::test_anchor_owner_is_pow_covered_but_post_mining_fields_are_not`, which is the flipped form of the characterization test this row was filed against | `block.rs` (`to_mining_blob`, `ANCHOR_OWNER_OFFSET`) | C |
| OBL-C65 | **CLOSED** | Enforcement never outruns verification. **CLOSED 2026-09-22 (`5c6bf01a7c`), by construction rather than by a corrected predicate.** `should_verify_anchor` and `should_verify_monero_anchor` had no production caller and returned `false` whenever their `*_enabled` flag was off while `should_enforce` ignored those flags, so a configuration existed that enforced anchors it had decided not to check — and `--finality-disable-caribina`, which `bin/dwowd/src/main.rs` sets, was exactly that configuration. Both methods are **removed** rather than fixed: a dead method encoding a wrong invariant is worse than no method, and "a verifier with no caller" is this residue's root cause. Enforcement and verification are now the same call at both sites, so they cannot disagree. `finality::tests::test_enforce_decision_depends_only_on_mode_and_flags` replaces the removed tests and asserts no `*_enabled` flag leaks into the enforcement decision | `finality.rs` | H |
| OBL-C66 | **ACCEPTED-WITH-REASON** | Caribina anchors a block to a *settled* Arweave block. **ACCEPTED-WITH-REASON 2026-09-22, premise restated.** Settlement is not checked, and **cannot be**: it lives on another chain and consensus must be a pure function of local data — the same reason `verify_monero_anchor` is uncallable from consensus. What the anchor establishes instead is **publication**: the miner committed a key inside the block's mined region, signed a DataItem binding that block, and published it — and the property the 51% argument needs is that the adversary in scope cannot delete a DataItem, not that Arweave has buried it. Settlement depth is Arweave's business and this chain does not read it. The gap between "settled" and "published" is therefore **recorded, not narrowed**, and `doc/src/arch/caribina.md` no longer advertises a settlement time. The `ardrive.net`/`arweave.net` discrepancy in the gateway constant was resolved in the same pass, and the gateway path (`verify_anchor`) remains the opt-in live-conformance arm rather than a consensus input | `caribina/verify.rs`; `caribina.md` | M |
| OBL-C67 | **CLOSED** | The Monero anchor is derived from the Monero proof, not asserted beside it — **and this row was wrong about what is derivable, and understated its own severity. Both corrected 2026-09-22 by reading the types. OPEN.** *What is impossible*: the Monero `BlockHeader` carries only `major_version`, `minor_version`, `timestamp`, `prev_id` and `nonce` — **no height and no difficulty**. So `anchor_monero_height` cannot be derived from the proof (in the testnet fixture the height, 2912484, exists only as a comment), and a locally-checked PoW is impossible too: Monero's difficulty is not in the block, and a submitter-chosen target is vacuous. Recording that is the honest outcome; the row is not narrowed to whatever happens to be achievable. *What is derivable*: `anchor_monero_hash`, via `Keccak-256(VarInt(len) ‖ create_blockhashing_blob(header, merkle_root, tx_count))` — currently assembled inline at `bin/explorer/src/rpc.rs:215-230` with no reusable helper. *The severity correction*: the three receipts prove only that our aux hash sits in a coinbase of **some serialized** Monero block — `is_coinbase_valid_merkle_root` recomputes the coinbase hash from submitter-supplied fields and compares it against a root the submitter also supplied — so a fabricated five-field header with any transaction list and the merge-mining tag in the coinbase passes all three. Merge-mined blocks skip native PoW entirely, so **this is a block-minting hole, not only a finality one: a fabricated merge-mined block is accepted today.** `get_block_by_hash` is absent from `src/linear/src/monero/rpc.rs`, which is what blocks the route its own `TODO(HAZOP F3)` at `mm_rpc.rs:574` recommends. Decision taken: add that RPC method and a **node-local admission policy** — a node with `monerod_url` admits a merge-mined block only if monerod confirms the Monero block exists at the claimed height and is `monero_min_confirmations` deep; with `monerod_url` unset (its default) it accepts as today **with a logged warning**, so the residual gap is stated in the log rather than implied away. Node-local because it is network-bound, exactly as `OBL-C66` is. Work named: `monero_block_hash` helper (**done** 2026-09-22, `359b1d6f3f`), derive-and-validate `anchor_monero_hash` (**done** in the same commit, with its three-direction test), `get_block_by_hash`, the admission policy, and the `bin/dwowd/src/tests/merge_mining.rs` assertions — plus a negative control the row lacks: a fabricated Monero block must not be admitted when the policy is on. **One item is deliberately deferred**: `src/sdk/src/blockchain.rs:885-891`'s doc comment on `MoneroBlockHeight` still claims the field "gates merge-mining finality", which it does not and has not since enforcement moved to the verified anchor proof. Correcting it is an `src/sdk` edit, `src/sdk/**` is in every contract's `SOURCE_MANIFEST`, and the genesis pin has already moved once this campaign — so it waits for the batched re-roll rather than invalidating all 32 artifacts for a comment | `monero/mod.rs` (`MoneroPowData`), `monero/rpc.rs`, `mm_rpc.rs:574`, `:630-631`; `src/sdk/src/blockchain.rs` | C | **Audited 2026-09-23: OPEN, and the residual is a genuine `C`.** With `monerod_url` unset — the default — the block is admitted, and `bin/dwowd/src/block_acceptor.rs:232` states it in the log: "Its receipts prove coinbase inclusion in some serialized block, which a fabricated block can also satisfy." **The policy's premise is corrected again, 2026-09-22, before any code was written.** The paragraph above states the policy as "monerod confirms the Monero block exists **at the claimed height**". There is no claimed height on this path, and there cannot be: production writes *both* `anchor_monero_height` and `anchor_monero_hash` **zero** for a merge-mined block (`mm_rpc.rs:638-639`, deliberately — nothing reads them), and the height is not derivable from the proof at all, which is this same row's first half. What *is* derivable is the **hash**: `MoneroPowData::block_hash()` (`monero/mod.rs:259-267`), which the header field must already equal when non-zero (`validation.rs:98-103`). So the admission policy must be **hash-driven**: derive the hash from the block's own proof, ask monerod whether it knows *that* hash, and take the confirmation depth from the height monerod reports for it. That is the strictly stronger direction — the lookup key is derived from the proof rather than supplied by the submitter — and the weaker one (query by claimed height, then compare) is not expressible for the blocks this policy governs. Recorded as a premise correction of the same kind as this row's "the height is derivable" half, not as a narrowing to what happens to be achievable. **Was `CLOSED 2026-09-22`; reopened 2026-09-23 on the residual, then re-closed the same day when that residual was split into its own row (`OBL-C80`).** The reopen was right that something remained, and wrong about *which row it belonged to*: what this row is about — the anchor being **derived** rather than asserted — is enforced (`validation.rs:98-103`: a non-zero `anchor_monero_hash` must equal the hash derived from the block's own proof), and the admission policy that consumes it landed with its seating test (53 + 3 tests green). What was left is a *deployment default*, a different proposition, and it now has its own row rather than leaving this one permanently open. **The docstring this row's plan item flagged is corrected in the same batched re-roll, 2026-09-23**: `sdk/blockchain.rs`'s `MoneroBlockHeight` said *"The `BlockHeader.anchor_monero_height` field gates merge-mining finality — confusing our height for the Monero anchor height silently breaks the finality guarantee."* The type is still worth keeping (both heights are `u64` and advance independently, so the nominal wrapper is the point), but the sentence asserting an enforcement that does not exist is gone, replaced by what does: a **verified anchor proof** confers finality and the anchor is derived. A comment citing an enforcement the code lacks is the thing a reader would use to skip the check, which is why it rides this batch rather than sitting in the register as prose-only drift. **And the rule itself is mechanized as of 2026-09-24.** `Consensus/BlockHeader.lean` states it as a predicate over two values — the claimed anchor and the one derived from the block's own proof — and proves its three cases at concrete numbers: `unreported_anchor_passes` (a zero field passes, which is what every production block carries since `mm_rpc` zeroes both anchor fields), `derived_anchor_passes` (so the rule is not simply narrower than the field), and `rewritten_anchor_is_refused` (the case this row exists for — before the fix the field was an independent claim about the same block the proof describes and nothing compared the two). The module note states in this row's own terms what the check does **not** establish — that the Monero block is real or on the Monero chain, since the three receipts prove only coinbase-inclusion in some serialized block — so the row's severity analysis and the model's scope statement are the same sentence in two places. |
- **`get_block_by_hash` exists** (`monero/rpc.rs`), mirroring `get_block_by_height` — same envelope, same `JsonRpcResult<GetBlockResponse>`, `params: {"hash": …}` — and it is the RPC client's first production caller. Four tests: success (asserting the height comes from monerod's reply, not the caller), an unknown hash (monerod answers with an `error` object, so the answer is an error rather than a silent pass), an empty hash, and connection-refused.
- **The policy is `verify_monero_powdata`** (`monero/verify.rs`), beside the sibling it extends: **hash-driven** (derive from the block's own proof, look *that* up, take the depth from the height monerod reports), and **fail closed on every failure to confirm** — transport, timeout, unknown hash, insufficient depth — which is this row's own decision ("only if monerod confirms") rather than a softened version of it. `monerod_url = None` returns `Ok` **having checked nothing**; the caller says so in its log.
- **It is seated in the node-local acceptor**, the `PowSource::Monero` branch of `block_acceptor.rs`, reached by all five entry points and never by `check_pow_stage`/`check_block_header`/`connect_block` — the same purity argument this row and `OBL-C66` make for settlement. When no URL is configured it emits a warning naming exactly what was not checked.
- **The negative control the row lacked now exists**: `test_merge_mined_block_rejected_when_monerod_cannot_confirm_it` drives the *same* block the acceptance test admits through a chain whose policy points at a closed port, and asserts the refusal comes from the policy and that the chain did not advance. What makes it a seating test rather than a policy test is that it fails if the acceptance path ever stops consulting the verifier — the campaign's recurring "verifier with no caller" shape, which no reading of the verifier can catch. The policy's own semantics (unknown hash, wrong hash, shallow depth, transport failure) are covered beside it in `monero::verify`, against the crate's real testnet fixture and the stub monerod.
- **Still deferred, and named**: `src/sdk/src/blockchain.rs:885-891`'s doc comment, which claims `MoneroBlockHeight` "gates merge-mining finality" — it does not, and correcting it invalidates all 32 contract artifacts, so it waits for the batched re-roll. **Also still true and now worth stating**: `verify_monero_anchor` itself remains without a production caller, because a *claimed* height-and-hash pair is a shape nothing produces — the policy above derives its hash instead. It is the arm a future height-carrying anchor would use, and dead until then |
| OBL-C80 | **ACCEPTED-WITH-REASON** | A node's *default* configuration either verifies a merge-mined block against the Monero chain or says plainly that it does not — with `monerod_url` unset (its default) the admission policy checks nothing | split out of `OBL-C67` on 2026-09-23, which is where the reopen pointed; `block_acceptor.rs` (the `None` arm and the warning it logs); `merge-mining.md` | C | **Split out rather than left inside `OBL-C67`, because the two propositions are different and only one of them is met.** **Severity raised `H` → `C`, 2026-09-23, and the reason the `H` did not hold.** The `H` was recorded when this row was split out, on the reasoning that "the derivation check, the loud log, the documented default" bound the hole. Read against the code, none of the three does. The derivation check bounds *mismatch*, not fabrication, and says so itself (`validation.rs:90-96`): *"Note what this does not establish: that the Monero block is real or on the Monero chain. The three receipts prove only coinbase-inclusion in some serialized block, so this check makes the field honest about the proof, not the proof honest about Monero."* The log fires **after** the admission decision, so it bounds nothing. The documented default **is** the gap. What remains: merge-mined blocks skip native RandomX — the acceptor's own comment, "the PoW comes from the Monero chain" (`block_acceptor.rs:189-191`) — so no PoW gate applies; `is_coinbase_valid_merkle_root` recomputes the coinbase hash from submitter-supplied fields and compares it to a root the submitter also supplied; the coinbase is exempt from the token-balance check; so an admitted block credits a reward. The path is nameable end to end — untrusted peer → `request_blocks` → this sync loop → `accept_block` → admitted → the coinbase mints — which is this register's stated bar for `C`, and the reason `OBL-C67` carried the same hole at `C` while calling it "a block-minting hole". With the producer-side gate now landed, this is the register's **only open critical**. `OBL-C67` is about the anchor being *derived* rather than asserted, and that is enforced: a non-zero `anchor_monero_hash` must equal the hash computed from the block's own proof (`validation.rs:98-103`). This row is about what a node does **by default**, and the answer is *nothing*: `verify_monero_powdata` returns `Ok` when `monerod_url` is `None`, which is the default, so a node that has not configured a Monero node admits a merge-mined block having checked only the three receipts — the ones that a fabricated block satisfies. **The gap is stated rather than implied**: the acceptor logs a warning naming exactly what was not checked, and `merge-mining.md` now says an anchor is verified only when the URL is set. What is *not* decided, and is the work this row carries: whether that is enough. Three options, each with a real cost — **(a) document the default as unverified**, which is what is done today and relies on an operator reading a log line; **(b) require `monerod_url` whenever merge mining is enabled**, which is honest but turns a missing config into a node that cannot accept blocks; **(c) refuse merge-mined blocks unless a Monero node is configured**, the strongest default, which breaks the p2pool flow for operators who rely on p2pool's own validation of the coinbase. Until one is chosen the row stays OPEN, and its severity is H rather than the C it inherits: the hole is real and reachable in the default configuration, and the mitigations — the derivation check, the loud log, the documented default — bound it without closing it **PARTLY 2026-09-23 — option (b) implemented, and the residue it does not reach is stated.** Decision taken with the operator: the configuration gate, not the per-block refusal. *The gate.* `bin/dwowd/src/main.rs` now refuses to start when an `mm_rpc` section is configured and no `monerod_url` is — the error naming the choice ("Set `monerod_url` (or pass `--monerod-rpc-url`), or remove the `mm_rpc` section to stop merging"). The combination "merge mining on, verifier off" is therefore unrepresentable rather than merely logged, and it fails at startup rather than as a later surprise: the check sits immediately after the finality config is final and before anything is opened. *Why the gate and not option (c), read rather than assumed.* `PowSource` is `Native | Monero(MoneroPowData)` (`src/linear/src/block.rs:36-41`), so merge-mined and natively-mined blocks are alternatives on one chain; refusing merge-mined blocks without a Monero node would make a default-configured node reject blocks the rest of the network accepts, which is a partition and not hardening. This cell's cost note for (b) is corrected in the same breath — (b) does **not** turn "a missing config into a node that cannot accept blocks", because a node without `mm_rpc` does not merge-mine and is untouched by the gate. *Measured, both ways.* Two runs, one config, `--network darkwow-devnet`, `[mm_rpc]` present: without the URL the gate's message is the last line and the process exits `ParseFailed("merge mining requires monerod_url")` **before** opening the database; with `--monerod-rpc-url` the gate is silent, `Monero RPC URL set via CLI` and `Starting DarkWow node...` are logged, and the run proceeds past it — into a database path this control made deliberately unopenable, so it touched no real database and bound no port. *Blast radius, checked rather than assumed.* The pipeline already kept this invariant and now has it enforced: `contrib/docker/darkwow-testnet/entrypoint.sh` writes the `mm_rpc` section only inside `if [ "$MERGE_MINING" = "true" ]` (`:245`, section at `:248`) and in that same branch defaults `MONEROD_RPC_URL` and writes `monerod_url` (`:258`, `:284`), so every pipeline node that merge-mines already sets it and no node that does not merge is affected. `contrib/docker/darkwow-testnet/reproduce_merge_mining.sh` did not — it injects `mm_rpc` and starts a monerod without pointing dwowd at it — so it now passes `--monerod-rpc-url` at its own monerod, which also means that script exercises the verified path instead of the unchecked one. A `darkwow-devnet`/`darkwow-testnet` node started from the shipped `dwowd_config.toml`, which enables `mm_rpc` for both, now needs the URL or must drop the section; that is the requirement the error states. *What remains, and why this is `PARTLY` and not `CLOSED`.* The gate bounds the **producer**, which is the configuration it can see. A node that does not merge-mine still receives merge-mined blocks from peers, and with `monerod_url` unset its only response remains the acceptor's warning at `block_acceptor.rs:232` naming what was not checked — so the proposition above holds for that node ("says plainly that it does not") while the hazard does not go away. The tail of this cell said *"Severity stays **H** for the reason it was H"*, which the raise at its head supersedes; those words are kept rather than rewritten, because the argument they make is the argument the raise rests on. The severity is **C**: the hole is still reachable in a configuration this change deliberately does not forbid, and it is not closable by configuration alone — that would mean making the Monero receipts verify without a Monero node, which is the derivation half `OBL-C67` has already closed, and separate work. **Two consequences of that gate, measured 2026-09-23, and neither was recorded when it landed.** The gate refuses `mm_rpc` without `monerod_url`; the shipped configuration enables `mm_rpc` for three networks and sets no `monerod_url`. So the *default* now refuses to start on exactly those three. `bin/dwowd/dwowd_config.toml` is not merely a sample — it is `const = include_str!("../dwowd_config.toml")` (`main.rs:46`), written out by the CLI on first run — so this is what every fresh node gets. The predicate reproduced over the file rather than recalled: `testnet`, `darkwow-devnet` and `darkwow-testnet` each have `mm_rpc` and no `monerod_url`, and `mainnet`/`localnet` have theirs commented out, so **the three networks that enable merge mining are the three that cannot start**. Dates settle the direction: those sections are from `2cd3b76f19` (2026-05-20), the gate from `3652157ca9` (2026-09-23) — a configuration that had started for four months is refused by the newer rule. It is not a *wrong* refusal (enabling merge mining without a verifier is what the gate exists to catch, and the error names both remedies), but it is a first-run experience no document mentions. **(ii) The documented bare-metal merge test can no longer pass.** `contrib/docker/darkwow-testnet/test_merge_mining_p2pool.sh` is cited as the E2E test in three documents and generates its config from that same template (`:406`), then starts the node with **no** `--monerod-rpc-url` (`:488`) — so the gate stops it before its RPC opens. Its sibling `reproduce_merge_mining.sh:85` was updated for this gate and passes the flag, with the reason in a comment; this one was missed. **What I did not do, and why**: I did not edit either the template or the script. The template's fix is a policy choice (should the shipped default enable merge mining at all?) that the gate's own message already makes actionable, and the script is a pipeline file whose repair I cannot test — `test_pipeline.sh` is not mine to run, and the memory rule is that pipeline files are edited for determinism, not to satisfy a new gate. So the repair is named rather than applied: **one flag, `--monerod-rpc-url`, mirroring `reproduce_merge_mining.sh:85`**, plus a decision on the template default **Both consequences repaired 2026-09-23, with the policy decided rather than deferred, plus a third consequence and a prior failure the first read missed.** *The template default — decided.* The shipped `bin/dwowd/dwowd_config.toml` no longer enables `mm_rpc` for any network: `testnet`, `darkwow-devnet` and `darkwow-testnet` are commented out in the same shape `mainnet` and `localnet` already used, each carrying a note that names `monerod_url` as the requirement and this row as the reason. Merge mining is opt-in — uncomment the section and supply the verifier, or pass `--monerod-rpc-url`. That is the answer to *"should the shipped default enable merge mining at all?"*: no, because the default is what a fresh node gets, and the gate's own message already treats the pairing as the invariant rather than a suggestion. *Control, run, and two-sided.* A release binary rebuilt from this tree, with `HOME` and `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` redirected into an empty directory: run 1 writes the template and exits with the documented *"Config file created … Please review it and try again."*, and the file it writes has **zero** uncommented `[network_config.*.mm_rpc]` sections; run 2 reaches `Initializing a DarkWow daemon...`; and the same written file with an `mm_rpc` section appended refuses with `merge mining requires monerod_url`. So removing the section is what stops the refusal, and the gate still bites when a section is present — the pair is what makes either single run evidence. *The script.* `contrib/docker/darkwow-testnet/test_merge_mining_p2pool.sh` no longer takes either half from the template: it appends its own `[network_config."darkwow-testnet".mm_rpc]` section, the shape `reproduce_merge_mining.sh:50-55` uses, and passes `--monerod-rpc-url` at the node start, so the E2E test three documents cite is back on the verified path rather than the unchecked one. Its sed range for the mm_rpc port went with it, because it edited a section that no longer exists. Verified by running that script's own config-generation commands against the new template and parsing the result — `mm_rpc`, the `database` override, `skip_sync`, `skip_fees` and the net overrides all land, and the file parses. *A third consequence, in a script this row did not name.* `contrib/docker/testnet-node/entrypoint.sh` has the same defect: in `MODE=merge` it writes an `mm_rpc` section and no `monerod_url`, then starts `/app/dwowd` with no flag. Filed as `OBL-C95`, not repaired here. *And the reason (i)'s conclusion was right while its cause was not.* The shipped default did not start for a reason that came **first**: `create_genesis` was missing from the template entirely, so the config never parsed and the gate above was never reached. Measured, fixed and controlled in the same pass as `OBL-C93`; the run described above is that control **ACCEPTED-WITH-REASON 2026-09-24: the proposition's *second* disjunct is what holds, and it is checked rather than assumed.** The row asks that a node's default configuration "either verifies a merge-mined block against the Monero chain **or says plainly that it does not**". The admission policy landed (`src/linear/src/monero/verify.rs:89-148`, `OBL-C67`), and the default path *is* the second disjunct: `monerod_url` is `None` unless an operator sets it, `verify_monero_powdata` then returns `Ok(())` having checked nothing, and `block_acceptor` says so in its own log at `:267` — *"is merge-mined and no `monerod_url` is configured — the … fabricated block can also satisfy. Set `monerod_url` to close this."* So the residual is stated where the operator will read it, which is what this row asked for. The disposition is **no behaviour change**, for two reasons that are recorded rather than implied: the check is node-local and network-bound (it reads another chain and the wall clock, so it cannot be a consensus rule — `OBL-C66`), and **failing closed by default would refuse every merge-mined block on every node that has no Monero node**, which is the default deployment. **What is *not* claimed**: that an unconfigured node is safe from a fabricated merge-mined block. It is not — that is the residual this row records — and the operator-facing cost of closing it is one config key. The severity stays `C` for the residual, not for the default's honesty; a reader who wants the gap closed should read `OBL-C67`'s policy comment, which states the asymmetry (every failure to confirm is a rejection) rather than softening it |

| OBL-C68 | **FIXED** | Anchor acquisition is off the consensus path. `stratum_submit` calls the blocking `anchor_block` directly inside the async handler **while `linear_submit_lock` is still held** (`stratum.rs:561`, guard taken at `:348`), so one slow ArDrive Turbo POST serialises every stratum submission — and the whole handler blocks the async executor thread while it waits. The built-in miner does this correctly via `smol::unblock` (`miner.rs:293-307`). `doc/src/arch/consensus/stratum.md:145` describes the anchor as "best-effort, non-blocking", which is false for this path. The delay is **bounded at 30 s by `ureq`'s `timeout_global`**, which is a wall-clock deadline and not the per-IO timeout an earlier version of this row called it: the crate documents it as "end-to-end, from DNS lookup to finishing reading the response body. Thus it covers all other timeouts" (`ureq-3.4.0/src/config.rs:663-669`), verified while fixing this row. So the hazard is 30 seconds of serialised submissions per stuck anchor, not an unbounded wait. **This is the one row in this residue with no characterization test**, deliberately: the lock scope is not observable without a stub Turbo server and a live handler, so the lock half is proven by the Stage 4 integration test — a second submission must complete while the first's anchor call is outstanding — and the deadline half needs no test, because it is the dependency's documented behaviour rather than ours | `stratum.rs:348`, `:561`; `caribina/anchor.rs:84-86` | H | **FIXED 2026-09-22; the behavioural half is still owed.** The lock half is verified by reading: `stratum.rs:631` `drop(submit_guard)` runs *before* the POST at `:633`, and the guard is re-taken at `:658` before `accept_block`; the comment there records both the 30-second bound and why the release is safe (an interleaved submission is rejected by `accept_block`'s own height check). The row's own test — a second submission completing while the first's anchor call is outstanding — remains the Stage 4 integration test, so this marker must not be read as behavioural verification |
| OBL-C69 | **FIXED** | A merge-mined block survives the **P2P broadcast** with the proof that makes it merge-mined. `BlockBroadcast`'s wire codec is `serde_json` in all three directions — `Encodable` (`linear_broadcast.rs:112`), `AsyncEncodable` (`:144`), `AsyncDecodable` (`:172`) — and `BlockHeader::pow_source` is declared `#[serde(default = "PowSource::native", skip)]` (`block.rs:131`), so `pow_source` is **never written and always decodes as `Native`**. A relayed merge-mined block therefore arrives reclassified as native, and `block_acceptor.rs:191` takes the *native* branch, verifying RandomX over a header xmrig never hashed — the exact check the Monero branch exists to replace, and one the block cannot satisfy. **Demonstrated** by `block_broadcast_wire_drops_pow_source` (`bin/dwowd/src/tests/wire_format.rs`): the encoded JSON carries no `pow_source` and the round-trip decodes to `Native`. The rejection itself is *inferred*, not demonstrated, and the register says which: `block_acceptor` is invoked with `BlockTarget::MAX` throughout the test harness, under which every hash passes, so the harness structurally cannot reproduce the failure it would cause on a chain with a real target. Following the mechanism through, a merge-mined block cannot propagate by broadcast at all; it reaches a peer only by the sync path, whose codec (`src/linear/src/serial_sync.rs:116-180`) encodes the discriminator correctly. **Three serializations of one consensus field now exist and only two preserve it**: the canonical one (correct), the storage path — `chain_state.rs:1135` uses `dwow_serialize`, and `chain_state.rs` contains **zero** occurrences of `serde_json` — and the wire, which does not. Note that the comment at `bin/dwowd/src/tests/merge_mining.rs:300-303` blames `serde_json` for storage and concludes the loss is a "pre-existing bug, tracked separately"; that comment is stale in its attribution (storage is fine) and was the only record of the real defect, which is on the wire | `linear_broadcast.rs:112`,`:144`,`:172`; `block.rs:131`; `block_acceptor.rs:191` | C | **FIXED 2026-09-22, and the characterization test is flipped.** `PowSource` gained a codec of its own (`serial_sync.rs:155`, `:168`) whose unknown-discriminator arm **errors** instead of returning `Native` (`:174-180`), plus `Serialize`/`Deserialize` impls that carry that codec's bytes (`:192`, `:199`); `block.rs`'s `#[serde(skip, default = "PowSource::native")]` is now `#[serde(default = "PowSource::native")]` (`block.rs:169-170`), so the wire and the canonical encoding cannot disagree. The demonstrating test was replaced by `block_broadcast_wire_carries_pow_source` (`wire_format.rs:123`), which round-trips a real `PowSource::Monero` through the actual JSON codec |
| OBL-C71 | **FIXED** | A `DataItem` decoded from untrusted bytes cannot panic its verifier. `DataItem::deserialize` (`caribina/data_item.rs:218`) accepts any buffer of at least `HEADER_LENGTH` (116) whose first two bytes are signature type 2, and validates nothing else. Two accessors then slice on `tag_bytes` — an unvalidated `u64` read straight from bytes 108..116 — and **which one panics depends on `tag_count`**: with `tag_count == 0`, `raw_tags` returns early without slicing and `raw_data` panics at `:149` on `bytes[116 + tag_bytes..]`; with `tag_count != 0`, `raw_tags` panics first at `:214` on `bytes[116..116 + tag_bytes]`. Both are "range end index out of range", both in release as well as debug, and both are reachable from `verify_signature` (`:193` builds its item list from `raw_tags` then `raw_data`). A `tag_bytes` near `u64::MAX` additionally overflows the `usize` addition in `data_start` (`:153`), panicking in debug and silently wrapping in release — in release `raw_data` then returns the wrong region, so the payload comparison reads attacker-chosen bytes. **Observed, not argued**: `test_data_item_hostile_tag_bytes_do_not_panic_the_verifier` exercises both branches and the panic is at `data_item.rs:149`; the test is written with `catch_unwind` so the effect is measured rather than described. **Currently reached only through the dead verifier, which is exactly why it must land before Stage 3 wires that verifier into consensus**: the plan's own design makes this path parse proof bytes carried in a block, at which point a single crafted block panics every node that accepts it. The six pre-existing tests in this file cannot see it: every one of them round-trips an item this code produced, so `tag_bytes` is always honest | `caribina/data_item.rs:149`, `:153`, `:193`, `:214`, `:218` | C | **FIXED 2026-09-22.** `DataItem::deserialize` now validates the declared tag area *before* constructing the item — `usize::try_from` on the `u64` (`:251`), then `checked_add` (`:254`) and a bound check against the buffer (`:257`) — so both previously-panicking accessor branches (`:149`, `:214`) and the near-`u64::MAX` `usize` overflow that silently wrapped in release are all unreachable. The test that asserted the panic is now the regression control that asserts rejection, `test_data_item_hostile_tag_bytes_are_rejected_not_panicked` (`:574`), and it covers all three hostile cases separately: `tag_count == 0`, `tag_count != 0`, and `tag_bytes == u64::MAX` |
| OBL-C70 | **FIXED** | `MAX_BLOCK_SIZE` bounds the size it claims to bound. `MAX_BLOCK_SIZE` is the block-wire cap ("single source of truth pinned across nodes, L1 barrier #7"), but both enforcement sites measure a `serde_json` encoding — `block_acceptor.rs:164` for acceptance and `linear_broadcast.rs:175` for the wire — and that encoding omits `pow_source` (`OBL-C69`). For a merge-mined block the measured length therefore excludes the entire `MoneroPowData`: the Monero block header, both merkle proofs, and a `Vec<u8>` `tx_extra`. This is **not** the concern the comment at `block_acceptor.rs:167-172` already addresses — that one is serde *version* drift, mitigated by a 1% margin; this is a systematic omission of a whole field, which a 1% margin does not cover. Severity is bounded by the same comment's own reasoning (`MAX_BLOCK_SIZE` is declared a DoS gate rather than a consensus rule), which is why this is M and not C | `block_acceptor.rs:164-181`; `linear_broadcast.rs:175`; `block.rs:131` | M | **FIXED 2026-09-22, as a consequence of `OBL-C69` rather than by its own change.** With `pow_source` no longer absent from serde, the acceptance measure — `serde_json::to_vec(block).len()` at `block_acceptor.rs:161-166` — now *includes* the whole `MoneroPowData`, which was the systematic omission this row objects to. The wire site measures the raw buffer (`linear_broadcast.rs:175`), which was always the true wire size. The serde-version margin the neighbouring comment describes is untouched and still the only remaining slack |

| OBL-C76 | **PARTLY** | A test that does not run in the configuration used to claim verification is not verification. Three mechanisms hide tests in this tree and they compound **(a fourth was found on 2026-09-23 — a module gated by its *parent* takes its `#[cfg(test)]` submodule with it, and the gate built for this row could not see it by walking files; see the evidence cell)**: **feature gates** — `src/linear/src/block.rs:703` declares `#[cfg(all(test, feature = "pow"))]`, so its entire test module is absent from a bare `cargo test -p dwow_chain --lib`, and `monero/mod.rs`'s `test_monero_powdata_serde` is `#[cfg(feature = "async")]`; **`#[ignore]`** — all eight Caribina integration tests; and the **name filters** in `heavyweight.sh`. Measured 2026-09-22: `cargo test -p dwow_chain --lib` lists **172** tests, `--all-features` lists **263**, and the 91 that differ are `validation` (26), `block` (23), `chain_state` (20), `consensus` (15), `sync_boundary` (4), `sync_connection` (1) and two Monero tests — precisely the consensus surface this campaign works on. **The gates are not affected**: `make test` runs `--release --all-features --workspace`, so `scripts/run-all-tests.sh` always exercised all 263. The defect is in the *ad-hoc verification command*, which is what one reaches for to check a single crate quickly — and this campaign's Stage 0 and Stage 1 commits both quoted `162 passed` from such a run, understating what had been verified and, for `chain_state.rs`, `block.rs` and the Monero receipts, claiming verification of tests that were never compiled. All pass under `--all-features` (re-measured the same day), so the findings stand and only the *claims* were under-run — which is the part worth pinning, because an unqualified pass count is indistinguishable from a run that skipped the test. A verification command must name its feature set | `block.rs:703`; `monero/mod.rs:484`; `caribina/integration_tests.rs` | M | **OPEN — it describes a verification *practice*, so it cannot be closed by a code change, only by a convention that a gate can enforce. Re-measured 2026-09-22, and it has not moved.** The three hiding mechanisms are all still present: `block.rs`'s test module is still `#[cfg(all(test, feature = "pow"))]` (`:703`), `monero/mod.rs`'s `test_monero_powdata_serde` is still `#[cfg(feature = "async")]`, and the Caribina integration tests are still `#[ignore]`d. The measurement is reproducible in one command — `cargo test -p dwow_chain --lib` lists **172** tests, `--all-features` lists **263** — and the 91 that differ are the consensus surface this campaign works on. What the row asks for is not a fix but a **convention**: every verification claim names its command and its feature set, which is a rule a reader follows and no test can enforce. This session is the first to apply it throughout — every command quoted in today's closures carries `--all-features` — and the practice still fails whenever someone writes "tests pass" without one, which is why the row stays open rather than being marked satisfied by improved behaviour. **Measured and mechanized 2026-09-23, and the measurement found a fourth mechanism plus nine sites this row did not name.** A gate now enumerates every test that does not run in its crate's *default* configuration and requires each to be declared in `script/hidden_test_exceptions.txt` with a reason and the command that runs it. **Twenty-nine sites, against the ten this row's prose named by file.** The mechanisms it named are all real and all present — the eight Caribina `#[ignore]`s, the two upstream-networking `#[ignore = "…"]`s (which the prose did not name), the four `pow`-gated test modules (`block`, `chain_state`, `consensus`, `validation`), and `monero/mod.rs`'s `async`-gated `test_monero_powdata_serde` — and **four sites were not in the row at all**: `monero/merkle_proof.rs`'s sibling serde test, and four `#[cfg(feature = "json")]` tests in `src/sdk/src/blockchain.rs:1221-1245` pinning serde transparency for the supply, fee, block-timestamp and Monero-height newtypes — the last of which is the field `OBL-C67` and `OBL-C80` are about. **The fourth mechanism was found by the gate's own first run, in its own coverage**: a module gated by its *parent* (`#[cfg(feature = "sync-p2p")] mod sync_boundary;` in `lib.rs`) takes its `#[cfg(test)]` submodule with it, and nothing inside the file can say so — walking files found 20 of the 29 and could not find the other 9, which include `sync_boundary` and `sync_connection` themselves. That is the five tests this row's own measurement attributed to `sync-p2p` without naming the gate, so the row had already seen the effect and not the cause. The remaining nine are `net/mod.rs`, `net/hosts.rs`, `net/known_hosts.rs`, `event_graph/mod.rs`, `util/pcg.rs`, `sdk/test_support.rs` (the module the heavyweight tests rely on is itself uncovered by a default sdk run) and `native_token`'s entrypoint module, whose gate is **negative** — `#[cfg(not(feature = "no-entrypoint"))]` — so it is present by default and absent under the `no-entrypoint` build the python SDK uses, which is a configuration someone can claim verification in. *The root cause, measured*: `src/linear/Cargo.toml` and `src/sdk/Cargo.toml` both declare `default = []`, and the features carrying these tests are enabled by their consumers (`bin/dwowd` enables `pow`). *The root-cause fix exists and is deliberately not taken*: `default = ["pow"]` would make the ad-hoc command complete, but a default feature propagates to every consumer, changing the dependency graph and the build of crates with no interest in PoW — a decision about the crate's feature layout rather than a verification row's to make, so it is recorded as the named option in the declarations file's header where the next reader will meet it. *Status `OPEN` → `PARTLY`*: the mechanisms are enumerated on every run, a new hidden test fails a gate rather than quietly reducing what a pass count means, and a *stale* declaration is reported rather than left to look like coverage — that is the half a gate can enforce. The convention itself — a claim names its command and its feature set — remains a rule a reader follows, and this row is where it is stated. `make test` was never affected **APPENDED 2026-09-24: the three counts are re-derived, and they have moved — the figures above are dated and this is today's.** `cargo test -p dwow_chain --lib -- --list` lists **184** (was 172) and `--all-features` lists **294** (was 263), so **110** differ (was 91), by module: `validation` 28 (was 26), `chain_state` 25 (was 20), `block` 23 (unchanged), `consensus` 18 (was 15), `sync_boundary` 8 (was 4), **`execution` 5 — new**, because that module had no test module until 2026-09-24 and its five tests are `OBL-C100`'s mechanism arriving on the hidden side of this very line, `monero` 2, `sync_connection` 1. **The drift is this row's own subject one level up, and it is worth the sentence**: the gate enumerates the hidden *set* on every run — a new hidden test fails rather than quietly reducing what a pass count means, and it did exactly that when those five were added — but the *count* lived in prose in three places (`script/hidden_test_exceptions.txt`'s header, `scripts/run-all-tests.sh:157`, this row) and no command re-derived it, so the gap grew by 19 while all three kept saying 91. That header now carries today's figures and the two commands that produce them; `run-all-tests.sh`'s copy is left alone because that file is another session's in-flight work, and this row is the record that it is stale. **And the same failure arrived in a human step rather than a gate**: `OBL-C100`'s completion commit claimed `cargo check -p dwow_chain` as its verification, and that command does not compile `execution.rs` at all — it is behind `#[cfg(feature = "pow")]` at `src/linear/src/lib.rs:39` and `pow` is not a default feature. The claim was empty in precisely the way this row describes, which is why the row's convention is stated as a rule a reader follows rather than one a gate can enforce |

**Fixed on 2026-09-22, in the rows above.** `OBL-C71` — `DataItem::deserialize` now rejects a declared
tag area that does not fit the buffer (`checked_add`, so the near-`u64::MAX` case cannot overflow), and
the test that used to assert the panic is the regression control asserting rejection.
`OBL-C69` — `PowSource` gained a codec of its own (`src/linear/src/serial_sync.rs`) and `Serialize`/
`Deserialize` impls that carry *its* bytes, so serde and the canonical codec cannot disagree about the
field; the `skip` is gone and the fail-open `_ => PowSource::Native` arm is now an error.
`OBL-C70` — follows from `OBL-C69`: the acceptance measure now grows with the merge-mining proof.
`OBL-C68` — the Arweave POST in `stratum_submit` is made with `linear_submit_lock` released; the
behavioural test is the Stage 4 integration test the row names, since the lock scope is not observable
without a stub Turbo server. `OBL-C76` — recorded, not fixed: it describes a verification *practice*.

**Landed 2026-09-22: the anchor proof rides in the block, and enforcement verifies it.** This is the
consensus-format change the design constraint above requires, and it **moves the header hash** — the
mining blob grew 260 → 292 bytes for `anchor_owner`, and the genesis pin was re-recorded from
`e281afa8…` to `538b1634…` as a declared consequence, not a masked one.

- **`OBL-C63` — CLOSED.** Both enforcement sites (`chain_state.rs:1011`, `:1546`) now require
  `caribina::verify_anchor_proof(&header)`, a pure local function. A block whose anchor cannot be
  verified confers **no** finality, and — deliberately — is not rejected outright either: rejecting
  would let any peer halt the chain with a malformed relay, whereas ignoring the claim costs only that
  block's finality. That asymmetry is what removes the free chain-freeze. The old predicate read
  `anchor_tx_id != 0 || anchor_monero_height != 0`, two fields a relaying peer chooses.
- **`OBL-C64` — CLOSED for the field that matters.** `anchor_owner` (a fresh per-block Ed25519 key)
  is inside the mining blob, so a peer cannot re-attribute an anchor to a block it did not mine
  without redoing the proof-of-work. The four post-mining fields remain outside the preimage — they
  must, or anchoring would invalidate the solution it anchors — and they are no longer consulted for
  finality, so their malleability buys an attacker nothing.
- **`OBL-C65` — CLOSED by construction, and the wrong invariant deleted.** `should_verify_anchor` and
  `should_verify_monero_anchor` had no production caller and returned `false` whenever their
  `*_enabled` flag was off while `should_enforce` ignored those flags — a node could enforce what it
  had decided not to check. They are **removed** rather than corrected: a dead method encoding a wrong
  invariant is worse than no method, and "a verifier with no caller" is this campaign's root cause.
  Enforcement and verification are now literally the same call.
- **`OBL-C66` — recorded here as still open; superseded later the same day, and now
  ACCEPTED-WITH-REASON.** An anchor is *verified*; Arweave *settlement*
  is not checked at all. It cannot be a consensus rule — settlement lives on another chain and
  consensus must be a pure function of local data — so what remains is the node-local settle policy
  the row's fix note describes. Recorded as open rather than quietly implied to be done.
  **Correction (2026-09-22, after this paragraph was written):** that "node-local settle policy" is
  **not** written, and the row does not wait on one. What the proof establishes is **publication** —
  the miner committed a key in the block's mined region, signed a DataItem binding that block, and
  published it — and publication is the property the 51% argument uses, because the adversary in scope
  cannot delete a DataItem. Settlement depth is Arweave's business and this chain does not read it, so
  the gap between *settled* and *published* is **recorded in the row rather than narrowed**, and
  `caribina.md` no longer advertises a settlement time. The paragraph above is kept as the record of
  what was believed before that decision, not as a task.
- **`OBL-C67` — still open.** The Monero anchor is not yet derived from `MoneroPowData`, and the local
  Monero PoW check is not yet in place.
- **Anchoring is now real on both miner paths.** The RPC miner anchored in a detached task that never
  mutated the header, so every block it committed carried zeroed anchor fields; the built-in `miner_task`
  never anchored at all. Both now build the proof after the nonce and attach it **before**
  `accept_block`, and publish it asynchronously afterwards — publication is best-effort and, crucially,
  no longer the thing finality depends on. Merge-mined blocks commit no `anchor_owner`: they have no
  DarkWow preimage of their own to bind one into, so Caribina is unavailable to that path by
  construction rather than by omission.
- **Tests.** `verify_anchor_proof` carries a positive control plus eight negatives, one per condition
  (no proof, zero owner, foreign signer, another block's proof, altered height, altered timestamp,
  truncated bytes, and a `tag_bytes` overflow); `anchor_commitment` is asserted invariant under the
  post-mining fields and sensitive to the owner and nonce; `chain_state` carries a verified-anchor
  positive control beside a forged-key control, with the unanchored case as the baseline. The
  `OBL-C63` and `OBL-C64` characterization tests are flipped, and two blob-length assertions moved.

**A design constraint derived while fixing the above, recorded because it is not obvious and it
determines the shape of the remaining change.** The tempting cheap fix for `OBL-C63`/`OBL-C64` is to
require only that a *descendant* commit the block's `anchor_tx_id` in its mined region — no proof in
the block, no `anchor_owner`, nothing verified at the enforcement site, and the anchor becomes
PoW-authenticated transitively by the next block's work. **That fix is unsound, and not marginally.**
`anchor_tx_id` would then be 32 bytes the miner chooses, so any miner could finalize any block they
mined by asserting an id and committing it in the next block; after two blocks every block would be
un-replaceable and the chain could never reorganise at all. A finality rule needs an artefact the
claimer cannot produce unilaterally, or it degrades into "no reorgs, ever". So the anchor **proof** —
the signed ANS-104 DataItem, authored by a key committed in the block's mined region and binding the
anchor-zeroed header hash — must ride inside the block and be verified by a pure local function. That
is why the remaining work is a consensus-format change rather than a predicate tweak, and why it must
be verified before it lands: it moves the header hash.

**One further defect, recorded but not opened as a finality row.** `mm_get_aux_block` parses p2pool's
`address` parameter, logs a warning and ignores it (`mm_rpc.rs:178-190`), so the node mines to its own
declared key and a merge miner's DarkWow reward does not route to the address it asked for. That is a
reward-routing and economic defect, not a finality one, and folding it into this residue would bury it
under the wrong root cause; it wants its own row and its own plan. Until then it is pinned by a test so
it cannot drift silently.

**Process items, not obligations.** From the same corpus, and they belong with the three the register
already carries: `compile-fragilities` `A4`/`A5` (`-j 8` and `--test-threads 4` for heavy sweeps —
both are standing rules in the repository's memory, not open findings); `A6` (`--no-fail-fast` on
sweeps, and the `aws-lc-rs`/`ring` `--all-features` conflict that makes `quic_transport` abort a
sweep); `build-resource` `B7` (BuildKit, not configured — a deliberate future enhancement); and the
`hazop-darkleaf-in-contractcall-data` proposal, whose own verdict was **REJECT** and which was never
implemented — recorded as closed-by-rejection rather than left to look like untracked work.

### The contract phase rules — found by reading the genesis contracts as a benchmark (`OBL-C72`+)

The nine genesis contracts were read end to end, not sampled, and set against the other 25. They
observe two rules **without exception**, and three of them state the rule in a comment:
`native_token/src/entrypoint/mod.rs:1405-1407` ("Exec computed the running total; Apply only writes
it — no read in Apply — db_get ACL excludes Update"), `box/src/entrypoint/mod.rs:143-144`, and
`purse/src/entrypoint/mod.rs:202`. Both rules are normative in
[contract-wasm-type-system.md](../arch/contract-wasm-type-system.md) §A.4.7 and §B.2.2 and enforced
mechanically by the host ACL. **Neither has a register row, a checklist line, or a gate script** —
`grep -rln "ContractSection\|acl_allow\|read triad" scripts/ script/*.py hooks/` returns nothing, and
no script in the repository references the ACL section model at all.

That absence is the finding, and it is what makes these rows worth separating from `OBL-C1`–`C71`.
Every other rule in this repository that *has* a gate — domain separation, instance derivation,
metadata alignment, pubkey binding — appears in this register as measured and is wholly or partly
closed. These two had none, and drifted to **86 violations across 12 contracts, none of them genesis**.

The measurement is `scripts/check-phase-host-functions.sh`, which parses each contract's declared
`define_contract!` entrypoints, builds the call closure from `exec` and `apply`, and takes the
permitted sections from the `acl_allow` call sites in `src/runtime/import/*.rs` **at run time** — so
the gate cannot drift from the ACL it checks, the RC5 failure this repository has had four times.
**The gate corrected this row's first draft**: an earlier ad-hoc scan reported 66 sites and missed
`insurance_market` entirely (15 sites — its apply handlers are `*_process_update_v1` in per-function
files, which a name-pattern scan does not match) and the read-only getters `get_verifying_block_height`
(four sites) and `get_call_index` (one), whose ACLs also exclude `Update`. The 86 is the gate's
number; 66 was a pattern-matching artifact.

**Re-measured 2026-09-23, and both of those numbers are now stale in the same direction.** The gate
reports **7 violations, in one contract**, all one class:

```
$ bash scripts/check-phase-host-functions.sh ; echo EXIT=$?
FAIL: src/contract/drain_protection/src/entrypoint.rs:368: init_fund_process_instruction_v1() -> db_set()  (legal in ('Deploy', 'Update'))
       … (the same shape at :439, :491, :542, :600, :630, :662 — seven sites, all drain_protection)
FAIL: 7 phase violation(s).
EXIT=1
```

So **read-in-apply is at 0 sites everywhere** — `OBL-C72`'s rule now holds without exception — and
**write-in-exec is at 7, all in `drain_protection`**. Two corrections follow from the same run: the
**86** above and the *script's own docstring* (`check-phase-host-functions.sh:20`, "66 sites across
11 non-genesis contracts") are both stale self-descriptions of a family that has moved; and the
seven survivors are the residue of a contract whose state cannot commit at all — its `process_update`
is a no-op (`drain_protection/src/entrypoint.rs:791-794`, `Ok(())` with the update discarded), so the
exec-phase `db_set` is the *only* write path and every transition fails the ACL. That is `OBL-C73`'s
proposition measured false, and it is recorded there rather than here.

**And the gate that found this is not wired into `run-all-tests.sh`.** Its `run_gate` list (lines
76-146) has no `check-phase-host-functions` entry, so seven live violations cannot fail a full run —
which is the same "a gate nobody runs" shape this family's own filing text names, one level up.

**Corrected 2026-09-23: it is wired, as a ratchet.** `run-all-tests.sh` now runs
`exec/apply phase rule` with the static audits, and `scripts/check-phase-host-functions.sh` takes
its seven known sites from `script/phase_host_function_exceptions.txt` (content-keyed on
`<file> : <fn>() -> <host>()` rather than on line numbers, following
`script/circuit_free_instances.txt`), printing each as `EXCEPTED` with the register ID that
schedules it and exiting 0 while a *new* violation still exits 1. Negative-controlled both ways
before wiring: with the exception file removed the gate exits 1 with all seven, and with one entry
dropped it exits 1 naming exactly that site — so the exceptions are demonstrably what suppresses
the findings rather than a bug in the filter. The paragraph above is kept as the record of what
was true when the row was filed; what it named as the gap is now the mechanism that closes it.

|---|---|---|---|
| OBL-C72 | **SATISFIED** | The `apply` entrypoint performs blind writes only: no `db_get`, `db_contains_key`, `get_object_size` or `get_object_bytes` is reachable from it, and any value apply needs is computed in `exec` and carried through the update struct | §A.4.7, §B.2.2; `src/runtime/import/db.rs:638`,`:787`,`:1325`,`:1551`; `vm_runtime.rs:954` | M |
| OBL-C73 | **CLOSED** | A state write (`db_set`, `db_del`, `merkle_add`, `sparse_merkle_insert_batch`) is not reachable from an `exec`-phase function; all mutation is in `apply` | §A.4.7, §B.2.2; `src/runtime/import/db.rs:358`,`:504`; `merkle.rs:50`; `smt.rs:142` | M | **Measured 2026-09-23, and the row is the whole of the class: seven sites, one contract, one shape.** `scripts/check-phase-host-functions.sh` reports exactly seven violations, all `drain_protection`, all `() -> db_set()`, and the register's own earlier note names the same seven lines (`entrypoint.rs:368`, `:439`, `:491`, `:542`, `:600`, `:630`, `:662`). *What they mean.* `vm_runtime.rs:954` runs apply as `ContractSection::Update`, the host returns `CALLER_ACCESS_DENIED` when the section is outside a function's `acl_allow` list, and **no write function admits `Exec` at all** — so each of the seven is a call that cannot succeed, not a slow path. *And the other half, which is worse than the plan's note recorded.* This contract's `process_update` is a no-op: `fn process_update(_cid, _update_data)` (`:791-792`) ignores both arguments and only logs, so even the apply phase writes nothing. The `encode_*_update_v1` helpers (`:711-775`) build update bytes that nothing consumes. So the contract's state is never written *anywhere*, which is why its heavyweight coverage cannot be green and why the seven are scheduled rather than merely tolerated. *The gate is now wired*, as a ratchet over exactly these seven with the reason per site in `script/phase_host_function_exceptions.txt` — see the paragraph above on this page for the negative controls, and `run-all-tests.sh` now runs it with the static audits. *The repair*, in this row's own terms and first in the contract-rework band: move each write into the corresponding apply function, carry the computed value through the update struct, and give `process_update` a body that decodes and writes rather than a signature that discards its argument. When that lands, the seven lines come out of the exception list in the same commit, and the gate's exception count goes from seven to zero without the gate itself changing. **CLOSED 2026-09-23, and the repair turned out to be a contract rework rather than a move — which is the finding worth recording, because the row expected the latter.** Each of the seven writes is now in an apply function beside a `process_update` that had been a no-op; but five of the seven **could not be moved as they stood**, because their update structs carried identifiers and nothing else. `InitializeUpdateV1` held `{instance_seed, fund_id}` while the write stores the whole `ProtectedFund`; the comment in the entrypoint admitted it ("Store fund directly (InitializeUpdateV1 only has fund_id)"). Apply may neither read nor validate (`§B.2.2`), so the value has to travel in the update: **the updates now carry what they write** — the fund for initialize/lock/unlock/update_config, the key and value for vote, the key and record for transfer — with the scalar fields that were exactly `fund.<field>` folded into the fund rather than copied beside it. The encoders gained a length prefix for the variable-length payloads, following `ProposeParamsV1`'s proof in the same file. The shape is not invented: `identity`'s entrypoint is the repository's worked example (`process_*_instruction` builds the update, `apply_*_update` writes it, `process_update` dispatches on the selector byte), and that is what this now mirrors. *Verified on its own terms*: `scripts/check-phase-host-functions.sh` → **PASS: no unexcepted phase violations**, with the seven entries gone from `script/phase_host_function_exceptions.txt` — which is now **empty**, emptied by repairing the defect rather than by widening the list. `cargo test -p dwow_drain_protection_contract --all-features` → **24 passed, 0 failed**, including the four rewritten update round-trips that now assert the payload apply writes survives the length-prefixed encoding. The artifact rebuilt; `check-artifact-freshness.sh` green. *And one thing this row had wrong*: it said the missing writes are why the heavyweight coverage cannot be green. They are **a** reason, but not the first — the test fails at its first endpoint inside the *harness*, `Proof::create failed`, because `drain_protection_spec.rs` runs every endpoint against placeholder proofs ("Client proof modules pending"). That is its own finding, filed as `OBL-C88`, and it is why this repair is verified by the gate and the unit round-trips rather than end to end. *The gate also gained the check that proves the repair complete*: an exception whose site no longer matches is reported as stale, so the seven entries announced their own removal in one run instead of being pruned by hand — before that, a stale entry would have gone on admitting the next finding of the same shape |
| OBL-C74 | **PARTLY** | Every contract declares in its manifest the barbs each action requires, the capabilities it defines, its note schema and its capability primitives — the declaration `type-system.md` §13 and `ocap.md` §7 make the basis of `wallet_construct` | `manifest.md`; `ocap.md` §7; `contract-wasm-type-system.md` §A.2.2, §A.0.3, §13 | M |
| OBL-C75 | **CLOSED** | The id a host uses to key state is the id its circuit derives and `get_metadata` publishes — the host does not independently recompute it from data the client cannot know | `safety.md` RC5 (one fact, two sources of truth); `contract-wasm-type-system.md` §A.1.6, §A.2 | M | **First instance measured 2026-09-23, in `subscription`, and it fails on both halves.** The row had no evidence recorded; this is what the code says. *The circuit does derive the id.* `SubscribeV2` computes `derived_id = poseidon_hash(DOMAIN_COMMITMENT, subscriber_pub_x, subscriber_pub_y, plan_id, deposit, asset_id, lock_until_block, subscriber_secret, nonce)` (`subscribe.zk:42-51`) and compares it against the witness `subscription_id` (`:52`) — an equality whose operands are both prover-chosen, since the circuit's only `constrain_instance` targets are `tx_binding` and `tx_nonce` (`:60-62`). *The metadata does not publish it.* The SubscribeV1 arm of `get_metadata` publishes `vec![pallas::Base::zero(), pallas::Base::zero()]` under the comment `// SubscribeV2 circuit: [tx_binding, tx_nonce]` (`entrypoint.rs:149-155`), so the derived id reaches no verifier — and, noted here because the two repairs interact, the zeros are `OBL-C78`'s and `OBL-Z2`'s defect: taken literally they make the arm's proof unverifiable, since the circuit instances `poseidon_hash(3, tx_commitment, tx_nonce)` and `tx_nonce`. *The host keys state on the client's value.* `subscribe_v1` builds the record with `id: params.commitment` (`:358-360`) and `subscribe_apply_v1` writes it with a blind `db_set` under `params.commitment.to_bytes()` (`:392-396`); the `db_mark_spent` beside it is itself `db_set(db, key, &[1])` (`sdk/src/wasm/db.rs:185-187`), so nothing checks whether the key already exists. *The consequence, as far as it was measured.* A second `SubscribeV1` carrying a `commitment` already present overwrites that record. It does **not** lock the first subscriber out of `update_usage`: `Subscription::compute_nullifier` is `poseidon_hash([self.id.inner(), secret])` (`model/mod.rs:415-417`), the id is unchanged by an overwrite, and the secret is the caller's, so the victim's own nullifier still matches (`entrypoint.rs:627-631`). What changes is the record those checks then act on — plan, duration, `uses_allowed`, `lock_until_block` are the attacker's, so this is a downgrade the attacker pays the plan's price for (the host enforces it from the stored plan, `:349-353`), not a theft. That cost is why this row stays **M**. **CLOSED 2026-09-24: the id is the derivation the circuit makes and publishes, the host keys on it, and it is written once — with the end-to-end replay still blocked by `OBL-C104`, stated below.** *The repair is the row's two halves, and they meet in one place.* (i) `subscribe.zk` now `constrain_instance`s `derived_id`; the equality above it relates two witnesses, so it held for any pair the caller chose. (ii) The `SubscribeV1` metadata arm publishes `[tx_binding, tx_nonce, params.commitment.inner()]` — the call's own commitment, which is also the key `subscribe_apply_v1` writes under. Those two halves are one fact: because the circuit instances the derivation and the verifier compares the published commitment against it, **a client cannot key a subscription under an id the circuit would not derive** — and the host recomputes nothing from data it cannot know, which is the row's title satisfied by construction. (iii) The third thing the row recorded in passing — *"the `db_mark_spent` beside it is itself `db_set`, so nothing checks whether the key already exists"* — is repaired: `subscribe_v1` refuses with `Custom(32)` when the key is in use, so a second subscribe under one id no longer silently overwrites the first record. *Found while working it, and it is the sharper half*: `Subscription::derive_id` computed the same hash **without** the domain constant the circuit prefixes (`DOMAIN_COMMITMENT = witness_base(4) = 4`, `subscribe.zk:42-51`) — two derivations of one value, which is `safety.md` RC5 and this row's own thesis, in the code the row was written about. It was a live trap rather than a tidiness problem: a client using the model's function would set a `subscription_id` the circuit could never derive, and the unsatisfiable proof would present as a proof failure rather than as a disagreement about a hash. The domain is part of `derive_id` now. *Controls*: `subscription_id_is_domain_separated_and_field_sensitive` asserts the domain is part of the derivation and that the plan, the deposit, the expiry, the secret and the nonce each move it — negative-controlled by removing the domain, where it fails on its first assertion (*the domain constant is part of the derivation*), and re-verified after a forced rebuild (`cargo clean -p`), because the out-of-band file restore left a stale artifact that reported the opposite. *Measured*: the metadata-alignment gate reports `subscription/subscribe — 3 constrain_instance, 3 metadata pushes, 3 client public inputs`, all three sides agreeing, the client's `to_vec()` having returned the two-value pair under a comment claiming the circuit had no instances at all. That gate also raises one **advisory** order warning for the new instance (`constrains derived_id vs pushes params.commitment.inner()`); the names differ because the push is the call's value and the instance is the derivation the circuit makes of its witnesses — the two halves this row is about, so the warning is expected rather than outstanding. *What is still not measured*: `SubscribeV1` cannot run in the fixture (`OBL-C104`), so this row rests on the model control and the gate, as `OBL-C84` does — and reading `subscribe_v1` for this repair turned up a second defect in the same constructor, filed as `OBL-C105`. |
| OBL-C105 | **CLOSED** | The allowances a subscription records are the ones its plan defines, and the limits those allowances express are enforced | `src/contract/subscription/src/entrypoint.rs` (the `Subscription` construction in `subscribe_v1`), `:652-708` (`update_usage_v1`), `src/contract/subscription/src/model/mod.rs` (`Plan`'s `uses_allowed`, `rate_period`) | found 2026-09-24 reading `subscribe_v1` for `OBL-C75`, whose repair is the id half of the same constructor | M | **NEW 2026-09-24: the plan defines the allowances, the record stores zeros, and the limit is therefore unenforceable.** `subscribe_v1` builds the record with `uses_allowed: 0`, `rate_period: 0`, `period_uses: 0`, `uses_remaining: 0` — literals, while the `Plan` it has just looked up and checked carries `uses_allowed` and `rate_period` of its own (`model/mod.rs`). *Read both sides before calling this a loss, and the direction of the failure is not the obvious one*: `update_usage_v1` computes `is_new_period = blocks_since_last >= subscription.rate_period` (`:682`), and with `rate_period` zero every call is a new period — so the `uses_remaining == 0` refusal (`:685-688`) is never reached, and each call resets the counters to `(period_uses 1, uses_remaining = 0.saturating_sub(1) = 0, last_access_block = now)`. `UpdateUsageV1` therefore **succeeds** — and so does the next call, and the next: the plan's `uses_allowed` and its rate period are enforced by nothing, and a subscription bought once buys unlimited use, which is what the plan was priced for. The `H`-looking reading ("the usage path is inert") is the wrong one; the correct one is a limit that is silently absent, which is why the severity here is `M` on the row's own terms — a subscriber underpays systematically rather than a third party taking anyone's value. *Also in the same constructor, and the same shape*: `spent_nullifier` is stored as `pallas::Base::zero()` and read by nothing — every nullifier check compares the computed value against `params.spent_nullifier` (`:502`, `:600`, `:744`), never against the record's field, which `cancel` and `renew` then overwrite with their own param (`:519`, `:650`). *Repair, and it is one change*: copy the plan's allowances into the record at subscribe and read the record's `spent_nullifier` where the checks now read the param — the record is what the host has, and a field it writes and never reads is a second source of truth waiting for a reader. **CLOSED 2026-09-24 — and this row's own premise was wrong: the correction is the sharper finding.** *What the row said*: `subscribe_v1` stores zeros "while the `Plan` it has just looked up and checked carries `uses_allowed` and `rate_period` of its own". **`Plan` carries neither** — its fields are `version, id, name_hash, price, asset_id, duration_blocks, treasury_share, endowment_share, active, dao_escrow_discount, required_dao_escrow`, read in full. The fields at `model/mod.rs:375-384` are the **record's**, under the comment *"Rate Limiting Fields"*. So the defect was sharper than the row recorded: **nothing in the tree defined the allowance at all** — the record's fields were documented as the rate limiting and were never settable — and with `rate_period` zero `update_usage`'s window check is true on every call, so the limit was not mis-set but *absent*. *The repair*: `Plan` gains `uses_allowed` and `rate_period` (the price buys an allowance), and `subscribe` copies them into the record (`uses_remaining: plan.uses_allowed`). *The codec half is what a reader should not have to rediscover*: `Plan` had **four** codecs — a sync `Encodable`/`Decodable` pair, an async pair, and the model's slice `encode`/`decode` — and a field added to three of them is silently dropped by the fourth, which is exactly what happened here: the wasm's slice `decode` (115 bytes) read past the end of what the host's slice `encode` wrote (99), and the run died with a `slice_index_fail` panic in `Plan::decode` rather than with a decode error. All four carry the allowance now, and `MIN_ENCODED_SIZE` moved 99 → 115 so an old-format blob is refused by the length guard rather than read shifted — filed as `OBL-C112`. **Correction 2026-09-24, measured while closing `OBL-C112`: the last clause is false.** `MIN_ENCODED_SIZE` for `Plan` was **99** on the tree before and after this row's commit. `Plan::encode`'s capacity arithmetic did move to 115/147 — which is why the writer appended the allowance correctly — but the guard's literal did not, so the reader still accepted the old 99-byte shape, an old-format blob passed the guard, and the reads below it ran off the end (`range end index 107 out of range for slice of length 99`). The fields did reach all four codecs; the *length* reached the writer only. That is `OBL-C112`, and it is closed there with the constant derived from the layout rather than restated. *The control, measured*: `update_usage_past_allowance` repeats the usage call the endpoint before it made, against a plan that sells one use per `PLAN_RATE_PERIOD` blocks while the fixture advances one block per endpoint — and the contract's own log says `[subscription::update_usage_v1] ERROR: No uses remaining` (`Custom(5)`). If the allowance stops being copied at subscribe, that endpoint starts succeeding, which is what it did before this row was worked. `test_heavyweight_subscription` **passes** (369s) with it. *One tooling note, because it cost runs*: the host-side crate and the wasm disagreed across a stale rebuild, and the fix that mattered was not `cargo clean` — it was the missing `encode` above. `OBL-C75` records the same trap from the other direction. |
| OBL-C106 | **CLOSED** | A heavyweight endpoint's proof is a proof of the circuit it names — or the spec declares it as a placeholder | `src/contract/test-harness/src/harness/subscription.rs:382` (`cancel`), `:417` (`renew`); `src/contract/subscription/src/client/` (no `cancel.rs`, no `renew.rs`) | found 2026-09-24 rebuilding the subscription fixture for `OBL-C104` | M | **NEW 2026-09-24: two endpoints of the subscription spec prove with fabricated witnesses.** `SubscriptionHarness::cancel` and `::renew` build their proofs with `dwow_core::zk::empty_witnesses(&self.<circuit>_zkbin)` and `Proof::create(pk, &[circuit], &[], …)` — one instance and no advice — while `cancel.zk` and `renew.zk` exist, are compiled, and are registered by `init_contract`. So a green `CancelV1` or `RenewV1` would mean less than its name says, and a red one could be red for a reason the endpoint does not name: this is exactly `OBL-C88`'s class, in the contract whose spec also carried the declaration that row removed. *The repair is the one `OBL-C88` used, and it is two files per endpoint*: a client module that builds the proof from the circuit's own witnesses (`src/contract/subscription/src/client/cancel.rs`, `renew.rs` — the shape `subscribe.rs`, `update_usage.rs` and `verify_access.rs` already have), and a harness that proves through it instead of through `empty_witnesses`, with the params carrying the public inputs the proof was made with. *Not repaired here*: it is a client, not a fixture, and `OBL-C104`'s row now names it as the reason two of its six endpoints cannot be accepted. **CLOSED 2026-09-24: both clients written, both endpoints prove, and the derivation they needed had the same defect as everything else in this contract.** *Two files plus a derivation.* `client/cancel.rs` and `client/renew.rs` now exist — a `*PublicInputs`/`*CallData` pair, `to_vec()` in each circuit's instance order and `to_witnesses()` in its declared order, mirroring the three clients beside them — and `SubscriptionHarness::cancel` and `::renew` build their proofs through them, with the params carrying the instances the proof was made with. The fabricated `empty_witnesses` proofs are gone, so a green `CancelV1` now means the circuit was satisfied. *The sharper half was the derivation*: both circuits constrain `computed_nullifier = poseidon_hash(DOMAIN_NULLIFIER, subscription_id, subscriber_secret)` (`cancel.zk:13`, `renew.zk:13`) while the **model's** `compute_nullifier` hashed two inputs with no domain — and the host compares the params' `spent_nullifier` against exactly that function. So no client could satisfy both halves at once: the circuit demanded the domain-ed value and the host the value without it. That is the **fourth** instance of one class in this contract (`OBL-C75`'s key derivation, `OBL-C104`'s `derived_id`, `OBL-C107`'s literal zero, this), fixed the same way — `model::nullifier_of`, one home, called by `compute_nullifier`, by both new clients, by the harness's usage endpoint and by the fixture instead of each deriving it. *Measured*: the contract suite is green (29 + 3 + 2) and `test_heavyweight_subscription` **passes end to end** (393s) — both chains, the determinism replay, and every endpoint proven by its own circuit. *And the flow exposed a limitation that is not this row's*: `cancel` and `renew` present the **same** nullifier, so only one of them can run against a subscription — filed as `OBL-C113`, with both orders measured. The fixture now declares which of the two it exercises (`cancel_after_renew`, an expected rejection whose cause is the nullifier the renew spent) rather than letting the endpoint order decide silently. |
| OBL-C107 | **CLOSED** | A proof that verifies against the circuit it names — for `SubscribeV2`, the one whose block fails at the L2 verify while every side agrees | `bin/dwowd/src/tests/specs/subscription_spec.rs` (the subscribe endpoint); `src/contract/subscription/src/client/subscribe.rs` (`to_vec`); `src/contract/subscription/proof/subscribe.zk` | measured 2026-09-24, first run of the rebuilt subscription fixture (`OBL-C104`) | M | **NEW 2026-09-24: `SubscribeV2`'s proof fails L2 verification while the fixture's inputs are consistent — the instrument that distinguishes the two possible homes does not exist yet.** *Measured*: with the plan registered and the subscribe's **exec succeeding** — `[subscription::subscribe_v1] Subscription created` and `[subscription::subscribe_apply_v1] Subscription stored` in the same run — the block is rejected at the L2 verify with `invalid proof: call[1] namespace 'SubscribeV2'`, before apply commits anything. *What is known, each read*: the instance count and order agree on all three sides (the metadata-alignment gate reports `subscription/subscribe — 3 constrain_instance, 3 metadata pushes, 3 client public inputs`); the client's witness list matches the circuit's `witness "SubscribeV2"` declaration in order; the values the arm publishes (`tx_binding`, `tx_nonce`, `commitment`) are the ones the client's `to_vec()` returns;
