It is wired into `run-all-tests.sh`, and it is deliberately shallow: an arm returning a *variable* is out of scope, because a shape check that tried to follow values is the cleverer classifier this register has twice rejected. **Found while doing this, and fixed in the same commit**: the `relayer` feature configuration **did not compile at all** — `src/relayer.rs:258` used `SerializedLen` without importing it — so the three gated instructions had never been built in any configuration, and my new arms for them could not be verified until it did. One import line; all three configurations (default, `--features relayer`, `--all-features`) now compile. **Not verified end to end, and the reason is `OBL-C88`**: `test_heavyweight_relayer_endowment` still fails at its *first* endpoint with `invalid proof: call[0] namespace 'InitializeV2'` — a proof that does not verify against the circuit it names, which is the harness's problem and not this row's, and it is why the eight now-repaired arms are covered by the gate rather than by that test |
| OBL-C78 | **FIXED** | A contract's `get_metadata` publishes, for each circuit, a public-input vector whose **count and order** agree with the circuit's `constrain_instance` list; the tx pair is `poseidon_hash([3, tx_commitment, tx_nonce])` and is never a literal zero | `pool_stake/src/client/create_pool.rs:84-90` (the worked template); `src/sdk/src/crypto/constants.rs:57`; `check-circuit-metadata-alignment.sh`; `privacy.md` §5.3 | M | **Measured 2026-09-23, and the rule is now enforced rather than asserted.** `check-circuit-metadata-alignment.sh` gained a hard literal-vs-value rule (see `OBL-C20`): a position where the circuit instances a value and the metadata pushes a literal `Base::zero()` is a **finding**, not an advisory warning, because a literal can only match a derived value by a preimage coincidence and cannot match a client-supplied witness either. The single reviewed exemption is `tx_nonce`, left zero by convention across 71 positions; the exemption list is in the script, so adding to it is a deliberate act. *The sites — 10 positions over 6 circuits, with **zero overlap** against the 19 circuits the count check already fails, so this is a class that was invisible rather than one restated:* `drain_protection/exit` instance 1 (`tx_binding`); `stablecoin/init` instance 1 (`tx_binding`); `subscription/subscribe` instance 1 (`tx_binding`); `subscription/update_usage` instance 1 (`tx_binding`); and `subscription/cancel` and `subscription/renew`, each with three — instances 1, 2 and 3, namely `subscription_id`, `spent_nullifier` and `tx_binding`. *The composition differs from the one this work was planned against and adds to it*: the plan named `dao_escrow`, `drain_protection`, `pool_stake` and `subscription`; the measurement confirms two of those, **adds `stablecoin/init`**, and does not report `dao_escrow` or `pool_stake` — not because they are clean, but because both already fail on *counts* and the order comparison does not run against a push shorter than the instance list. That is a limitation stated in the script, and the honest reading of their absence is "not measured", not "clear". *Why the `subscription` four carry the row's proposition hardest*: `cancel` and `renew` push four zeros for a vector whose circuit instances `subscription_id`, `spent_nullifier`, `tx_binding` and `tx_nonce` (`entrypoint.rs:180-193`), and `subscribe`'s arm pushes `vec![zero, zero]` under the comment `// SubscribeV2 circuit: [tx_binding, tx_nonce]` (`:149-155`) — a comment naming the pair it does not derive, which is the shape this register treats as a claim contradicted by its own code. *The repair is this row's own*: publish `poseidon_hash([3, tx_commitment, tx_nonce])` and the real `tx_nonce`, and publish the values the remaining positions instance. Neither stablecoin nor subscription is genesis, so none of it moves the pin — but a repaired arm must also be matched by its **client**, which is what makes this larger than a metadata-only edit. **Measured again 2026-09-23 while working Band 5, and the size is an order of magnitude above this row's own estimate: the *count* half is the live defect, 19 circuits over 6 contracts, and the worst of it is one contract's eight arms publishing two literal zeros each.** The gate's worklist, read as a worklist rather than as a summary: `drain_protection` **8 circuits** (`5 constrain_instance vs 2 pushed values`); `insurance_market` **4** (`5/6/5/5` vs 3); `tender` **5** (`create_tender` pushes nothing at all for its namespace, `reveal_bid` 7 vs 5, `select_winner` 6 vs 4, `submit_bid` 6 vs 4, `submit_bid_with_capability` 8 vs 6); `lottery/initialize` and `subscription/verify_access` push **nothing** for their namespaces. And the shape in `drain_protection` is not a count slip — every one of its eight ZK arms pushes `vec![pallas::Base::zero(), pallas::Base::zero()]` (`entrypoint.rs:207`, `:216`, …), two literal zeros, under the circuit's real five. So the count is wrong *and* the values are placeholders, and the host compares the published vector against the proof's actual instance vector: these instructions could never verify. **Why that was invisible, and it is `OBL-C88` again**: `drain_protection`'s heavyweight test dies at its first endpoint on `Proof::create failed`, so its metadata has never been checked against a real proof by anything — the harness cannot build one and the metadata cannot describe one, and each defect hides the other. **What the repair requires per circuit, measured on one example**: `lock.zk` instances `authority_pub_x, authority_pub_y, authority_nullifier, tx_binding, tx_nonce`; the arm publishes two zeros. `authority_pub_x/y` are derivable from state (`fund.spend_authority` is stored and the witness is its secret), but `authority_nullifier = poseidon_hash(DOMAIN_NULLIFIER, fund_id, authority_secret)` **is not** — it needs a value only the client has, so it must be carried in the *params*. That is the same `C73` shape one level over: the params must carry what the metadata publishes, which means a wire-format change per instruction *and* the matching client change, across 19 circuits. **This is therefore a campaign with the gate as its worklist, not a band item**, and it is recorded as such here rather than started at the end of a long one: the ordering inside it is by contract (`drain_protection` first, both because its eight are uniform and because repairing them is what would let `C88`'s fixture work be checked against something). **`tender` taken first instead, 2026-09-23, and the reason is what the first pass found: it is the one contract in the list whose *client* exists.** Its five circuits each instance a `tx_binding`/`tx_nonce` pair the metadata omitted, so the fix is the three-sided one the row describes — and all three sides were wrong, which is the finding. (i) The **params** carried no tx pair at all, so the metadata had nothing to publish: the pair is now appended to all five (`CreateTender`, `SubmitBid`, `RevealBid`, `SelectWinner`, `SubmitBidWithCapability`), encoded after the last existing field so every prior position is unchanged and an old-format call is refused by the min-length guard rather than decoded shifted. (ii) The **metadata** published 2/5/4/4/6 values under circuits that instance 4/7/6/6/8 — the pair missing from each, with the bidder's or requester's coordinates already read from stored state. (iii) The **client** instanced `tx_binding: pallas::Base::zero()` — a literal, in all five proof builders — which no circuit derives for any input, so every tender proof failed verification against its own circuit. One derivation now feeds all of it: `client::tx_binding_of(tx_commitment, tx_nonce)` = `poseidon_hash([DRK_POSEIDON_DOMAIN_TX_BINDING, …])`, used by the five clients, the five params builders (whose `tx_pair` setter is the way a caller binds a real transaction) and the harness fixtures. **The gate had been calling this a different defect and only half of it**: `tender/create_tender` was reported as "no metadata push carries TENDER_CONTRACT_ZKAS_CREATE_TENDER_NS_V2" while its arm pushed under `TENDER_CONTRACT_ZKAS_CREATE_NS_V2` — the same namespace string, an alias pair — so the gate's value→name table kept one name and missed the other. It now maps a value to *every* constant carrying it, which also cleans `subscription`'s `VERIFY_ACCESS`/`VERIFY` pair. **Result: the count half is 19 → 13, and all five tender circuits read `OK: N constrain_instance, N metadata pushes, N client public inputs`** — the gate's strongest form, which checks the client's `to_vec` against the circuit as well. `cargo test -p dwow_tender_contract` → 25 passed, 0 failed, including two length controls updated by exactly the 64 bytes the pair adds. **And the heavyweight test still fails at the same endpoint, which is what the localisation is for.** `test_heavyweight_tender` reports `invalid proof: call[0] namespace 'CreateTenderV2'` before and after — so a new test was written to answer *where*: `test-harness/tests/tender_create_proof.rs` builds the client's proof and verifies it against **the same zkbin the contract embeds**, with the inputs `to_vec()` produces, never touching the host. It fails: `InvalidProof`, with the zero pair **and** with a non-zero one. So the contract's metadata is no longer the reason — the client's proof does not verify against its own circuit with its own public inputs, and the defect is below this row's layer (the witnesses, the circuit, or the fixtures). That is the next pass's subject and it now has a red test naming it rather than a green gate hiding it. `tender` is not genesis, so all of this is pin-neutral **The literal class re-read 2026-09-23, three sides per circuit, and it is 11 positions over 7 circuits — one circuit more than this row recorded.** `subscription/verify_access` instance 1 joins the list, so the composition is this row's ten plus that one. Re-run rather than recalled: `bash scripts/check-circuit-metadata-alignment.sh` → *"11 position(s) push a literal `Base::zero()` where the circuit instances a value"* over `drain_protection/exit`, `stablecoin/init`, `subscription/{subscribe,update_usage,verify_access}` and `subscription/{cancel,renew}` (three each). *The class confirmed on all three sides for the new member, and the confirmation is what makes it a finding rather than a count.* The circuit **derives** the value it exposes — `tx_binding = poseidon_hash(DOMAIN_TX_BINDING, tx_commitment, tx_nonce)` then `constrain_instance(tx_binding)` (`verify_access.zk:44-45`) — while the host's metadata arm answers with `vec![pallas::Base::zero(), pallas::Base::zero()]` under the comment `// VerifyAccessV2 circuit: [tx_binding, tx_nonce]` (`entrypoint.rs:157-161`), and the runtime checks the proof *against that vector* (`src/linear/src/zk_verifier.rs:234-238`). The client's half is the third side and it does not rescue it: `client/verify_access.rs:157` puts `tx_binding: pallas::Base::zero()` into the public inputs and `:179` puts zero in the witness too, while the circuit overwrites the witness with the derived hash. So the instance column would have to hold zero *and* satisfy `instance = poseidon(3, tx_commitment, tx_nonce)` — satisfiable only by a preimage of zero — and the same read reproduces for `subscribe`, `update_usage`, `cancel`, `renew`, `stablecoin/init` and `drain_protection/exit`: every one **derives** the binding, every arm answers with a literal, so none of those seven instructions can be called successfully by anyone. That is the `OBL-C77`/`OBL-C90` class (an instruction with no path that works) reached through the binding rather than the declaration, and it is why the repair is this row's own across all seven rather than a metadata-only edit: the *client* must build the same derivation, which is the shape `tender`'s landed fix already has and the reason `pool_stake`'s client is the worked template above **The `subscription` arm repaired 2026-09-23 on all three sides, and the literal class is down from 11 positions over 7 circuits to 2 over 2.** *What was wrong, read rather than inherited from the gate's summary.* Five circuits instance the pair — `tx_binding = poseidon_hash(DOMAIN_TX_BINDING, tx_commitment, tx_nonce)`, then `tx_nonce` — and five arms published literals in their place: `subscribe` and `verify_access` two zeros each; `update_usage` two zeros beside a correctly derived `derived_id`; and `cancel`/`renew` **four** zeros each, including `subscription_id` and `spent_nullifier`, which the params already carried. No satisfiable proof can put a derived value into a literal instance slot, so all five instructions were unbuildable. *The repair, in this row's own shape.* The pair joined the params of all five — `SubscribeParamsV1`, `UpdateUsageParamsV1`, `VerifyAccessParamsV1`, `CancelParamsV1`, `RenewParamsV1` — appended after each struct's last existing field, so every prior position is unchanged and an old-format call is refused by the length guard rather than decoded shifted; the three clients that exist derive it through one helper (`client::tx_binding_of`, copied from `tender/src/client/mod.rs:59` rather than re-derived) and expose `tx_pair`; the arms publish the params' values in the circuit's order. *Two things the read turned up beyond the literals.* `update_usage`'s client `to_vec` was in a **different order** from its circuit — `[derived_id, tx_binding, tx_nonce]` against the circuit's `[tx_binding, tx_nonce, derived_id]` — which no count check can see and no proof survives; it is fixed here. And `cancel`/`renew` have **no client at all**: `src/contract/subscription/src/client/` holds `subscribe`, `update_usage`, `verify_access`, `rate_limit` and `zkbins`, so their pair is the zero default the harness derives and their proofs stay unbuildable until a client is written. That is recorded rather than papered over, and it is the remaining half of those two instructions. *Verified.* Gate 2 reads the strongest form for the three with clients — `subscription/subscribe` 2 constrain_instance, 2 pushes, 2 client public inputs; `update_usage` 3/3/3; `verify_access` 2/2/2 — and count-agreement for the two without (`cancel` 4/4, `renew` 4/4, `no client to_vec found`); the instance checker classifies all five with nothing unclassified (`subscribe 2 derived:1, redundant:1`; `cancel`/`renew` `4 bound:1, derived:1, redundant:2`); the contract's own 27 tests pass, including the round-trips that now exercise the appended pair; the artifact was rebuilt and is fresh, and this contract is not genesis so nothing moved the pin. *And the end-to-end run says where the next blocker is, which is not here.* `test_heavyweight_subscription` still fails, but **not at the proof**: `grep -c "invalid proof"` over the run's log is **0**, where this row's symptom was `invalid proof: call[0] namespace …`, and the failure is now the contract's own child-call check — *"Expected 1 child call (promissory_note::transfer_v1), got 0"*, `ContractError(Custom(30))`. The spec's five endpoints all declare `children: vec![]` while `subscribe_v1` requires the payment child, so the fixture lacks it; that is a test-side gap this row does not own and it is named as the next step rather than closed from here. *Severity unchanged at `M`*: five instructions move from unbuildable to buildable, two stay unbuildable for want of a client, and gate 2's remaining literal findings are `drain_protection/exit` and `stablecoin/init` — the former this plan's Band 3 cluster, the latter the same shape in a contract already rebuilt once today **And `stablecoin/init` fixed 2026-09-23 by the same recipe, which leaves the literal class at 1 position over 1 circuit.** `init.zk` instances `tx_binding`, `tx_nonce` and `deployer_auth`; the arm published `[zero, zero, params.deployer_auth]`. What the read found is that **the client was already right** — `client/initialize.rs`'s `InitV1CallData::tx_binding()` derives `poseidon_hash([3, tx_commitment, tx_nonce])` and `compute_public_inputs` uses it — so the literal was the only thing standing between a real init proof and the vector the host expected: client and host disagreed about a value neither could see, and the disagreement was invisible because no count differs when a zero is pushed. The pair joined `InitializeParams` after `governance_pub_y`, with the length guard moved 245 → 309 so an old-format call is refused rather than decoded shifted; the arm publishes the params' values; `InitV1CallData` gained `tx_pair`; and the two fixtures that build the params on the deploy path (`stablecoin_spec.rs`'s `deploy_ix`, `tests/pipeline.rs`) carry the zero pair's derived binding. Verified: gate 2 reads `stablecoin/init — 3 constrain_instance, 3 metadata pushes`; the contract's own 15 tests pass; `test_heavyweight_stablecoin` — which deploys through `deploy_ix` and is also `OBL-C82`'s control — passes at **563.64s**, so the deploy path and the accrual check are both exercised on the changed params. **What remains of this row's literal class is one position in one circuit: `drain_protection/exit`**, whose repair is not a literal edit but the client proof module that does not exist — `OBL-C88`'s cluster, and Band 3's largest item. **And the cluster measured larger than this plan's estimate, which is worth writing down before someone starts it from the estimate.** The plan's Band 3 calls it "one job with three names" needing the client proof module; read against the tree it is **three** jobs. (i) *The metadata half needs a params interface change, not an arm edit*: all nine arms push two literals, and every one of the eight 5-instance circuits (`execute`, `initialize`, `lock`, `propose`, `transfer`, `unlock`, `update_config`, `vote`) needs `authority_pub_x`, `authority_pub_y`, `authority_nullifier`, `tx_binding` and `tx_nonce` — while their params carry **none** of them. `ExecuteParamsV1` is `{ proposal_id, signature }` (`model/mod.rs:361`), 64 bytes and no `proof` field at all, so the fields the arm must publish do not exist to publish. (ii) *The client half is nine modules* — `src/contract/drain_protection/src/client/` holds `mod.rs` and `zkbins.rs`, where `pool_stake` by contrast holds four proof modules and `tender` five — each needing the circuit's witnesses (`authority_secret`, `fund_id`, …) which the params do not carry either, so the witness data has to come from the client's own callers. (iii) *The fixture half* is the harness's nine fabricated proofs plus the specs' flows, the shape the `subscription` fixture turned out to be. Nothing here opposes the work; the point is that an E13 estimate of "a client module" understates it by two interfaces, and a pass that starts at the arms will find itself rewriting the params and every fixture behind them | **And the subscription fixture's gap is wider than the child calls, read out so the next pass does not start from the wrong end.** The spec creates no **plan** either, and `subscribe_v1` looks one up and rejects an unknown id (`:379-387`), so the fixture needs a `DaoControlV1::UpdatePlan` first — that is the action which stores a plan (`:795-800`, and the arm's own comment records that it once persisted nothing while exec wrote and apply's arm said "nothing to do here") — then notes issued through the PN harness, and only then a child whose value commit satisfies `validate_child_value_commit(child, plan.price, poseidon_hash([plan.price, params.commitment.inner()]))` (`:396-400`). *One claim I nearly recorded and did not*: that no path creates a plan at all. `grep` for a `plans_db` write finds one at `:857` inside `dao_control_apply_v1`, and reading `:795-800` shows it is a creation rather than a toggle — so the plan half has a mechanism and the fixture simply does not use it. The host half of this row is done; the fixture is a flow, not a patch **The `drain_protection` cluster started 2026-09-23 with its simplest circuit, end to end, and the literal dimension of this row is now empty.** `exit.zk` instances `[tx_binding, tx_nonce]` and its arm answered with two literal zeros. Fixed on all three sides and then *proved*: `ExitParamsV1` gained the pair (appended **after** the proof, which is that struct's variable-length tail, so the decoder reads them from the last 64 bytes and the length check refuses an old-format call rather than mis-slicing the proof); the arm publishes `params.tx_binding`/`params.tx_nonce`; and `src/contract/drain_protection/src/client/exit.rs` is the contract's **first client proof module** — `client/` held `mod.rs` and `zkbins.rs` and nothing else, which is why no proof of any of these nine circuits could be built, only fabricated by the harness. Gate 2 now reads `drain_protection/exit — 2 constrain_instance, 2 metadata pushes, 2 client public inputs`, its strongest form, and the literal-vs-value dimension is **zero positions over zero circuits** for the first time: `FAIL: 13 count mismatch(es) over 13 circuit(s)` with no literal clause at all. *The proof, not just the counts*: `tests/exit_client_proof.rs` builds the client's proof and verifies it against **the same zkbin the contract embeds** with the inputs `to_vec()` produces, and refuses it against a public input it was not made with — so a verifier that accepted anything would fail the test rather than pass it. Two things the work measured that the cluster's estimate did not have: the eight remaining circuits are **uniform** — `execute`, `initialize`, `lock`, `propose`, `transfer`, `unlock`, `update_config` and `vote` share one witness list and one 5-instance vector (`authority_pub_x`, `authority_pub_y`, `authority_nullifier`, `tx_binding`, `tx_nonce`), so the recipe below applies to all eight identically; and `pub mod client;` in this contract's `lib.rs:42` is **ungated** where `subscription/src/lib.rs:88` gates its own behind `#[cfg(feature = "client")]` — which is why the new module needs its own gate (it does now) and why the wasm build broke the first time it was added. The count dimension's remainder is those eight circuits, each needing five params fields, one client module and its arm; the shape is proven once **And the eight are done 2026-09-23, so this row's whole class is closed for `drain_protection` and the gate is red on nothing of it.** All nine circuits now read gate 2's strongest form or its count-agreeing form: the eight uniform ones at `5 constrain_instance, 5 metadata pushes`, `exit` at `2, 2, 2 client public inputs`. Gate 2's count dimension fell **13 → 5 circuits**, and the five that remain are not this contract's (`insurance_market` 4, `lottery` 1 — the former another session's working tree). The literal dimension stayed at zero. *What landed, in the shape the previous pass proved on `exit`*: five fields (`authority_pub_x`, `authority_pub_y`, `authority_nullifier`, `tx_binding`, `tx_nonce`) appended to each of the eight params structs — `InitializeParamsV1`, `ProposeParamsV1`, `VoteParamsV1`, `ExecuteParamsV1`, `TransferParamsV1`, `LockParamsV1`, `UnlockParamsV1`, `UpdateConfigParamsV1` — appended after the last existing field where the tail is a fixed set, and located by an exact length check where it is a proof or an optional, so an old-format call is refused rather than read shifted; eight arms that decode their params and publish the five in the circuit's order; and **one** client module for all eight, because their witness lists and instance vectors are identical — `client::create_authority_proof` + `AuthorityCallData`, which derive the point from `authority_secret`, the nullifier from `poseidon_hash([1, fund_id, authority_secret])` and the binding from the pair. Proved with two of the eight (`tests/authority_client_proof.rs`: client proof verified against the contract's own zkbin, refused against a foreign public input). *Three things this pass learned by being wrong first.* (i) The three decoders whose tail is not a fixed field — `initialize`, `propose`, `transfer` — each carried a **64-byte** pair where the five fields are **160**, and the round-trip tests caught two of them; the third (`initialize`) had no round-trip test at all, which is why one was written. The fixed-size encodings had the arithmetic right, so the error was exactly where the length is computed rather than declared. (ii) A `//` comment placed *mid-line* in these one-line impls swallows the rest of the line — three impls silently lost their closing braces, and the compiler's "unclosed delimiter" pointed at the impls rather than at the comments. (iii) `pub mod client;` was ungated here while `subscription/src/lib.rs:88` gates its own, so adding a `dwow_core`-using module broke the wasm build until the module was gated the same way. None of the three was a modelling question, and each is the kind a pass with a build in the loop finds in minutes and a pass without one ships**Measured 2026-09-24 while closing `OBL-C107`: the literal-zero half of this row is live in four clients, and one of them was blocking a contract's whole fixture.** The shape: a client passes `Witness::Base(Value::known(pallas::Base::zero()))` for a **declared** `tx_binding` witness where the circuit assigns that name the computed hash — which constrains the witness rather than shadowing it, so the zero is a constraint no proof can satisfy, and `Proof::create` returns bytes for an unsatisfied circuit all the same. That is why this presents as `invalid proof: call[N] namespace '…'` at the L2 verify and never in the exec phase. Sites, each read against its circuit: `subscription/src/client/{subscribe,verify_access,update_usage}.rs` — **fixed 2026-09-24**, the computed `tx_binding_of` in place of the zero, against `subscribe.zk`, `verify_access.zk` and `update_usage.zk:19,33-34`, which declare `Base tx_binding` and instance the assignment; and, **not touched here**, `tender/src/client/select_winner.rs:110` against `select_winner.zk:43,84-85`, and `insurance_market/src/client/{purchase_coverage_with_capability,underwrite_with_capability}.rs:136` against `purchase_coverage_with_capability.zk:10,24-25` and `underwrite_with_capability.zk:20,37-38` — the last two in the contract whose client files another session is editing, so they are declared rather than changed, by the same rule `OBL-C103` follows. `subscription/src/client/rate_limit.rs:176` carries the same line and is **not** a site: its circuit does not exist and the module is referenced by nothing (`OBL-C108`).  **And a fourth site of the *derivation* kind, measured 2026-09-24 closing `OBL-C104`**: `subscription/src/client/update_usage.rs` computed `derived_id` as `poseidon_hash([subscription_id, pub_x, pub_y, usage_timestamp, nonce])` while `update_usage.zk` hashes six inputs prefixed with `DOMAIN_COMMITMENT` — and the metadata arm had the domain. The client published an instance no proof could match, and the block failed at the L2 verify. Fixed by one derivation with one home (`model::usage_derivation`, called by the client and by the arm). That is three domains missed in three files in one contract (`OBL-C75`, this, and `OBL-C107`'s literal zero), which is the argument for the rule rather than for the fixes. **Measured 2026-09-24: the tender site is fixed and its control is two-sided; the two insurance_market sites are still fenced, so the proposition stays false and the row stays `FAILS`.** *The tender site, and why it could not be measured where the others were.* The row named `tender/src/client/select_winner.rs:110` against `select_winner.zk:43,84-85`; the client passed `pallas::Base::zero()` for the **declared** `tx_binding` witness where the circuit *assigns* that name `poseidon_hash(DOMAIN_TX_BINDING, tx_commitment, tx_nonce)`, so the assignment constrained the witness to a value the zero could not be. The value is now `super::tx_binding_of(&self.tx_commitment, &self.tx_nonce)` — the same derivation `compute_public_inputs` already published, so the vector and the witness come from one home. *The measurement, and it took two attempts to be an honest one.* The instrument is `test_harness/tests/client_proof_self_verification.rs`, which is the file that exists for exactly this and is **staged by the session editing `insurance_market`** — so the tender case was added instead to `tender/tests/integration.rs`, as a `proof_self_verification` module that proves the client's proof and verifies it against the same `select_winner.zk.bin` the contract's `init_contract` embeds, with the inputs the client's own `to_vec()` produces. **The first run failed, and the reason was the test rather than the client**: it passed `requester_public` derived from one secret beside a witness `requester_secret` of another, and the circuit constrains `ec_mul_base(requester_secret, NULLIFIER_K)` against the instanced pair — so no client change could have made it verify. With the key derived from the witness secret (`PublicKey::from_secret` *is* the `NULLIFIER_K` point, `crypto/keypair.rs:355`), the pair is: witness at zero → **2 failed, `InvalidProof`**; witness derived → **2 passed**. The two-sided run is the control; a single green run would have been consistent with the test being wrong in both directions. *And a second thing the first draft got wrong, recorded because it is a row of its own (`OBL-C76`)*: the module was first written `#[cfg(feature = "client")]`, which is inert here — the `dwow-contract-test-harness` dev-dependency enables that feature and cargo unifies it into the test build — and an always-true gate is harmless while the *capability* to be false is not, because it makes the test vanish rather than fail to compile. The attribute was removed and the tests now run in the default configuration (measured: `cargo test --release -p dwow_tender_contract --test integration` → **27 passed**, the two proof tests listed). Tender's artifact was rebuilt through its Makefile rather than hand-hashing: `src/` is in its `SOURCE_MANIFEST`, so the client edit moves the source hash, and `make clean && make all` is what refreshes it — `check-artifact-freshness.sh` then reports tender fresh. *The remaining half, unchanged and fenced*: `insurance_market/src/client/{purchase_coverage_with_capability,underwrite_with_capability}.rs:136` still pass the literal zero against their circuits, and those two files are in the set another session is rewriting for the V1-vs-V2 client hazard — the same rule `OBL-C103` follows, declared rather than changed. So the proposition remains measured-false in two places, `FAILS` stays the honest status, and what changed is that the third place no longer is. *And one gate FAIL that is this row's by symptom and not by proposition, adjudicated here so it is not re-derived*: `lottery/initialize — circuit has 5 constrain_instance but no metadata push carries LOTTERY_CONTRACT_ZKAS_INIT_NS_V2`. Read on both sides, `get_metadata`'s `InitializeV1` arm returns a valid encoding of an **empty** list under a comment that says so — *"Non-ZK (setup-step, like roulette/slot InitializeV1)"* — while `initialize.zk` instances five values (`house_pub_x`, `house_pub_y`, `house_nullifier`, `tx_binding`, `tx_nonce`) and `init_contract` embeds its `.zk.bin` in the wasm (`entrypoint.rs:61-62`). So the count disagreement is real and deliberate: the circuit is compiled, declared by a live namespace constant, and never proven by anything — no client builds an init proof and no arm publishes its instances. The host derives `lottery_id` from the caller-supplied `house_pub` and does no verification (`entrypoint/initialize.rs:34-79`), and the values the circuit would constrain are values the contract's only init path takes on the caller's word. That is a **dead artifact or an unproven init** rather than a metadata vector that disagrees with a circuit in use, which is why it is adjudicated in this cell instead of counted as a seventh site: the freshness gate already buckets constants that resolve to nothing under "dead legacy constant(s) … cleanup, not a blocker", and this one is not in that bucket only because the `.zk` file still declares the matching name. Whether init should be proven is a design decision the comment claims to have taken; the gate has no way to declare a by-design non-ZK function, and that — a gate whose only responses are PASS, WARN and FAIL — is the thing worth naming **FIXED 2026-09-24, on the count-and-order clause its title states.** The four `insurance_market` circuits were the entire residue: their metadata vectors had not followed when the circuits gained `tx_binding`/`tx_nonce`, and two clients were emitting a **V1 design that no longer exists** — eight values where the V2 circuits constrain six and five, with `capability_predicate_result` and `derived_pub_x/y` occupying positions that displaced `tx_binding`, `tx_nonce` and `buyer_nullifier`. The gate names that class in its own words ("the proof would be created over a different public-input vector than the verifier uses"), which is exactly what the V1-vs-V2 hazard memory records for auction and dao_escrow — and here it was caught by the gate rather than by a proof failing. The literal-zero clause is at **zero sites** (`drain_protection/exit` was the last, and it is adjudicated), so both clauses the title states now hold. The observation above about the gate's three responses is kept: it is a *gate-capability* note, not a clause of this proposition, and it is the thing to name if a fourth by-design case ever appears |

| OBL-C81 | **OPEN** | A capability check authorizes the holder it names, only for the issued capability — the purse's own instance of it: the ownership a purse **records** is the ownership its withdraw **requires**, so `owner_commit` is compared against something | `scripts/check-pubkey-binding.sh` — 3 of its 57 candidates, and the only shape in the genesis slice that does not resolve; `purse/proof/{balance,deposit,withdraw}.zk`; `purse/src/model/mod.rs:108` | M | **Found 2026-09-23 by adjudicating `OBL-Z18`'s genesis-contract candidates (see that row), and recorded here because it is a different proposition from the detector's.** The shape is `constrain_equal_base(derived_owner, owner_pub)`, where `derived_owner = poseidon_hash(DOMAIN_SIGNATURE_SECRET, owner_secret)`. **The row said "all three purse circuits" and that is wrong for one of them, measured by reading both sides on 2026-09-23 — so the claim is corrected here rather than left, because a row that overstates its own extent is the thing this register exists not to be.** `balance.zk` is **not** an instance: `owner_pub` is consumed at `balance.zk:38` by `dp_circuit = poseidon_hash(DOMAIN_COMMITMENT, owner_pub, asset_id, purse_id)`, constrained to the caller's `derived_purse_id` (`:39`) and exposed (`:40`), so the equality at `:44` transmits that exposure to `derived_owner` — the transitive arm the `escrow` slice earned, applied here. The exception-list entry that carried the false claim was reworded under this row. **The two that remain are `deposit.zk` and `withdraw.zk`, and for them every clause holds**: `owner_pub` appears only at its witness declaration and at the equality (`deposit.zk:32`, `:49`; `withdraw.zk:32`, `:50`), `derived_owner` is exposed nowhere, and the host contains the substring `owner` **zero** times in its 225 lines. **Two things found by re-reading, and one of them is worse than the row states.** (i) *The prescribed repair cannot be built as written.* The row's remedy is "expose `derived_owner` and have the withdraw path compare it against the stored `owner_commit`" — but there is no stored `owner_commit` to compare against. `Purse { version, purse_id, token_commit, balance_commit, owner_commit }` (`purse/src/model/mod.rs:108`) is constructed **only in a test** (`purse/tests/integration.rs:73`); `Purse {` appears nowhere else under the contract, the host never writes it to a tree and never reads it from one, and `model/mod.rs:106`'s own note says "When wire-format usage begins, this doc comment must be removed" — i.e. the record has never been wired. So one half of the pair the repair names does not exist, and the repair is "build the purse record's persistence, then bind it", not "compare against it". (ii) *The harm is stronger than the row's narrative*. The row says the merkle-leaf preimage is the only real gate, so a leak of it is what the missing check would backstop. Reading `withdraw.zk`'s nullifier: `nf_circuit = poseidon_hash(DOMAIN_NULLIFIER, owner_secret, purse_id, state_nonce)` is exposed and **nothing compares it to any stored nullifier**, so `owner_secret` is not even constrained to a registered owner — it is free. Severity stays M on the same reasoning the row gives (the leaf preimage gates it, and no path from untrusted input to stolen funds is demonstrated), but the second lock is missing on both the ownership and the nullifier side | So the constraint binds one witness to a hash of another and states an ownership property the verifier never receives. What makes it a finding rather than a style point is that the *record* expects it: `Purse { version, purse_id, token_commit, balance_commit, owner_commit }` (`purse/src/model/mod.rs:108`) stores an `owner_commit` that **nothing in the tree ever compares against** — not the circuit, not the host. A half-built lock, and the half that is missing is the side that would check it. *Why this is M and not C or H, stated as the path that fails rather than as an assurance.* For a withdraw to be accepted the prover must reproduce `old_leaf` and a merkle path to the exposed `expected_root`, which requires `old_balance` and `state_nonce`. Both are private to the owner's wallet and appear on chain only inside a Pedersen commitment (`new_commit_x/y`) and a leaf hash, so an attacker without them cannot build the path — which means the search for a path from untrusted input to stolen funds fails *at the same gate the vacuous check would have failed at*. So there is no demonstrated harm, and this register does not rank an undemonstrated one. What the missing check costs is the second lock: **if the leaf preimage ever leaks — a wallet bug, a log, a backup — nothing else stands between the holder of those two field elements and the balance**, and the `owner_commit` that was supposed to be that lock is inert. Defence-in-depth, therefore M. *The repair*, when it is worked: expose `derived_owner` and have the withdraw path compare it against the stored `owner_commit` — the shape `Z17`'s remedy uses for identity's capability, and the same shape `labor_market` uses as the repository's one working capability check. **That repair is ordered, not available**: it presupposes a stored `owner_commit`, and per (i) above nothing persists the `Purse` record at all, so the work is "wire the purse record, then bind against it". Recorded so the next reader does not start from a comparison whose right-hand side does not exist |
| OBL-C82 | **PARTLY** | Interest accrued on the pool's debt is determined by the contract's configured rate and the elapsed time since the last accrual, and the accrual is authorized by the authority registered at init | `accrue_interest.zk:32-36`, `:49-67`; `stablecoin/src/entrypoint.rs:1528-1593`, `:1279`, `:1701`; `CDP_LAST_INTEREST_UPDATE_KEY` (`:100`, set only at `:203`), `CDP_BASE_RATE` (`lib.rs:84`, stored at `:128`), `STABLECOIN_CONTRACT_GOVERNANCE_PUBKEY_KEY` (read only at `:1381`) | M | **Found 2026-09-23 by adjudicating `OBL-Z18`'s `stablecoin` candidates, from the two `OBL-Z9`-shaped ones; the sweep of the two sides then produced the rest.** *The circuit.* `accumulator_public = ec_mul_base(accumulator_secret, NULLIFIER_K)` (`:32`) and `:35-36` compare its `ec_get_x/y` against the witnesses `accumulator_pub_x/y`. Neither operand is a `constrain_instance` target — the circuit's exposed set is `old_total_debt` (`:39`), `tx_binding` (`:70`) and `tx_nonce` (`:71`) — so the equality is between two prover-chosen values and holds for any secret. The comment above it, `# Verify accumulator is authorized`, names the property; the constraint states nothing. This is `OBL-Z9`'s shape verbatim — *"an equality between two prover-chosen values, holding for any secret"* — in a contract that row's scope does not list. *The host does not supply the missing check.* `process_accrue_interest_instruction` (`:1528`) reads the config DB only for `CDP_TOTAL_DEBT_KEY` (`:1548`) and `CDP_ACCUMULATED_FEES_KEY` (`:1576`), and never compares `params.accumulator_pub` against the point stored at init. That comparison exists two functions away, for governance reports (`:1381`, written at `:197`) — so the mechanism is present in this contract and simply unused here. *The rate and the clock are equally free.* `rate_per_second` and `time_elapsed` are witnesses; `:51-63` verifies `interest_amount = floor(old_total_debt * rate_second * time_elapsed / 315360000000)` by quotient-remainder, which is satisfiable for any `interest_amount` by choosing the rate to suit it. The contract stores both of the inputs the accrual should be derived from — `base_rate` (the constant `CDP_BASE_RATE = 500`, written at `:128`) and `CDP_LAST_INTEREST_UPDATE_KEY` — and the second is **written once at init to `0` (`:203`) and never read or updated anywhere in the tree**, so the elapsed time the accrual needs has no recorded source at all. *What is actually enforced* is two comparisons: `params.old_total_debt == stored` (`:1553`) and `params.new_total_debt - params.old_total_debt == params.interest_amount` (`:1566`). Both are the prover's to satisfy for any chosen `new_total_debt >= stored`. *The demonstrated consequence.* Any account can therefore submit `AccrueInterestV1` with a proof it can make itself and set `CDP_TOTAL_DEBT_KEY` to any value at or above its current one. That key is ordinary reading away from value, and both consumers were read in the same pass: `total_debt` is the denominator of the redemption exchange rate (`collateral_return = redeem_amount * total_collateral / total_debt`, `:1701`) and the input to the liquidation gate (`collateral_ratio = total_collateral * 10000 / total_debt`, compared at `:1279`). So the write controls both how much collateral a stablecoin redeems for and whether the pool is liquidatable at all. `CDP_ACCUMULATED_FEES_KEY` is written by the same path (`:1581`, `:1603`) and read by nothing today. *Why H and not C.* The two consumers above are read and named; the **payout** side of `LiquidateV1` — who funds the child transfer that pays the liquidator — is one file away and unread, and this register does not rank a value path it has not walked. If that read shows the pool funds it, this is C and belongs in Band 1. *The repair needs both halves, in this order, and a host-side comparison alone would be a no-op*: compare `params.accumulator_pub` against the point stored at init, **after** the circuit instances `accumulator_public_x/y`, because until the pair is exposed the value being compared is prover-supplied and a caller would simply copy the stored authority out of chain state — which is exactly the two halves `OBL-Z14`'s fix is made of. The rate and elapsed time need their own half on the host side (`base_rate` against the config, `time_elapsed` against a maintained `CDP_LAST_INTEREST_UPDATE_KEY`, written in `apply_accrue_interest_update`), because authenticating the caller does not make a free rate safe: the authority itself would still be able to write arbitrary debt. Stablecoin is not genesis, so all of it is pin-neutral **The payout read this row held its severity open for is done, and the answer holds it at H rather than C. *The child arms.* `MintStableV1` (`:1033-1108`) and `LiquidateV1` (`:1229-1337`) each require **exactly one** child call (`children_indexes.len() != 1` → reject) and it must be `promissory_note::transfer_v1`, opcode `0x04`. *The funding side of that child, which is the half this row named as unread.* Every input of a `transfer_v1` carries its own `Revoke_V2` proof: `promissory_note/proof/revoke.zk:47-72` derives `nullifier = poseidon_hash(DOMAIN_NULLIFIER, spend_secret, coin)` with `coin = poseidon_hash(DOMAIN_COMMITMENT, poseidon_hash(DOMAIN_SIGNATURE_SECRET, spend_secret), value, asset_id, coin_spend_hook, user_data, commitment_blind)` and constrains both the nullifier and `merkle_root(leaf_pos, path, coin_incl)` — so an input can only be supplied by a party that knows that note's `spend_secret`, and there is no delegation path in the circuit. The entries are then summed by `verify_value_conservation` (`promissory_note/src/entrypoint/mod.rs:516-557`, called at `:822`), which requires **every `token_commit` present in the outputs to appear in the inputs with an equal Pedersen sum and forbids any token type that appears only in the outputs** — so a transfer cannot exchange one token type for another and cannot create value. *So the pool does not fund it.* The stablecoin parent contributes nothing a child can draw on: all three arms' `process_*` return an update that writes ledger totals and nothing else, and **no stablecoin client builds the child transfer at all** — `grep -rln TransferParamsV1 src/contract/stablecoin/src/` returns `entrypoint.rs` alone, which merely decodes the child it is given. The payout is therefore bounded by notes the transaction already owns, and an unauthorized write to `CDP_TOTAL_DEBT_KEY` cannot convert into created or stolen value through this arm. **H, not C, and the reason is now a read rather than a deferral.** The consequence remains exactly as filed: `total_debt` is the denominator of the redemption exchange rate (`:1701`) and the input to the liquidation gate (`:1279`). What the same read did find is a separate row — the redeem arm, where the ledger is decremented against a payout that is zero-value by circuit: `OBL-C92` **The authorization half landed 2026-09-23, and the clause it does not reach is blocked on a design decision rather than on work.** *Two clauses, and the proposition has to be read as both.* The authorization clause — the accrual is authorized by the authority registered at init — is **now enforced**. The determination clause — the interest is determined by the contract's configured rate and the elapsed time since the last accrual — is **not**, and cannot be made so by the repair this cell named, for a reason the same cell's prescription does not contain. *What landed, three sides, in the order this row gave.* (i) `accrue_interest.zk`: the circuit already derived the accumulator point from the secret and compared it with `accumulator_pub_x/y`, but **neither operand was exposed**, so the equalities were between two prover-chosen values. It now instances the params pair (`constrain_instance(accumulator_pub_x); constrain_instance(accumulator_pub_y);`) — the same shape and the same reason as `governance_report.zk`'s `reporter_pub_x/y` one circuit over, which is the `OBL-Z14` precedent ported rather than reinvented. The checker classifies both as **bound** by the equality pair above them (`check-circuit-instance-derivation.sh`: `accrue_interest.zk 5 bound:2, declared-free:1, derived:1, redundant:1`; `unclassified` stays 11). (ii) The metadata arm publishes the pair first, in the circuit's instance order, refusing the identity by name — gate 2 reads the strongest form, **5 constrain_instance, 5 metadata pushes, 5 client public inputs**, which checks the client's `to_vec` against the circuit as well. (iii) `process_accrue_interest_instruction` reads the point stored at init (`STABLECOIN_CONTRACT_GOVERNANCE_PUBKEY_KEY`, written only at `init_contract`) and refuses any other, in the exact shape of the governance check this cell pointed at. The circuit and artifact were rebuilt (`make -C src/contract/stablecoin clean all`); stablecoin is fresh in `check-artifact-freshness.sh` and is not genesis, so nothing moved the pin. *The control, and it is two-sided by construction.* The spec gained `AccrueInterestV1ByStranger` immediately after its positive endpoint, with **every field identical except the accumulator secret** — including `old_total_debt`, which is why a rejection cannot be a statement about state. Run: `DWOW_TEST_LOGS=1 cargo test --release -p dwowd --all-features --lib test_heavyweight_stablecoin -- --test-threads 4` → **ok, 564.44s**, and the log carries the pair this needs: `AccrueInterest: old_debt=500, new_debt=500, interest=0` accepted for the registered key, followed for the stranger by *"AccrueInterest: accumulator is not the registered authority"*. The refusal is that check and not a proof-verification failure — the accrual's own log line precedes it — and the positive endpoint succeeding in the same run is what rules out a blanket refusal. Before the fix that endpoint had nothing to compare and accepted. *What the residual is, and why the cell's own prescription does not close it.* This cell says the rate and the clock "need their own half on the host side (`base_rate` against the config, `time_elapsed` against a maintained `CDP_LAST_INTEREST_UPDATE_KEY`)". Read against the code, that prescription has two prerequisites it does not name and one hole it does not close. The prerequisites: **the configured rate is stored nowhere** — `init_contract` writes five config keys (`total_debt`, `total_collateral`, `accumulated_fees`, `last_interest_update`, `total_redeemed`) and no rate; `params.base_rate` is consumed into `InitializeParams` and never persisted; and **there is no clock to maintain** — `CDP_LAST_INTEREST_UPDATE_KEY` is written once to `0` (`:203`) and read nowhere. The hole: `interest = debt × rate × elapsed / DENOM`, so **a checked rate with a caller-chosen period is cosmetic** — the caller sets `time_elapsed` to whatever makes the interest it wants, which is the same freedom the row is about. And the period cannot simply be required to equal the host's own computation, because **the client cannot know the block's timestamp**: a transaction is built before the block it lands in, so `params.time_elapsed == now − last` is unsatisfiable by any honest client. The coherent alternative is therefore not a check but a change of who computes: the host derives the period from the chain clock (`wasm::util::get_blockchain_time`) and the maintained stamp, computes the interest itself with checked arithmetic, and carries both in the update — which makes the params' `rate_per_second`, `time_elapsed` and `interest_amount` inert or removable, i.e. **an interface change to `AccrueInterestParams`** and to its client, not a two-line hardening. That is a design decision this row records rather than takes, and it is the reason the clause stays unmet. *Severity `H` → `M` on the register's own bar*, which asks whether a path from an untrusted input to the consequence can be stated: after this change the accrual requires the secret behind the point registered at init, so the demonstrated harm path — *"any account can submit `AccrueInterestV1` with a proof it can make itself"* — is gone, and what remains is the authority writing debt the way its own `UpdateConfig` already lets it set the rate. *One observation in the same arm, recorded and not filed as a defect*: the arm still publishes a **constant** tx pair (`poseidon_hash([3, 0, 0])` and zero), which matches the circuit's derivation only because the client's `AccrueInterestCallData::new` leaves `tx_commitment` and `tx_nonce` at zero — the reviewed "left zero by convention" shape this register already names for `tx_nonce`, and consistent on both sides rather than a `OBL-C78` literal. *Residue this fix did not touch, in the same contract and named here so it is not read as closed*: `OBL-C83` (the mint/liquidate arms record commitments that bind nothing) and `OBL-C92` (the redeem arm writes the ledger down against a zero-value payout) are unchanged |
| OBL-C83 | **CLOSED** | A stablecoin position operation is bound to the position it acts on — the commitment the host records is the one the proof established, and an operation that consumes a position consumes it once | `mint_stable.zk:48,51,57,63`; `liquidate.zk:50,60` and `:42-44`; `stablecoin/src/entrypoint.rs:1116-1117`, `:1345`; `stablecoin/src/client/mint_stable.rs:47-53`; `stablecoin/src/model/mod.rs:498-505` | M | **Found 2026-09-23 in the same `OBL-Z18` slice, from the six non-resolving candidates that remain after `OBL-C82`, plus the two consumers their consequence runs into.** *The vacuity, measured on both sides.* In `mint_stable.zk` the exposed set is `nullifier_check` (`:43`), `tx_binding` (`:71`) and `tx_nonce` (`:72`); the four flagged equalities — `mint_check == new_debt` (`:48`), `old_collateral == new_collateral` (`:51`), `old_position == old_commitment` (`:57`), `new_position == new_commitment` (`:63`) — are each between two values that are neither exposed nor used anywhere else in the circuit, so each is satisfied by any witness. `liquidate.zk` is the same shape at `:50` and `:60`. `process_mint_stable_instruction` (`:1033-1108`) reads **neither field**, and `mint_stable.zk` contains no `less_than_or_equal` at all — unlike its four siblings, which each carry one (`open_position.zk:74`, `add_collateral.zk:80`, `remove_collateral.zk:80`, `repay_stable.zk:80`). *The same half-built model shows in liquidate's bookkeeping:* the nullifier that `liquidate.zk:42-44` exposes is consumed by nobody — `process_liquidate_instruction` never reads it and `apply_liquidate_update` (`:1345`) keys the liquidation record on `debt_covered.to_le_bytes()`, so two liquidations of the same size collide on one key and neither is marked spent. *Why M and not higher, stated as the read that is missing rather than as an assurance.* The unbound commitment and the absent ratio check are read on both sides, but what settles their consequence is the child call: both arms act through `promissory_note::transfer_v1`, and whether that transfer's **inputs** fund the mint or the liquidation is the half this slice did not read — so the value path is unranked and the severity is held at M. The next read is named: the PN transfer's input side for `MintStableV1` and `LiquidateV1`. These sites also sit inside the known V1-host/V2-circuit divergence (`v1-vs-v2-client-hazard`): the mint client documents a `BurnV1`/`MintV1` flow (`client/mint_stable.rs:26-33`) while the host implements `transfer_v1`, so the pair is mid-migration and part of this row's repair belongs to that work rather than beside it **The named read is done, and it holds this row at M.** Both arms act through `promissory_note::transfer_v1`, and that child cannot fund either operation with anything but notes the transaction already holds: every input carries a `Revoke_V2` proof (`promissory_note/proof/revoke.zk:47-72`, nullifier derived from the input's own `spend_secret`, merkle inclusion constrained) and `verify_value_conservation` (`promissory_note/src/entrypoint/mod.rs:516-557`) forces the output `token_commit` set to equal the input set with equal Pedersen sums. So an unbound `mint_commitment` or an absent ratio check cannot be turned into created value *through this contract's own arms* — the value path is bounded one layer down, which is why the severity does not move up. What remains true is everything the row already states and does not depend on that read: the host records a commitment it compares to nothing (`:1116-1117`), the four `mint_stable.zk` equalities are vacuous, `mint_stable.zk` carries no `less_than_or_equal` while its four siblings each do, and the liquidation record keys on `debt_covered.to_le_bytes()` so two liquidations of one size collide and neither marks the exposed nullifier spent (`:1345`). The read did produce one thing this row did not have: the same pass over the redeem arm found the ledger decremented against a payout that is zero-value by circuit, filed separately as `OBL-C92`, because that proposition — a redemption releases collateral — is not this one **CLOSED 2026-09-24: both clauses hold, and the repair began with a measurement that changed what "the position it acts on" could mean.** *The finding the row did not have, and which the row's own prescription presupposed.* The remedy this cell named — expose `derived_owner`-style values and bind them host-side — cannot be built until the two arms named agree on what a position commitment *is*, and they did not. Measured by reading every position hash in the contract's ten circuits: `open_position.zk`, `add_collateral.zk`, `remove_collateral.zk` and `repay_stable.zk` derive `poseidon_hash(DOMAIN_COMMITMENT, collateral_commit, debt_commit, owner_pub, collateral_type)` — arity **5** — while `mint_stable.zk` and `liquidate.zk` derive the same concept at arity **4**, without `collateral_type`. So the commitment `OpenPositionV1` registers in the positions tree could never be the one a mint or a liquidation acts on: the two arms computed a different number for the same position, and the host's `db_contains_key` would have refused every honest mint even after the exposes landed. The repair is therefore one derivation, not two: both circuits gained the `collateral_type` witness and the fifth argument, and both clients with them. *Clause 1 — the recorded commitment is the proven one.* `mint_stable.zk` now instances `old_position` and `new_position` (on the derived expressions, the shape `open_position.zk` uses for `position_check`, so the checker classifies both **derived** rather than merely bound: `check-circuit-instance-derivation.sh` moves from `3  derived:2, redundant:1` to `5  derived:4, redundant:1` for each of the two, measured), and the host joins them to the call's own fields in `mint_stable_binding` / `liquidate_binding`, enforcing the circuit's instance count first so the indexing is total. `apply_mint_stable_update` now writes the **proven** `new_commitment` rather than re-reading `params.mint_commitment`, so there is one reading and not two. *Clause 2 — consumed once.* `apply_mint_stable_update` and `apply_liquidate_update` mark the exposed nullifier spent in `STABLECOIN_CONTRACT_POSITION_NULLIFIERS_TREE` — the tree `remove_collateral` and `repay_stable` already used for exactly this, and which the mint and liquidation arms alone never wrote — and the exec phase requires the position to be a registered one (`positions_db` contains it) and its nullifier unspent. The nullifier is `H(DOMAIN_NULLIFIER, owner_secret, commitment)`, the derivation `open_position.zk` instances, and `old_position` includes `owner_pub`, so one nullifier marks one position consumed for good. The liquidation record is re-keyed on that nullifier; it had been keyed on `debt_covered.to_le_bytes()`, a value two liquidations of the same size share. `MintStableUpdateV1` and `LiquidateUpdateV1` carry the nullifier (and the mint the consumed commitment) so `apply` can act on it — layouts 48→112 and 40→72 bytes; stablecoin is not genesis, so nothing moves the pin. *Controls, and the first run is worth recording because it failed.* `test_heavyweight_stablecoin` **ok, 700.86s**. Clause 2 is asserted on chain state, not inferred: the positive mint's `verify_state` recomputes `H(1, sk, position_commitment)` and hard-fails unless `position_nullifiers` holds it — a query `apply_mint_stable_update` could not have satisfied before this change. Clause 1's control is a negative endpoint, `MintStableV1MismatchedCommitment`, which is the positive in every respect but one: the commitment recorded in `MintStableParams` is not the `new_commitment` the proof established, while the proof, the public-input vector and the child transfer's blind seed (derived from the *mismatched* commitment) are all self-consistent, so every check downstream of the join would accept it. Measured: `Block 9 rejected … call_idx=1`, the child `[promissory_note::transfer_v1] Transfer valid` and executed, and the parent's only failure `[stablecoin::MintStable] Error: recorded commitment 0x28ad65cc… is not the proven one 0x28…` (`ContractMismatch`, `Custom(17…)`) — then `Block 9 at height 9 committed` with the positive mint consuming the same position and the same note. **The first run placed that endpoint *after* the positive and the control was a lie the test accepted**: the negative was refused, `submit_result.is_err()` held, and the stated cause was the child's `Nullifier already spent for input 0` — the positive had already spent the shared note, so the endpoint was turned away a step earlier, for a reason it does not name. The placement was corrected and the run repeated; the endpoints are now ordered negative-then-positive, and exactly one of the two can be accepted. *Residuals, stated rather than implied.* (i) **The liquidation arm has no end-to-end control**, and this is a fixture gap rather than an untested read: the host's pool gate needs `collateral_ratio < CDP_LIQ_THRESHOLD` (13000), the fixture's pool is 10000 collateral against 0 debt before the mint, and every note it can spend sums to 6500 — a ratio of 15384, never below the threshold. So the liquidate arm's join and single-consumption are read, structurally gated, and pinned at the codec level, but never exercised on chain. (ii) The `mint_stable.zk` collateralization claim is a *different proposition* and is filed as `OBL-C119`, not folded in here. (iii) Observed and not filed: `position_root` in both circuits and `liquidation_penalty` in `liquidate.zk` are declared witnesses that no constraint references. (iv) `RepayStableBuilder` was deleted — it returned `MintStableCallData` with a zero `old_commitment` and `mint_amount = 0`, an unused builder for the wrong circuit, and `phantom-code-removed-first` asks for that to go before the question is asked. The other three builders (`OpenPositionBuilder`, `AddCollateralBuilder`, `RemoveCollateralBuilder`) are the same class of unused wallet grammar in a contract crate and are left for their own row. (v) `bin/gen_init_params.rs` did not compile — `InitializeParams` gained the `tx_binding`/`tx_nonce` pair in `OBL-C78` and this tool was not carried with it, so `cargo check -p dwow_stablecoin_contract` was failing on a target before this work began; it is fixed here with the zero-pair binding `client/initialize.rs` derives for the same no-transaction case |
| OBL-C85 | **ACCEPTED-WITH-REASON** | The per-uncle Pedersen audit commitment `C_uncle_i = u_i·G_v + r_i·G_r` exists where the spec says it does — as EC points in the **stored cumulative supply chain** — so the subtractive mass balance `C_effective + Σ C_uncle_i = C_base` is auditable from durable state | `uncle_merkle.md` §Coinbase Split ("Pedersen audit commitments"); found by `OBL-C26`'s deletion | M | **Found 2026-09-23 while deciding what to do with `OBL-C26`'s dead cache: the spec's storage location for these points is not implemented anywhere. The disposition — accepted with reason, no `CumulativeSupplyEntry` change and no write path — is at the end of this cell.** Measured, both sides: `uncle_merkle.md:437-442` says the audit commitments are *"EC points in the stored cumulative supply chain (`blockchain.get_cumulative_supply`)"* and that they are **not spendable** — "no nullifier, no note, no merkle path attaches to them" — while `CumulativeSupplyEntry` (`src/linear/src/supply_chain.rs`) carries exactly one **aggregate** `value_commit` per height plus a plaintext `total_supply`, and `verify_uncle_split` checks the split as **integer arithmetic** (`canonical + Σ pin == base`), not as a commitment equation. So the *mass balance* is enforced on the plaintext amounts while the per-uncle points that the spec calls the audit trail are materialised nowhere durable: the only code that ever computed them was the in-memory `uncle_commitment_set` deleted under `OBL-C26`, and that was never read and never persisted. Severity M and not C: nothing here mints or destroys value — the split itself is enforced — the gap is that a property the spec states is auditable from stored state is not. **Not fixed here and not deferred silently**: fixing it means adding per-uncle points to the stored entry, i.e. a `CumulativeSupplyEntry` format change and the write path that fills it, which is a design change to stored state rather than a repair, and it belongs with whoever decides whether the audit trail is per-uncle or aggregate. Until then the honest reading of `get_cumulative_supply` is "the aggregate chain, reconciled against the schedule by `OBL-C45`" — not "the per-uncle audit commitments the spec describes" **RESTATED 2026-09-23: the first clause is false, the second is true by a mechanism the spec names two sections later, and the repair is a specification correction rather than a stored-format change — which is a smaller outcome than this row assumed and is reached by reading the spec's other half rather than by deciding the property is unachievable.** *What is false:* `uncle_merkle.md` §Two-commitment distinction said the points "are EC points in the stored cumulative supply chain (`blockchain.get_cumulative_supply`)", and the file's own status table claimed "Pedersen uncle commitment precompute — Implemented — `chain_state.rs::connect_block()` pre-computes `C_uncle_i = pedersen_commitment_u64(u_i, Blind(r_i))`" and "Deterministic uncle blind `r_i` — Implemented". Both are wrong: `CumulativeSupplyEntry` carries one aggregate `value_commit` and a plaintext `total_supply`, `verify_uncle_split` checks `canonical + Σ pin == base` as integers, and `from_uniform_bytes` has **no occurrence in `src/linear/src/`** — the formula has no implementation at all, not merely no storage. Three claims corrected in that file. *What is true, and this is the restatement:* §Audit Compatibility says the split "is auditable because `r_i` is deterministic — an auditor can recompute every `C_uncle_i` and verify `C_effective + sum(C_uncle_i) == C_base` for every block independently." Every input to that recomputation is durable: `uncle_hash = blake3(to_mining_blob())` from the uncle's stored header, `u_i = pin_confirmed_i` persisted in `UncleBlock` (`block.rs:184-200`), `H` the canonical height, and `C_base` the cumulative supply chain's own `value_commit`. So the property the row asks for — *auditable from durable state* — **holds**, by recomputation rather than by stored points, and the equation's own direction makes storage redundant: `C_effective := C_base − Σ C_uncle_i` is satisfied by construction, so a stored copy could only be tamper-evidence for a tautology, while a *recomputed* copy binds `u_i` to the uncle identity and height that produced it. *The decision, made rather than left open:* no `CumulativeSupplyEntry` format change and no write path. That would have been stored state added to carry a value the chain does not consult, in service of an audit that already works from what the chain does store. The three false spec claims were corrected instead, and `uncle_merkle.md` now says plainly that nothing computes `r_i` today. Severity stays M: the gap was never that value could be minted or destroyed — the split is enforced — it was that a stated audit path described storage that did not exist, and a specification that overstates its own implementation is exactly what this register exists to find. **Mechanized as this row's own reading, 2026-09-24.** The conclusion measured here — the split is enforced as **integer arithmetic** (`canonical + Σ pin == base`), *not* as a commitment equation — is now a Lean predicate: `Consensus/CoinbaseSplit.lean` models exactly the five plaintext equations and no Pedersen points anywhere, so the enforcement the audit trail is *not* is visible in the model's type rather than only in this cell. Its module note states the residual in this row's terms, that `uncle_merkle.md`'s `C_uncle_i = u_i·G_v + r_i·G_r` is materialised nowhere, and the model does not pretend otherwise. |
| OBL-C86 | **CLOSED** | A record field named for the holder carries the holder | `identity/src/entrypoint.rs:681`; found while re-reading `OBL-Z17` | M | **NEW 2026-09-23, split out of `OBL-Z17`'s re-read rather than left inside it, because it is a different proposition.** The verify path builds `VerifyCapabilityUpdateV1 { capability_id, holder_pub: params.capability_proof.issuer_pub, verified: true }` — the **issuer's** key under a field named `holder_pub`. Both sides read: the circuit carries `holder_pub_x/y` as witnesses that enter `credential_data` (`verify_capability.zk:80-90`) and attaches them to the credential, but **does not expose them** — correctly, since disclosing the holder to a verifier is the privacy break the whole check is built to avoid — while the stored `Credential` also carries a `holder_pub` (`model/mod.rs:245-254`) that the verify path never compares against anything. So the update states one principal and names another. **Why M and not L**: nothing reads it *today* — `apply_verify_capability_update` writes no state (`entrypoint.rs:689-692`) and no consumer contract reads a child call's return data (they check the call's byte, its target contract id, and their own proof's emptiness) — so there is no demonstrated harm, and this register does not rank an undemonstrated one. What it is, is an authorization datum in the primitive that gates every genesis contract's auth, labelled as its own opposite: the first consumer to trust `update.holder_pub` would learn the issuer. **The repair is one line and it is decided rather than offered**: `holder_pub: credential.holder_pub` — the value the name declares, read from the stored record the function has already loaded and whose commitment the proof was just required to match (`:635-638`). Disclosure to the caller is not the privacy break the circuit avoids: the update is what `process_update` returns to the caller that produced the proof, `apply` persists nothing (`:689-692`), and the circuit's own choice not to expose the holder is what keeps the *verifier* from learning it. Renaming the field instead was rejected because the holder is the datum the record exists to report. It is in a genesis contract, so it lands with the batched re-roll rather than as a drive-by edit **CLOSED 2026-09-23 — one line, `holder_pub: credential.holder_pub`, landed in the batch with `OBL-C62` and `OBL-Z19`.** The record it reads from is the one the function had already loaded and whose commitment the proof was just required to match, so no new lookup and no new failure mode. Verified by the contract rebuilding clean (`make -C src/contract/identity clean && make all`) and by `scripts/check-artifact-freshness.sh` → 32 artefacts fresh. What is **not** claimed: that the field is now read by anyone — it still is not, which is why this closed as M and not as a behaviour change. `identity` is genesis contract 4, so the fix moved the pin in the same re-roll. **And the contract was re-run end to end rather than only rebuilt**: `DWOW_TEST_LOGS=1 RUST_MIN_STACK=67108864 cargo test --release -p dwowd --all-features test_heavyweight_identity -- --test-threads 4` → **1 passed, 215.72s**, which drives issue-credential, register-capability, issue-capability and verify-capability through the real client against the real circuits. The field this row is about is not asserted by that test — nothing reads it, which is the row's own point — so what the run establishes is that the change is not a regression, not that the value is observed. **Re-measured on a later tree: 1 passed, 225.16s**, after a concurrent session's `src/sdk` edit — see `OBL-Z19`'s note on why a green figure carries the tree it was taken on |

It is the *test's own* error string (`purse_spec.rs:77`), so the recorded "cause" was a post-condition failing with no stated cause anywhere, exactly the shape `OBL-C76`'s note describes. **Both sides printed, and the spec was the wrong one.** The producer is `harness/purse.rs:89-91`: the withdraw proof is built with `os = 42` and `sn = 1`, so `nf = poseidon_hash([dnl=1, os=42, pid=1, sn=1])`, and `apply` writes exactly that — `entrypoint/mod.rs:215-216`, `db_mark_spent(ndb, &u.nullifier.to_bytes())`, where `Nullifier::to_bytes()` is `to_repr()` and `from_bytes` applies no domain hash, so the key is the value. Both figures are kept so the green one carries the tree it was taken on | L |

| OBL-C84 | **CLOSED** | A capability a circuit issues authorizes the access it names: the capability is one the verifier receives, and a caller cannot produce one for a subscription it does not hold | `subscription/proof/verify_access.zk:33,34,47`; `subscription/src/entrypoint.rs:156-162`, `:230-233`, `:273-277` | M | **Found 2026-09-23 in `OBL-Z18`'s `subscription` slice, and it settles the doubt that row had recorded about this exact site — it said the site "needs re-adjudicating with the credential lifecycle traced", and the lifecycle turns out to be empty, which is why the trace is short.** *The exposure set, measured on both sides.* `VerifyAccessV2` instances `tx_binding` and `tx_nonce` and nothing else (`:44-46`). `expected_capability` — the value the circuit is named for — is never a `constrain_instance` target, and the host does not publish it either: the VerifyAccessV1 arm of `get_metadata` pushes `vec![pallas::Base::zero(), pallas::Base::zero()]` with the comment `// VerifyAccessV2 circuit: [tx_binding, tx_nonce]` (`entrypoint.rs:156-162`), so what a verifier would compare a capability against is a literal zero rather than a value. The host adds nothing in either phase: exec logs `VerifyAccessV1 has no state update` and returns `vec![]` (`:230-233`), update logs the same and returns `Ok(())` (`:273-277`). There is no stored-record read, no nullifier, no comparison. *So the constraint states nothing.* `constrain_equal_base(derived_capability, expected_capability)` (`:47`) is an equality between two prover-chosen values, and `:33-34` is the same for the pubkey pair the capability hashes into (`derived_capability = poseidon_hash(DOMAIN_COMMITMENT, subscriber_pub_x, subscriber_pub_y, plan_id, subscription_id, lock_until_block, nonce)`, `:36-43`). Any caller can produce a valid `VerifyAccessV1` proof, for any capability it chooses, with no subscription at all. This is `OBL-Z16`'s class — a policy the prover can parametrise away — but it is not a `Z16` instance and is filed separately for the reason `OBL-C81` is: `Z16` is the *detector's* residue over unclassified `constrain_instance` sites, and a value that is never instanced is invisible to that detector. *Why M and not H, and both reasons are measured.* First, nothing in this tree consumes the predicate: the host performs no check and no state update, and the value a consumer would need is not published (it is a zero in the metadata), so even an off-chain consumer could not learn what to compare against. Second, the arm as written cannot verify at all — the metadata's `[0, 0]` against the circuit's `[poseidon_hash(3, tx_commitment, tx_nonce), tx_nonce]`, which is `OBL-C78`'s and `OBL-Z2`'s class — and a gate that does not open is not a live hole. Both reasons are `OBL-Z14`'s shape, attribution without value. *The ordering constraint is why this is filed now rather than after that repair*: fixing `OBL-C78` makes this arm verify again, and the first proof it would then verify is one any caller can produce. So the two must land together, this one first or with it. **CLOSED 2026-09-24: the capability is an instance, the host compares it against the record, and access is then more than ownership — with the end-to-end replay blocked by a fixture that cannot run, stated below rather than left implied.** *The repair is the row's own two halves, in that order.* (i) `verify_access.zk` gains `constrain_instance(derived_capability)`, so the capability is a value the verifier receives. The circuit's three equalities related witnesses to witnesses only — the secret, the plan, the id, the expiry and the nonce were all the prover's — so the proof said "there exists a subscription this caller can open", which is true for every caller about every subscription. (ii) The exec arm, which read *"No state update needed - just verification"* and verified nothing, now recomputes the capability from the **stored record** (`Subscription::access_capability`, delegating to the free `model::access_capability` so there is one derivation) with the nonce the call carries, and refuses unless it equals the published value. Because the circuit's instance *is* the hash of its witnesses, a match forces those witnesses to be the record's own subscriber key, plan, id and expiry — which is what "a subscription it does not hold" means, made checkable. *Access is then more than ownership*: `Subscription::access_is_live` requires the record to be `Active` and inside its lock, so a cancelled or expired subscription authorizes nothing, and the arm's failure codes say which half refused. The metadata arm publishes `[tx_binding, tx_nonce, capability]` — the capability from the call, which is what the circuit instances, since the arm can publish only what the call carries (`OBL-C78`). The client's `to_vec()` returned `[tx_binding, tx_nonce]` under a comment saying the circuit had no instances at all; it kept returning two values after the circuit gained them, and returns three now. *Controls.* The model test `access_capability_binds_the_record_and_liveness_is_a_boundary` pins what the chain run cannot reach — that the id, the plan, the expiry and the nonce each change the capability, and that the lock is exclusive at its boundary and a cancelled record is never live. The binding gate's two remaining candidates for this file are re-adjudicated as arm (b) transitive (the exposure is carried by `subscriber_pub_x/y` being inputs to the instanced capability), and the third entry — `derived_capability == expected_capability` — is **removed** because the site is no longer a candidate at all. *And the end-to-end control is written and cannot run*, which is this row's honest residual: the spec's `VerifyAccessV1` now presents the capability the *record* derives (plan 1, expiry 200, nonce 1 — the values `SubscribeV1` writes) and `verify_access_wrong_plan` is the negative half, a **real** capability derived exactly as the circuit derives it, with every witness agreeing, for plan 2 instead of the record's plan 1 — so the proof verifies and only the host's comparison can refuse it. Neither runs, because the subscription heavyweight test fails before them: see `OBL-C104`. The unit controls above are what this row rests on today. |
