| OBL-C129 | **FIXED** | A client's witness vector supplies the value its circuit constrains, not a placeholder | `tender/src/client/{create_tender,submit_bid,reveal_bid,submit_bid_with_capability}.rs` | found 2026-09-24 by the first full workspace test run | M | **NEW 2026-09-24, and it is four clients rather than the one a test named.** `create_tender.zk:72` *assigns* `tx_binding = poseidon_hash(DOMAIN_TX_BINDING, tx_commitment, tx_nonce)`, and because `tx_binding` is a **declared witness** the compiler emits an equality constraint on that witness cell rather than shadowing it (`src/zkas/compiler.rs:207-224`, the `CRIT-1 Option B` path) — so the witness must *equal* the hash. Four tender clients passed `pallas::Base::zero()` there (`create_tender.rs:85`, `submit_bid.rs:121`, `reveal_bid.rs:124`, `submit_bid_with_capability.rs:144`), and `poseidon_hash(3, ·, ·)` is never zero for any input, so each proof was unsatisfiable: `Proof::create` still returned bytes — the prover commits whatever instance and witness values it is given — and verification returned `InvalidProof` for every honest caller. **The consequence is that the operation could not be performed at all**, not merely that it verified wrongly, which is why the severity is `M` as a *liveness* break rather than a value one. The sequence that produced it: `6d308e3b8f` (2026-09-23) repaired the public-input *side* of all five tender clients and left these witnesses at zero; `96d3046a0f` (2026-09-24) repaired `select_winner`'s alone, and that file's comment (`select_winner.rs:110-116`) is where the reasoning and the exact fix already lived — so this row's repair is one line copied four times. **Found by a test run and by nothing else**: `cargo check`, all fifteen static gates and both circuit gates passed over it, because an unsatisfiable witness is not a type error. Verified by the run's own line afterwards: `client_proof_self_verification` **3 passed, 0 failed** (was 1 passed; 2 failed). `tender` is not one of the genesis nine, so the rebuild moved no pin |
| OBL-C130 | **PARTLY** | The wallet selects a capability by `ContractId` **and barbs** — §6.4.1's invariant 2 — and it does not: the write path filters by asset id and contract only | `bin/dww/src/dispatch.rs`, `bin/dww/src/lib.rs` (measured: no `Barb::`, no `required_barbs`, no `covers(` in either); `scripts/check-wallet-kernel.sh` (the declared absence) | `scripts/check-wallet-kernel.sh`, which fails if the absence expires | M | **NEW 2026-09-24, found while correcting `wallet.md`'s status bullets and then mechanized rather than left as prose.** §6.4.1's invariant 2 reads "*the selected capability is filtered by `ContractId` + barbs, never `caps[0]`*", and §6.2's rule is the same requirement stated at the read path — with `Capability/Selection.lean` modelling it and `contrib/capability_type_diff.sh` checking the type-construction side. The **call site** is a different matter: measured 2026-09-24, `dispatch.rs` and `lib.rs` contain no barb predicate at all, so a capability whose type does not cover the action's required barbs is selectable on the write path provided its asset id matches. **This is a gap rather than a crash**, which is why `PARTLY` and not `FAILS`: the *barbs* the action requires are declared in the manifest (that is `OBL-C74`), the selection does filter by contract, and the path's own tests pass — but the invariant as written is not implemented, and nothing else in the tree noticed. **Mechanized rather than narrated**: `scripts/check-wallet-kernel.sh` declares the absence per file *with its reason* and fails if it expires (a `Barb::` appearing in either file means the declaration must be updated or removed) and if a **second bespoke scan path** appears — §6.4's "exactly one bespoke citizen" and §9's "a second bespoke path SHALL be rejected", which had no instrument either. Falsified in three directions on a copy under `/tmp` before landing: a second bespoke path, the declared absence expiring, and a declared path renamed. Both statements of the rule (`wallet.md:832`, `:1168-1205`) are prose; this is the check. |
| OBL-C131 | **OPEN** | A deploy that carries initialization parameters decodes the payload the deploy path actually delivers — the parameters reach `__initialize` and are used, or the call refuses for a stated reason | `src/contract/dex/src/entrypoint/mod.rs:149-157` (the `decode(&ix[1..])`), `src/contract/dex/src/model/mod.rs:77` (the strict 37-byte guard and the 37-byte encoder, both on that one line), `src/serial/src/lib.rs:60-66` (`serialize` adds no length prefix) | the three runs below, each read from its own `test result:` line, logs at `/tmp/dex-repro.log`, `/tmp/pipe-stable.log`, `/tmp/deploy-all.log` | M | **MEASURED 2026-09-25, and it is the first cause ever recorded for a red this register has carried by name only since 2026-09-20 — the entry that says in as many words "do not read a cause into them, and re-run rather than infer".** `test_pipeline` (`bin/dwowd/src/tests/pipeline.rs:412`), the lightweight pipeline's one-contract deploy, fails reproducibly in 53.81s with `Error: Custom("accept_block deploy: DeployV1 init: Contract execution failed: IO error: [dex::init_contract] Initializing DEX contract")` — the reproduction is one targeted run, not a suite. **The mechanism, both sides printed before the claim.** The test builds `InitializeParams { timeout: 100, fee: 0, transparency_config: default }` (`pipeline.rs:228-236`) and encodes it with `dwow_serial::serialize`, which writes the body and **no length prefix** (`src/serial/src/lib.rs:60-66`); dex's encoder writes exactly **37** bytes (`model/mod.rs:77`); the contract then calls `InitializeParams::decode(&ix[1..])`, dropping a byte nobody added, and dex's guard is strict — `if data.len() != 37` — so 36 bytes is a decode error. **The wrapper hides exactly that**: the guard's message would read "expected 37 bytes, got 36", but the call site maps every decode failure to `IoError("Decode error")`, and the host renders the failure with the contract's *last log line*, which is why the surface reads `IO error: [dex::init_contract] Initializing DEX contract` — the message the reader gets was printed *before* the decode ran. **The discriminator that fixes the direction of the repair**: the same test, same deploy path, with `CONTRACT_NAME=stablecoin` — a contract whose init decodes `ix` with no strip (`stablecoin/src/entrypoint.rs:163`) — **passes** ("Deployed stablecoin at [225, 200, 37, …]", 64.05s), and the host's call is verbatim by construction (`src/runtime/vm_runtime.rs:875`, `self.call(ContractSection::Deploy, payload)`). So the byte is dex's to remove from its own decode, not the caller's to add. **Not repaired here, deliberately, and the reason is not the difficulty**: removing `[1..]` is one line, but which framing is authoritative is shared with the heavy pipeline — `bin/dwowd/src/tests/heavyweight_pipeline.rs:244` deploys dex through a different in-process path — so the change belongs beside a run of `test_heavyweight_dex`, not beside a measurement of this row. `dex` is not among the genesis nine, so either way it moves one `.source_hash` and no pin |
| OBL-C132 | **OPEN** | A test asserts what its name says it verifies — a batch check that collects failures, prints them and then returns `Ok(())` cannot report a failed batch | `bin/dwowd/src/tests/pipeline.rs:426-490` (`test_all_contracts_deploy`: `failed.push((name, …))` inside the loop, `Ok::<_, dwow_core::Error>(())` after it, then a `println!` of the failures) | the run below, read from its own output: `Deployed 30/32 contracts (2 failed)` beside `test result: ok. 1 passed; 0 failed` | M | **MEASURED 2026-09-25, found because it and `OBL-C131` disagree about the same call — and the dishonest one is the one that passes.** `test_all_contracts_deploy` performs the same `ensure_ready_and_deploy()`, hence the same `deploy()` and the same `build_init_params()`, over all 32 contracts, and its own output names two failures: `FAILED: dex — accept_block deploy: DeployV1 init: Contract execution failed: IO error: [dex::init_contract] Initializing DEX contract` (exactly `OBL-C131`, character for character) and `FAILED: deployooor — Block at height 2 is 9209523 bytes — within 1% of MAX_BLOCK_SIZE 4194304 (soft limit 4152361)`. It then returns `Ok(())`, so the suite records `ok` for a run that deployed 30 of 32 contracts, and that `ok` is evidence about neither dex nor the deployooor. **The second failure earns its own mention for a second reason**: a 9209523-byte block is not "within 1%" of 4194304 — it is more than twice the limit — so that sentence's arithmetic contradicts its own numbers, and a reader who trusts the wording learns the opposite of what happened. **Why this is a row and not a bug report**: a check that cannot fail is not a check, which is the class this campaign's instruments exist for; it is also what kept `OBL-C131` invisible to every suite run before today. The fix is a decision — `panic!` on a non-empty `failed`, or return `Err`, or assert on the collected list — not a line that is obviously right |
| OBL-C103 | **OPEN** | A `dao_escrow` builder's params are the params the contract decodes | `src/contract/dao_escrow/src/client/mod.rs:134` (`InitializeParams`), `:190` (`PayPremiumParams`) against `model/mod.rs`'s `InitializeParamsV1`, `PayPremiumParamsV1`; `script/client_params_alignment_exceptions.txt` | found 2026-09-24 by the gate written for `OBL-C99`, which measured the class instead of the row | M | **NEW 2026-09-24: the same defect as `OBL-C99`'s three, in the one contract whose client is being rewritten in flight.** `InitializeBuilder::build()` returns a two-field `InitializeParams` (`owner_pubkey`, `gov_asset_id`) beside the model's `InitializeParamsV1`, which also carries `instance_seed`, `dao_bulla`, `endowment_asset_id` and `bulla_blind`; `PayPremiumBuilder::build()` returns a four-field `PayPremiumParams` (`dao_escrow_bulla`, `value`, `asset_id`, `period`) beside `PayPremiumParamsV1`, which also carries the membership note, the Pedersen value commitment and the member's exit fields. Neither client type has a codec, so neither *encodes* a wrong call — they are the shape the codec defect grows from, and a wallet following them cannot build a decodable call at all. *Not repaired here, deliberately*: all six files of that client are modified in the working tree by the session rewriting them for the V1-vs-V2 client hazard, and an edit from here would land on top of that work. Declared instead in the gate's rule-(A) exception list, which prints them as EXCEPTED with this row's ID on every run — a ratchet, not an amnesty, since a *new* shadowing site still fails and a stale entry is exit 2. When that rework lands the two types should go the way `OBL-C99`'s did — deleted in favour of the model's — and the exception lines with them. |
| OBL-C133 | **OPEN** | A commit that passes its own artifact gate is a commit whose artifacts match the sources it carries — a clean checkout of it can build and test | `src/contract/baccarat/.source_hash`, `src/contract/dao_escrow/.source_hash`, `src/contract/insurance_market/.source_hash` — all three committed and unmodified in the working tree, checked against the sources those commits carry; enforced by `check-source-hash` in every contract Makefile (`src/contract/dex/Makefile:70`, which exits 1) and reached by `make test` through its `contracts` prerequisite (`Makefile:201`) | 2026-09-25, the same gate in both directions: it exits 0 in the working tree (`PASS: 32 artifacts fresh`) and exits 1 on the sources of `06e0a41617` extracted with `git archive --format=tar`, where it prints `STALE (3 of 32)` and names baccarat, dao_escrow and insurance_market | H | **MEASURED 2026-09-25 — the first finding of the thorough-test campaign, and it is about the commit rather than about the code.** The three `.source_hash` files are committed and carry no uncommitted edit; the three contracts nonetheless have uncommitted source edits; and those edits are inside each contract's `SOURCE_MANIFEST` (`WASM_SRC = ... $(shell find src -type f -name '*.rs')` plus `manifest.toml`, `src/contract/dao_escrow/Makefile:56-69`). So the committed hashes were computed over the *working tree*, and no commit can satisfy them — which is why the gate's own advice (`make -C src/contract/<name> clean all`) does not apply here: nothing is stale in the tree, the **commit** is. **The consequence is total, not partial.** A fresh clone of HEAD fails gate 1, and `make test` — the umbrella's gate 23 — dies inside its `contracts` prerequisite before a single test executes, with the bare `WARNING: ... is stale` that `check-artifact-freshness.sh:1-11` was written to replace. Stated the other way round, so it cannot be misread as a complaint about a dirty tree: **the suite is green today only because uncommitted work is present.** Every verdict this campaign publishes therefore carries the delta hash of the tree it was taken on, and the campaign's own HEAD-runnability claim is the two-command instrument above rather than an inference. Filed and not repaired: the dirty sources are another session's in-flight rework (`dao_escrow/src/client/*.rs`, `insurance_market/src/{lib.rs,entrypoint/*}`), so refreshing the hashes would either commit that work or attest artifacts built from it — the repair belongs to the session that owns the changes, and the defect is the act of committing the hashes without the sources |
| OBL-C134 | **ACCEPTED-WITH-REASON** | The plaintext status of native_token's three consensus functions (`PoWRewardV1` 0x05, `FeeCollectV1` 0x06, `UncleMintV1` 0x07) is stated where each is tabulated, and no text still presents the deleted ZK-gated coinbase as current | `src/contract/native_token/src/lib.rs:45-66`; `src/contract/native_token/README.md:18-40`; `doc/src/contract/native_token.md:20-32`; `doc/src/dev/contracts/native_token.md:350-390`; `doc/src/arch/circuit-versioning.md` (§"When a Function Stops Requiring a Proof", new); `src/contract/native_token/src/client/transfer/mod.rs:26`; `doc/src/arch/sync.md:221`; `doc/src/arch/composition.md:198`; `doc/src/arch/consensus/fee-spec.md:56,71,1624`; `doc/src/arch/contract-wasm-type-system.md:1320`; `doc/src/dev/testing/test-audit.md:93`; `contrib/model/native_token_zk_audit_2026-06-07.md`; `sim/tests/test_zk_hardening.py` | `bash scripts/check-doc-index.sh` — index and link integrity only, it cannot see this claim; the sweep itself, read 2026-09-25 | M | **MEASURED 2026-09-25. The premise was wrong in one half, and writing the wrong half would have made the docs lie.** `UncleReward` has **0** occurrences repo-wide and `git log -S 'UncleReward'` names no commit — it has never existed here; `PoWReward` (8 hits) and `FeeCollect` (46) are prose abbreviations only. The three functions are **live consensus code** — dispatched at `entrypoint/mod.rs:719-721`, declared in `manifest.toml`, chosen by the coinbase classifier — and **none is deprecated**; what was dropped is their **ZK proof requirement** (`Mint_V2` removed from the coinbase path in `b6bf44f79`, `FeeCollect_V2` dropped 2026-09), with no `.zk` source and no `.zk.bin` for any of them. The sweep therefore states *plaintext, no circuit, no proof* and never "deprecated", which would assert a removal path that does not exist. **Four statements were false rather than merely stale, and the fourth is the serious one.** (1) `client/transfer/mod.rs:26` claimed Transfer V1 "uses the same mint proof as PoWReward" — the coinbase carries no proof, and transfer proves with `Mint_V2`/`Burn_V2`. (2) `sync.md`'s usage example builds the coinbase with `PoWRewardCallBuilder` + a `Mint_V1` namespace + `block.zkbin_data` public inputs — three things that do not exist (`zkbin_data`: **0** occurrences under `src/` and `bin/`) — and it sat in a document whose banner covers only the *sync* API. (3) `composition.md:198` gave the coinbase's boundary as `public_inputs: [[u8;32];9]`, where the plaintext call has none and that shape now means the **nine genesis contract IDs** (`sdk/src/crypto/contract_id.rs:150`); both of its line citations had also drifted (`PoWRewardParamsV1` is at `model/mod.rs:496`, not 251-290; `CoinbaseTransaction` at `transaction.rs:244-264`, not 211-235). (4) **`fee-spec.md:1624` claimed "the fee amount is hidden behind a Pedersen commitment. Only the threshold is public" — a false privacy claim**, contradicting the same document's §1 ("there is no ZK proof hiding the fee amount … the fee amount is plaintext"): the document promised privacy the system does not provide, and it now states the public-fee trade-off instead. Also repaired: `contract-wasm-type-system.md:1320` listed `mint_v2` for native token with no note that the coinbase and uncle paths are plaintext; `test-audit.md:93` cited `test-harness/src/native_token.rs`, **which does not exist**; `contrib/model/native_token_zk_audit_2026-06-07.md` audited the `Mint_V1` circuit with no superseded marking; and `sim/tests/test_zk_hardening.py` was pinned to `Mint_V1`/`FeeV1` with **no script referencing `sim/`** (measured: 0 of `scripts/*.sh`), so those assertions cannot fail a gate. Two adjacent reward defects were fixed, both found by the sweep and neither about plaintext: `for-node-operators.md:36` said "Hard cap: 21,000,000 DRKW" where the spec says **not a cap** (perpetual 1%/annum tail emission), and `heavyweight-spec.md:153,203` said "Every block SHALL include FeeCollectV1 unconditionally" where the consensus rule is present **iff** the block has fee calls (`OBL-C117`) — the harness manufactures a non-zero fee (`coinbase_coordination.rs:132`) rather than collect on a zero-fee block, and the text now says which rule is which. **What is not enforced, by decision**: no gate asserts the plaintext status, so this proposition can drift again — recorded here rather than built, which leaves this row as the only instrument that would notice. **Owed, not done**: the integrator-facing SDK table (`src/sdk/src/contracts/native_token.rs:46-53`, which stops at 0x05, calls it "Block rewards for miners" and omits 0x06/0x07/0x08) is the stalest surface in the tree and needs an `src/sdk/**` edit, which stales every contract artifact — measured, **32 of 32** Makefiles hash `$(shell find ../../sdk -type f -name '*.rs')` — so it is gated on a 32-contract `make clean all`, a dwowd rebuild and a genesis-pin measurement, and on the peer session holding eight contract `.wasm` files dirty. And `FeeCollectV1`'s rejection arms (`entrypoint/mod.rs:1303-1357`: zero claim, total mismatch, duplicate commitment, token mismatch) still have **no test** — the coinbase and uncle locks exist (`tests/plaintext_rewards.rs`), the fee-collect lock does not |

| OBL-C136 | **OPEN** | A test that fails states its own cause, and a test that is green alone is green in its suite | `src/event_graph/tests.rs:175` (the DAG-count assertion inside `assert_dags`, reached from `eventgraph_propagation` at `:227` with `expected_len = 1`); the hook that swallows the message is the event-graph harness's own, which printed `Panic occurred: PanicHookInfo { payload: Any { .. }, location: Location { file: "src/event_graph/tests.rs", line: 175, column: 9 }, can_unwind: true }` and nothing else | 2026-09-25, T-B chunk B1 (`--workspace --exclude dwowd --exclude dwow-contract-test-harness -- --test-threads 4`): `test result: FAILED. 86 passed; 1 failed; 2 ignored`, one name in the failures list. Four green runs after: the test alone twice (`test result: ok. 1 passed`, 25.03 s and 25.02 s) and its whole binary twice (`test result: ok. 87 passed; 0 failed; 2 ignored`, 41.04 s and 41.03 s) | M | **MEASURED 2026-09-25 — a red that four re-runs could not reproduce, and whose log carries no cause.** The mechanism is a fixed wait rather than a race hidden in the code: `bootstrap_nodes` starts real TCP meshes and logs "Waiting 5s until all peers connect", sleeps 5 s, and `assert_dags` then requires every node's DAG to hold exactly one event. Under B1's concurrency — four test binaries, each running its own mesh, on a host at load ~15 — one node held a different count; alone, the same binary is green twice. **Two consequences.** First, B1 is otherwise identical to the 2026-09-25 baseline to the event: 1314 passed + 1 failed = 1315, 24 ignored, 112 binaries + 45 doctest suites, so this one test is the whole difference between the two measurements. Second, and the reason this is a row rather than a note: the failure printed **no assertion message anywhere** — the panic hook logs the `PanicHookInfo` struct instead of its payload, so `payload: Any { .. }` is all a reader gets and the cause must be recovered from the source line. A layer-1 failure in this tree is supposed to state its cause; this one structurally cannot. Not repaired here, deliberately: whether the suite should tolerate a loaded host is a decision about the test's contract (a longer wait, or a wait-until-consistent loop), and the payload loss is the sharper half |
| OBL-C135 | **OPEN** | The per-test verdict table is an index that can be aggregated into a whole-chunk tally | `contrib/test_verdicts.sh`'s `parse` mode — specifically the target tracking driven by a `Running` banner — read against a `--workspace`-scale log | 2026-09-25, one log read two ways: `parse` yields 1329 rows over **19 distinct target names** and only **57** `(target-summary)` rows, while that same log's own `test result:` lines number **157** (112 binaries + 45 doctest suites); summing the TSV's summaries gives 1166 passed where the log gives 1314 | L | **MEASURED 2026-09-25, and it caught this campaign's own arithmetic.** Two structural causes, neither a bug in the sense of a wrong line: the target is set only by a `Running` banner, so the 45 doctest suites (whose banner is `Doc-tests`, which carries no target) are attributed to whatever binary preceded them; and 33 contract crates each name their file `tests/integration.rs`, so the target column is not unique — **19 distinct strings cover 157 suites**. A column summed over that silently under-counts by 148 tests, and nothing in the output hints that rows are missing. The per-test rows themselves are sound (1329 of 1339) and the wrapper propagates the run's exit code faithfully (`test_verdicts: cargo exit=101`), so this is a lossy *aggregation*, not an instrument that cannot fail — which is why it is `L` and not `M`. The rule it leaves behind: **a chunk's tally is read from the log's own `test result:` lines, never summed from the TSV**, which is what this campaign now does |

| OBL-C137 | **OPEN** | A gate whose header says "Runs on every push" runs, and the tree knows its verdict | `contrib/ci/check_fee_guardrails.sh`, `contrib/ci/check_heavyweight_coverage.sh`, `contrib/ci/check_sync_conformance.sh`, `contrib/ci/scan_heavyweight_antipatterns.sh`, `contrib/ci/check_compiles.sh` — none is invoked by `scripts/run-all-tests.sh`, by `Makefile`, by `hooks/pre-commit`, or by any other script (a grep for `contrib/ci/` across `scripts/`, `Makefile`, `hooks/` and the top-level `contrib/*.sh` returns nothing) | 2026-09-25, the four non-cargo ones run by hand for the first time: `check_sync_conformance.sh` **exit 0**; `check_fee_guardrails.sh` **exit 1** — `[Check-12]` missing `ThresholdAmount`, `[Check-9]` `AccumulatorPoint`, `[Check-13]` `decrypt_fee_for_miner` returns `FeeAmount, not u64`; `check_heavyweight_coverage.sh` **exit 1** — `Covered: 17  Gaps: 38` over 55 genesis functions; `scan_heavyweight_antipatterns.sh` **exit 1** — 8 violations | M | **MEASURED 2026-09-25 — three of the five are red, and nothing has ever run them.** Each states its own provenance as a push-time gate, and the repository's one git hook runs `check-pubkey-binding.sh` instead. Whether each red is a stale assertion or a live defect is *not* claimed here and needs the spec: the fee checks name types a later migration may have renamed, and the coverage checker reports `accept_block=NO_ACCEPT_BLOCK` for **all nine** genesis contracts including the two it marks OK, which reads as a checker that predates the spec-engine runner rather than as nine simultaneous regressions. The row is filed for the part that does not depend on either reading: a gate nobody runs has a verdict nobody has seen, and this tree treats that as a defect class in its own right. Wiring them is a decision — two of the three would go red on the day they were wired |
| OBL-C138 | **OPEN** | A prohibited harness anti-pattern is either absent or declared, so the scanner's report is a verdict rather than noise | `src/contract/test-harness/src/harness/dex.rs:467-484` (`set_transparency_level`, `update_config`) and the same shape at `src/contract/test-harness/src/harness/insurance_market.rs:169-186`, against `heavyweight-spec.md` §4 as enforced by `contrib/ci/scan_heavyweight_antipatterns.sh`; the declaration the scanner cannot read is `bin/dwowd/src/tests/specs/dex_spec.rs:254` and `:258` — `mk_ep("SetTransparencyLevelV1", false, …)` and `mk_ep("UpdateConfigV1", false, …)`, whose second parameter is `is_zk` (`bin/dwowd/src/tests/specs/helpers.rs:6-18`, which fixes `expectation: EndpointExpectation::Success`) | 2026-09-25: 8 violations reported — `empty-witnesses` at `dex.rs:470,481` and `insurance_market.rs:169,183`, `empty-proof-stub` at `dex.rs:472,483` and `insurance_market.rs:171,185`. The dex pair are the entire bodies of two endpoints whose call data is `vec![0x06]` and `vec![0x05]` — the function byte and no parameters — over witnesses the harness never binds | M | **MEASURED 2026-09-25, and the row is the disagreement rather than a verdict.** The scanner reads `empty_witnesses` plus `Proof::create(&pk, …)` and reports "proves nothing", which is right about the proof; the spec reads the same two endpoints as `is_zk = false` with a `Success` expectation, which would license exactly that stub. One of the two is wrong, and which one is a decision this row does not take: if `is_zk = false` means "this endpoint carries no proof obligation", the scanner needs to read the declaration and the 8 violations collapse to 0; if it means only "the harness does not generate a real one", then two endpoints are asserted `Success` while being exercised with an empty payload and a vacuous proof — the `OBL-C88` class, where a green heavyweight test is green for a reason other than the property it names. `insurance_market.rs` is a peer-held file (`dao_escrow`/`insurance_market` are dirty in the working tree), so only the dex half is this campaign's to touch |
| OBL-C139 | **OPEN** | A genesis contract's heavyweight coverage is measured against the runner that exists | `contrib/ci/check_heavyweight_coverage.sh` — its contract list, its per-enum parsing and its `accept_block` detection — against `bin/dwowd/src/tests/heavyweight_pipeline.rs` and the `EndpointSpec` engine in `bin/dwowd/src/tests/modules/` | 2026-09-25: `Contracts: 9  Total functions: 55  Covered: 17  Gaps: 38`, with per-contract lines naming `oracle: 0/6`, `attestation: 1/14`, `identity: 2/8`, `native_token: 3/8`, `promissory_note: 2/6`, `multisig: 1/4`, `deployooor: 1/2`; and `accept_block=NO_ACCEPT_BLOCK` on **all nine**, including `box: 3/3` and `purse: 4/4`, which it nonetheless marks OK | M | **MEASURED 2026-09-25.** The `NO_ACCEPT_BLOCK` column is the load-bearing half: a checker that cannot see `accept_block` usage in *any* of the nine — including the two it calls fully covered — is measuring against a runner shape the tree no longer has, so its 38 gaps cannot be taken as 38 uncovered endpoints without re-deriving them. Both readings still leave work: either the heavy suite genuinely exercises 17 of 55 genesis functions through the block path, or the checker must be rewritten against `EndpointSpec` before its number means anything. It also reports coverage per *function enum*, which is the measure `OBL-C88`'s repair used for `drain_protection`, so a rewritten checker has a template to follow |

| OBL-C140 | **OPEN** | A contract artifact carries no panic machinery and no first-party source path — the invariant `wasm_artifact_check.sh` states as SHALL | `contrib/wasm_artifact_check.sh --genesis`, over the nine genesis contracts' `dwow_*_contract.wasm`, against the build that produces them (`src/contract/native_token/Makefile:17-31`, whose `--remap-path-prefix` covers `$HOME/.cargo` and `$HOME/.rustup` and nothing else) | 2026-09-25: `bash contrib/wasm_artifact_check.sh --genesis` **exit 1**, all nine DIRTY — `rust_begin_unwind(1)` in every one, `panic_bounds_check(1)` in eight, and five also embedding first-party paths: `src/sdk/src/crypto/merkle_node.rs` in native_token, promissory_note, purse and box, plus `src/contract/native_token/src/model/mod.rs`, `src/sdk/src/crypto/contract_id.rs` (deployooor) and `src/sdk/src/crypto/pedersen.rs` | M | **MEASURED 2026-09-25. Nothing in the repository runs this check** — it is a sibling of `OBL-C137`, with the difference that its subject is the nine genesis artifacts, so the reading has to distinguish two invariants that are easy to conflate. **Purity is not obviously breached.** Genesis's root requirement is that it be a pure function of its inputs, and the paths that survive are *relative* (`src/sdk/...`), which is exactly what makes them host-independent — the remap's `$HOME` rules exist because an absolute path would break the pin on any other machine, and no absolute path was found. What **is** breached is the check's own stricter claim, and the counts say why it is worth a row rather than a shrug: `panic_bounds_check(1)` and `rust_begin_unwind(1)` are counts of *one* surviving site each, in artifacts this tree has spent a campaign removing panic machinery from — so either one indexing site per contract escaped the `deny(clippy::unwrap_used, expect_used)` rollout, or the symbols are reachable through a path that rollout cannot see. The header's own warning is the reason to care: a panic location is a data-section string plus a line number, `strip` cannot remove either, and any edit that shifts line numbers after such a site moves the genesis hash. Not repaired here: finding the surviving site is its own investigation, and rebuilding any genesis artifact is a pin-moving action that needs sign-off before it starts |

| OBL-C141 | **OPEN** | A runner reports its run's verdict through the channel callers check | `contrib/test_verdicts.sh:96-106` — `run` mode: `cargo test "$@" > "$log" 2>&1`, `rc=$?`, `echo "test_verdicts: cargo exit=$rc" >&2`, then `parse_one "$log"` and no `exit "$rc"` | 2026-09-25, T-B chunk B1: the wrapper printed `test_verdicts: cargo exit=101` on stderr while the process exited **0**, and a background-task harness reading only the exit status recorded the chunk as a success | M | **MEASURED 2026-09-25, on the campaign's own chunk, and it is the reading that almost made B1 a green result.** The information is present and honest — cargo's status is captured and printed, and the log is complete — but it is printed to *stderr* and then dropped: the script's exit status is `parse_one`'s, which is 0 for a log it can read. So every caller whose verdict channel is the exit code (which is how `scripts/run-all-tests.sh` reports every gate it runs) sees a failing run as a passing one. The counter-reading, stated so the row is not over-claimed: `run` is documented as a *reporting* wrapper, its product is the TSV on stdout, and a human reading either stream gets the truth. What makes it `M` rather than `L` is the same thing that makes a gate that cannot fail `M`: the failure mode is a silent green, and this wrapper's whole purpose is to be the thing that tells you what the suite did. The fix is one line, `exit "$rc"` after `parse_one`, and it belongs with `OBL-C135`, whose subject is the same script's report |

| OBL-C142 | **OPEN** | A staleness verdict is decided by content, not by a timestamp — so a check that fires sends its reader to a real defect and not to a needless rebuild | `bin/dwowd/src/tests/check_zkbin_freshness.sh` (its `-nt` mtime comparison and the "VK mismatch will occur" claim it derives from it), over `src/contract/box/proof/take.zk.bin` (mtime 2026-09-24 23:32:33) against `src/contract/box/dwow_box_contract.wasm` (mtime 2026-09-24 22:02:07); the decision the timestamp cannot make is made by the wasm's own bytes, since the contract embeds its circuits with `include_bytes!` | 2026-09-25: `check_zkbin_freshness.sh` **exit 1** — `[STALE] box: take.zk.bin is newer than dwow_box_contract.wasm`, then `[FAIL] 1 stale WASM(s) found`. The content test in the same session: `take.zk.bin`'s **206 bytes are present verbatim in the 300162-byte wasm** (`put.zk.bin`'s 228 bytes likewise), so harness and wasm embed identical circuit bytes and no mismatch exists. box is the only one of the 32 contracts in this relation — a sweep of every contract's `.zk.bin` against its own wasm finds this single pair, by 5426 s | M | **MEASURED 2026-09-25 — a false positive that points at a genesis artifact and asks for a pin-moving rebuild.** The comparison is `-nt`: anything that rewrites a `.zk.bin` — including a generator run that produces byte-identical output — moves the mtime and trips the check, whose message then asserts a VK mismatch that it has not established. The direction is what makes this worth a row rather than a note: the remedy it prints is "run with `--fix` to rebuild", box is one of the nine genesis contracts, and rebuilding a genesis artifact is exactly the action this register requires sign-off for because it can move `bin/dwowd/genesis_hash.txt`. A false positive whose suggested fix is a consensus-constant change is worse than a check that stays quiet, so the instrument's rule should be the one already measured here: **decide by content** — `python3 -c 'print(open("proof/x.zk.bin","rb").read() in open("dwow_x_contract.wasm","rb").read())'` is the whole test, and it answers both directions. Nothing in the repository runs this checker either (sibling of `OBL-C137`), and its own note records that a stale pair caused a real identity-test failure once (k=12 vs k=13) — which is the reason it exists and the reason its signal has to be trustworthy |

**OBL-C72 — read-in-apply. 66 sites, 9 contracts, zero genesis.** §A.4.7 states the rule as a list
of four denied functions and §B.2.2 supplies the consequence verbatim: *"An `apply` function that
calls any read-triad function will fail at runtime with `CALLER_ACCESS_DENIED`."* The mechanism is
not partial: `vm_runtime.rs:954` runs apply as `ContractSection::Update`, and **no** read function
admits `Update` — not `db_get`, not its `_local` variant, not `db_contains_key`. The class is wider
than the four names §A.4.7 lists: every read-only getter carries the same `[Deploy, Metadata, Exec]`
ACL, so `get_verifying_block_height` (four sites) and `get_call_index` (one) are in it too. There is
no workaround idiom, so every one of these sites is a call that cannot succeed.

| contract | sites | first example |
|---|---|---|
| `labor_market` | 24 | `entrypoint.rs:920 create_job_apply_v1()` → `db_contains_key` |
| `insurance_market` | 15 | `entrypoint/close_market.rs:63 …_process_update_v1()` → `db_get` |
| `dao_escrow` | 12 | `entrypoint.rs:554 pay_premium_apply_v1()` → `db_get` |
| `pool_stake` | 9 | `entrypoint.rs:487 apply_join_pool_update()` → `db_get` |
| `subscription` | 2 | `entrypoint.rs:669 update_usage_apply_v1()` → `db_get` |
| `bearer_bond` | 1 | `entrypoint/mod.rs:1237 apply_prove_coverage()` → `db_get` |
| `bridge` | 1 | `entrypoint.rs:811 get_current_timestamp()` → `db_get` |
| `relayer_endowment` | 1 | `relayer.rs:379 apply_register_fee_schedule()` → `db_get` |
| `stablecoin` | 1 | `entrypoint.rs:1738 apply_redeem_stable_update()` → `db_contains_key` |

**Confirmed at runtime, not only by reading**: `pool_stake`'s heavyweight suite fails at apply with
`ContractError(CallerAccessDenied)`, and the first statement of `apply_join_pool_update` is a
`db_get` on the registry tree. This row exists because that failure was previously recorded as "a
fresh thread" while §B.2.2 had already named both the rule and the error — the row is placed here so
the next reader does not have to rediscover it.

**OBL-C73 — write-in-exec. 20 sites, 4 contracts, zero genesis.** The mirror rule. `db_set`/`db_del`
admit `[Deploy, Update]` only and `merkle_add` admits `[Update]`, so a write in exec fails with the
same `CALLER_ACCESS_DENIED`:

| contract | sites | first example |
|---|---|---|
| `tender` | 9 | `entrypoint.rs:484 create_tender_v1()` → `db_set` |
| `drain_protection` | 7 | `entrypoint.rs:368 init_fund_process_instruction_v1()` → `db_set` |
| `pool_stake` | 2 | `entrypoint.rs:532 process_leave_pool_instruction()` → `db_set` |
| `dex` | 2 | `entrypoint/set_transparency_level.rs:42` → `db_set` |

`pool_stake:532` is the already-recorded LeavePool write; `:1112`
(`process_rebalance_pool_shares_instruction`) is its sibling. Union with `OBL-C72`: **12 distinct
contracts, 86 sites, zero genesis.**

**OBL-C74 — the manifest capability block exists only in genesis.** `type-system.md` §13 makes
`wallet_construct` *"a pure function of primitives and required barbs, not contract names"*, and
§A.2.2 makes the barb declaration mandatory: *"Every contract SHALL declare in its manifest which
barbs each action requires."* Measured across all 32 manifests: `[[actions]]` is present in 9 (the 8
genesis contracts that have it, plus `dex`), `required_barbs` / `note_schema` / `primitives` in **8,
all genesis** — and in **none** of the 24 non-genesis contracts. For those contracts the declaration
§A.0.3 builds the fundamental invariant on does not exist, so the invariant cannot be stated and the
generic wallet has nothing to compose against. `identity` is the genesis exception, missing all six
of `[[capabilities]]`, `[[actions]]`, `required_barbs`, `note_schema`, `primitives` and `witness_map`
— consistent with it being the one genesis contract that documents a `Box::Take` child call it never
enforces (`entrypoint.rs:670-677`, `OBL-Z17`). `witness_map` is present in 3 of 32, and §A.6.1
already records that nothing validates it. **Not part of this row, and recorded so it is not read as
one:** `[[cost_profiles]]` is absent from **all 32** manifests, genesis included — a schema-wide gap
rather than a genesis/non-genesis one.

**OBL-C75 — a published public input the host does not use.** Found while fixing `pool_stake`'s
`OBL-C72`/`OBL-C73` sites, and stated separately because it is a different failure: the circuit
derives an id, `get_metadata` publishes it, and the host then recomputes a *different* id and keys
state by that. The published input is decorative — the proof binds a value nothing consumes — and
because the recomputation fed on `get_verifying_block_height()`, **the client could not predict the
id of the record it had just created**: the height is not known when a proof is built. Three live
instances, all in `pool_stake`: `CreatePoolV1` ignored `derived_pool_id` and keyed the pool by
`poseidon_hash([height])`, `JoinPoolV1` ignored `derived_member_id`, and `AllocateCoverageV1` ignored
`derived_allocation_id`. Two pools created in one block also collapsed onto one key. Fixed
2026-09-22 by using the proof-bound field in each case and deleting the two `derive_*` helpers, which
had no other caller.

**The class is bounded, and this is how.** A scan for host-side id derivations that depend on the
verifying block height — the sharp form of the defect, since a derivation over values the client
already knows is merely a second source of truth, not unknowable — now finds **one** remaining, and
it is **dead code**: `game_room/src/model/mod.rs:367 derive_room_id()` with zero call sites. The
register carries it here rather than in a row of its own because an uncalled function cannot key
anything; it should be deleted when `game_room` is next touched, not before, so the deletion is not
mistaken for a behaviour change.

**OBL-C77 — the bare-empty metadata arm, and why it is not a style nit.** `contract-standards.md` §3
makes an *empty* metadata buffer the documented rejection signal, and the host implements exactly
that: it decodes the metadata as `Vec<(String, Vec<pallas::Base>)>` (`execution.rs:423`) and, when the
buffer is zero bytes, reports *"contract signalled EMPTY metadata, which is the documented rejection
signal"*. So an arm written `F => vec![]` — a bare `Vec`, not an *encoded* empty one — is a function
that **cannot be called at all**: the host rejects every invocation before exec runs. The correct
form is what `native_token`'s `plaintext_call_get_metadata` (`entrypoint/mod.rs:922`) does for a
no-proofs call: encode both empty vectors, so the buffer is a valid 4-byte (or 8-byte) encoding
rather than nothing. `pool_stake`'s `LeavePoolV1` reached this at runtime
(`metadata-decode-zkp … contract signalled EMPTY metadata`, block 6) and is fixed.

**Measured scope, separated by reachability, because most of these arms are dead.** Across the tree
the bare-empty arm appears for:

- **Reachable** — real callable non-ZK functions: `insurance_market` 11 (`CreateMarketV1`,
  `RegisterRiskTypeV1`, `UnderwriteV1`, `FileClaimV1`, `ResolveClaimV1`, `WithdrawPremiumV1`,
  `UpdatePremiumV1`, `RetireRiskTypeV1`, `CloseMarketV1`, `DeactivateUnderwriterV1`,
  `ResolveClaimWithCapabilityV1`), `tender` 3 (`CancelTenderV1`, `RejectBidV1`,
  `CreateTenderWithCapabilityV1`), `pool_stake` 5 (fixed), and **`labor_market` 3**
  (`CancelV1`, `CreateJobWithCapabilityV1`, `CreateJobWithMilestonesAndCapabilityV1`,
  `entrypoint.rs:274-277`, `:341`, `:359`) — added 2026-09-22, having been absent from this
  scope in its first revision. `CancelV1` carries the comment *"CancelV1 has no ZK circuit"*: the
  intent is right and the encoding is wrong, which is why it is the arm that most looks
  deliberate and is still uncallable.
- **Unreachable by construction** — the `Initialize` arm in `box`, `purse`, `multisig`, `dex`,
  `otc_swap` and `escrow`. Initialization is the separate `__initialize` entrypoint, so no
  `ContractCall` ever dispatches to that arm. Recorded because it is the reason three *genesis*
  contracts (box, purse, multisig) carry the pattern without moving the pin — the arm is dead, not
  correct. It should still be fixed when each contract is next touched.
- **Catch-alls needing per-contract determination** — `baccarat` and `darktoshi_dice` write `_ =>
  vec![]`, so whether any function falls through depends on their enum coverage; that is a read per
  contract, not a global claim.

**Why these three and not more.** The other candidate classes found in the same pass were left out
deliberately, because the measurement that produced them is not trustworthy and a row that overstates
its evidence is worse than no row. The `set_return_data` selector-byte rule (`A.1.5`) was scored
wrongly three times — twice because the byte is pushed inside the `encode_*_update` helper rather
than at the call site — and its residual list (`bearer_bond` 8, `betting_stake` 1, `insurance_market`
1, `roulette` 1) is a **candidate list, not a finding**, needing each exec/apply pair read by hand.
The "version every state struct" rule was not measured at all: the scan counted `*PublicInputs` and
`*CallData` structs, which are not persisted state. The lesson is the one `safety.md` RC5 keeps
teaching — a scan that reads one side of a pair manufactures defects — and it is recorded here rather
than left in a scratch file.

**OBL-C78 — the host's public inputs and the circuit's instances must agree in count *and* order.**
The rule this row names is old; what is new is that it is measurable, and that measuring it shows the
class was never closed. `check-circuit-metadata-alignment.sh` has always been described as comparing
"position-for-position, three ways" — `circuit constrain_instance order == entrypoint metadata push
order == client to_vec order` — while the code compared **counts**, and only for the eleven contracts
in its allowlist. So `subscription/cancel` passed the gate while pushing `pallas::Base::zero()` at
**all four** of the positions its circuit constrains, and `stablecoin/init` passed while pushing
zeros where its circuit constrains `tx_binding` and `tx_nonce`. A vector of the right length whose
entries are wrong is what "counts agree" cannot express.

The convention is **not** an open design question, and the register previously deferred it as one
(*"it needs a decision about the convention, not a patch"*, on `dao_escrow`). It is implemented and
green in genesis: `tx_binding = poseidon_hash([3, tx_commitment, tx_nonce])`, the domain constant at
`src/sdk/src/crypto/constants.rs:57`, carried in params and published by
`box/src/entrypoint.rs:70-83` and `purse/src/entrypoint.rs:75-106`, with clients at
`promissory_note/src/client/issue.rs:181`, `register_type.rs:187`, `revoke.rs:293`. The worked
template is `pool_stake/src/client/create_pool.rs:73-144` — the contract repaired earlier in this
campaign — whose four parts are `compute_tx_binding()`, `compute_public_inputs()`, `to_witnesses()`
and `*_v1_proof()`, and whose comment names what it replaced: *"This was a literal `Base::zero()`
written as **both** the public input and the witness, while the circuit constrains the witness to
equal this hash — so the proof was unsatisfiable, not merely unbound."*

Seven contracts were known to be dead at their first ZK endpoint, in two shapes — **literal zero**
(`dao_escrow`, `drain_protection`, `pool_stake`, `subscription`: the client satisfies the circuit
with a value the circuit rejects, so the proof is unsatisfiable) and **omitted** (`insurance_market`,
`labor_market`, `tender`: the host publishes no tx pair at all while the circuits constrain both, so
the proof is unverifiable). `pool_stake` is fixed. The rest are measured per circuit in the plan
file, read from both sides of the pair: `labor_market` publishes three values where `create_job`'s
circuit constrains five, publishes `dao_escrow_bulla` where `dispute` requires `dispute_reason_hash`,
publishes `milestone_count` and `completed_payment` that `refund` does not constrain, and leaves
`milestone_payment`'s namespace pushed by nobody. This is why those suites are dead, and why every
other fix on those contracts lands unverified until it is closed.

**OBL-C79 — a gate must name what it did not check, and prove it checked the rest.**
`scripts/check-circuit-metadata-alignment.sh:71` defined an eleven-name list called `GENESIS` (holding
three non-genesis contracts, added one at a time as each was repaired) and iterated only that list.
The gate examined **11 of 32** contracts and printed `Passed: 70  Failed: 0` /
`PASS: All circuits have matching metadata push counts`, saying nothing about the 21 it skipped —
every broken-proof contract among them. `script/circuit_metadata_exceptions.txt` was empty, so this
was not a declared exception: the coverage was simply absent, and the scope was not reported. This is
the OBL-C78 class surviving inside the instrument built to detect it.

The other four gates do **not** share the shape, which bounds the row: `check-circuit-domain-
separation.sh:27`, `check-pubkey-binding.sh:44`, `check-artifact-freshness.sh:67`,
`check-phase-host-functions.sh:175` and `script/circuit_instance_derivation.py:520` all glob the whole
tree. This was the only allowlist.

Widening the coverage then exposed three latent parse bugs in the same gate, each of which would have
been filed as a defect against correct code — and did, before being caught by reading the file:
a path-qualified constant (`crate::DAO_ESCROW_ZKAS_INIT_NS_V2.to_string()`, which the pattern
`([A-Z0-9_]+)` cannot match because `crate` is lowercase) accounted for 34 phantom "no metadata push
carries" findings; the `let zk_public_inputs = vec![(NS.to_string(), vec![…])]` idiom, used by
`dao_escrow` and `game_room` with no `push` call at all, accounted for 19 more; and `game_room`'s
`get_metadata` living in `src/lib.rs:236` rather than an entrypoint file accounted for twelve. None
of the three was visible while the allowlist constrained which contracts were parsed.

The generalisation is the row. A checker's coverage is part of its claim, and a checker that reports
a verdict must report the scope it did not examine — which is why the script now prints its covered
and circuit-free sets, reconciles the walked set against the declared set so a future `continue`
cannot shrink coverage invisibly, and refuses a hand-picked single contract that the enumeration did
not itself find.

**And the register recorded this gate as satisfying an obligation it was not satisfying.** `OBL-C20`
— *"Public-input ordering is verified by parse-and-compare, not by count"* — was listed under
"Verified 2026-09-22: satisfied, with the evidence", citing this script as comparing
"position-for-position, three ways". It compared counts. **`OBL-C20` is not satisfied**, and the
verification pass that declared it satisfied is itself an instance of the failure the register
exists to catch: the evidence was the script's *description of itself* rather than its code.

## Verified 2026-09-22: what these rows actually are

Every row added or already present was re-read against the code before a remediation campaign was
planned against it. **Twenty are satisfied** and need no fix; five rest on premises that have
moved; one is worse than its row said; and **one was recorded as satisfied and is not** — `OBL-C20`,
corrected on 2026-09-22 when the gate it cited was read instead of trusted (`OBL-C79`). Recording
this is the point — a campaign that starts by
fixing things that are already fixed is the failure mode this register exists to prevent.

**A counting correction, appended 2026-09-22 because the two counts disagreed with each other and with
the list.** This section said "twenty-one" and the section below said "twenty-two"; the paragraph
immediately following enumerates **twenty** satisfied rows. A reader who greps the paragraph for IDs
finds **twenty-one**, because `OBL-C45` is named there only as a cross-reference — "the concern folds
into `OBL-C45`" — and `C45` is not satisfied; it carries that concern. The count is **twenty**, which is
the list; the two bare numerals were miscounts, and they are corrected in place rather than by rewriting
the paragraphs that carried them. This is the same defect class as `OBL-C10`'s ungreppable closure
marker: a register whose counts and statuses cannot be re-derived from its own rows stops being
auditable, which is the only thing it is for.

**A gap this correction found, and which was then closed rather than left recorded.** The twenty rows
enumerated below are satisfied *by that paragraph*; as of the correction above their own table rows
carried **no status marker** (`OBL-C18`, `C23`, `C24`, `C34`, `C36`, `C38`–`C41`, `C46`–`C49`, `C51`,
`C59`, `C60`, `Z15`, `T3`, `T6`, `T10`), so a reader grepping the tables for a closure marker missed
**eighteen of the twenty** — only `Z15` and `T10` carried one. Those markers were added on
2026-09-22 to the fourteen still missing one (`Z15`/`T10` already had theirs, and `T6` already read
**proved**), each phrased as what it is — *"the evidence is named in the 'Verified 2026-09-22'
section below"* — rather than as a fresh verdict, because the verification was that section's work
and this pass only made its result countable. The same was done for the four finality rows whose
fixes were recorded only in prose (`OBL-C68`–`C71`). With those, `scripts/register-status.sh` reports
the register's per-row status without a human counting by hand, which is the whole point.

**Satisfied, with the evidence.** `OBL-C18` — the cached `target[H-1]` fast path
(`src/linear/src/consensus.rs:359`, the "M-1 fix") bounds the traversal by `TIMESTAMP_WINDOW`, not by
genesis. `OBL-C23` — `HANDSHAKE_TIMEOUT = 15s` (`src/linear/src/sync_connection.rs:70`, carrying
the `M7.3` reference). `OBL-C24` — `disconnect_block` reverses all ten trees `connect_block` writes,
including `uncles`/`uncles_by_height` ("M7 — symmetric") and `contracts` via the per-block undo batch.
`OBL-C34` — the handshake now **admits** a peer presenting no genesis, validating it downstream
against the pin (`sync_connection.rs:441-455`); this is precisely the bootstrap path the daemon-sync
analysis said was missing, so that finding is closed. `OBL-C36` — no zero-vector fallback survives;
`OBL-C38` — `chain_state.rs` has **0** `.lock().unwrap()` and 66 poison-recovering sites (note: 7 bare
ones remain elsewhere in `bin/dwowd`, which is a *different* site set); `OBL-C39` — `uncle_coin_set`
is gone, uncle dedup is sled-backed via `stored_uncle_hashes()`; `OBL-C40` — `execution.rs:656-682`
rejects uncle-vs-uncle key collisions; `OBL-C41` — four `miner_task` error paths re-insert mempool
transactions; `OBL-C46` — the non-ZK fallback no longer exists, the template is plaintext by design;
`OBL-C47` — `Clone for PoWConsensus` builds fresh atomics and `save_to_batch` reads under `Acquire`;
`OBL-C48` — `execution.rs:717-748` detects Deployooor duplicate keys; `OBL-C49` (M-4, M-14) and
`OBL-C51` (M-13) — metadata decode failures now carry `fail_stage`, `total_supply` uses a typed
conversion, and `block.rs:612` uses `checked_sub` with a supply-invariant error; `OBL-C59` —
`prev_coin` no longer exists (the concern folds into `OBL-C45`); `OBL-C60` —
`expected_reward(height: BlockHeight)` is u64 throughout. On the other surfaces: `OBL-Z15`
(`circuit_metadata_exceptions.txt` is empty; `bearer_bond_commitment_vectors.rs` pins the values),
`OBL-T3` (all five barb representations agree), `OBL-T6` (`Capability/Inversion.lean:101`, proved and
one-directional), `OBL-T10` (zero code occurrences of `native_decide`/`ofReduceBool`/`trustCompiler`).

**Premises that moved, so the rows must not be read as written.** `OBL-C17` —
`reject_nondeterministic_features` is **not** a no-op; it is live at
`src/runtime/vm_runtime.rs:354`, so the question is now whether `0xFE` alone is covered.
`OBL-C22` — `perform_reorg` no longer exists; reorg is `reorg_to_heavier_chain`
(`bin/dwowd/src/task/consensus_linear.rs:137`), so the depth-cap question must be re-asked of it.
`OBL-C42` — **downgraded from "three of four call sites" to one of three**: `prepare_block` takes
last, and the other two add a compensating `put_competing_blocks` recovery, so data is no longer lost
even though the take still precedes the fallible work. `OBL-C29` — **better than "absent"**: Path A is
implemented, Path B is deferred *with its reason recorded in the Rust* (a plurality vote over peer
tips is a multi-peer decision a single connection cannot make), and what remains absent is the
`Off`/`Relaxed`/`Strict` mode parameterization and the Path-B vote itself.
`OBL-C31` — **downgraded to partial**: a forward-referenced derived operand is no longer unhandled
(`prover_impl.rs:487` errors on it), but nothing enforces the DAG *at parse time* as the row requires.

**One row is worse than recorded.** `OBL-C52` is not merely "dedup uses a header serialization" — the
two sides of the same set **disagree**. Insert uses `block.hash_with_vm()` (`chain_state.rs:890`,
`:966`); take, put and prune use `blake3::hash(&dwow_serialize(&b.header))` (`:700`, `:776`, `:794`).
So `take_competing_blocks` removes the wrong key, the block-hash entry survives, `put_competing_blocks`
inserts a second key, and a legitimately re-submitted block is dedup-rejected. That is a live
correctness bug in the competing-block path.

**Stale prose in three rows, corrected here.** `OBL-Z5` — `script/circuit_free_instances.txt` holds
**36** entries, not 43; the five whose mechanism is *absent* are confirmed as the dex fee and
bearer_bond's four coverage-report quantities. `OBL-Z6` — the cited
`src/sdk/src/crypto/sinsemilla.rs` **does not exist**; Sinsemilla lives at
`src/sdk/src/crypto/constants/sinsemilla.rs`, `merkle_node.rs` and `src/zk/vm.rs`. `OBL-Z16` — the
residue list now names `stablecoin`'s `report_timestamp` and `attest_value`'s `attestation_id`, both
since classified, so the live sites are the two `insurance_market` ones. `OBL-T5` and `OBL-T9` — the
counts moved with `oracleOperatorType`: **14** capability resources, not 12 or 13, and 17 primitives,
not 16.

## Audited 2026-09-23: every row, adversarially

All **107** rows were re-read against the code, not re-checked against their own prose. The pass had
one rule, and it is the rule this register has needed since `OBL-C20`:

> **A row earns `C` or `H` only if a path can be named from an untrusted input to the consequence.**
> A check that cannot fail, an absent gate, a doc/code drift, a detector's raw candidate, an unpinned
> test and a moved count are all **observations** — recorded, not ranked.

Closed rows are exempt: on a `CLOSED`/`SATISFIED`/`FIXED` row the letter names the class of property
the closure guards, which needs no path. That distinction is now stated in the status-vocabulary note
above, because its absence is what let this column be read two ways for as long as it has existed.

### The genuine findings — four, of 107 rows

| Row | Sev | What survived |
|---|---|---|
| `OBL-C21` | **C** | With `bridge-verify` off — and nothing in the tree enables it, the only mention being its declaration at `bridge/Cargo.toml:51` — an Ethereum deposit is accepted when `merkle_proof` is merely **non-empty** (`bridge/src/entrypoint.rs:320-333`; the `else` binds to the inner `if`, so a grep-based read concludes the opposite). The deposit requires a `promissory_note::issue_v1` child call (`:258-294`), so it **mints a wrapped note against no external backing**. Precondition: the bridge must be deployed — it is not genesis |
| `OBL-C67` | **C** | The merge-mining admission policy is opt-in on `monerod_url`; unset is the default, and `block_acceptor.rs:225-240` admits the block, its own warning stating that the receipts "prove coinbase inclusion in some serialized block, which a fabricated block can also satisfy". **A fabricated block is minted** |
| `OBL-C34` | **H** | **The genesis pin is not enforced on the bootstrap path.** `check_genesis_pin`'s only production call sites are `bin/dwowd/src/lib.rs:893`/`:910`, both inside `init_linear`'s `create_genesis == true` branch. Every miner and observer runs `CREATE_GENESIS=false`, dials with no genesis, is admitted, and the **only** gate on the received genesis is a 4-byte magic check (`consensus_linear.rs:382-388`) the code itself calls "defense-in-depth". `apply_genesis_deployments` then deploys whatever WASM the block carries — there is no pin comparison in that file. The comment the closure rested on (`sync_connection.rs:446`) names a call that does not happen |
| `OBL-Z16` | **H** | The two live sites are real, but they are `OBL-Z17` arriving at a consumer: `constrain_equal_base(capability_predicate_result, ONE)` pins a witness to a **constant**, so the circuit asserts a predicate it never computes, and the host checks only that the market *has* a capability configured |

`C21` and `C67` are the same shape — **verification implemented, not enabled by default** — which is
`RC9`, "safety that is opt-in", with two live value-bearing instances sitting in this register's own
rows and documented as accepted trade-offs rather than as one pattern.

`OBL-C34` is a different shape and worth naming separately: **a comment asserts the enforcement.** The
verification that a closure rests on was a description of a check, not the check — `OBL-C20`'s failure
mode, in the code rather than in a script.

### The refutations — this register's own failure mode, ten times

Ten rows were **refuted**: their claim is false of the code. Four of them failed the same way.

| Row | What was believed | What is true |
|---|---|---|
| `OBL-C31` | "nothing enforces the derived-rule DAG at parse time" | It is enforced at `src/sdk/src/prover.rs:201-217`, pinned by two tests — added `4a6e5c5609`, **before the row was written**. The row searched `bin/dww/src/prover_impl.rs` |
| `Z9` | "`multisig` still FAILS" | Fixed by `55a2de9c42`, the commit its own sibling `Z11` records. `signer_pub` survives only in comments and a stale README |
| `Z6` | cites `src/sdk/src/crypto/sinsemilla.rs` | **That file does not exist** — the constants live in `crypto/constants/sinsemilla.rs` |
| `C73` | the exec-write rule holds | It does not: `scripts/check-phase-host-functions.sh` reports **7** violations, all `drain_protection` |

**The generalisation: the register's method — search one file for a keyword, conclude "not enforced" —
is how four of these ten went wrong.** `OBL-C20` is the same error against a *script's* self-description
instead of a *file's* contents. A negative claim ("nothing enforces this") needs a whole-tree search
before it may be recorded, and this pass found four that did not have one.

The other six: `C12` (a clone *is* zeroized — `SecretKey` derives `Clone` **and** has `Drop`; the real
leak is raw `pallas::Base` copies through `inner()`), `C16` (the arithmetic 72/20/69 reproduces
exactly, but the classifier tests `Err`-**adjacency**, not guard direction — **4** sites of the shape
`if cid != ContractId::ZERO { validate }` skip the check entirely, and init writes `[0u8;32]` with no
other writer), `C28` (the dedup key is inserted *before* the per-height cap, so an over-cap key has no
removal path), `C51` (`saturating_add` is live on the cumulative supply in three places and a
`checked_add` was never introduced), `C37` (the decreasing-timestamp route is closed and the
**equal**-timestamp route to the same zero interval still reaches a branch that makes the target
~11% *easier* while its comment says "10% harder"), `Z18` (its one adjudicated-live site is
mis-cited; no host path consumes it).

`OBL-Z14` is the mirror image: recorded `FAILS`, and it is **closed** — the circuit instances the
reporter pair and the host rejects on the stored governance point.

### The status column, and why it is now enforced

Added to all seven tables immediately after `ID`. Before it, **40 of 107 rows were invisible to the
guard**: 31 carried their token somewhere other than the cell it read, and 9 carried none — while it
printed "107 rows walked". `scripts/register-status.sh --check` now reads a fixed cell and *requires*
a token there, negative-controlled (`REGISTER=<copy> … --check` fails on a blanked cell, exits 0 on
this file). Each row carries one word from the vocabulary above.

### What this pass did not do

- **It did not fix the defects.** Four genuine findings and ~24 severity demotions are recorded; the
  fixes are separate work. `OBL-C21` and `OBL-C67` are the two that should be decided first.
- **It did not adjudicate every candidate.** `OBL-Z18`'s remaining 56 sites and 13 of `OBL-Z16`'s 15 *(superseded in count only, both halves: 56 of the 57 were adjudicated in six slices by 2026-09-23 — see `OBL-Z18` — and `OBL-Z16`'s thirteen are classified in that row, which was reconciled against the gate the same day. The rule this pass calibrated is unchanged, and the sentence below remains true of this pass.)*
  are still unread — the bar forbids calling them defects, and this pass does not call them clean.
- **It did not build or test.** One runnable check was executed — `check-phase-host-functions.sh`, for
  the `C73` refutation — and `check-circuit-metadata-alignment.sh` for `C78`/`C20`. Where a claim's
  truth needed a build (a Lean count, a test's pass count), the row says so rather than implying
  coverage.

## What this register implies

Rewritten **2026-09-22**, after the docs clean-up promoted the remediated corpus's residue into this
register and every row was re-read against the code (see "Verified 2026-09-22" above — the **twenty**
rows enumerated there were already satisfied). Ordered by what unblocks the most, with severity
breaking ties:

1. ~~**OBL-C35, C52, C36-class: the consensus wrong-block and lost-block defects.**~~ **DONE, and
   this item was stale when it was written — corrected 2026-09-23.** Both named rows had already
   closed on 2026-09-22 and this list was not updated with them, which is the failure the register's
   own rule about prose exists to prevent ("a fix recorded only in prose is not recorded" — and its
   converse: a fix recorded in a row must not survive as an open item in prose).
   `OBL-C35` — closed `e252e4f35c`; `stratum_submit`'s `CanonicalExtension` arm now calls
   `broadcast_block` with the block's uncles (`bin/dwowd/src/rpc/stratum.rs:711`). `OBL-C52` —
   closed 2026-09-22; **all eight uses** of the dedup key now call one `competing_dedup_key`
   (`src/linear/src/chain_state.rs:763`), including the insert at `:834` and the take and prune at
   `:739`/`:852`, and `:949` carries the note recording that the key is that function's and not
   `hash_with_vm`'s. Verified on both sides of the set, 2026-09-23.
   **The live consensus residue is elsewhere**: see `OBL-C19` (a call that overruns its wall-clock
   budget is warned about, not terminated), `OBL-C44` (a reorg does not re-admit the disconnected
   transactions) and `OBL-C80` (split out of `OBL-C67` on 2026-09-23, which is now `CLOSED`; a fabricated merge-mined block is admitted when `monerod_url` is
   unset, which is the default). **Severities in this paragraph were re-measured 2026-09-23**: `OBL-C80` was raised `H` → `C` and is now this register's **only open critical**, and `OBL-C82` is a new `H` (`stablecoin`'s interest accrual, found by the `OBL-Z18` sweep) — so an order that once placed one critical here and one in item 2 now places the critical here alone, with item 2's `OBL-C21` landed fail-closed at `M` and item 1's `OBL-C67` closed.
2. **OBL-C21 — the Bridge's cross-chain verification.** The largest item in the register. Two of five
   chains (Monero DLEq, Litecoin Merkle) are **already written and have never been compiled**, because
   no build enables `bridge-verify`; Ethereum MPT needs the traversal its RLP decoder is missing; Zcash
   Groth16 and Aztec PLONK need a pairing dependency that does not exist in this tree. The live
   vulnerability is narrower than the row's framing: with the feature off, an Ethereum deposit is
   accepted when its proof is merely *non-empty* (`entrypoint.rs:320-333`), which is RC1's shape.
3. **OBL-Z17 — the capability check's remaining half is *possession*.** Stage 1 and Stage 2 landed
   2026-09-21: the predicate is proved, and the proof is about a credential an issuer actually signed
   (schema, issuer, threshold and commitment are all checked against the stored record). What remains
   is that the credential is a *box* and nothing consumes it — issuance emits no `Box::Put`, and
   verification requires no `Box::Take`. It is plumbing before it is a check: no fixture has a real
   box in the tree with a real merkle path, so the host check that *is* possession turns a passing
   flow red today. Touches genesis `identity` and `box`.
4. **OBL-Z16 — 15 instances a prover can parametrise away**, two of them live (both
   `insurance_market`'s `with_capability` circuits, which expose a `required_capability_id` nothing
   verifies). The other thirteen are inert or prover-parametrised and the repair per site is small
   once read. `OBL-Z1` — the same rule as a checker — stands at **897 instances over 181 circuits,
   15 unclassified**.
5. **OBL-C16 — 72 `ContractId::ZERO` guards across 20 contracts**, every one of them fail-open: an
   unconfigured contract id skips the validation instead of rejecting. All non-genesis (verified:
   zero such sites in any genesis contract), so no re-roll.
6. **OBL-C5 and OBL-C3 are the supply chain**, and both are only partly proved: the Lean
   `SupplyChain` theorems are structural inductions that hold *for any* `reward`, conditional on the
   Pedersen assumptions. Making `reward` a definition transcribed from `blockchain.rs` and proving
   non-increase plus the tail floor is what turns them into statements about the real schedule.
   `reward_monotone` remains an assumption; the odd case is the obstruction, and `Emission.lean`
   states precisely why the current lemma does not reach it.
7. **OBL-Z6 and OBL-Z12 are Lean models, not code changes.** Z6 wants Sinsemilla formalized in place
   of the model's substitution (`HashOps.lean:189`) — the deployed `vm.rs` already uses the real
   primitive. Z12 wants the comparison chip's semantics modelled so "253-bit comparison plus
   `range_check`" implies the integer reading over `ZMod p`. Neither needs `src/zk/vm.rs` edited.
8. **OBL-T7 is still a name, not a proof.** `NoFreeInstances` is carried as a hypothesis of
   `capabilityType_of_circuitDerivable` but no theorem discharges it; interpreting it needs a Lean
   model of Halo2 constraint-system semantics, the PCS and Fiat–Shamir. Related and next: `OBL-T9` —
   the barb alphabet does not separate the types it claims to (13 of 14 resources subsumed, and
   `purse_deposit`/`purse_withdrawal` have identical barb sets) — which touches all five
   representations including `src/sdk/src/capability.rs` and so carries a re-roll.
9. **`OBL-Z3`, `OBL-Z4`, `OBL-Z8` are gates that check the wrong thing** — domain *presence* rather
   than correctness, a hook that cannot see an inline `constrain_instance(ec_get_x(pk))`, and a
   validator that checks structural validity rather than whether the committed `.zk.bin` matches the
   current `.zk` source. All three are shell and Python, with no build impact.

**Re-measured 2026-09-24, item by item — the list was two days and roughly thirty units behind its
own rows, and a reader following it was sent at work already done.** Every figure below was re-run in
this pass rather than recalled, and each item's own date is kept. The process narrative *below* this
list is deliberately **not** re-measured here and cannot be: its claims are about heavyweight
`cargo test` sweeps and artifact staleness, which need a `--workspace` run this pass does not make, so
they stand as the dated records they are.

* **Item 1 — the live-residue list has moved twice under it, and one severity is overstated.**
  `OBL-C44` is `CLOSED` and `OBL-C19` is `ACCEPTED-WITH-REASON`, where this item names both as live
  residue; what survives is `OBL-C80`, and the severity sentence survives in substance while its
  wording does not. Measuring the severity cell of **all 154 rows** gives exactly one unresolved
  severity-`C` row — `OBL-C80`, status `PARTLY` — so "this register's **only open critical**" should
  read *only live critical*, `OPEN` being a status this row does not hold. And **`OBL-C82` measures
  `M` today, not the `H` this item calls it a new one of**, so that clause overstates in the direction
  a reader acts on.
* **Item 2 — the live vulnerability is closed, and this item cites the lines that record its
  closure.** `OBL-C21` is `ACCEPTED-WITH-REASON`, and `src/contract/bridge/src/entrypoint.rs:318-334`
  — the range named as the hole — now carries the fix's own account: *"With `bridge-verify` off,
  `verify_chain_proof` is fail-closed at every arm … The arm that stood here did not call this
  dispatch: it accepted `ExternalChain::Ethereum` whenever `merkle_proof` was merely non-empty — a
  shape check, not verification … all five chains refused in one place and one chain accepted in
  another, and the reachable one was the permissive one."* The item's first clause survives and was
  measured again: `bridge-verify` is declared at `src/contract/bridge/Cargo.toml:51`, and no
  `Cargo.toml`, `Makefile` or `scripts/*.sh` enables it.
* **Item 3 — `OBL-Z17` is `RESTATED` and its severity is `M`, not the weight its position implies.**
  Its own restatement narrows the residual rather than deferring it: possession *is* held by the proof
  — a caller must know the credential preimage, and the holder's key is derived from the credential
  secret — so what remains is not "require a call that exists" but the credential-as-box lifecycle
  across `identity`, `box` and the wallet, which is a design change, and that is why the row moved
  `C` → `M`.
* **Item 4 — both counts are stale, and the row's own cells lag its own last append.** Re-running the
  gate this item names (`bash scripts/check-circuit-instance-derivation.sh`) gives **181 circuits, 905
  `constrain_instance`, 40 declared-free, 11 unclassified**, with the eleven named by the gate. The
  item says 897 instances and 15 unclassified: the classification has moved 15 → 11, and the *total*
  has moved **897 → 905**, so eight `constrain_instance` sites entered the tree since — the
  classification is not the only thing that drifted. `OBL-Z16`'s summary cells still read "**15**
  instances" and "181 circuits, 894 instances, 15 unclassified", both dated 2026-09-21, while that
  row's own final append carries today's 905/11 — so the row is internally dated-lagged as well, and
  the fresh measurement belongs in the append rather than as a rewrite of the dated cells.
* **Item 5 — "72 guards" is low, and a grep count is not the guard count.** `grep -rn
  'ContractId::ZERO' --include=*.rs src/contract/` finds **78** occurrences across the same **20**
  contracts; classified by the surrounding line, **75** are comparisons against a stored id (68
  `promissory_note_cid`, 4 `identity_cid`, 2 `dao_cid`, 1 `multisig_cid`) and **3** are not
  comparisons at all — a comment and a deploy-path default in `labor_market`, and a
  `promissory_note_contract_id` struct-literal field in `stablecoin`. `OBL-C16`'s fail-open reading of
  these sites is that row's own and is not re-adjudicated here; what this pass corrects is the number
  and the method, because writing the grep's 78 down as the guards' count would be the same error in
  the other direction.
* **Item 6 — one of the two is `SATISFIED`.** `OBL-C3` is `SATISFIED` and `OBL-C5` is `PARTLY`, where
  this item says "both are only partly proved". The `reward_monotone` clause survives: it is one of
  the layer's **eight** assumptions today, and `Emission.lean` now also carries the two refutations of
  the natural lemmas for the odd case, so "the current lemma does not reach it" is true of a set of
  lemmas that is now smaller and named.
* **Item 7 — the citation has drifted off the thing it cites, and both residues have narrowed.**
  `HashOps.lean:189` is now `orchEmptyLeaf`; the substitution this item is about is `sinsemillaCrh` at
  **`HashOps.lean:195`**. `OBL-Z12`'s remaining half is no longer the chip's reading — that is proved,
  `BaseDivGadget.less_than_or_equal_integer_reading` — but the one declared exception,

* **Item 8 — the route is dead, and the re-roll is not owed.** "Interpreting it needs a Lean model of

  `ACCEPTED-WITH-REASON` with the alphabet **held by decision** and its two diff scripts now

  `src/sdk/src/capability.rs` and so carries a re-roll" is stale in both halves: the representations

* **Item 9 — the three descriptions are right and only their dispositions are missing.** Each was read
  against its own row and each matches that row's summary cell to the word — `OBL-Z3`'s
  domain-*presence* check that never asks whether the constant is the right one, `OBL-Z4`'s
  line-anchored staged-files hook that cannot see an inline `constrain_instance(ec_get_x(pk))`, and
  `OBL-Z8`'s structural validation rather than a `.zk.bin`-versus-`.zk` comparison. This is the one
